# JAN-EXECREM — Design Working Papers (appendix, machine-generated 2026-07-24)
*Full output of the remediation design run (`wf_2ea2700b-c4c`, 13 agents): six per-family designs, each with its adversarial critique, plus the integration synthesis. The distilled decisions live in **JAN-EXECREM-DS-001**; this appendix is the evidence behind them and is not itself normative.*

---

## F1-unenforced-rules — Unenforced / vacuous rule enforcement (the tautology family)

### Root cause
Every defect in this family is one mechanism: **the authority to refuse is expressed as hand-inlined, per-call-site code over locally-convenient data, with no declaration of which rule is being enforced where.** The ratified rule catalogue (`packages/rph-domain/vocab/m11-execution.json`, `m12-conformance.json`) and the pure kernel (`packages/rph-domain/src/execution.ts`) are correct and unit-tested; the *wiring* is where the rules die, and it dies in four distinguishable ways that all come from the same absence:

1. **SELF-CANCELLING ARGUMENTS.** `execution.ts:801-803` computes `hasOutput` then passes `explicitNoOutput: !hasOutput` into a kernel whose rule is `hasOutput || explicitNoOutput` — verified byte-exact; the predicate is `b || !b`. The kernel is not wrong; the caller supplied one fact twice. The contract has no field for the *second* fact (`CompleteExecutionStepPayloadSchema`, `messages.ts:163-172`, has no explicit-no-output field), so the distinction §2.6 requires ("state is explicit, never inferred from absent output") is *unrepresentable on the wire*. The guard cannot be non-vacuous while the contract cannot carry its input.
2. **RE-IMPLEMENTED WEAKER.** `pwu.ts:686` re-implements "this plan produced work" as `steps.some(s => s.stepState === 'SUCCEEDED')` with no `s.status` term, beside `execution.ts:473-499`'s three-limb allow-list whose own docstring (`:453-457`) claims the two planes are aligned. Two definitions of one concept, the weaker one guarding the premise (`pwuGuards.ts:20` gates `EXECUTING->EVIDENCE_PENDING` on exactly `executionState === 'SUCCEEDED'`).
3. **UNDECLARED OMISSION.** `plan.status !== 'ACTIVE'` is hand-inlined at five sites (`:709` Start, `:919` Retry, `:967` Skip, `:1049` Prune, `:1119` ResolveWait) and absent at four (Complete, Fail, Cancel, EnterWait). Two of the four absences carry a written rationale + a positive test (`:991-997`/`execution-step-skip-cancel.test.ts:178`; `:1074-1080`/`execution-start-gate.test.ts:613`); Complete carries neither. Because the policy is prose in nine docblocks rather than one table, an omission is invisible — nobody can see the hole in a list that does not exist.
4. **BUILT AND NEVER CALLED.** `canResumeExecutionOnPwu` (`packages/rph-domain/src/execution.ts:73`) has, repo-wide, exactly two references: its definition and its own unit test (`execution.test.ts:11,50-51`) — I re-ran the grep. Meanwhile `conformance-manifest.ts:32` maps `'RPH-PWU-010' -> 'packages/rph-domain/src/execution.test.ts'` and `conformance.test.ts` marks it COVERED. A pure-predicate unit test was accepted as evidence for a rule whose ratified statement is *"the command is rejected"* — so the accounting gate certified an unenforced rule.

Underneath all four: **`advanceStep` (`execution.ts:598-676`) has exactly three extension points — `precheck`, `requireFrom`, `checkTransition` — all optional, all per-call-site, none declared anywhere a reader or a test can enumerate.** Nothing in the system can answer "which rules does StartExecutionStep enforce, and which does CompleteExecutionStep not?" without reading nine docblocks and trusting them. Both the code and the roadmap then drifted from that unstated truth (F-42 is that drift, on paper).

The correction is not five patches. It is to make execution authority **declared data, consumed by one evaluator, proved by one registry-driven census** — so that a rule with no enforcement site, a site with no kill test, and a guard whose inputs cannot disagree are all *structurally* impossible to ship.

### Why it survived
Four compounding reasons, each verified rather than assumed:

**(a) A tautology is worse than an unkilled mutant — it is dead code, and mutation discipline cannot see it.** JAN-CMDPRE's B7 practice is "weaken the guard, watch the test go RED". Deleting `execution.ts:801-805` outright changes no test, because the branch was never reachable from the application layer. `RPH_STEP_RESULT_MISSING` occurs in exactly two places repo-wide — `rph-domain/src/execution.ts:178` and `execution.test.ts:122`, which calls `validateStepCompletion({hasOutput:false, explicitNoOutput:false})` *directly*: a combination the wire format makes unconstructible. The kernel test was green and meaningless as evidence about production.

**(b) The coverage accounting rewarded the wrong artefact.** `conformance-manifest.ts` maps rule -> *some* test file. It has no notion of *layer*: it cannot tell a pure-predicate unit test from an `Engine.dispatch` test. `RPH-PWU-010`'s conformance row says "then: the command is rejected", and its cited coverage is a function call. The gate that exists to stop unaccounted rules certified an *unwired* one.

**(c) Tests codified the holes as intent.** `execution-start-gate.test.ts:88-104`'s `complete` helper passes `outputArtifactIds: []`/`proposedEvidenceIds: []` on every step with the comment *"an explicit no-output result (no floor subject -> no assurance gate)"* and expects ACCEPTED. `execution-step-skip-cancel.test.ts:151` skips a MANDATORY step with `waiverOrRevisionId: 'dec_waiver_1'` — an id recorded nowhere in that fixture — and asserts ACCEPTED. `pwu.test.ts`'s single fixture `succeededPlanFor` builds a *one-step* plan that reaches SUCCEEDED and leaves the plan ACTIVE, so `.some`->`.every` and adding a status term are both green mutants. A test suite that encodes the defect as the expected behaviour is a *ratchet*: it converts a hole into a regression barrier.

**(d) The reviews looked at the guards that existed, not the guards' inputs.** The fail-closed lens verified `mandatory ?? true` at `execution.ts:976` and stopped; the other argument to the same call, `hasAuthorizedWaiverOrRevision: !!p.waiverOrRevisionId`, is a truthiness test on a free `z.string().optional()` and was never examined. Same shape at `explicitNoOutput`. **The guard was audited; the guard's premises were not.**

### Fixes

**Findings F-01 (limb A: the RPH-EXE-006 tautology)**
- *rule:* RPH-EXE-006 (canonical): "A succeeded step MUST record outputs or an EXPLICIT no-output result; completion with neither is rejected — state is explicit, never inferred from absent output (§2.6)." Restated so it is enforceable: `hasOutput` is DERIVED from the payload's id arrays; `explicitNoOutput` is ASSERTED by the caller in a dedicated payload field and is never derived. The full truth table is total and every cell is reachable: (hasOutput=T, asserted=F) -> ACCEPT; (F, T) -> ACCEPT; (F, F) -> REFUSE `RPH_INVARIANT_VIOLATION` (kernel reason `RPH_STEP_RESULT_MISSING`); (T, T) -> REFUSE `RPH_VALIDATION_SEMANTIC_FAILED` (contradictory: naming a result and asserting none). Additionally: a no-output assertion whose `reason` is failure-shaped may not drive a step to SUCCEEDED — a TIMEOUT is a FailExecutionStep, not a success.
- *sites:* VOCAB (change the source, regenerate — never hand-edit the generated file):
• `packages/rph-contracts/vocab/m1-object-fields.json` -> `helperSubTypes` += `NoOutputResult` = { `reason`: required, inline literal union `'NO_DOWNSTREAM_CONSUMABLE_RESULT' | 'SIDE_EFFECT_ONLY' | 'TIMEOUT' | 'NO_CANDIDATE_OUTPUT'` (inline union, NOT a canonical enum — `gen-objects.ts:144` explicitly sanctions pinning a small closed set without minting a ratified enum); `detail`: required string (the rationale — a bare boolean would repeat REG-Q-012's mistake) }.
• `packages/rph-contracts/vocab/m3-commands-events.json` -> command `CompleteExecutionStep`.payloadFields += { field: `noOutputResult`, required: false, type: `NoOutputResult` }; AND event `ExecutionStepSucceeded`.payloadFields += the same field (the assertion must be a governed, replayable fact — otherwise replay still cannot distinguish an explicit no-output from a silent one).
• Regenerate: `bun run gen` in `packages/rph-contracts`, then prettier.
GENERATED (verify, do not edit): `packages/rph-contracts/src/objects.ts` (`NoOutputResultSchema`), `packages/rph-contracts/src/messages.ts` (`CompleteExecutionStepPayloadSchema`, `ExecutionStepSucceededPayloadSchema`).
KERNEL: `packages/rph-domain/src/execution.ts:163-181` — `StepCompletionInput` gains `readonly noOutputReasonIsSuccessCompatible?: boolean`; `validateStepCompletion` returns the four-cell table with distinct `errorCode`s: `RPH_STEP_RESULT_MISSING`, `RPH_STEP_RESULT_CONTRADICTORY`, `RPH_STEP_RESULT_NOT_SUCCESS_SHAPED`.
HANDLER: `packages/rph-application/src/handlers/execution.ts:800-805` (inside `completeExecutionStep`'s `precheck`) + `:790-799` (`eventPayload` carries `noOutputResult` when present).
- *mechanism:* `hasOutput = (p.outputArtifactIds?.length ?? 0) > 0 || (p.proposedEvidenceIds?.length ?? 0) > 0` (unchanged). `explicitNoOutput = p.noOutputResult !== undefined` — INDEPENDENT of `hasOutput`, which is the whole fix: the two arguments can now disagree, so the kernel's disjunction is a real decision instead of `b || !b`. `noOutputReasonIsSuccessCompatible = p.noOutputResult === undefined || p.noOutputResult.reason === 'NO_DOWNSTREAM_CONSUMABLE_RESULT' || p.noOutputResult.reason === 'SIDE_EFFECT_ONLY'`. The handler maps kernel errorCode -> refusal code per constraint 7: MISSING -> `RPH_INVARIANT_VIOLATION` (a ratified conformance invariant), CONTRADICTORY and NOT_SUCCESS_SHAPED -> `RPH_VALIDATION_SEMANTIC_FAILED` (the payload is internally inconsistent / names the wrong command). The NOT_SUCCESS_SHAPED message must name the remedy verbatim: "a timed-out or candidate-less attempt is a FAILURE — use FailExecutionStep; SUCCEEDED credit is what backs the PWU's execution-success claim."
- *killTest:* NEW `packages/rph-application/src/handlers/execution-exe006-explicit-result.test.ts` — the four-cell table + the reason split, all through `Engine` + `SqliteStorageAdapter`:
1. `exe006/missing`: RUNNING step, both id arrays empty, no `noOutputResult` -> REJECTED `RPH_INVARIANT_VIOLATION`, message contains `RPH_STEP_RESULT_MISSING`, step stays RUNNING, ZERO `ExecutionStepSucceeded` events, plan revision unchanged. **This is the kill test: it is exactly the input that is ACCEPTED today (live-reproduced by both verifiers).**
2. `exe006/asserted`: same, plus `noOutputResult: {reason:'NO_DOWNSTREAM_CONSUMABLE_RESULT', detail:'...'}` -> ACCEPTED, and the persisted `ExecutionStepSucceeded` payload CONTAINS `noOutputResult` (proves the assertion is replayable).
3. `exe006/contradictory`: `proposedEvidenceIds:[evd]` AND `noOutputResult` -> REJECTED `RPH_VALIDATION_SEMANTIC_FAILED`.
4. `exe006/timeout-is-not-success`: `noOutputResult:{reason:'TIMEOUT'}` -> REJECTED; then `FailExecutionStep` on the same step -> ACCEPTED (proves no deadlock).
5. `exe006/named-output`: `proposedEvidenceIds:[recorded evd]`, no assertion -> ACCEPTED (the seed's shape).
DECLARED MUTATIONS that must turn these RED (recorded in the register row, run live at implementation):
 M1 restore `explicitNoOutput: !hasOutput` -> test 1 goes GREEN-ACCEPT => RED. M2 delete the reject at `:804-805` -> tests 1,3,4 RED. M3 drop the `noOutputResult` field from the event payload -> test 2 RED. M4 add `'TIMEOUT'` to the success-compatible set -> test 4 RED.
- *behaviourChange:* NEWLY REFUSED (deliberate, enumerated): (i) `CompleteExecutionStep` with both id arrays empty and no `noOutputResult` — previously ACCEPTED, live-reproduced; (ii) a completion naming a result AND asserting no-output; (iii) a completion asserting `TIMEOUT`/`NO_CANDIDATE_OUTPUT` while targeting SUCCEEDED.
REMAINS ACCEPTED: every completion naming >=1 recorded artifact/evidence (the reference seed, `pwu.test.ts:235`'s `succeededPlanFor`); every genuine no-output completion that SAYS SO (the demo's HUMAN TRANSFORMATION step, coordination/verification steps, side-effect-only steps).
CALLERS THAT MUST BE UPDATED (all verified, complete list): `packages/rph-application/src/handlers/execution-start-gate.test.ts:88-104` (`complete` helper — add the assertion and REWRITE its comment, which currently teaches the bypass); `apps/rph-demo/src/routes/undertakings/[id]/+page.server.ts:548-563` (`beginExecute`) and `:671-690` (`completeStep`, when no `outputArtifactId` is supplied); `apps/rph-demo/e2e/execution-plan.e2e.ts:332-352`. **`packages/rph-engine/src/reference-undertaking.ts:604-613` needs NO change** — it passes `proposedEvidenceIds: [evidenceId]`, so `hasOutput` is true.
COMMENT CORRECTION (per the register): `execution-floor-gate-ai.test.ts:126-130` claims the named artifact "satisfies validateStepCompletion (RPH-EXE-006)" — a distinguishing claim the code could not support. After this fix it CAN; keep the comment and cite the new table.
- *risk:* LOW-MEDIUM. The blast radius is exactly six call sites (grep for `outputArtifactIds: []` returns 6 non-generated hits; one is `condition-grammar.ts:215`, a subject seed, unaffected). **The subtle risk is a masked e2e assertion:** `execution-plan.e2e.ts:332` completes a still-QUEUED step and asserts only `not.toBe('ACCEPTED')` — because `advanceStep` runs `precheck` BEFORE `requireFrom` (`:645-647`), that test would start passing for the NEW reason (result missing) while silently ceasing to prove the RUNNING->SUCCEEDED machine guard it was written for. It MUST be given a valid payload so the refusal it asserts is the refusal it names. FIX-3's ordering change also settles this class permanently.

**Findings F-01 (limb B: the §8.4 floor gate is skipped entirely when a step names no subjects)**
- *rule:* §8.4 floor, fail-closed restatement: "AI-produced professional content may not enter the governed stream without an assessable subject." Concretely, at `CompleteExecutionStep`: when `aiProduced` is true, the completion is REFUSED if it yields ZERO floor subjects AND it carries professional content — where 'carries professional content' means `structuredResult` is present and non-empty. An AI step that genuinely produced nothing (empty `structuredResult`) and SAYS SO (`noOutputResult` asserted) is ACCEPTED: the Coding Agent Guide L1964 case ('a timeout/no-output Attempt has no candidate output to review') must not be over-refused. A non-AI step with zero subjects is unaffected.
- *sites:* `packages/rph-application/src/handlers/floor-gate.ts` — NEW exported PURE predicate beside the existing floor logic (no ctx, so its truth table is unit-testable in isolation):
```
export interface FloorSubjectAdmissibility { readonly aiProduced: boolean; readonly subjectCount: number; readonly explicitNoOutput: boolean; readonly structuredResultHasContent: boolean; }
export function unassessableAiContentBlock(i: FloorSubjectAdmissibility): string | null
```
plus a small exported helper `structuredResultHasContent(v: unknown): boolean` (true when v is a non-empty object/array/non-empty string/number/boolean; `{}`/`[]`/`''`/`undefined`/`null` are EMPTY — this is what keeps the seed's `structuredResult: {}` and the demo's `{}` accepted).
CALL SITE: `packages/rph-application/src/handlers/execution.ts:827-840`, immediately AFTER `aiProduced`/`subjects`/`unresolved` are computed and AFTER the existing `unresolved.length > 0` reject at `:833`, and BEFORE the `for (const subject of subjects)` loop at `:841`.
DOC: update the DISCLOSED GAP paragraph at `floor-gate.ts:116-118` — it currently discloses this exact hole as unfixed.
- *mechanism:* `unassessableAiContentBlock` returns a refusal reason when `aiProduced && subjectCount === 0 && structuredResultHasContent`; null otherwise. The handler wraps it as `RPH_INVARIANT_VIOLATION` (§8.4 L854: a missing required review cannot permit its protected transition), message: "CompleteExecutionStep blocked: step <id> is AI-produced and carries a structuredResult, but names no recorded, assessable result. Record the content as an Artifact (RecordArtifact) or Evidence (ProposeEvidence) and name it, so its de minimis assurance floor can be judged." Composition with FIX-1 is what closes the bypass completely: after FIX-1, `subjectCount === 0` implies the caller ASSERTED no-output; this rule then forbids asserting no-output while shipping content. So the cheapest escape ("name nothing, still ship `structuredResult`") is refused on both limbs, and `structuredResult` — which `execution.ts:798` persists verbatim and `condition-grammar.ts:128/197` gives BRANCH-selection authority to — can no longer reach the governed stream from an AI step without a floor-judged subject.
- *killTest:* NEW `packages/rph-application/src/handlers/execution-floor-zero-subject.test.ts`, built on the EXACT fixture of `execution-floor-gate-ai.test.ts:120` (MODEL_INVOCATION step, HIGH on all five risk axes, `executionProvenance.executedBy.actorType='MODEL'`, `originType='MODEL_GENERATION'`, NO `ASSURANCE_ASSESSMENT` ever recorded):
1. `floor/zero-subject-with-content`: both id arrays empty, `noOutputResult` asserted, `structuredResult: { architecture: 'generated by the model' }` -> REJECTED `RPH_INVARIANT_VIOLATION`, step stays RUNNING, no `ExecutionStepSucceeded`. **This is the headline BLOCKER's kill test — the register reproduced this exact input returning `ACCEPTED | state=SUCCEEDED` today.**
2. `floor/zero-subject-empty-content`: same but `structuredResult: {}` -> ACCEPTED (the timeout/no-candidate case is NOT over-refused).
3. `floor/non-ai-zero-subject`: TRANSFORMATION step, HUMAN provenance, HUMAN issuer, zero subjects, asserted no-output -> ACCEPTED (the demo's path).
4. `floor/named-subject-unsatisfied`: AI step naming one recorded artifact with a MISSING floor -> REJECTED (the existing sibling behaviour, pinned as a non-regression).
PURE unit tests in `floor-gate.test.ts` (or a new `floor-gate-admissibility.test.ts`) enumerating all 16 cells of the 4-boolean table — a pure predicate has no excuse for an untested cell.
DECLARED MUTATIONS: M1 `aiProduced && ...` -> `false && ...` => test 1 RED. M2 `structuredResultHasContent` returns constant true => test 2 and 3 RED (proves the over-refusal guard is itself guarded). M3 `subjectCount === 0` -> `subjectCount < 0` => test 1 RED.
- *behaviourChange:* NEWLY REFUSED: an AI-produced completion with zero assessable subjects that carries a non-empty `structuredResult`. Nothing else.
REMAINS ACCEPTED: AI completion naming >=1 subject whose floor is SATISFIED or waived (the reference seed — MODEL_INVOCATION, subject = the evidence, floor satisfied by `satisfyFloor`); AI completion with zero subjects and empty `structuredResult` (genuine no-output); every non-AI completion regardless of subjects; every path the existing `execution-floor-gate-ai.test.ts` / `execution-floor-subject.test.ts` / `execution-floor-signal0-live.test.ts` assert.
DEMO: `+page.server.ts:671` `completeStep` with `aiProduced=true` and no `outputArtifactId` must now assert `noOutputResult` — and since it sends `structuredResult: {}`, it remains ACCEPTED. That is the honest demo: an AI step that produced nothing must say so.
- *risk:* MEDIUM — this is the one fix that can over-refuse, and the over-refusal is guarded by test 2/3 above and by the `structuredResultHasContent` emptiness semantics. RESIDUAL, disclosed not closed: an AI step that names one floor-satisfied artifact AND ALSO ships a large `structuredResult` still gets that inline content into the event stream un-individuated. That is §8.4 L844's individuation gap, already disclosed at `floor-gate.ts:116-118`; closing it means requiring `structuredResult` content to be recorded as an Artifact, which is a contract change beyond this family. Registered as an open question, NOT silently carried.

**Findings F-26 (Complete/Fail have no plan-ACTIVE precheck and no test declaring the omission) + F-42 (PLAUSIBLE — DWP-04's "Both plan-ACTIVE-guarded" vs the shipped single guard)**
- *rule:* **The axis is not "opens new work" — it is "mints success credit or a durable decision".** The codebase already behaves this way and says otherwise: Skip and Prune open no work whatsoever, yet both carry the plan-ACTIVE precheck (`:967`, `:1049`), because both MINT terminal-success. Restated canonically: "A command that drives a step to TERMINAL SUCCESS, or records a durable branch DECISION, requires an ACTIVE plan (RPH-EXE-002/§35.1). A command that terminates or suspends already-open work with an honest non-success record requires no plan status, and MUST remain available so a dead plan's live steps are never stranded." This yields the definitive table — and, decisively, `CompleteExecutionStep` sits on the credit side:

| command | target | class | ACTIVE plan required | status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| StartExecutionStep | RUNNING | opens work, consumes an attempt | YES | already (`:709`) |
| RetryExecutionStep | QUEUED | re-opens the attempt cycle | YES | already (`:919`) |
| ResolveExecutionStepWait | RUNNING | re-opens RUNNING | YES | already (`:1119`) |
| SkipExecutionStep | SKIPPED | mints terminal-SUCCESS under a waiver | YES | already (`:967`) |
| PruneExecutionStep | SKIPPED | mints terminal-SUCCESS under branch logic | YES | already (`:1049`) |
| **CompleteExecutionStep** | SUCCEEDED | **mints terminal-SUCCESS + a durable BRANCH decision; is the fact the PWU claim rests on** | **YES** | **CHANGE** |
| FailExecutionStep | FAILED | honest negative record | NO | keep — DECLARE + test |
| CancelExecutionStep | CANCELLED | cleanup | NO | already declared + tested |
| EnterExecutionStepWait | WAITING | suspend | NO | already declared + tested |

THE ARGUMENT FOR GATING COMPLETE (not a guess — three independent grounds): (1) SUCCEEDED is the ONLY step state in `completeExecutionPlan`'s positive limb (`:492`) and the ONLY state `rejectUnbackedExecutionSuccess` (`pwu.ts:686`) accepts as backing — minting it on a plan the sponsor killed manufactures downstream authority on dead state, live-verified: COMPLETE ACCEPTED on a SUPERSEDED plan with `selectedTransitionId` written. (2) Consistency: the surface refuses the LESSER act (ResolveWait: "a superseded/terminal plan opens no new work... Cancel the step instead") and permits the GREATER one, on the same plan at the same instant. (3) No deadlock is created: `cancelExecutionStep` (`requireFrom ['READY','QUEUED','RUNNING','WAITING']`, deliberately ungated) is the legal exit, and is exactly what ResolveWait's own refusal message already prescribes.
THE ARGUMENT FOR NOT GATING FAIL: `pwu.ts:666-668` states it and it is right — "a system that makes failure harder to report than success is worse than one that checks neither." A FAILED step backs no success claim anywhere. Fail's omission is CORRECT and merely UNDECLARED at its own site.
F-42 DISPOSITION: **code unchanged, documents corrected.** Both verifiers converged: `enterExecutionStepWait` is escape-complete (Cancel from WAITING is legal under a CANCELLED plan, probed), mints no attempt (`attemptsMadeForStep` counts `ExecutionStepStarted` only), and is precedented at four sites including the read-model's own comment (`execution-view.ts:170-171`). Changing the code to match DWP-04 would strip a running step of the ability to record honestly that it is blocked. The residual is real and is a RECORD defect: amend DS-004 §6 D6 and DR-004 lines 177/185/191/199, and ADD the divergence to DR-004 §19 (which today affirmatively certifies "Divergences from DS-004: none intended").
- *sites:* NEW `packages/rph-domain/src/execution-authority.ts` (pure, no I/O — sited in rph-domain because `packages/rph-projections/src/execution-view.ts` must consume the same table and already depends on `@janumipwb/rph-domain`; `.dependency-cruiser.cjs` forbids projections->application):
```
export type PlanLiveness = 'REQUIRES_ACTIVE_PLAN' | 'CLEANUP_EXEMPT';
export type PwuOpenness  = 'REQUIRES_OPEN_PWU'   | 'CLEANUP_EXEMPT';
export interface StepCommandAuthority { readonly planLiveness: PlanLiveness; readonly pwuOpenness: PwuOpenness; readonly rationale: string; readonly ruleIds: readonly string[]; }
export const STEP_COMMAND_AUTHORITY: Readonly<Record<StepCommandType, StepCommandAuthority>>
export type StepCommandType = 'StartExecutionStep' | 'CompleteExecutionStep' | 'FailExecutionStep' | 'RetryExecutionStep' | 'SkipExecutionStep' | 'CancelExecutionStep' | 'PruneExecutionStep' | 'EnterExecutionStepWait' | 'ResolveExecutionStepWait';
export function stepCommandAuthority(commandType: string): StepCommandAuthority | undefined;
```
The `Record<StepCommandType, …>` key type makes a MISSING classification a COMPILE ERROR: a new step command cannot ship unclassified.
CONSUMER: `packages/rph-application/src/handlers/execution.ts:598-676` — `advanceStep` looks the row up by `command.commandType` and evaluates it BEFORE the per-command `precheck`. `stepCommandAuthority` returning `undefined` (an unregistered step command) REFUSES — fail-closed applied to the mechanism itself, not just to its rules.
DELETIONS (the hand-inlined copies collapse into the table): `execution.ts:708-714` (Start), `:918-924` (Retry), `:966-972` (Skip), `:1048-1054` (Prune), `:1119-1126` (ResolveWait) lose their `plan.status !== 'ACTIVE'` blocks. Their remaining precheck bodies (start-gate, retry cap, canSkipStep, prunability) are untouched.
RATIONALE RE-SITING: `execution.ts:1076-1079` currently carries EnterWait's AND Fail's rationale. Move Fail's to `failExecutionStep` (`:862-871`) and Cancel's stays at `:990-997`; each now also cites its register row.
READ-MODEL MIRROR: `packages/rph-projections/src/execution-view.ts:163-174` — `advanceCommandsFor`/`controlCommandsFor` gain a plan-status-aware caller-side filter driven by `STEP_COMMAND_AUTHORITY`, so the UI stops offering Complete on a non-ACTIVE plan (otherwise F-29's "no affordance the engine would reject" invariant is re-broken by this very fix).
DOCS: DS-004 §6 D6; DR-004 lines 177/185/191/199 and §19.
- *mechanism:* In `advanceStep`, after `loadOrReject` and the step lookup, before `args.precheck`:
```
const authority = stepCommandAuthority(command.commandType);
if (!authority) return reject(command,'RPH_INVARIANT_VIOLATION',`${command.commandType} is not classified in STEP_COMMAND_AUTHORITY …`);
if (authority.planLiveness === 'REQUIRES_ACTIVE_PLAN' && plan.status !== 'ACTIVE')
  return reject(command,'RPH_ILLEGAL_STATE_TRANSITION', `${command.commandType} requires an ACTIVE plan: ${planId} is ${String(plan.status)} — a superseded/terminal plan mints no new success credit (RPH-EXE-002). Cancel the step instead.`, [args.stepId]);
// FIX-4's PWU-openness limb evaluates here, second.
```
The per-command `precheck` closures keep ONLY their genuinely command-specific logic. **ORDERING (INV-3, JAN-CMDPRE precedent): declared authority -> `requireFrom` -> `precheck` -> `checkTransition`.** Today `precheck` runs before `requireFrom` (`:645-647`), so a wrong-state step with an invalid payload refuses with the payload guard's code — which is how a test can assert a refusal it did not actually cause. Moving `requireFrom` ahead of `precheck` changes NO accept/reject outcome (both refuse); it changes only WHICH refusal wins, and it makes every state-fact refusal carry `RPH_ILLEGAL_STATE_TRANSITION` as constraint 7 requires.
- *killTest:* NEW `packages/rph-application/src/handlers/execution-plan-liveness-table.test.ts` — a MATRIX test generated FROM `STEP_COMMAND_AUTHORITY` itself, so a new command cannot be added without a case:
• For every row with `planLiveness === 'REQUIRES_ACTIVE_PLAN'`: seed the step in a legal source state, `SupersedeExecutionPlan` (and, as a second axis, `CancelExecutionPlan`), dispatch -> REJECTED `RPH_ILLEGAL_STATE_TRANSITION`, step state UNCHANGED, no event appended, plan revision unchanged.
• For every row with `planLiveness === 'CLEANUP_EXEMPT'`: same setup -> ACCEPTED, and the step reaches its target. **These positive cases are the kill tests for the DECLARED EXEMPTIONS: if someone later "hardens" Fail/Cancel/EnterWait by adding a plan gate, they go RED** — which is precisely the declaration F-26 says Complete never had.
NAMED CASES (each must exist by name, per the register's "whichever policy is chosen, that test is the one that must exist"): `liveness/complete-under-superseded-REJECTS` (the behaviour change — RED against today's code, which ACCEPTS and writes `selectedTransitionId`); `liveness/complete-under-cancelled-REJECTS`; `liveness/fail-under-superseded-ACCEPTS`; `liveness/cancel-under-superseded-ACCEPTS` (moves `execution-step-skip-cancel.test.ts:178` into the matrix); `liveness/wait-under-cancelled-ACCEPTS` (moves `execution-start-gate.test.ts:613`); `liveness/branch-complete-under-superseded-records-no-selection` — asserts the superseded plan's BRANCH step gains NO `selectedTransitionId`, closing the durable-decision-on-dead-state limb specifically.
DECLARED MUTATIONS: M1 flip Complete's row to `CLEANUP_EXEMPT` -> the two complete-REJECTS cases RED. M2 flip Fail's row to `REQUIRES_ACTIVE_PLAN` -> `fail-under-superseded-ACCEPTS` RED. M3 delete a row -> COMPILE ERROR (the strongest gate). M4 make `stepCommandAuthority` return a default row -> the unregistered-command test RED.
- *behaviourChange:* NEWLY REFUSED: `CompleteExecutionStep` on a plan in any status other than ACTIVE (SUPERSEDED, CANCELLED, COMPLETED, FAILED, and the pre-activation statuses). Live-verified as ACCEPTED today on SUPERSEDED and CANCELLED.
REMAINS ACCEPTED, now positively pinned: `FailExecutionStep`, `CancelExecutionStep`, `EnterExecutionStepWait` on any plan status.
CODE-ONLY CHANGES (outcomes identical, codes move — enumerate them in DR-004 §19 as JAN-CMDPRE did for INV-7): a wrong-source step carrying an invalid payload now refuses `RPH_ILLEGAL_STATE_TRANSITION` instead of the payload guard's code. Affected today: `apps/rph-demo/e2e/execution-plan.e2e.ts:332` (asserts only `not ACCEPTED` — still green, and now green for the right reason) and the skip negative at `execution-start-gate.test.ts:699` flagged by F-13.
NO WORK IS LOST by gating Complete: the attempt is already recorded (`ExecutionStepStarted`), and the step's honest exit on a dead plan is `CancelExecutionStep` with a reason. What is refused is the SUCCESS CREDIT on a plan the sponsor killed.
- *risk:* MEDIUM. Two couplings must be honoured or the fix regresses a sibling family: (1) the read-model mirror — if `execution-view.ts` keeps offering Complete on a non-ACTIVE plan, F-29's invariant breaks again; (2) the ordering change touches refusal codes that F-11/F-13/F-14/F-18/F-19's `requireFrom` kill tests will be written against, so it must land BEFORE those tests are authored, or they will be written to the old ordering. NOT a risk: a superseded plan's live steps stranding — that gap exists today (the machine's `*->SUPERSEDED` step arrows at `transitions.data.ts:1478-1482` have no command) and is unchanged by this fix; minting the step-level supersede command is a separate item and must NOT ride here.

**Findings F-28 (RPH-PWU-010 unenforced; `canResumeExecutionOnPwu` has no production caller)**
- *rule:* RPH-PWU-010 (ratified, verbatim from `m11-execution.json`): "Baselined PWU cannot resume execution in place: new execution against the same semantic version of a BASELINED PWU is rejected; a successor revision or successor PWU is required." Enforceable restatement, fail-closed and generalised to the machine's own terminal set: "A command that GRANTS runtime privilege (ActivateExecutionPlan) or that MINTS execution credit or opens an attempt on a step (the `REQUIRES_OPEN_PWU` class) is REJECTED when the owning PWU's `workLifecycleState` is TERMINAL — {BASELINED, ABANDONED, SUPERSEDED}, read from the machine, not hardcoded. Cleanup commands (Cancel/Fail/EnterWait step) remain available on a closed PWU so no step is ever stranded. ProposeExecutionPlan and ApproveExecutionPlan remain ACCEPTED: authoring and approving are not executing, and §20.2 is explicit that approval grants no runtime privileges."
WHY THE TERMINAL SET AND NOT ONLY BASELINED: `m2-transitions.json` declares `PWU.workLifecycleState.terminalStates = ['BASELINED','ABANDONED','SUPERSEDED']` and BASELINED has NO out-arrows (verified: the only BASELINED arrows are SATISFIED->BASELINED and RECOMPOSED->BASELINED). Opening execution on an ABANDONED or SUPERSEDED unit of work is at least as wrong as on a baselined one, and deriving the set from the machine follows the codebase's own stated preference (`otherActivePlanExistsForPwu`'s docblock: derive from authoritative state, never from a remembered flag).
WHY LIFECYCLE-STATE AND NOT SEMANTIC-VERSION: the rule's "same semantic version" qualifier is currently untestable — `pwu.ts` writes `newSemanticVersion: loaded.semanticVersion` at every hop (`:340`, `:831`) and no command bumps a PWU's semantic version, so there is no in-place successor revision to admit. Recorded as an open question with the exact trigger for revisiting.
- *sites:* KERNEL: `packages/rph-domain/src/execution.ts:69-81` — `canResumeExecutionOnPwu(pwuLifecycleState)` keeps its signature and its `RPH_BASELINED_PWU_NO_RESUME` code for BASELINED, and additionally refuses any state for which `isTerminalState('PWU.workLifecycleState', state)` is true (code `RPH_CLOSED_PWU_NO_NEW_EXECUTION`). Its existing unit test (`execution.test.ts:50-51`: EXECUTING ok, BASELINED not-ok with that code) still passes unchanged.
TABLE: `packages/rph-domain/src/execution-authority.ts` — the `pwuOpenness` column of `STEP_COMMAND_AUTHORITY` (FIX-3's module): `REQUIRES_OPEN_PWU` for Start, Retry, ResolveWait, Skip, Prune, Complete; `CLEANUP_EXEMPT` for Fail, Cancel, EnterWait.
STEP CONSUMER: `packages/rph-application/src/handlers/execution.ts` `advanceStep` — the second limb, evaluated after plan-liveness.
PLAN CONSUMER: `packages/rph-application/src/handlers/execution.ts:411-426` — `activateExecutionPlan`'s `guard` gains the `canResumeExecutionOnPwu` call; it already has `state.workUnitId` in hand for `otherActivePlanExistsForPwu`, so nothing new is loaded.
TOTALITY HARDENING: `packages/rph-application/src/handlers/execution.ts:242-249` — `proposeExecutionPlan` currently asserts only that `workUnitId` RESOLVES; add `objectType === 'PROFESSIONAL_WORK_UNIT'`. This makes the downstream PWU read TOTAL rather than best-effort. Verified safe: every application test seeds its work unit via `ProposePwu` (checked `execution-start-gate`, `execution-plan-supersede`, `execution-assumption-guard`, `execution-plan-completion`, `dwp05-precondition-coverage`), and `ProposePwu` seeds `workLifecycleState: 'PROPOSED'` (`pwu.ts:211`) — non-terminal, so every existing activation still passes.
MANIFEST: `packages/rph-domain/src/conformance-manifest.ts:32` — `RPH-PWU-010`'s coverage moves from the pure-predicate unit test to the new application-layer test (and FIX-7 GATE B makes that move MANDATORY, not optional).
- *mechanism:* One helper in `execution.ts`, used by both consumers:
```
function pwuOpennessRefusal(ctx, command, workUnitId: string) {
  const pwu = ctx.store.loadObject(workUnitId);
  if (pwu?.objectType !== 'PROFESSIONAL_WORK_UNIT')
    return reject(command,'RPH_VALIDATION_SEMANTIC_FAILED', `… plan's workUnitId ${workUnitId} does not resolve to a PROFESSIONAL_WORK_UNIT …`);   // fail-closed: unknown => refuse
  const state = String((pwu.state as Record<string,unknown>).workLifecycleState ?? '');
  const check = canResumeExecutionOnPwu(state);
  return check.ok ? null : reject(command,'RPH_INVARIANT_VIOLATION', `… PWU ${workUnitId} is ${state}: ${check.reason} (${check.errorCode}, RPH-PWU-010). A closed unit of work admits no new execution — create a successor PWU or a successor revision.`, [workUnitId]);
}
```
Code mapping follows the existing precedent at `:419-423`, where `canActivatePlan`'s `RPH_ACTIVE_PLAN_CONFLICT` is surfaced inside an `RPH_INVARIANT_VIOLATION` message rather than as a bus code.
**Why BOTH activation and the step commands (the finding suggests activation alone):** gating only activation leaves the reachable hole where a plan was activated BEFORE the PWU closed and still has QUEUED steps — StartExecutionStep would remain ACCEPTED on a frozen PWU. Gating only the step commands leaves the privilege grant itself unguarded. Both, one table, one predicate.
- *killTest:* NEW `packages/rph-application/src/handlers/execution-pwu-openness.test.ts`, driving the FULL legitimate path to BASELINED (no shortcuts, mirroring the verifier's probe): CaptureIntent/BeginIntentDiscovery/ProvisionIntent -> ProposePwu/BeginPwuShaping/MarkPwuReady -> a REAL succeeded plan (propose/approve/activate/start/ProposeEvidence/CompleteExecutionStep) -> CompleteExecutionPlan (removes the one-ACTIVE-plan confound, so RPH-EXE-001 cannot masquerade as the guard) -> a real satisfied assessment -> the ChangePwuState hops to SATISFIED -> a real governance baseline (ProposeDecision PROMOTE_BASELINE / Approve / CreateBaseline / Submit / Approve / PromoteBaseline) -> SATISFIED->BASELINED.
1. `pwu010/activate-on-baselined-REJECTS`: propose+approve a SECOND plan (both still ACCEPTED, deliberately), then `ActivateExecutionPlan` -> REJECTED, message contains `RPH_BASELINED_PWU_NO_RESUME`. **RED today at every one of the five commands (verifier output: all five ACCEPTED, step reached SUCCEEDED).**
2. `pwu010/start-on-baselined-REJECTS`: activate a plan while the PWU is EXECUTING, leave a QUEUED step, drive the PWU to BASELINED, then `StartExecutionStep` -> REJECTED. (Covers the hole an activation-only gate would leave.)
3. `pwu010/cancel-on-baselined-ACCEPTS` and `pwu010/fail-on-baselined-ACCEPTS`: the no-stranding guarantee, and the kill tests for the CLEANUP exemptions.
4. `pwu010/propose-and-approve-on-baselined-ACCEPT`: the deliberate non-refusal, pinned so a future "hardening" that over-refuses goes RED.
5. `pwu010/abandoned-and-superseded-also-refuse`: the terminal-set generalisation.
6. `pwu010/executing-pwu-ACCEPTS`: the seed's own shape — the anti-over-refusal case.
DECLARED MUTATIONS: M1 remove the `activateExecutionPlan` guard call -> test 1 RED. M2 flip Start's `pwuOpenness` to `CLEANUP_EXEMPT` -> test 2 RED. M3 make `canResumeExecutionOnPwu` return `{ok:true}` always -> tests 1,2,5 RED. M4 flip Cancel's to `REQUIRES_OPEN_PWU` -> test 3 RED.
- *behaviourChange:* NEWLY REFUSED: `ActivateExecutionPlan` on a PWU in {BASELINED, ABANDONED, SUPERSEDED}; `StartExecutionStep`/`RetryExecutionStep`/`ResolveExecutionStepWait`/`SkipExecutionStep`/`PruneExecutionStep`/`CompleteExecutionStep` on such a PWU; `ProposeExecutionPlan` whose `workUnitId` resolves to a non-PWU object (`RPH_VALIDATION_SEMANTIC_FAILED`).
REMAINS ACCEPTED (deliberate, each pinned by a test): Propose and Approve on a closed PWU; Cancel/Fail/EnterWait step on a closed PWU; every command on a PWU in any non-terminal state including PROPOSED (which is what every existing test's plan is activated against).
SEED: unaffected — `reference-undertaking.ts` activates and completes while the PWU is PLANNED/EXECUTING and only reaches BASELINED afterwards; `seed-workbench.ts` creates no execution plans at all (9 `send(` calls, none an execution command).
- *risk:* MEDIUM-LOW, and materially reduced by FIX-6's sequencing. The one live-strand scenario — a PWU closing while a multi-step plan still has RUNNING work — becomes UNREACHABLE once FIX-6 lands, because reaching BASELINED requires `executionState=SUCCEEDED`, which will then require the cited plan's steps to ALL be in terminal success. Until FIX-6 lands, the CLEANUP exemptions (Cancel/Fail/EnterWait) are the guarantee that nothing is stranded. Secondary risk: the `objectType === 'PROFESSIONAL_WORK_UNIT'` hardening at propose — verified against every existing test, but it is the one change that could break an unexamined fixture, so land it with the propose-time negative test in the same commit.

**Findings F-30 (`waiverOrRevisionId` is never resolved — any non-empty string authorizes a mandatory skip)**
- *rule:* §21.1 / STA-8 (DOC-003 §203): "Skipping a mandatory plan step is a governed act requiring an authorized plan revision or waiver, never a silent omission" — enforced as RESOLUTION, not truthiness. Canonically: "`waiverOrRevisionId`, WHENEVER PRESENT, must resolve to a governed authorization: a DECISION object whose `decisionType` is WAIVER (a waiver) or REPLAN (an authorized plan revision), whose `status` is EFFECTIVE, whose `subjectObjectIds` include THIS execution plan, whose `executionSkipAuthorization` names THIS plan and lists THIS stepId, and which has not expired at the command's `issuedAt`. Any other value is refused `RPH_VALIDATION_SEMANTIC_FAILED`. Separately and unchanged, a mandatory step (or a step whose `mandatory` is omitted — fail-closed to TRUE) with NO `waiverOrRevisionId` is refused `RPH_INVARIANT_VIOLATION` by `canSkipStep`."
WHY A NEW, EXECUTION-SCOPED FIELD RATHER THAN REUSING `WaiverDetail`: `WaiverDetailSchema` (`objects.ts:323-332`) is the ASSURANCE-plane waiver — `waivedPolicyId`, `waivedCriterionId`, `waivedFindingIds`. Those fields are meaningless for a step skip, and routing a step skip through them would let a FLOOR waiver (an assurance-plane object) authorize an EXECUTION-plane act — a genuine INV-5 boundary crossing and a real bleeding hazard. A distinct optional field keeps the two planes' authorizations distinct by construction.
WHY PLAN-SCOPED SUBJECT PLUS AN EXPLICIT STEP LIST: an ExecutionStep is not a Professional Work Object — no envelope, no registry row, no semantic version (the argument `stepResultSubjects` already makes at `floor-gate.ts:94-101`), so the finest governed subject a Decision can NAME is the plan. The step list inside the authorization detail supplies the step-exactness that RPH-GOV-005 ("a waiver does not bleed to another criterion, another object, or another version") demands, without inventing a version for a non-object. Without it, one decision retires EVERY mandatory step in the plan — exactly the unscoped waiver REG-Q-012 forbids.
- *sites:* VOCAB: `packages/rph-contracts/vocab/m1-object-fields.json`
• `helperSubTypes` += `ExecutionSkipAuthorization` = { `executionPlanId`: required string; `executionStepIds`: required string[]; `expiresAt`: optional RFC3339 string; `rationale`: required string }.
• `objects[DECISION].fields` += { name `executionSkipAuthorization`, type `ExecutionSkipAuthorization`, required FALSE }. OPTIONAL is load-bearing: existing floor/assurance waivers (`floor-waiver-scope.test.ts`, `waiver-rules.test.ts`) do not set it and are unaffected for their own purpose, while an ABSENT detail authorizes NO skip — the fail-closed default, mirroring `effectiveFloorWaivers`' treatment of a WAIVER Decision with no `waiver` detail (`floor-gate.ts:235-238`).
• Also add the field to the ProposeDecision command payload in `m3-commands-events.json` so it can be supplied on the wire, and to the corresponding `DecisionProposed` event.
• Regenerate `bun run gen` + prettier.
HANDLER: `packages/rph-application/src/handlers/execution.ts:966-986` — `skipExecutionStep`'s `precheck` gains the resolution, ahead of `canSkipStep`.
NEW MODULE (keeps the resolution out of the already-dense handler and makes it independently unit-testable): `packages/rph-application/src/handlers/skip-authorization.ts` exporting `resolveSkipAuthorization(ctx, { planId, stepId, authorizationId, now }): { ok: true } | { ok: false; reason: string }`.
TEST TO REWRITE (it currently enshrines the fail-open): `packages/rph-application/src/handlers/execution-step-skip-cancel.test.ts:151` must mint a REAL EFFECTIVE decision instead of the literal `'dec_waiver_1'`.
- *mechanism:* `resolveSkipAuthorization` performs six ordered checks, each with its own message so a caller can see WHY the authorization was insufficient (the same reason `effectiveFloorWaivers` returns views rather than a boolean): (1) `loadObject(id)` exists; (2) `objectType === 'DECISION'`; (3) `decisionType ∈ {'WAIVER','REPLAN'}` — the two kinds §21.1 names, and a category error otherwise, exactly as JAN-CMDPRE DWP-04's `denyWaiver` refuses on KIND before STATE; (4) `status === 'EFFECTIVE'` (PROPOSED is not yet authority; REVOKED/SUPERSEDED are dead — `Decision.status` terminal set is {REVOKED, SUPERSEDED}); (5) `subjectObjectIds.includes(planId)` AND `executionSkipAuthorization.executionPlanId === planId` AND `executionSkipAuthorization.executionStepIds.includes(stepId)`; (6) `!expiresAt || expiresAt > command.issuedAt` — resolved against the COMMAND's `issuedAt`, never a wall clock, so the gate replays deterministically (the identical ruling `effectiveFloorWaivers` makes at `floor-gate.ts:247-250`).
Handler wiring:
```
if (p.waiverOrRevisionId !== undefined) {                    // limb 1: a named authorization must RESOLVE
  const r = resolveSkipAuthorization(ctx, {...});
  if (!r.ok) return reject(command,'RPH_VALIDATION_SEMANTIC_FAILED', `Cannot skip step ${p.stepId}: ${r.reason} …`, [p.stepId, p.waiverOrRevisionId]);
}
const check = canSkipStep({ mandatory: p.mandatory ?? true, hasAuthorizedWaiverOrRevision: p.waiverOrRevisionId !== undefined });  // limb 2: unchanged kernel
```
Two INDEPENDENT limbs. Limb 1 fires whenever an id is supplied — so a dangling id refuses even for `mandatory:false`, mirroring the `unresolved.length > 0` rejection in `completeExecutionStep` (`:833`) which likewise fires regardless of the downstream outcome. Limb 2 is the ratified kernel, untouched: its existing kill tests (`:136` mandatory-with-no-waiver, `:144` mandatory-omitted) stay green and stay meaningful. The kernel remains PURE — it still takes a boolean — but the boolean is now EARNED rather than typed.
- *killTest:* NEW `packages/rph-application/src/handlers/execution-skip-authorization.test.ts` — one case per refusal branch (a resolution with six branches and no per-branch test would reproduce the very defect):
1. `skip-auth/nonexistent-id` -> REJECTED `RPH_VALIDATION_SEMANTIC_FAILED`. **This is the kill test: it is `execution-step-skip-cancel.test.ts:151` inverted — that test passes today with `'dec_waiver_1'` recorded nowhere.**
2. `skip-auth/wrong-object-type` (cite an ARTIFACT id) -> REJECTED.
3. `skip-auth/wrong-decision-kind` (a real EFFECTIVE PROMOTE_BASELINE decision) -> REJECTED.
4. `skip-auth/not-effective` (a PROPOSED WAIVER; then a REVOKED one) -> REJECTED.
5. `skip-auth/other-plan` (an EFFECTIVE WAIVER scoped to a DIFFERENT plan) -> REJECTED — the no-bleeding limb.
6. `skip-auth/other-step` (correct plan, `executionStepIds` lists s2, skip is aimed at s1) -> REJECTED — the step-exactness limb, and the one that stops one waiver retiring a whole plan.
7. `skip-auth/expired` (`expiresAt <= command.issuedAt`) -> REJECTED; and `expiresAt > issuedAt` -> ACCEPTED, proving expiry is resolved against the command clock.
8. `skip-auth/valid-waiver-ACCEPTS` and `skip-auth/valid-replan-ACCEPTS` -> ACCEPTED, step SKIPPED (both ratified kinds exercised — the two-source positive fixture JAN-CMDPRE INV-5 requires).
9. `skip-auth/optional-with-garbage-id-REJECTS` -> the enumerated behaviour change, pinned deliberately.
10. `skip-auth/optional-no-id-ACCEPTS` -> the demo's skip button, pinned against over-refusal.
DECLARED MUTATIONS: M1 restore `hasAuthorizedWaiverOrRevision: !!p.waiverOrRevisionId` with limb 1 deleted -> tests 1-7 RED. M2 drop the `executionStepIds` check -> test 6 RED. M3 admit `status === 'PROPOSED'` -> test 4 RED. M4 drop the `decisionType` filter -> test 3 RED.
- *behaviourChange:* NEWLY REFUSED: any `SkipExecutionStep` supplying a `waiverOrRevisionId` that does not resolve to an EFFECTIVE WAIVER/REPLAN Decision scoped to this plan AND this step and unexpired — including the previously-accepted case `{mandatory:false, waiverOrRevisionId:'garbage'}`.
UNCHANGED: `{mandatory:false}` with no id -> ACCEPTED (the demo's only skip path, `+page.server.ts:600-606`); `{mandatory:true}` or omitted with no id -> REJECTED `RPH_INVARIANT_VIOLATION` (existing tests `:136`, `:144` stay green with the same codes).
SEED: unaffected — `reference-undertaking.ts` never dispatches `SkipExecutionStep`; the only repo-wide caller is the demo's `skipStep` action.
DOWNSTREAM: the `ExecutionStepSkipped` event keeps carrying `waiverOrRevisionId` verbatim (`:963`) — but the value is now guaranteed to name a real, resolvable authorization, so the governed stream stops recording strings that resolve to nothing.
- *risk:* LOW on regression (one test to rewrite, no seed impact), MEDIUM on contract surface: it adds an optional field to a RATIFIED object (DECISION). The precedent is exact and recent — `WaiverDetail` itself was "AUTHORED under sponsor grant 2026-07-16" to give DOC-004 §12.2's ratified invariants a wire home, and `executionProvenance`/`structuredResult` were authored atop ratified event shapes. The vocab note must say so explicitly rather than implying ratification (`m1-object-fields.json` already carries exactly this style of AUTHORED annotation). Residual: REPLAN authorization does not require the successor plan to exist or be cited — there is no ratified field for it; registered as an open question.

**Findings F-08 (a non-ACTIVE / barely-started plan backs a PWU executionState=SUCCEEDED claim)**
- *rule:* ONE definition of execution success, shared by both planes: "A plan EVIDENCES execution success iff its status is ACTIVE or COMPLETED, it has >=1 step, EVERY step is SUCCEEDED or SKIPPED, and >=1 step is SUCCEEDED." `completeExecutionPlan`'s guard and `rejectUnbackedExecutionSuccess` both call the SAME predicate. The plan plane adds nothing (its `precondition: fromStates('ACTIVE')` already fixes the status); the PWU plane stops running a second, strictly weaker rule.
WHY `ACTIVE || COMPLETED` AND NOT `COMPLETED` ALONE: verified against the ratified happy path — `reference-undertaking.ts:614` and `pwu.test.ts:367` both claim `executionState: SUCCEEDED` while citing a plan that is still ACTIVE and never issue `CompleteExecutionPlan`. Requiring COMPLETED would break the reference seed, which is a hard constraint. COMPLETED must also be admitted, because the natural order (finish all steps -> CompleteExecutionPlan -> claim PWU success) is equally legitimate.
WHY `every`, NOT `some`: `some` is what makes 1-of-5 succeeded steps back a full success claim while four sit QUEUED — reproduced live by both verifiers. `every(SUCCEEDED|SKIPPED)` is the exact allow-list `execution.ts:483-491` already enforces at the plan plane, and it is a SUCCESS ALLOW-LIST rather than a negation for the reason stated there: the reachable terminal step set includes CANCELLED and SUPERSEDED, which a negation would silently admit.
WHY A STATUS TERM AT ALL: without it a SUPERSEDED, CANCELLED or FAILED plan backs the claim identically to an ACTIVE one — all three reachable, two reproduced live (SUPERSEDED and, sharpest, FAILED: "a plan the engine itself records as FAILED backs the PWU claim 'execution succeeded'").
- *sites:* KERNEL (new, in the module that already owns plan lifecycle): `packages/rph-domain/src/execution.ts`, beside `canActivatePlan`/`canStartStepUnderPlan`:
```
export interface PlanSuccessEvidenceInput { readonly planStatus: string; readonly stepStates: readonly string[]; }
export interface PlanSuccessEvidence { readonly ok: boolean; readonly errorCode?: string; readonly reason?: string; readonly offendingStepStates: readonly string[]; }
export function planEvidencesExecutionSuccess(i: PlanSuccessEvidenceInput): PlanSuccessEvidence
```
Refusal order (message quality): status not in {ACTIVE, COMPLETED} -> `RPH_PLAN_NOT_LIVE_FOR_SUCCESS`; zero steps -> `RPH_PLAN_EMPTY`; any step outside {SUCCEEDED, SKIPPED} -> `RPH_PLAN_STEPS_NOT_TERMINAL_SUCCESS` with `offendingStepStates` populated; no SUCCEEDED step -> `RPH_PLAN_PRODUCED_NOTHING`.
CONSUMER 1: `packages/rph-application/src/handlers/execution.ts:473-499` — `completeExecutionPlan`'s guard becomes a thin wrapper that calls the kernel and composes its existing rich messages from `offendingStepStates` (the §20.1/§19-L3 rationale comments at `:443-457` stay; they now describe one predicate rather than one of two).
CONSUMER 2: `packages/rph-application/src/handlers/pwu.ts:670-698` — `rejectUnbackedExecutionSuccess`'s inner predicate becomes: object is an EXECUTION_PLAN AND `state.workUnitId === id` AND `planEvidencesExecutionSuccess({planStatus: state.status, stepStates: steps.map(s => s.stepState)}).ok`. The refusal code stays `RPH_EVIDENCE_MISSING` (its two existing negative tests at `pwu.test.ts:617` and `:641` assert it), with the kernel's reason appended so the caller sees WHICH limb failed.
DOC: `execution.ts:453-457`'s alignment claim becomes true rather than aspirational; state that both planes now call one function and name it.
- *mechanism:* The two planes cannot diverge because there is one function. Note the asymmetry that remains and is CORRECT: `completeExecutionPlan` additionally has `precondition: fromStates('ACTIVE')`, so the plan plane refuses a COMPLETED re-issue on the state fact (JAN-CMDPRE) while the PWU plane accepts a COMPLETED plan as backing. Same predicate, different admissible statuses at the two sites, and the difference is declared by the precondition rather than hidden in a second implementation.
- *killTest:* EXTEND `packages/rph-application/src/handlers/pwu.test.ts`. **The fixture is the defect**: `succeededPlanFor` (`:159-243`) always builds a ONE-step plan that succeeds and leaves the plan ACTIVE, which is why `.some`->`.every` and adding a status term are both green mutants today. Parameterise it — `succeededPlanFor({ stepCount, completeSteps, finalPlanStatus })` — and add:
1. `unbacked/partial-plan-REJECTS`: 5 steps, only s1 completed, s2-s5 QUEUED -> `CompleteExecutionPlan` REJECTS (unchanged) AND `ChangePwuState(executionState:'SUCCEEDED', supportingObjectIds:[plan])` REJECTS `RPH_EVIDENCE_MISSING`. **RED today — the second assertion ACCEPTS, live-reproduced.**
2. `unbacked/superseded-plan-REJECTS`: 1-step succeeded plan, `SupersedeExecutionPlan` to a real successor, then the claim -> REJECTS.
3. `unbacked/failed-plan-REJECTS`: `FailExecutionPlan` after s1 succeeded and s2 QUEUED, then the claim -> REJECTS. (The sharpest variant; the lens did not name it.)
4. `unbacked/cancelled-plan-REJECTS`.
5. `unbacked/active-all-succeeded-ACCEPTS`: the reference seed's exact shape -> ACCEPTED.
6. `unbacked/completed-plan-ACCEPTS`: all steps succeeded then `CompleteExecutionPlan` -> the claim ACCEPTED (the natural order, pinned against an over-tightening to ACTIVE-only).
7. `unbacked/skipped-and-succeeded-ACCEPTS`: one SKIPPED (under a real authorization from FIX-5) + one SUCCEEDED -> ACCEPTED (the allow-list, not a negation).
8. `unbacked/all-skipped-REJECTS` -> the >=1-SUCCEEDED limb at the PWU plane, matching the plan plane.
PLUS pure kernel tests in `packages/rph-domain/src/execution.test.ts` enumerating every refusal branch of `planEvidencesExecutionSuccess`.
DECLARED MUTATIONS: M1 `every` -> `some` -> tests 1,3 RED. M2 drop the status term -> tests 2,3,4 RED. M3 drop the >=1-SUCCEEDED limb -> test 8 RED. M4 narrow to COMPLETED-only -> test 5 RED **and the rph-engine reference-undertaking suite RED** (the seed constraint made executable).
- *behaviourChange:* NEWLY REFUSED: `ChangePwuState(executionState:'SUCCEEDED')` citing a plan that is not ACTIVE|COMPLETED, or has zero steps, or has any step outside {SUCCEEDED, SKIPPED}, or has no SUCCEEDED step. Three of these were reproduced ACCEPTED today (partial, SUPERSEDED, FAILED).
REMAINS ACCEPTED: the reference seed's hop (`reference-undertaking.ts:614`); `pwu.test.ts:367`'s happy path; the demo's `beginExecute` (`+page.server.ts:564`) — all three are single-step ACTIVE plans whose only step is SUCCEEDED.
UNCHANGED BY DESIGN: only `executionState: SUCCEEDED` is guarded. PLANNED/QUEUED/RUNNING/WAITING/FAILED remain controller-owned scheduling facts — `pwu.ts:666-668`'s rule stands: failure must never need permission to record.
CONSEQUENCE WORTH STATING: a plan carrying a CANCELLED step can now back NO PWU success claim (it already could complete no plan). The correct move for that plan is `SupersedeExecutionPlan` or `FailExecutionPlan`. That is the ratified plan-level answer; this fix propagates it rather than inventing it.
- *risk:* LOW-MEDIUM. `pwu.ts` is one module outside the enumerated Tier 3C-ii surface — but `execution.ts:453-457` makes an alignment CLAIM about it from inside the surface, and that claim is false; fixing the claim's referent is in scope. Real risk: the `every` limb is the one that could over-refuse a legitimate mid-flight controller. Mitigated because the controller's mid-flight axis values (RUNNING/WAITING/etc.) are ungated — only the terminal SUCCESS claim tightens. Sequencing risk: land BEFORE FIX-4, because FIX-6 is what makes 'a PWU can only close over a fully-succeeded plan' true, which is what makes FIX-4's terminal-PWU gate strand nothing.

**Findings ANTI-RECURRENCE for the whole family (F-01, F-08, F-26, F-28, F-30) — the structural check that would have caught a tautological, weaker-than-ratified, undeclared or uncalled guard**
- *rule:* "A ratified rule is ENFORCED only when a named production site refuses a named input at the layer the rule speaks about, and a named test observes that refusal end-to-end. Enforcement is DECLARED DATA, not prose; the declaration is executable; and a rule that claims coverage without a live refusal fails CI."
- *sites:* NEW `packages/rph-application/src/handlers/enforcement-register.ts` — one row per enforced rule:
```
export interface EnforcementRow {
  readonly ruleId: string;                    // 'RPH-EXE-006'
  readonly statement: string;                 // verbatim from m11/m12 vocab
  readonly kernel: string | null;             // 'validateStepCompletion' | null (handler-local)
  readonly sites: readonly string[];          // 'completeExecutionStep' — production symbols
  readonly refusalCode: RphErrorCode;
  readonly refusalMarker: string;             // a stable substring of the refusal message
  readonly declaredMutations: readonly string[];   // CON-000 B7, as data
  readonly probe: (h: Harness) => CommandResult;   // REQUIRED — builds the fixture and dispatches the refused input
}
export const ENFORCEMENT_REGISTER: readonly EnforcementRow[]
```
NEW `packages/rph-application/src/handlers/enforcement-register.test.ts` — GATE A.
EXTEND `packages/rph-domain/src/conformance-manifest.ts` + `packages/rph-domain/src/conformance.test.ts` — GATE B.
NEW `packages/rph-domain/src/kernel-wiring.test.ts` + a `DISCLOSED_UNWIRED` allow-list in `conformance-manifest.ts` — GATE C.
EXTEND the JAN-CMDPRE handoff practice with `declaredMutations` as data — GATE D.
PROMOTE `docs/_working/dead-kernel-census.txt` (which already records "canResumeExecutionOnPwu DEAD (tests only)") from a document into GATE C's allow-list, so a known-dead kernel is a CI fact rather than a note in a working file.
- *mechanism:* **GATE A — the live-refusal census (kills the TAUTOLOGY and the WEAKER-REIMPLEMENTATION classes).** `enforcement-register.test.ts` iterates `ENFORCEMENT_REGISTER` and, for each row, runs the row's own `probe` through the real `Engine` + `SqliteStorageAdapter` and asserts the result is REJECTED with `refusalCode` and a message containing `refusalMarker`. A `probe` is a REQUIRED field, so a row cannot be added without a demonstrated refusal, and a guard that becomes vacuous stops refusing and turns its own row RED. This is the check that would have caught F-01 on day one: `RPH_STEP_RESULT_MISSING`'s only two occurrences repo-wide were its definition and a DIRECT kernel call — a register row would have demanded a dispatch that produces it, and no such dispatch was constructible, which IS the finding. Deliberately a single test file driving its own fixtures rather than cross-file instrumentation: vitest isolates workers, so a module-level recorder cannot aggregate, and a file-based collector would be fragile.
**GATE B — layer-correct coverage (kills the DEAD-KERNEL-CERTIFIED-AS-COVERED class).** `m12-conformance.json` already carries, per conformance test, a `then` clause. Add a mechanical check to `conformance.test.ts`: **for every conformance test whose `then` contains "the command is rejected" (or "is rejected"), the rule's coverage must cite an APPLICATION-LAYER test path (`packages/rph-application/**` or `apps/rph-demo/e2e/**`), never a `packages/rph-domain/*.test.ts` unit test — and the rule must have a row in `ENFORCEMENT_REGISTER`.** No new metadata is required; the catalogue already says which rules are command-level. This is exactly what would have caught F-28: RPH-PWU-010's `then` is "the command is rejected; a successor revision or successor PWU is required", and `conformance-manifest.ts:32` cited `packages/rph-domain/src/execution.test.ts`. It generalises well beyond this family — every "the command is rejected" rule in the 125-rule catalogue is audited by one assertion.
**GATE C — the dead-kernel gate (kills the BUILT-AND-NEVER-CALLED class).** A test that reads `packages/rph-domain/src/*.ts`, extracts every exported function, and asserts each is either imported by a NON-TEST file outside `rph-domain` (or used inside a rph-domain production module) or listed in `DISCLOSED_UNWIRED` with a reason and a ticket. Disk reads and path-existence checks are already established practice in `conformance.test.ts`. The point is not to forbid unwired kernels — it is to make "we deferred wiring this" a declaration rather than a silence, since, as the verifier put it, *a dead allowlist reads as an enforced one*.
**GATE D — declared mutations as data.** Each row's `declaredMutations` names the exact weakening and the test it must turn RED. Run live at implementation and recorded in the handoff (the JAN-CMDPRE practice), and available for a later automated mutation job. This closes the loop CON-000 B7 opens: a guard whose negative test survives its own declared mutation is a finding, not a pass.
**INITIAL ROWS (this family, minimum):** RPH-EXE-006 x3 (missing / contradictory / not-success-shaped), §8.4-zero-subject, RPH-EXE-002 x6 (one per REQUIRES_ACTIVE_PLAN command) + 3 declared EXEMPT rows (whose probe asserts ACCEPTANCE — the register must be able to pin a deliberate non-refusal, which is the thing F-26 says Complete never had), RPH-PWU-010 x2 (activate, start), §21.1-skip-authorization x6, and the PWU-backing rule x4.
- *killTest:* The gates ARE the tests; each is itself falsifiable, which matters — an anti-recurrence mechanism that cannot fail is the same disease one level up:
• `enforcement-register.test.ts` — replace any guard with `return null` and its row goes RED. Verify at implementation by mutating `validateStepCompletion` back to the tautology and observing the RPH-EXE-006 rows fail.
• `conformance.test.ts` GATE B — temporarily point `RPH-EXE-002`'s coverage at a rph-domain unit test and assert the gate fails; and add a fixture rule whose `then` says "the command is rejected" with no register row, asserting the gate names it.
• `kernel-wiring.test.ts` GATE C — remove `canResumeExecutionOnPwu`'s new production import and assert the gate names it as UNWIRED (this is precisely the state of the repo today, so the gate is verifiable against HEAD before the fix lands).
• Meta-assertion: `ENFORCEMENT_REGISTER` rows must have DISTINCT `(ruleId, refusalMarker)` pairs, so two rows cannot be satisfied by one refusal.
- *behaviourChange:* No runtime behaviour change — this is test/gate infrastructure. One process change: adding a step command, or a rule whose ratified `then` is "the command is rejected", now REQUIRES a register row with a working probe and an application-layer test. That is the intended friction.
- *risk:* LOW technically; MEDIUM as a discipline. The failure mode is a register that becomes a rubber stamp — rows whose probes are written to pass rather than to interrogate. Two mitigations: (1) every probe must be a DISPATCH through `Engine`, never a direct predicate call (the exact confusion that produced F-28's false coverage), enforceable by making the probe's only tool a `Harness` whose surface is `dispatch` + `stateOf`; (2) `declaredMutations` is required and non-empty, so each row states what would falsify it. Do NOT let the register become a second source of truth for the rules themselves — `ruleId`/`statement` must be copied verbatim from the vocab, and a follow-on gate should assert that equality against `m11-execution.json`/`m12-conformance.json`.

### Invariants
- INV-EXE006-EXPLICIT: `explicitNoOutput` is CALLER-ASSERTED and structurally independent of `hasOutput`. No call site may derive one from the other. The RPH-EXE-006 decision has four reachable cells (accept/accept/refuse/refuse) and every cell has a named test. Falsifier: a call site where the two arguments are syntactic complements.
- INV-AI-CONTENT-ANCHORED: AI-produced professional content never enters the governed stream without an assessable subject. Formally: `aiProduced && subjectCount === 0` implies `!structuredResultHasContent`. A step may produce nothing, but it may not produce something unassessable. Falsifier: an ACCEPTED `ExecutionStepSucceeded` whose provenance is MODEL/AGENT (or whose stepType is MODEL_INVOCATION) carrying a non-empty `structuredResult` with `outputArtifactIds` and `proposedEvidenceIds` both empty.
- INV-CREDIT-NEEDS-LIVE-PLAN: no command may drive a step to TERMINAL SUCCESS (SUCCEEDED or SKIPPED) or record a durable BRANCH `selectedTransitionId` under a non-ACTIVE plan. Falsifier: any ACCEPTED Complete/Skip/Prune on a plan whose status != ACTIVE.
- INV-CLEANUP-ALWAYS-AVAILABLE: for every step in a non-terminal stepState, at least one command reaching a terminal state is ACCEPTED regardless of plan status and regardless of PWU lifecycle state. This is the anti-deadlock counterweight to the previous invariant, and it is why Cancel/Fail/EnterWait are CLEANUP_EXEMPT on BOTH axes. Falsifier: a (stepState, planStatus, pwuState) triple with no accepted exit.
- INV-CLOSED-PWU-NO-NEW-EXECUTION: a PWU in a TERMINAL `workLifecycleState` ({BASELINED, ABANDONED, SUPERSEDED}, read from the machine) admits no plan activation and no execution credit. Falsifier: an ACCEPTED ActivateExecutionPlan or StartExecutionStep whose plan's PWU is terminal — the exact sequence the verifier's live probe drove end-to-end.
- INV-ONE-SUCCESS-DEFINITION: exactly one predicate (`planEvidencesExecutionSuccess`) answers 'did this plan produce work'. Both the plan plane and the PWU plane call it; neither restates it. Falsifier: a store in which `CompleteExecutionPlan` refuses and `ChangePwuState(executionState:'SUCCEEDED')` citing the same plan is accepted.
- INV-AUTHORIZATION-RESOLVES: every id in a payload that carries AUTHORITY (a waiver, a plan revision, a result subject) is RESOLVED against the store and checked for type, status, scope and expiry before it can authorize anything. A truthiness test on an authorization id is a defect by definition. Falsifier: `!!p.<someId>` reachable in any handler decision.
- INV-DECLARED-AUTHORITY: every step command has an explicit, compiler-enforced row in `STEP_COMMAND_AUTHORITY`; an unclassified command REFUSES. There is no default and no silent exemption — both a requirement and an exemption are declarations with a test.
- INV-ENFORCEMENT-OBSERVED: every rule the conformance catalogue states as 'the command is rejected' has a production refusal observed end-to-end through `Engine.dispatch` by a named test. A pure-predicate unit test is never sufficient evidence for a command-level rule.
- INV-5 PRESERVED THROUGHOUT: none of these fixes moves an assurance axis. FIX-2 refuses an EXECUTION transition on an assurance premise (the §8.4 protected-transition pattern already established at `execution.ts:841-856`) but sets nothing; FIX-6 gates an EXECUTION-axis claim on EXECUTION facts only; FIX-5's execution-plane authorization is deliberately a DIFFERENT field from the assurance-plane `waiver`, so a floor waiver can never authorize a step skip.

### Anti-recurrence
**The mechanism: an executable Enforcement Register + three CI gates + declared mutations.** Full construction in FIX-7; the answer to \"what would have caught a tautological guard\" is:

**GATE A, the live-refusal census, is the direct answer.** A tautology is invisible to mutation testing (there is nothing to mutate — the branch is unreachable) and invisible to coverage tools (the call site IS covered; only the refusal is not). It is visible to exactly one question: *has any test ever observed this refusal come out of the command bus?* For `RPH_STEP_RESULT_MISSING` the answer was no — its only two occurrences repo-wide were the kernel and a direct kernel call — and that fact was mechanically checkable at any point in the last several months. The register makes the question a required field: each row supplies a `probe` that dispatches the input the rule says must be refused, and asserts the refusal. **A vacuous guard cannot produce its own refusal, so it cannot satisfy its own row.** This also catches the weaker-reimplementation shape (F-08): a row for the PWU-backing rule with a probe citing a SUPERSEDED plan fails against today's code.

**GATE B, layer-correct coverage, catches the dead-kernel-certified-as-covered shape (F-28)** with a single assertion over data that already exists: any conformance test whose ratified `then` reads \"the command is rejected\" must cite an application-layer test, and must have a register row. RPH-PWU-010's row says exactly that and cited a pure predicate unit test. This generalises to all 125 rules and needs no new metadata.

**GATE C, the dead-kernel gate, catches the built-and-never-called shape at the source:** every exported rph-domain predicate must have a production importer or be listed in `DISCLOSED_UNWIRED` with a reason. The census already exists as prose (`docs/_working/dead-kernel-census.txt:17` literally names `canResumeExecutionOnPwu` as DEAD); promoting it to a gate converts a note somebody wrote into a fact CI enforces. As the verifier put it: *a dead allowlist reads as an enforced one.*

**GATE D turns CON-000 B7 into data.** Each row carries `declaredMutations` — the exact weakening and the test that must go RED. The three defects here would each have been caught by their own declared mutation had one been declared: \"restore `explicitNoOutput: !hasOutput`\", \"change `.every` to `.some`\", \"remove the plan-ACTIVE precheck from Complete\".

**Three things deliberately NOT chosen.** (i) A lint for `f({a: X, b: !X})` — too narrow; a tautology can be spelled a hundred ways, and a syntactic rule invites a syntactic workaround. (ii) Cross-file runtime instrumentation of `kit.reject` — vitest isolates workers, so it needs a file-based collector, and a fragile gate is a gate that gets disabled. (iii) Making the register a second source of truth for the rules — `ruleId`/`statement` are copied verbatim from the vocab and a follow-on gate should assert that equality, or the register becomes the next thing that drifts.

**The one-line rule for reviewers, which is what actually generalises:** *audit the guard's PREMISES, not the guard.* Every defect in this family was a correct guard fed a fact that could not disagree with itself — `!hasOutput`, `!!waiverId`, `steps.some(SUCCEEDED)` with no status, a predicate with no caller. The fail-closed lens verified `mandatory ?? true` and stopped one argument short.

### Seed impact
**VERIFIED AGAINST THE ACTUAL SEED — every fix is a no-op for `driveReferenceUndertaking`, and the seed is a live regression test for two of them.**

`packages/rph-engine/src/reference-undertaking.ts`, `shapeAndExecute` (`:518-616`) is the only place the seed executes: ProposeExecutionPlan (single MODEL_INVOCATION step, `stepState:'QUEUED'`, `transitions: []`) -> Approve -> Activate -> `chg` READY->PLANNED->EXECUTING -> StartExecutionStep -> ProposeEvidence -> `satisfyFloor(evidenceId)` (three floor policies SATISFIED at v1) -> CompleteExecutionStep -> `chg(... executionState 'SUCCEEDED', supportingObjectIds:[planId])`. `packages/rph-engine/src/seed-workbench.ts` contains 9 `send(` calls and creates NO execution plan at all.

Per fix:
• **FIX-1 (RPH-EXE-006):** `:604-613` passes `proposedEvidenceIds: [evidenceId]` -> `hasOutput === true` -> accepted on the first limb; `noOutputResult` is never supplied, so the contradiction limb cannot fire. **No change.**
• **FIX-2 (zero-subject floor):** the step is MODEL_INVOCATION with `executionProvenance: { evaluator: ACTOR }` -> `aiProduced` true; `subjects = [evidenceId]` -> `subjectCount === 1`, so the zero-subject rule does not apply; `structuredResult: {}` is EMPTY under `structuredResultHasContent`. **No change.** The seed remains the system's demonstration that the floor gate lets AI work count.
• **FIX-3 (plan liveness):** the plan is ACTIVE at Start and at Complete. **No change.** Nothing in the seed dispatches Fail/Cancel/EnterWait.
• **FIX-4 (PWU openness):** the PWU is PLANNED/EXECUTING at Activate, Start and Complete; BASELINED is reached only afterwards, via the assurance and governance loops. `ProposePwu` seeds `workLifecycleState: 'PROPOSED'` and every plan's `workUnitId` is a real PWU, so the propose-time `objectType` hardening also passes. **No change.**
• **FIX-5 (skip authorization):** the seed never dispatches `SkipExecutionStep` (repo-wide, the only caller is the demo's `skipStep` action). **No change.**
• **FIX-6 (one success definition):** at the `chg(...SUCCEEDED...)` hop the cited plan is ACTIVE with a single step in SUCCEEDED — `status ∈ {ACTIVE, COMPLETED}` ✓, `steps.length > 0` ✓, `every(SUCCEEDED|SKIPPED)` ✓, `some(SUCCEEDED)` ✓. **No change.** This is also why the tempting `status === 'COMPLETED'` variant is REJECTED as a design: the seed never issues `CompleteExecutionPlan`, so that variant is a seed regression, and mutation M4 of FIX-6's kill test uses the rph-engine suite to prove it.
• **FIX-7:** test infrastructure only.

**Callers that DO change (none of them the seed):** `apps/rph-demo/src/routes/undertakings/[id]/+page.server.ts:548-563` and `:671-690` (add `noOutputResult`); `apps/rph-demo/e2e/execution-plan.e2e.ts:332-352` (add `noOutputResult`, and tighten the assertion so it proves the guard it names); `packages/rph-application/src/handlers/execution-start-gate.test.ts:88-104` (the `complete` helper and its comment); `packages/rph-application/src/handlers/execution-step-skip-cancel.test.ts:151` (mint a real authorization); `packages/rph-application/src/handlers/pwu.test.ts:159-243` (parameterise `succeededPlanFor`). The rph-engine 69-test suite needs no edits and becomes a live guard on FIX-6.

### Open questions
- `executionSkipAuthorization` on DECISION vs. a first-class execution-waiver object. I chose the optional field on `DecisionObjectSchema` (precedent: `waiver: WaiverDetailSchema.optional()`, authored under the 2026-07-16 grant). A first-class object would carry its own lifecycle and authority, but is a much larger contract change and would need its own machine. If the sponsor prefers the object, FIX-5's resolution logic is unchanged — only the load target moves.
- A REPLAN decision authorizing a skip is not required to NAME the successor plan, because no ratified field carries it. So 'an authorized plan revision' is checked as kind + status + scope + step list, not as 'a revision that actually happened'. Adding `successorExecutionPlanId` to `ExecutionSkipAuthorization` would close it; I did not, to avoid inventing a second unratified relationship in the same change.
- `structuredResult` that IS professional content on a step that ALSO names a floor-satisfied artifact is still not individually floor-assessed. FIX-2 closes the zero-subject bypass completely but leaves this narrower residual — the same §8.4 L844 individuation gap `floor-gate.ts:116-118` already discloses. The honest close is to require `structuredResult` content to be recorded as an Artifact (making it a subject); that is a contract change and a behaviour change for every AI step, and belongs in its own work packet rather than riding this family.
- RPH-PWU-010 says 'against the same SEMANTIC VERSION of a BASELINED PWU'. I gate on lifecycle state alone because no command bumps a PWU's semanticVersion today (`pwu.ts:340`/`:831` both pass `loaded.semanticVersion`) and BASELINED has no out-arrow, so an in-place successor revision is unreachable. If versioned PWU successors are ever minted, the gate MUST become version-aware or it will over-refuse the exact case the rule carves out. Flagged in the register row so the trigger is visible.
- Should ProposeExecutionPlan / ApproveExecutionPlan also refuse on a terminal PWU? I chose NO — §20.2 is explicit that approval grants no runtime privileges, and refusing the authoring act would over-refuse someone drafting the successor's plan. But it does mean a closed PWU can accumulate APPROVED plans that can never activate, which is arguably noise. Cheap to flip if the sponsor prefers the earliest possible signal; the table makes it a one-line change.
- The `requireFrom`-before-`precheck` reorder changes refusal CODES (never accept/reject outcomes) for wrong-state commands carrying invalid payloads. I recommend applying it to ALL step commands at once as an enumerated INV-7-style change, because a partial application produces two orderings in one function. It does interact with F-13's observation that Skip's only negative test is refused by a different check than the one it names, so the two should be reconciled by whoever lands second.
- A plan carrying a CANCELLED step can, after FIX-6, back no PWU execution-success claim (it already could complete no plan). That is the ratified plan-level answer propagated, not an invention — but if a cancelled parallel arm is meant to be survivable, the right fix is at the plan-completion allow-list (`execution.ts:483-491`), not at the PWU plane, and it is out of this family's scope.

### Adversarial critique — verdict `NEEDS_REVISION`

### Over-refusal risks
- FIX-1 breaks `execution-start-gate.test.ts`'s `complete` helper (:88-104) and therefore the bulk of that 42-test file, and both demo completion paths (`+page.server.ts:548-563` beginExecute, `:671-690` completeStep default) — all three send `outputArtifactIds: []` + `proposedEvidenceIds: []`. The design names them; they must be same-commit requirements, not follow-ups.
- FIX-6 would break the reference seed if the `currentExecutionState === 'SUCCEEDED'` early exit at `pwu.ts:677` is removed: `reference-undertaking.ts:676`, `:698`, `:806` carry `executionState: 'SUCCEEDED'` with supportingObjectIds naming assessments/evidence, not the plan. The seed passes only because the guard is entry-scoped. The design never mentions this and INV-ONE-SUCCESS-DEFINITION invites the removal.
- FIX-5's kill test #8 `skip-auth/valid-waiver-ACCEPTS` cannot be built: `approveDecision` (governance.ts:245-257) refuses `decisionType === 'WAIVER'` outright, and the only EFFECTIVE-waiver path (RequestWaiver -> GrantWaiver) forces `waivedPolicyId`/`waivedCriterionId` plus `waiverRules` satisfaction. An implementer forcing it produces the exact assurance-plane-authorizes-execution bleed FIX-5 exists to prevent.
- FIX-4's generalisation from BASELINED to the full terminal set {BASELINED, ABANDONED, SUPERSEDED} is a NEW refusal with no ratified backing (RPH-PWU-010 and `canResumeExecutionOnPwu` both name BASELINED only). It is defensible but must be enumerated as an authored extension with a DR-004 §19 divergence entry, not presented as enforcing the ratified rule.
