- FIX-2's over-refusal guard depends entirely on `structuredResultHasContent`'s emptiness semantics, which the design defines inconsistently (`0`/`false` = content, `{}`/`''` = empty). A stricter reading refuses the demo (`structuredResult: {}`) and the seed (`structuredResult: {}`); an ad-hoc reading refuses a legitimate numeric result. The predicate must be pinned before the 16-cell table is written.
- FIX-3 gating CompleteExecutionStep on plan-ACTIVE plus FIX-4 gating Skip/Prune/Complete on PWU-openness narrows exits for steps on dead plans to Cancel/Fail/EnterWait only. That is correct EXCEPT for NOT_READY, whose `cancelExecutionStep` requireFrom `['READY','QUEUED','RUNNING','WAITING']` (execution.ts:1004) excludes it — so INV-CLEANUP-ALWAYS-AVAILABLE is already false today (F-27) and must be stated as aspirational-pending-F-27, not asserted.

### Symptomatic-not-systemic
- FIX-1 converts `explicitNoOutput` from a tautology (`b || !b`) into a FREE caller assertion with no corroborating state — structurally identical to the `mandatory ?? true` / `!!waiverOrRevisionId` shape the design itself names as the family's root cause. The AI-step-reaches-SUCCEEDED-with-no-floor path stays fully open via asserted no-output + empty structuredResult, and that SUCCEEDED remains real authority (STEP_SUCCEEDED grammar guards, FIX-6's `some(SUCCEEDED)` backing, terminal-success for the start gate). Corroborate against `ExecutionStep.outputBindings` (empty in seed and demo, so free) or the root cause survives the fix.
- FIX-3 collapses five inlined `plan.status !== 'ACTIVE'` copies into one table — genuinely systemic. But `execution-view.ts`'s `advanceCommandsFor`/`controlCommandsFor` is the SIXTH copy of the same policy (its docblock at :168-172 restates the split in prose), and FIX-4 introduces a SEVENTH axis (PWU openness) that the projection structurally cannot see because `ExecutionPlanInput` carries no PWU lifecycle field. Two of the three copies the design set out to eliminate remain.
- FIX-6 unifies the two definitions of execution success behind one predicate, but leaves the guard's ENTRY-SCOPED trigger untouched, so 'a PWU's SUCCEEDED claim is backed' is true only at the moment of the claim and never thereafter. The weaker-reimplementation class is closed; the stale-premise class is not, and the design's invariant is written as if it were.
- FIX-7 GATE A rows are authored in the same commit as the guards they certify, and the vocab-equality assertion is deferred to a 'follow-on gate'. Until that assertion exists, the register is a second, self-consistent source of truth — the exact drift mode the design warns about, deferred by its own sequencing.

### Conflicts / gaps
- GATE B cites the wrong source: `m12-conformance.json` contains zero `"then"` keys (its 125 ruleCatalog entries are `{id, statement, layer, sourceRef}`). The reject-shaped `then` clauses are in `m11-execution.json` (7 of 27), `m10-governance.json` (4 of 17), `m9-decomposition.json` (4 of 14) under `conformanceTests[]`.
- GATE B collides with prefix-level coverage: `COVERAGE_BY_PREFIX['RPH-EXE']` -> `packages/rph-domain/src/execution.test.ts` (COVERED, 'RPH-EXE-001..009 by id') and `COVERED_BY_ID` holds only 11 ids. GATE B would immediately fail RPH-EXE-001/002/006 plus the RPH-DEC and RPH-GOV reject rules — ~15 rules, most outside this family — with no disclosed-exemption mechanism (GATE C has one; GATE B does not).
- FIX-5 conflicts with the ratified governance surface: `approveDecision` refuses WAIVER decisions by design ("Approving it here would discharge the floor with no WaiverGranted fact recorded"), and `RequestWaiverPayloadSchema` mandates assurance-plane scope fields. The design's route to an EFFECTIVE execution-plane waiver does not exist.
- FIX-5's stated precedent is inaccurate: `ProposeDecisionPayloadSchema` has no `waiver` field. `WaiverDetail` reaches the Decision object via RequestWaiver, so 'add the field to ProposeDecision, mirroring waiver' does not mirror anything.
- INV-CLEANUP-ALWAYS-AVAILABLE is falsified at HEAD by NOT_READY (F-27): `cancelExecutionStep`'s requireFrom is `['READY','QUEUED','RUNNING','WAITING']` and excludes NOT_READY. The sequencing notes acknowledge this, but the invariant is stated unconditionally in `invariants[]` and FIX-3's matrix is told to assert it.
- FIX-3's ordering change (`requireFrom` before `precheck`) is outcome-neutral for accept/reject (all prechecks in `advanceStep` are pure predicates returning refusals), so the claim holds — but `execution-start-gate.test.ts:699` asserts a Skip refusal currently produced by `canSkipStep` (F-13). After the reorder it is produced by `requireFrom` with a different code and message. That is a REQUIRED test edit in FIX-3's commit, not a coordination note.
- Two records still assert `canResumeExecutionOnPwu` is deferred (`hollow-kernel-triage.md:45` -> W2/W3 WP-2-004/007; `docs/_working/dead-kernel-census.txt:17`). Wiring it without retiring them leaves GATE C's `DISCLOSED_UNWIRED` seeded from stale sources.
- Verified as CORRECT and needing no change (stated here so an implementer does not re-derive them): `packages/rph-projections` may depend on `@janumipwb/rph-domain` (package.json + .dependency-cruiser.cjs rule at :50-52), so siting `STEP_COMMAND_AUTHORITY` in rph-domain is sound; `isTerminalState` is exported from `packages/rph-domain/src/stateMachine.ts:28` and `PWU.workLifecycleState.terminalStates` is `['BASELINED','ABANDONED','SUPERSEDED']` (transitions.data.ts:154); `DecisionTypeSchema` includes both `WAIVER` and `REPLAN`, `DecisionStatusSchema` is `['PROPOSED','EFFECTIVE','REVOKED','SUPERSEDED']`; every application test that proposes a plan seeds its work unit via `ProposePwu` (11/11 files checked), so FIX-4's `objectType === 'PROFESSIONAL_WORK_UNIT'` hardening at propose is safe; the `advanceStep` order really is precheck -> requireFrom -> checkTransition; and the e2e masking risk at `execution-plan.e2e.ts:332` (asserts only `not.toBe('ACCEPTED')` on a QUEUED step) is real exactly as described.

### Missed findings
No assigned finding is silently dropped. All six are addressed: F-01 limb A (FIX-1) and limb B (FIX-2); F-08 (FIX-6); F-26 including the Fail sub-limb the register's verifier partly refuted — correctly handled as a DECLARE-and-test-the-exemption rather than a new gate (FIX-3); F-28 (FIX-4); F-30 (FIX-5); F-42 disposed as docs-only, matching both verifiers' converged verdict that changing the code would strip a running step of the ability to record that it is blocked. Two sub-items are deferred with stated reasons and should stay deferred: F-26's rider to mint the step-level supersede command (the machine's *->SUPERSEDED step arrows at transitions.data.ts:1478-1482 have no command), and F-01's §8.4 L844 individuation residual. One genuine omission inside an addressed finding: F-28's register explicitly asks that, if the rule is not wired, the deferral be recorded on THIS surface — the design wires it but never retires the two existing records that say it is deferred (hollow-kernel-triage.md:45, dead-kernel-census.txt:17), leaving three artefacts disagreeing about the rule's status.

Require both to be retired/reconciled in the same commit, or GATE C's `DISCLOSED_UNWIRED` will be seeded from a stale source.
- IMPLEMENTATION-DETAIL CORRECTIONS THAT WILL OTHERWISE COST A BUILD. (i) `helperSubTypes` in `packages/rph-contracts/vocab/m1-object-fields.json` is a JSON ARRAY of `{name, ...}` objects (54 entries: ActorReference … WaiverDetail), not a map — the design's `helperSubTypes += NoOutputResult = {...}` notation will be mis-read. (ii) Generation ORDER is load-bearing: `gen-messages.ts` resolves a helper type name to `<Name>Schema` only if that symbol already exists in the generated `objects.ts`, so a new helper requires the FULL `bun run gen` (gen-enums -> gen-objects -> gen-messages -> emit), never `gen:messages` alone. (v) Specify `structuredResultHasContent` precisely against `z.unknown()`: the design calls `0` and `false` CONTENT while calling `''` and `{}` EMPTY, which would refuse an AI step whose result is the number 0 while admitting one whose result is `{}` — an arbitrary line the 16-cell unit table would then enshrine. Define it as: content iff (plain object with >=1 own key) OR (array with length > 0) OR (string with a non-whitespace character); everything else — including `null`, `undefined`, `0`, `false`, `{}`, `[]`, `''` — is EMPTY.

CONFIRMED SAFE and requiring no edit: `packages/rph-engine/src/reference-undertaking.ts:604-613` (`proposedEvidenceIds: [evidenceId]` -> hasOutput true), `packages/rph-application/src/handlers/pwu.test.ts:159-243` `succeededPlanFor` (also `proposedEvidenceIds: [evidenceId]`, HUMAN_INTERACTION, `executionProvenance: {}`), and `packages/rph-engine/src/seed-workbench.ts` (no execution commands). State the helper/demo edits as REQUIRED deliverables of FIX-1's commit.

## F2-graph-liveness — Graph seeding, liveness, and the barrier (the reachability family)

### Root cause
ONE question — "can this in-edge ever conduct?" — is answered by THREE independently-maintained code paths in e:\Projects\hestami-ai\JanumiCode\janumiprofessionalworkbench\packages\rph-domain\src\transition-gate.ts, and each new way an edge can die was taught to only one of them.

(1) `inEdgeDisposition` (:204-235) — the BARRIER's answer. Knows about: terminal-non-success sources, BRANCH first-match, false guards, structurally-dead sources.
(2) `branchExcludes` (:162-175) — the REACHABILITY answer, the sole exclusion rule feeding `liveStepIds` (:147). Knows about ONE thing: a resolved BRANCH did not select this arm (it returns false immediately unless the source is in TERMINAL_SUCCESS, :169).
(3) `inEdgesOf(...).length === 0` (:139) vs `buildAdjacency`'s in-degree (:483-488) — two answers to "what is a plan ENTRY". The first counts a source-less plan-entry edge as an in-edge; the second ignores it. The shape is contract-legal (objects.ts:247 `sourceStepId: z.string().optional()`), explicitly blessed (:467 "A missing SOURCE is legitimate"), handled by the barrier (:209) and rendered by the read-model (execution-view.ts:353).

The three consequences follow mechanically:
- (3) disagreeing ⇒ for ANY plan carrying an entry edge the BFS frontier is EMPTY ⇒ `live = {}` ⇒ every source is NEUTRALIZED at :217 (whole graph unstartable) AND every step passes `!live.has(s.id)` at :329 (whole plan offered for waiver-free prune). F-03/04/05. I re-derived this from the code: `frontier = plan.steps.filter(s => inEdgesOf(plan, s.id).length === 0)`, and `inEdgesOf` filters on `targetStepId` alone.
- (2) being narrower than (1) ⇒ a CANCELLED/SUPERSEDED source NEUTRALIZES its edge at the barrier but still CONDUCTS REACHABILITY, so the downstream is simultaneously "unreachable — it should be pruned" (startStepGate :394-398) and "still reachable" (prunableStepIds :329 → handler refusal execution.ts:1065). F-17/F-20. And because the downstream stays live, a waiver-SKIP of the intervening step leaves it terminal-success AND live, so :217 does not fire and the whole dead arm resurrects. F-06.
- (1) and (2) both collapsing FAILED into "terminal-non-success" ⇒ the barrier releases a JOIN over an arm that is REOPENABLE (FAILED→QUEUED is the machine's only terminal out-arrow, transitions.data.ts:1456), so the join runs on partial input and the sanctioned retry then closes the plan as fully SUCCEEDED. F-16.

The deeper cause of the cause: the disposition alphabet conflates "does not block the barrier" with "can never conduct". Those are the same fact for a not-taken arm and for CANCELLED, and DIFFERENT facts for FAILED — so the codebase grew a second predicate rather than fixing the alphabet.

F-34's cubic cost is the same root cause in the cost dimension: because reachability and disposition are separate functions, `inEdgeDisposition` re-runs the whole BFS per edge (:217) with no shared computation.

### Why it survived
Every fixture that exercises the neutralize/prune pair was shaped so the two answers agree.

- transition-gate.test.ts:411-417 is the only FAILED-source prune test and it asserts BOTH ingredients of the contradiction as CORRECT (`prunableStepIds === []` AND `inEdgeDisposition === 'NEUTRALIZED'`) while never asking what `startStepGate` says on the same fixture.
- transition-gate.test.ts:64-70 is titled "a NEUTRALIZED (FAILED-source) in-edge does not, by itself, make a join startable" and its BODY asserts `startableStepIds === ['s4']` — it locks in the opposite of its own title.
- transition-gate.test.ts:166-172, the "cannot diverge" pin, asserts `startStepGate(p,id).ok || !startable.has(id)` — vacuously true for every not-ok step. It only pins the startable direction; the unreachable direction has no assertion anywhere. `grep 'should be pruned'` returns zero test hits monorepo-wide.
- No fixture at ANY layer puts a plan-entry edge into a MULTI-step plan. The two that touch the shape (transition-gate.test.ts:245 passes a loose edge that is not in `plan.transitions`; execution-view.test.ts:570 uses a ONE-step plan) both pass through the :209 short-circuit BEFORE the liveness test at :217 — they are the masking tests, not refutations.
- No CANCELLED/SUPERSEDED step appears as a GRAPH predecessor in any test; execution-view.test.ts:252 covers the linear degenerate, which is the branch that works.
- No parity test compares the empty-transitions degenerate against the explicit-linear graph on the same states — the single cheapest test that would have caught F-06, F-16 and the FAILED conflation at once.
- F-34: zero call-count or complexity assertions exist anywhere (`toHaveBeenCalledTimes` returns nothing in transition-gate.test.ts), so the module's own "this is O(V+E)" header comment was never verified and the cubic regression re-landed silently.

### Fixes

**Findings F-03, F-04, F-05 (all three lenses of the same BLOCKER)**
- *rule:* THE FRONTIER SEEDING RULE (canonical). In a graph plan (transitions.length > 0), a step S is a FRONTIER SEED — a plan entry — iff:  inEdgesOf(S).length === 0  OR  inEdgesOf(S).some(e => e.sourceStepId === undefined).  In words: S is an entry iff no declared step inside the plan leads to S, or the plan itself declares a source-less entry edge into S. `liveStepIds` seeds its BFS with EXACTLY this set. `validateTransitionGraph` computes its 'exactly one entry' set with the SAME exported helper. A step whose in-edges MIX source-less and sourced edges is REFUSED at propose (RPH_VALIDATION_SEMANTIC_FAILED), which makes `some` and `every` coincide for every accepted plan and leaves exactly one meaning of 'entry' in the system.
- *sites:* e:\Projects\hestami-ai\JanumiCode\janumiprofessionalworkbench\packages\rph-domain\src\transition-gate.ts — NEW exported `entryStepIds(stepIds: readonly string[], transitions: readonly GateTransition[]): string[]`; `liveStepIds` (:135-151) seeds from it instead of the inline `inEdgesOf(...).length === 0` filter at :139; `validateTransitionGraph` (:566-589) replaces `stepIds.filter(id => (inCount.get(id) ?? 0) === 0)` (:578) with `entryStepIds(...)`; `buildAdjacency` (:477-490) drops its now-unused `inCount` half and returns only `outEdges`; `unreachableFrom` (:493-508) is seeded from the entry SET; NEW limb `checkEntryEdgeShape(transitions, stepIds)` called from `validateTransitionGraph` BEFORE the entry-count limb.  Unchanged and deliberately preserved: `inEdgeDisposition`'s source-less short-circuit (:209 → SATISFIED) and `checkDanglingIds`'s carve-out (:467-471) — the plan-entry edge stays a first-class, supported shape.
- *mechanism:* Today `inEdgesOf` (:76-77) filters on `targetStepId` alone, so a source-less edge counts as an in-edge and the BFS seed at :139 is empty for the whole class of plans that carry one; `buildAdjacency` (:483-488) counts only edges with BOTH endpoints, so propose-time validation sees the same step as the single entry and accepts. One exported helper removes the possibility of the two planes drifting again: there is no second expression of 'in-degree 0' left in the file. The mixed-shape refusal exists because a step with BOTH an entry edge (always SATISFIED) and a sourced in-edge would be a seed under `some` and a non-seed under `every`, which is the only shape where the coherence invariant INV-GATE-1 could be ambiguous; refusing it fail-closed is cheaper than defining it.
- *killTest:* KT-1.1 (rph-domain/src/transition-gate.test.ts, NEW describe 'a plan-entry edge is an ENTRY, not an in-edge'): steps s1,s2,s3 all QUEUED; transitions [{id:'t0', targetStepId:'s1'}, s1→s2, s2→s3]. Assert validateTransitionGraph.ok === true, startableStepIds === ['s1'], prunableStepIds === [], and — the assertion that kills the mutant — `startableStepIds ∩ prunableStepIds === ∅`. Then with s1 SUCCEEDED assert startableStepIds === ['s2'] and startStepGate(p,'s2').ok === true. Reverting the seed to `.length === 0` makes prunableStepIds ['s1','s2','s3'] and this goes RED on four assertions.
KT-1.2 (same file): the CONTROL — byte-identical plan with the entry edge deleted must produce identical values, proving the entry edge is not doing anything semantically.
KT-1.3 (rph-application/src/handlers/execution-start-gate.test.ts, live Engine + SqliteStorageAdapter): propose the s1(PARALLEL_GROUP)→{s2,s3}→s4 plan WITH `{id, executionPlanId, targetStepId: s1, transitionType:'SEQUENTIAL'}`; after start(1)+complete(1) assert start(2) ACCEPTED and prune(2) REJECTED with 'still reachable'. Both invert under the old seed.
KT-1.4 (transition-gate.test.ts): the mixed shape [{targetStepId:'s2'}, s1→s2] is REFUSED with a message containing 'plan-entry edge'; and the pure entry-edge shape is ACCEPTED (the over-refusal control).
KT-1.5 (transition-gate.test.ts, the anti-drift pin): a table of 6 shapes asserting `entryStepIds(...)` equals the set validateTransitionGraph treats as entries AND equals the set liveStepIds seeds — RED the moment either call site re-implements the predicate.
- *behaviourChange:* (a) A multi-step plan carrying a plan-entry edge now executes normally instead of collapsing (previously: entry step starts, everything downstream refused as 'unreachable', everything offered for waiver-free prune, plan reports COMPLETED after one step). (b) NEWLY REFUSED at ProposeExecutionPlan: a plan where one step has BOTH a source-less entry edge and a sourced in-edge (RPH_VALIDATION_SEMANTIC_FAILED). No shipped plan, seed, fixture or e2e authors that shape. (c) NEWLY REFUSED (by the pre-existing exactly-one-entry limb, now correctly computed): entry edges into two different steps. STILL ACCEPTED: zero entry edges (every current plan), one entry edge into the sole entry step, several entry edges into the SAME step.
- *risk:* Low. The only behaviour that changes for existing plans is none — no plan in the repo carries an entry edge. The risk is the reverse: this is the first code that TRUSTS the shape, so the mixed-shape and multi-entry limbs must land with the seeding change, or a malformed graph reaches a now-permissive runtime. Also note apps\rph-demo\src\routes\undertakings\[id]\+page.server.ts:137 reshapes an empty-string sourceStepId into an entry edge via a TRUTHY test (`...(t.sourceStepId ? {sourceStepId} : {})`) — change it to `!== undefined` in the same commit so the UI plane cannot mint an entry edge the engine never saw.

**Findings F-06 (BLOCKER, third recurrence of the dead-arm class), F-17, F-20, F-38 (code side)**
- *rule:* ONE CANONICAL IN-EDGE DISPOSITION, in two stages, answering ONE question: CAN THIS EDGE STILL CONDUCT?
  SATISFIED  = it HAS conducted (and cannot un-conduct).
  PENDING    = it has not conducted and MAY still.
  NEUTRALIZED = it has not conducted and NEVER will.
Stage 1 — LOCAL disposition (edge-local; reads only the source's own state, the source's stepType/out-edges, and the guard; NEVER liveness):
  1. no sourceStepId                         → SATISFIED   (a plan-entry edge; the plan itself grounds it)
  2. source id does not resolve              → PENDING     (dangling; refused at propose — conservative: never start, never prune)
  3. source state not TERMINAL               → PENDING
  4. source state is REOPENABLE-TERMINAL     → PENDING     (FAILED: the machine can still leave it) [see Fix 3]
  5. source state not TERMINAL-SUCCESS       → NEUTRALIZED (CANCELLED/SUPERSEDED: irrecoverable)
  6. source is BRANCH with ≥1 conditional out-edge → first-match selection: this edge SATISFIED, every other out-edge NEUTRALIZED
  7. edge is unconditional                   → SATISFIED
  8. otherwise                               → guard holds ? SATISFIED : NEUTRALIZED
Stage 2 — REACHABILITY: forward BFS from the frontier seeds (Fix 1); an edge propagates reachability IFF its LOCAL disposition is not NEUTRALIZED. That is the ONLY exclusion rule; `branchExcludes` is DELETED.
Stage 3 — EFFECTIVE disposition (what the barrier and the read-model consume): if the source is not LIVE → NEUTRALIZED; else the local disposition.

THE SKIPPED QUESTION, SETTLED: a LIVE SKIPPED source is TERMINAL-SUCCESS and SATISFIES its out-edges (a waiver-skip advances the plan). A STRUCTURALLY-DEAD SKIPPED source conducts nothing (stage 3). The answer therefore does NOT differ between a PRUNED and a waived SKIP — they are the same state and MUST be indistinguishable; what differs is whether the source is reachable. The ratified prose (DS-004 §5 bullet 2, DR-004 §3) which lists 'source SKIPPED' as a NEUTRALIZER is WRONG and is corrected in Fix 6.

**Findings F-16 (BLOCKER-class harm: a COMPLETED plan whose join provably ran before one of its inputs existed), F-17 (the wrong operator instruction), F-06 (the linear-vs-graph parity break)**
- *rule:* THE BARRIER / JOIN RELEASE RULE. A step may START iff: (i) the plan is ACTIVE, (ii) the step is not TERMINAL, (iii) the step is LIVE, and (iv) NO in-edge is PENDING. The '≥1 SATISFIED' clause is NOT a separate test — it is implied by (iii)+(iv) under INV-GATE-1, and stating it twice is how the two halves drifted.
A JOIN therefore releases when every in-edge has SETTLED: each is either SATISFIED (that arm delivered) or NEUTRALIZED (that arm can never deliver). A FAILED arm has NOT settled — the machine's only terminal out-arrow is FAILED→QUEUED (transitions.data.ts:1456) — so it reads PENDING and the join WEDGES until the arm is retried to terminal-success or explicitly abandoned (Fix 7). This replaces the D7 reading 'a terminal-non-success in-edge is neutralized', which conflated the retryable and the irrecoverable.
TWO SAFETY PROPERTIES, both provable from the rule:
- NO FALSE COMPLETE: Prune reaches only non-live steps; a step is non-live only if every path into it crosses a not-taken BRANCH arm or an irrecoverable-terminal source. In the latter case the plan can never COMPLETE (the CANCELLED/SUPERSEDED step fails the allow-list). So no mandatory taken-path step can be waiver-free skipped, and no join can succeed on an input that never arrived.
- NO DEADLOCK (exhaustive exit table): PENDING-because-unfinished → wait for the predecessor; PENDING-because-FAILED → RetryExecutionStep, or CancelExecutionStep on the arm (Fix 7), or Fail/CancelExecutionPlan; UNREACHABLE and in {NOT_READY, READY, QUEUED} → PruneExecutionStep (guaranteed accepted by INV-GATE-1); UNREACHABLE and in {RUNNING, WAITING} → CancelExecutionStep; already TERMINAL → nothing to do.

**Findings F-17, F-20, and the structural half of F-03/F-04 (the same step simultaneously startable and prunable)**
- *rule:* THE COHERENCE INVARIANT — INV-GATE-1. For every step S of an ACTIVE graph plan:  S ∉ live(plan)  ⟺  every in-edge of S has effective disposition NEUTRALIZED  ⟺  startStepGate(plan, S).code === 'UNREACHABLE'  ⟺  S ∈ prunableStepIds(plan) whenever S.stepState ∈ {NOT_READY, READY, QUEUED}.
And its structural guarantee: startStepGate, startableStepIds and prunableStepIds are all DERIVED FROM ONE COMPUTATION over one context — they are not two mirrored implementations that must be kept in agreement, so they CANNOT disagree.
Proof (both directions, from the Fix-2 stage definitions). (⇒) If S ∉ live then S is not a seed, so every in-edge has a declared source; for each, either its source ∉ live (stage 3 ⇒ NEUTRALIZED) or its source ∈ live and the BFS declined to traverse it, which happens only when the local disposition is NEUTRALIZED (⇒ effective NEUTRALIZED). (⇐) If every in-edge is effectively NEUTRALIZED then no in-edge is source-less (a source-less edge is unconditionally SATISFIED), so S is not a seed by that route; and no in-edge can have been traversed by the BFS, since traversal requires a live source and a non-NEUTRALIZED local disposition. Hence S ∉ live. ∎
CONSEQUENCE: the '!anySatisfied' branch of startStepGate is provably unreachable once the '!live' test precedes it, and is DELETED rather than left as a dead guard (CON-000 B7: a guard no mutation can kill is not a guard).

**Findings F-34 (cubic cost; measured 226-244 ms per start-gate call at fan-out width 200, ~700 ms per page render, against the module's own O(V+E) header claim)**

Every public entry point builds exactly one `GateContext` and threads it; nothing recomputes liveness, adjacency, a local disposition, or a guard evaluation twice within a call. KT-5.2 (transition-gate.test.ts, semantics-unchanged): a table asserting `edgeDispositions(plan)` agrees with `inEdgeDisposition(plan, e)` edge-by-edge across the KT-4.1 fixture matrix — the wrapper and the batch API can never diverge.
KT-5.3 (execution-view.test.ts): extend the existing by-reference guard test at :545-565 to assert that a CLONED edge is absent from the `edgeDispositions` map (fail-loud) rather than silently reported NEUTRALIZED.
- *behaviourChange:* None for pure guard evaluators — which is every evaluator in the system (guardEvaluatorFor and conditionEvaluatorFor are closures over a pre-folded, immutable subject). An IMPURE test double that returned different values on successive calls for the same edge would now see one call; document that in the test helper. The module header's false 'O(V+E)' claim (:132-134) becomes true and is now backed by KT-5.1.
- *risk:* Low. The one subtlety is construction ordering: `localDisposition` must never read `ctx.live` (only `effectiveDisposition` may), or the context construction becomes circular. Encode that by building `live` last and by keeping `localEdgeDisposition` a free function that takes only the index/memo half of the context.

**Findings F-38 (CONFIRMED, doc), F-46 (PLAUSIBLE — dispositioned: behaviour correct, pin missing)**
- *rule:* F-38 — the ratified prose is corrected to match the code, and the correction is DISCLOSED. DS-004 §5 bullet 2 and DR-004 §3 currently say 'NEUTRALIZED = ... or source SKIPPED/FAILED/CANCELLED/SUPERSEDED'. Both become: 'NEUTRALIZED = the edge can never conduct: a not-selected or false CONDITIONAL arm off a resolved BRANCH; a source in an IRRECOVERABLE terminal state (CANCELLED/SUPERSEDED — a terminal state the machine cannot leave); or a source that is STRUCTURALLY DEAD, however it reached its state. PENDING = the edge has not conducted and may still: a non-terminal source, OR a source in a REOPENABLE terminal state (FAILED — the machine can still leave it). SATISFIED = the edge has conducted: a plan-entry edge, or a LIVE terminal-success (SUCCEEDED/SKIPPED) source whose guard holds. A LIVE SKIPPED source is terminal-success and SATISFIES its out-edges — a waived skip advances the plan; a structurally-dead SKIPPED source satisfies nothing. The disposition never depends on WHICH COMMAND produced a state.' DS-004 D7's 'which correctly neutralizes pruned/branch/non-success in-edges rather than wedging on them' is amended to 'pruned/branch-excluded/irrecoverable in-edges'; DR-004 §19 gains an explicit DISCLOSED DIVERGENCE entry (§15 currently asserts 'none intended').
F-46 — DISPOSITION: the behaviour is CORRECT and stays. STEP_SUCCEEDED is a literal-state predicate and is deliberately NARROWER than the gate's terminal-success set. I DECLINE the proposed STEP_TERMINAL_SUCCESS operator: the composition {op:'ANY', operands:[STEP_SUCCEEDED(x), STEP_STATE(x,'SKIPPED')]} already expresses it, and a new op adds contract surface, a new exhaustive-switch case in describeCondition, and a new unkilled-mutant surface for zero new capability. What is missing is the PIN, which the verifier proved by mutation (widening line 122 to include SKIPPED killed 0 of 734 tests).
- *sites:* Docs: e:\Projects\hestami-ai\JanumiCode\janumiprofessionalworkbench\docs\Execution Plan View Design and Implementation Planning\JAN-EXECPLAN-DS-004 Transition Grammar & Flow Interpreter (Tier 3C-ii) Design.md §5 (the three disposition bullets) and §6 D7; ...\JAN-EXECPLAN-DR-004 Detailed Implementation Roadmap.md §3 (the in-edge disposition bullet), §15 ('Divergences from DS-004: none intended') and §19 (add the disclosure).
Code comments (they are the operative spec for the next implementer): e:\...\packages\rph-domain\src\transition-gate.ts header :10-14 and :122-134 (the false O(V+E) claim and the 'byte-identical' claim, both now true and both now tested); :153-160 (branchExcludes' rationale — delete with the function); :198-203 (the disposition docblock — replace with the three-way 'can this edge still conduct?' definition). e:\...\packages\rph-projections\src\execution-view.ts:293.
F-46: e:\...\packages\rph-domain\src\condition-grammar.ts:121-122 gains one line — 'STEP_SUCCEEDED is deliberately NARROWER than the gate's terminal-success set (transition-gate.ts TERMINAL_SUCCESS includes SKIPPED). For the gate's notion write ANY(STEP_SUCCEEDED(x), STEP_STATE(x,"SKIPPED")).' Plus the test below.
- *mechanism:* F-38 is a live, wrong normative statement about shipped behaviour, and it is the WRONG side of the argument: implementing the §5/§3 wording would cascade-prune the downstream of every waived skip. Because DR-004 §3 is a one-sentence digest with no counterweight, a second-consumer implementer derives 'terminal-success = SUCCEEDED only' and builds the wrong predicate — which is exactly how this family's defects propagate. Correcting the prose in the same commit as Fix 2 keeps the single-definition discipline across the doc/code boundary. F-46's asymmetry is real and correct; the only defect is that no test pins it, so a future 'harmonization' could widen STEP_SUCCEEDED to match the gate and no test would notice.
- *killTest:* KT-6.1 (rph-domain/src/condition-grammar.test.ts — the F-46 pin, proven to kill by the verifier's mutation run): with a subject containing a SKIPPED step, assert `evaluateCondition({op:'STEP_SUCCEEDED', stepId:'x'}, subject) === false` AND `isTerminalSuccessStepState('SKIPPED') === true` in the SAME test, named 'STEP_SUCCEEDED is deliberately narrower than the gate's terminal-success set'. RED under the widening mutant that survives all 734 tests today.
KT-6.2 (rph-domain/src/transition-gate.test.ts — the F-38 pin, so the doc rule and the code rule are asserted as ONE named decision): a test named after the corrected sentence, asserting on one fixture that a LIVE SKIPPED source SATISFIES while a DEAD SKIPPED source NEUTRALIZES (this is KT-2.5; cite it from the doc correction so the prose has a test address).
Doc conformance has no runtime test — the enforcement is that DR-004 §19's disclosure entry names the two kill tests above by file and title.
- *behaviourChange:* None. F-38 is doc-only (the code is the correct side). F-46 is test+comment only. The behaviour changes described in the corrected prose are those introduced by Fixes 2 and 3, which the §19 disclosure must state explicitly rather than presenting as a clarification.
- *risk:* Very low. The only risk is a doc edit that describes the OLD behaviour for FAILED — write the doc correction after Fix 3 is settled, and state the FAILED reclassification as a disclosed divergence from D7, not as a restatement of it.

**Findings Fix 3's enumerated capability removal (no finding of its own; required by the 'no over-refusal' constraint)**
- *rule:* ABANDONING A FAILED ARM IS AN EXPLICIT, GOVERNED ACT. Add the arrow FAILED → CANCELLED to the ExecutionStep.stepState machine, triggered by cancelExecutionStep / ExecutionStepCancelled, and widen CancelExecutionStep's drivesFrom to READY|QUEUED|RUNNING|WAITING|FAILED. An operator who will not (or cannot) retry a failed arm CANCELS it, which records the abandonment and its reason in the governed stream; the arm's in-edge then reads NEUTRALIZED (irrecoverable), the join releases if another arm delivered, and the arm's own downstream becomes structurally dead and prunable. The plan remains non-completable (a CANCELLED step fails the §20.1 allow-list) and must end via FailExecutionPlan or CancelExecutionPlan — which is the honest record of what happened.
- *sites:* e:\Projects\hestami-ai\JanumiCode\janumiprofessionalworkbench\packages\rph-domain\vocab\m2-transitions.json — add `{ "from": "FAILED", "to": "CANCELLED", "trigger": "cancelExecutionStep / ExecutionStepCancelled", "note": "AUTHORED (JAN-EXECREM): abandoning a failed arm is an explicit governed act; it is what makes the barrier's FAILED=PENDING rule non-over-refusing" }` to the ExecutionStep.stepState transitions (near the existing FAILED→QUEUED at :1551-1555), then `bun run gen:transitions` + prettier (NEVER hand-edit packages\rph-domain\src\transitions.data.ts).
e:\Projects\hestami-ai\JanumiCode\janumiprofessionalworkbench\packages\rph-contracts\vocab\m3-commands-events.json:911 — `drivesFrom` becomes 'READY|QUEUED|RUNNING|WAITING|FAILED'; regenerate contracts.
e:\Projects\hestami-ai\JanumiCode\janumiprofessionalworkbench\packages\rph-application\src\handlers\execution.ts:1004 — `cancelExecutionStep`'s `requireFrom` becomes ['READY','QUEUED','RUNNING','WAITING','FAILED'], mirroring the declared drivesFrom (the JAN-CMDPRE discipline: the handler enforces exactly what the vocab declares).
- *mechanism:* Fix 3 makes a FAILED in-edge wedge its successors. Without an abandon primitive that is a capability loss: a plan with a permanently failed arm could no longer drive its remaining independent work. Cancel is already the system's 'terminate an existing step' verb (it is CLEANUP, deliberately not plan-ACTIVE-gated, execution.ts:991-997), and CANCELLED is already an irrecoverable terminal with no out-arrow — so the abandonment is permanent and cannot resurrect the arm behind a join that has already fired. Note this also removes the last route to F-16's corruption: after an abandon, the arm can never be retried at all. Deriving REOPENABLE_TERMINAL from the machine (Fix 2) is stable under this change: FAILED already had an out-arrow, and CANCELLED still has none.
- *killTest:* KT-7.1 (rph-domain/src/transitions.test.ts or transition-gate.test.ts): `canTransition('ExecutionStep.stepState','FAILED','CANCELLED') === true`, AND the KT-2.4 derivation still yields REOPENABLE_TERMINAL === {'FAILED'} and zero out-arrows from CANCELLED/SUPERSEDED — i.e. the new arrow must NOT change the barrier classification.
KT-7.2 (rph-application/src/handlers/execution-step-skip-cancel.test.ts, the positive path): on the parallel plan, complete(2), fail(3), start(4) REJECTED ⇒ CancelExecutionStep(3, reason) ACCEPTED ⇒ start(4) now ACCEPTED and complete(4) ACCEPTED ⇒ CompleteExecutionPlan REJECTED naming CANCELLED ⇒ CancelExecutionPlan ACCEPTED. RED without the arrow (the cancel is refused by checkTransition) and RED if the abandoned arm's edge is not NEUTRALIZED.
KT-7.3 (the re-issue/anti-vacuity pin the whole Cancel command lacks per F-12): after the arm is CANCELLED, a second CancelExecutionStep is REJECTED with RPH_ILLEGAL_STATE_TRANSITION and appends no second ExecutionStepCancelled — with one positive fixture per source state in the widened requireFrom set, so a mutant that drops any single source state goes RED.
KT-7.4: the abandoned arm's own downstream is now prunable and NOT startable (the Fix-2 rule applied to the new state).
- *behaviourChange:* NEWLY ACCEPTED: CancelExecutionStep on a FAILED step. Previously refused by both the machine and requireFrom. This is a widening, so KT-7.3 is mandatory — a widened source set with no re-issue test is precisely the unkilled-mutant shape this repo has been bitten by before. Nothing that was accepted becomes refused.
- *risk:* Medium — it touches two GENERATED surfaces (m2-transitions.json → transitions.data.ts; m3-commands-events.json → contracts) and it widens a requireFrom that the JAN-CMDPRE series treats as a declared contract. It also adds one more Cancel shape for F-36's attempt-closing fix (another family) to handle. If the sponsor prefers to defer, Fix 3 must be deferred with it: shipping Fix 3 alone leaves an enumerated capability removed with no replacement, which violates the no-over-refusal constraint.

### Invariants
- INV-GATE-1 (COHERENCE). For every step S of an ACTIVE graph plan: S ∉ live(plan) ⟺ every in-edge of S is effectively NEUTRALIZED ⟺ startStepGate(plan,S).code === 'UNREACHABLE' ⟺ S ∈ prunableStepIds(plan) whenever S.stepState ∈ {NOT_READY, READY, QUEUED}. Guaranteed structurally: all three verdicts read one `ctx.live` produced by one computation. Pinned by KT-4.1/KT-4.2.
- INV-GATE-2 (SINGLE DISPOSITION). Exactly one function in the codebase answers 'can this in-edge conduct?'. `branchExcludes` is deleted; reachability is the transitive closure of non-NEUTRALIZED local dispositions. Any future way an edge can die is added to `localEdgeDisposition` or it is not added at all. Pinned by KT-2.5 + KT-4.1 (a second rule set cannot satisfy both).
- INV-GATE-3 (SINGLE ENTRY DEFINITION). Exactly one function answers 'what is a plan entry?' — `entryStepIds` — used by both propose-time validation and runtime liveness; and a step's in-edges may not mix source-less and sourced. Pinned by KT-1.4/KT-1.5.
- INV-GATE-4 (DISPOSITION MEANING). SATISFIED = the edge has conducted; PENDING = it has not and may still; NEUTRALIZED = it has not and never will. Every state that produces SATISFIED or NEUTRALIZED is irreversible in the ExecutionStep.stepState machine, so a disposition never regresses — which is what makes the barrier safe to release on. Pinned by KT-2.4 (machine derivation) + KT-3.4 (parity matrix).
- INV-GATE-5 (LINEAR/GRAPH PARITY). For the same steps and states, the empty-transitions degenerate and the explicit-linear graph return identical startableStepIds and identical startStepGate verdicts, for every state of the machine. This is the seed's protection and the test that would have caught F-06 and F-16 on the day they landed. Pinned by KT-3.4.
- INV-GATE-6 (NO FALSE COMPLETE). PruneExecutionStep can reach only a non-live step; a step is non-live only if every path into it crosses a not-taken BRANCH arm or an irrecoverable-terminal source; in the latter case the plan can never satisfy §20.1's allow-list. Therefore no mandatory taken-path step can be driven to terminal-success SKIPPED without a waiver, and no plan can report COMPLETED holding a step that never legitimately ran.
- INV-GATE-7 (NO DEADLOCK). Every non-startable step has a legal, named exit: unfinished predecessor → wait; FAILED predecessor → Retry / Cancel-the-arm / Fail-or-Cancel the plan; UNREACHABLE in {NOT_READY,READY,QUEUED} → Prune (guaranteed accepted by INV-GATE-1); UNREACHABLE in {RUNNING,WAITING} → Cancel. The engine never names a command it will refuse.
- INV-GATE-8 (COST). Each public entry point is O(V+E) and evaluates each guard at most once per edge. Pinned by KT-5.1 (call counts, not wall-clock).
- INV-5 preserved: every symbol in this family reads execution state and writes nothing; no assurance axis is touched. The one new write path (Fix 7's FAILED→CANCELLED) moves stepState only.

### Anti-recurrence
WHY THE DEAD-ARM CLASS RECURRED THREE TIMES, AND WHY THIS CLOSES IT.

DWP-07 keyed deadness on WHICH COMMAND produced SKIPPED (a persisted `prunedAsUnreachable` flag). A waiver-skip drove the same arrow and resurrected the arm. DWP-08 correctly re-keyed deadness on the GRAPH — but implemented the graph rule as a SECOND predicate (`branchExcludes`) beside the existing barrier predicate (`inEdgeDisposition`), and taught it only the BRANCH case. F-06 is what happens when the surface learns a third way an edge can die (an irrecoverable predecessor): the knowledge lands in one predicate, the two disagree, and the disagreement shows up as either resurrection or deadlock. Each fix was CORRECT and each was LOCAL, and locality is the disease. The same pattern produced the entry-edge collapse (two definitions of in-degree-0) and the cubic cost (liveness recomputed because it was not a shared value).

The structural close is therefore not 'handle CANCELLED too'. It is:
1. ONE predicate for edge conductivity (INV-GATE-2). There is no longer a second place to teach.
2. ONE definition of entry (INV-GATE-3), used by both planes.
3. ONE computation feeding all three exported verdicts (INV-GATE-1) — startStepGate, startableStepIds and prunableStepIds are the same function evaluated three ways, so a future divergence is not a bug that can be introduced by editing one call site.
4. The state sets DERIVED FROM THE MACHINE (transitions.data.ts), never re-hardcoded: 'irrecoverable' means 'terminal with no out-arrow', which is a fact about the vocab, not a list someone maintains.

Three PERMANENT tests make regression loud rather than silent, and each is a class-killer rather than an instance-killer:
- KT-4.1, the INV-GATE-1 property over a fixture matrix: any future divergence between the gate and the prune set is RED regardless of which new state or shape caused it.
- KT-3.4, the linear/graph parity matrix iterated over STEP_MACHINE.states: any new step state, or any new asymmetric treatment of an existing one, is RED without anyone thinking to write a test for it.
- KT-2.4, the machine-derivation pin: a vocab change that alters terminal reachability forces a deliberate re-decision instead of silently reclassifying an edge.

STANDING RULE for this surface, to be recorded in DR-004 §19 and in the transition-gate.ts header: 'A new way for an edge to die is added to localEdgeDisposition and nowhere else. If you find yourself writing a second predicate about an edge, you are re-introducing the DWP-07/08/F-06 defect.' And the test-shape rule this family teaches: a test that asserts ONE side of a two-sided fact (prunable OR gate; disposition OR startability) is not a pin — the assertion must name both sides on one fixture, which is exactly what transition-gate.test.ts:411-417 and :166-172 failed to do.

### Seed impact
NO IMPACT EXPECTED; the seed is the linear degenerate and the linear rule is preserved verbatim.

- packages\\rph-engine\\src\\reference-undertaking.ts:556 proposes `transitions: []` for every plan, so every seeded plan takes the empty-transitions path. Fixes 1, 2 and 3 are graph-path-only: the entry-edge seeding rule, the reachability closure and the FAILED reclassification are all inside `if (transitions.length > 0)`.
- Fix 4 MOVES the linear rule (from `linearFrontier` and the linear limb of `startStepGate`) into `stepStartability` without changing it: for step i, startable iff every EARLIER array-index step is terminal-success and step i is non-terminal. That yields the identical singleton the scalar `linearFrontier` produced (proof: the first non-terminal-success step is the only index that can qualify, and it is excluded when terminal). KT-3.4's parity matrix asserts byte-identity across all 10 machine states; run it BEFORE the graph-side changes so a linear regression is attributable.
- `TERMINAL` becomes machine-derived. Verified identical today: transitions.data.ts:1436 `terminalStates: ['SUCCEEDED','FAILED','SKIPPED','CANCELLED','SUPERSEDED']` equals the hardcoded set at transition-gate.ts:19. `TERMINAL_SUCCESS` is unchanged.
- The seed never drives a step to FAILED or CANCELLED (it runs Start → Complete on a single MODEL_INVOCATION step per PWU, gated by the §8.4 floor), so the Fix-3 reclassification cannot touch it.
- Fix 7 regenerates packages\\rph-domain\\src\\transitions.data.ts and the contracts. Risk to the seed is limited to regeneration hygiene: run `bun run gen:transitions` + prettier, and check packages\\rph-engine\\src\\emitted-event-conformance.test.ts, replay-conformance.test.ts and transitions.test.ts, which read the generated machine. No arrow-count assertion exists in rph-domain/src/transitions.test.ts (checked), so adding an arrow should be inert there.
- EXPECTED RESULT: rph-engine's 69 tests green with no edit. Treat any rph-engine failure as a defect in this design, not as a fixture to update — the seed is the regression bar.

### Open questions
- Should the downstream of an irrecoverably-CANCELLED predecessor be PRUNED (SKIPPED, as designed here) or CASCADE-CANCELLED? Prune reuses the existing arrow and needs no new command, and the plan is non-completable either way, so the governed record is honest in both. But 'SKIPPED' reads as 'deliberately not done' while 'CANCELLED' reads as 'stopped' — a cascade would be semantically sharper at the cost of a new command and a NOT_READY/READY/QUEUED→CANCELLED arrow set. I have CHOSEN prune; flagging because it is a records-quality judgement a sponsor may hold differently.
- Fix 7 gives an abandoned arm's plan no route to COMPLETED (a CANCELLED step fails the §20.1 allow-list). Is 'abandon an optional arm and still complete the plan' a capability the sponsor wants? It would require either a FAILED→SKIPPED arrow routed through canSkipStep's waiver rule, or an authored step-level `mandatory` field (F-30 notes waiverOrRevisionId is never resolved today). Out of scope here; it is a §21.1 governance question, not an interpreter question.
- Should the barrier consider the retry cap? A FAILED arm whose cap is exhausted can never be retried, so arguably it is irrecoverable rather than reopenable. I deliberately did NOT do this: attemptsMade lives in the event log, not in GatePlan, and importing it would make the gate impure with respect to its read-model and would give the read-model and the authority different inputs — the exact class of defect this family is about. Consequence: a cap-exhausted arm reads PENDING and must be abandoned via Fix 7 or the plan terminated. Confirm that is acceptable.
- F-34's verifier noted `transitionRows` renders a per-edge disposition for EVERY edge on every page render. With Fix 5 that is O(V+E), but nothing bounds plan size — rph-contracts declares `steps`/`transitions` as unbounded arrays (no `.max()`). A propose-time size cap is a separate, cheap hardening I have NOT specified because it is a contract change outside this family. Flagging for whoever owns the contracts family.
- DS-004 §5 promises the linear degenerate is 'byte-identical'. After Fix 4 that is true for startableStepIds and startStepGate, but prunableStepIds deliberately differs (a linear plan never prunes, pinned by transition-gate.test.ts:223) while an explicit-linear GRAPH with a cancelled predecessor now does prune. I have stated parity as an invariant over the two START verdicts only. If the sponsor reads 'byte-identical' as covering prune too, the doc needs a further amendment — or explicit-linear graphs need a prune carve-out, which I would resist as a special case.

### Adversarial critique — verdict `NEEDS_REVISION`

### Over-refusal risks
- RC-1: the literal reading of INV-GATE-1 refuses the FIRST start of every graph plan (every entry step is vacuously 'all in-edges NEUTRALIZED'). This would break e2e execution-flow.e2e.ts (both the BRANCH and PARALLEL specs), execution-start-gate.test.ts's activeParallelPlan(), and transition-gate.test.ts:51/:160. It does NOT reach the reference seed (linear), which is precisely why the seed would not catch it.
- RC-3: if a terminal-SUCCESS state ever gains an out-arrow, the step-4-before-step-5 ladder makes every SUCCEEDED/SKIPPED predecessor PENDING — total system wedge, seed included. Not reachable today (verified: only FAILED has a terminal out-arrow), but the ordering is the difference between a wrong answer and a dead system.
- RC-4: Fix 3 + Fix 7 as written are a NET capability removal at the UI, because CONTROL_BY_STEP_STATE.FAILED = [] and that table is deliberately not machine-derived. An operator with a permanently failed arm has retry-or-nothing; the join never starts. This is the exact over-refusal the design's own 'no over-refusal' constraint forbids, and the design believes it has been discharged.
- Fix 3 in the single-chain (non-join) case: s1→s2(FAILED)→s3 previously refused the start AND refused the prune; it still refuses both, so no NEW over-refusal there. Confirmed against transition-gate.test.ts:411-417 — that test's prunableStepIds === [] assertion stays green and must not be 'fixed'.
- Fix 2's newly-accepted prune (downstream of a CANCELLED predecessor) is a WIDENING of PruneExecutionStep, which is the command whose unchecked form was a universal §21.1 waiver bypass. The design's argument that this cannot launder a plan to success (the CANCELLED step itself fails completeExecutionPlan's allow-list at execution.ts:483-491) is correct and I verified it — but it depends entirely on that allow-list, so the design must name execution-plan-completion.test.ts:160 ('REJECTS completing with a CANCELLED or SUPERSEDED step') as a load-bearing co-dependency that must not be relaxed by any other family.
- Fix 1's mixed-shape refusal (a step with both a source-less and a sourced in-edge) is genuinely inert today — no plan in packages, apps or e2e authors an entry edge in a multi-step plan; the only two fixtures that touch the shape are transition-gate.test.ts:245 (a loose edge not in plan.transitions) and execution-view.test.ts:570 (a one-step plan). Both remain accepted. This one is clean.
- Fix 4's move of the plan-ACTIVE test into stepStartability is safe: `startStepGate` has exactly ONE call site (handlers/execution.ts:723) and it checks plan-ACTIVE first (execution.ts:709). Verified by grep across packages and apps.

### Symptomatic-not-systemic
- THE HEADLINE ONE (RC-2). The design closes one PRODUCER of an empty liveness set and leaves the AMPLIFIER — `prunableStepIds`' `!live.has(s.id)` filter turning empty-live into prune-everything-waiver-free — completely untouched. That amplifier is what makes F-03/04/05 BLOCKERs rather than cosmetic. Because `validateTransitionGraph` runs only on the ProposeExecutionPlan PAYLOAD while `liveStepIds` runs on the persisted bag via `toGatePlan`, any legacy plan, any future validation-limb regression, or any payload/persistence shape drift re-opens the identical universal waiver bypass. The design's own anti-recurrence section says 'locality is the disease' and then applies a local fix to the very finding that most needs the consumer-side fail-closed rule.
- RC-6. Fix 4 unifies startStepGate/startableStepIds/prunableStepIds on one `ctx.live`, which genuinely kills the F-17/F-20 contradiction for the live-vs-dead axis. But the SECOND axis of the same contradiction — dead-but-not-in-PRUNABLE_SOURCE_STATES — is left with the old message, and Fix 2 newly populates that state. The unified computation does not by itself make the engine stop naming a command it will refuse; the refusal COMPOSITION has to be exhaustive over the gate code too, and the design specifies it only for `startExecutionStep`, not for `pruneExecutionStep`.
- RC-4/RC-10. Fix 7 is presented as the systemic replacement for a removed capability, but it is scoped to the command layer only: the affordance table (execution-view.ts), the UI, and the partial-input record are all left as the previous shape. That is the same 'each repair was local' pattern the design correctly diagnoses in DWP-07/08 — the knowledge that FAILED can now be abandoned lands in the machine and the handler and in neither of the two other planes that must know it.
- RC-11. INV-GATE-2 ('exactly one function answers can-this-edge-conduct') is real and good, but the design does not close the *temporal* half of the same class: a BRANCH with no recorded selection re-derives its answer on every read, so the single predicate still returns different answers at different times. One predicate with an unstable input is not materially safer than two predicates. The fail-closed interim in RC-11 (unrecorded selection ⇒ PENDING, never SATISFIED/NEUTRALIZED) makes the single-predicate guarantee actually hold rather than deferring it to another family.

### Conflicts / gaps
- RC-12: the Fix-7 vocab instruction cites 'the handler enforces exactly what the vocab declares' as discipline, but packages/rph-application/src/handlers/command-reissue-guard.test.ts:15, kit.ts:494 and intent.ts:240 all state that `drivesFrom` 'has no ratified authority' and that requireFrom is deliberately hand-authored from the machine's in-arrows instead. Also verified: `drivesFrom` is generated into NO file under packages/rph-contracts/src, so the 'regenerate contracts' step is a no-op and the stated risk profile is wrong.
- RC-5: Fix 7 conflicts with the documented RPH-EXE-002 boundary at execution.ts:991-997 — cancelExecutionStep is exempt from the plan-ACTIVE precheck specifically because it terminates EXISTING work rather than opening new work. A FAILED step is not existing work. The exemption must not silently extend to it.
- RC-11: Fix 3's barrier-safety argument and INV-GATE-4 depend on the BRANCH-DECISION family (F-15/F-21/F-23) landing. The design flags the dependency for INV-GATE-1's value but not for the two places where it is load-bearing for SAFETY. Add the fail-closed interim so this family is correct standalone.
- RC-4: Fix 7 conflicts with the F-11 affordance discipline as implemented (execution-view.ts:16, CONTROL_BY_STEP_STATE) — adding a machine arrow does not add an affordance here, by explicit design. The design assumes it does.
- Fix 7 hands a new Cancel shape to the ATTEMPTS family (F-36). Note the specific novelty the design does not: a FAILED step's attempt is ALREADY closed, so ExecutionStepCancelled will arrive with no open attempt — F-36's fix must not synthesize a spurious attempt record for it. State that constraint rather than just 'hand them the extra case'.
- F-17 sub-claim (c) — 'cancelling ONE parallel arm still releases the join but silently converts the plan into one that can never COMPLETE, with no diagnostic at the moment of the cancel' — receives no disposition. Both verifiers called it design-consistent, so declining is defensible, but the design gives an explicit disposition only for F-46. Add one, since Fix 7 makes this shape MORE common (every abandoned arm produces it deliberately).
- F-34's un-capped plan size is correctly identified and correctly handed off, but note the handoff has no owner named: rph-contracts declares steps/transitions as unbounded arrays with no `.max()`, and after Fix 5 the cost is O(V+E) but the memory and the render are still unbounded. State it as a disclosed residual with a named recipient family rather than an open question.

### Missed findings
All ten assigned findings (F-03, F-04, F-05, F-06, F-16, F-17, F-20, F-34, F-38, F-46) receive a named fix or an explicit disposition — none is silently dropped, and the F-46 decline (pin, not new operator) is well-argued and matches the verifier's own mutation evidence. Three are only PARTIALLY closed. (1) F-05 — the design closes the entry-edge producer of an empty liveness set but not the amplifier that turns any empty set into a universal waiver-free prune; F-05's stated harm ("Prune becomes a universal waiver bypass") therefore survives via other producers (see RC-2). (2) F-16 — the causality inversion is closed at the source, but DR-004:223's second clause ("nor count as a real contribution") is still unimplemented and Fix 7 deliberately re-creates the partial-input-unrecorded shape for an abandoned arm, with no disclosure (RC-10). (3) F-17/F-20 — the live-vs-dead contradiction is genuinely killed by the single-computation design, but the same defect shape (the engine asserting reachability the gate has denied) survives on the dead-RUNNING/WAITING path that Fix 2 newly creates, because only startExecutionStep's message is made code-derived (RC-6). One sub-claim, F-17 (c), gets no disposition at all.

### Required changes
- RC-1 (BLOCKER — the invariant text is itself vacuous and, if implemented literally, is a total over-refusal). INV-GATE-1 is stated as `S ∉ live ⟺ every in-edge of S has effective disposition NEUTRALIZED`. That biconditional is FALSE for a frontier seed with ZERO in-edges: 'every in-edge is NEUTRALIZED' is VACUOUSLY TRUE over an empty set, yet the step is live. The design's own (⇐) proof only refutes the source-less-edge route to seed-hood ('a source-less edge is unconditionally SATISFIED') and silently drops the `inEdgesOf(S).length === 0` route — the route EVERY current graph plan's entry step takes (activeParallelPlan's s1, the e2e BRANCH's s1, transition-gate.test.ts:157-164's s1). Today the code is protected by transition-gate.ts:385 `if (inEdges.length === 0) return { ok: true }` sitting BEFORE the `!anySatisfied` limb; Fix 4 DELETES both. The prose is what an implementer will follow when 'simplifying' `stepStartability`, and it says every entry step is UNREACHABLE. Required: restate INV-GATE-1 as `S ∉ live ⟺ (S is not a frontier seed) ∧ (S has ≥1 in-edge and every in-edge is effectively NEUTRALIZED) ⟺ gate.code === 'UNREACHABLE'`, and make KT-4.1's fixture matrix carry BOTH seed shapes (zero-in-edge entry, source-less-entry-edge target) with an explicit positive assertion that each is live, `gate.ok === true`, and NOT in prunableStepIds. Without that assertion the mutant 'replace `!ctx.live.has(step.id)` with `inEdges.every(NEUTRALIZED)`' is unkilled by every test the design proposes.
- RC-2 (BLOCKER — this is the 'symptomatic not systemic' charge; the design fixes one PRODUCER of an empty liveness set and leaves the AMPLIFIER intact). F-05's harm is not 'entry edges are mis-seeded'; it is that `prunableStepIds` (transition-gate.ts:326-333) turns ANY empty `live` into a waiver-free prune of EVERY step in the plan — the universal §21.1 bypass. Fix 1 removes the entry-edge producer. It does not remove the amplifier, and the amplifier has other reachable producers: `validateTransitionGraph` runs ONLY inside `rejectMalformedTransitionGraph` at ProposeExecutionPlan, against the COMMAND PAYLOAD, whereas `liveStepIds` runs against `toGatePlan(plan)` — the persisted bag — so any plan persisted by an earlier build (pre-half-edge limb, pre-BRANCH-stepType limb), any future validation limb regression, or any payload/persistence shape drift reaches the runtime unvalidated and re-opens the identical BLOCKER. Given this class has recurred three times because each repair was local, the systemic fix must be fail-closed at the CONSUMER: for a graph plan, if `entryStepIds(...)` is empty while `steps.length > 0`, `prunableStepIds` MUST return [] and `stepStartability` MUST return a distinct refusal code (`GRAPH_INCOHERENT`), never 'everything is prunable'. Kill test: a persisted graph plan whose transitions form a cycle over all steps (bypassing propose validation by constructing the GatePlan directly, as transition-gate.test.ts already does) ⇒ prunableStepIds === [] and startStepGate refuses with GRAPH_INCOHERENT. Reverting to the current filter makes it return every step and the test goes RED. This is constraint 5 (fail-closed) applied to the exact defect being remediated.
- RC-3 (ladder ordering makes a future vocab change catastrophic rather than merely wrong). Fix 2 derives `REOPENABLE_TERMINAL = terminalStates.filter(s => transitions.some(t => t.from === s))` and places it as step 4, BEFORE the terminal-success test at step 5. I verified against the machine (transitions.data.ts, ExecutionStep.stepState): terminalStates = ['SUCCEEDED','FAILED','SKIPPED','CANCELLED','SUPERSEDED'] and the ONLY terminal out-arrow is FAILED→QUEUED, so today the set is {'FAILED'} as claimed. But if any terminal-SUCCESS state ever gains an out-arrow (e.g. a SKIPPED→QUEUED un-skip, or SUCCEEDED→SUPERSEDED at step level — the machine already has five →SUPERSEDED arrows from non-terminal states and a step-level supersede is a plausible next increment), step 4 fires first and EVERY SUCCEEDED/SKIPPED source becomes PENDING: the whole system wedges, the seed included. KT-2.4 catches the drift only if someone runs it before shipping. Make the failure mode safe by construction instead: `REOPENABLE_TERMINAL = terminalStates.filter(s => !TERMINAL_SUCCESS.has(s) && machine.transitions.some(t => t.from === s))`, and order the ladder terminal-success-FIRST (3. not terminal ⇒ PENDING; 4. terminal-success ⇒ fall through to branch/guard; 5. non-success terminal AND reopenable ⇒ PENDING; 6. non-success terminal ⇒ NEUTRALIZED). Equivalent today, fail-closed under vocab drift.
- RC-4 (the capability Fix 3 removes is NOT actually restored — Fix 7 stops at the command layer). Fix 3's enumerated capability removal ('continue the plan around a permanently failed arm') is replaced by CancelExecutionStep-from-FAILED. But the operator surface cannot reach it: `packages/rph-projections/src/execution-view.ts` CONTROL_BY_STEP_STATE.FAILED = [] with the comment 'terminal — no control action (retry is a progress affordance, not control)', and that allowlist is DELIBERATELY not machine-derived (execution-view.ts:16 'Do not change: advanceCommands MUST come from this allowlist, NOT from the machine's legal-transition topology' — the F-11 discipline). Adding the FAILED→CANCELLED arrow to m2-transitions.json therefore mints NO button. Net effect of shipping Fixes 3+7 as written: every UI operator sees a join that will not start, a FAILED arm whose only offered affordance is `retry`, and no way to abandon — a strict capability loss on the only surface an operator actually uses. Required: add `FAILED: ['cancel']` to CONTROL_BY_STEP_STATE, wire the button in apps/rph-demo/src/routes/undertakings/[id]/+page.svelte, and pin it — note the `Record<StepState, …>` totality typing does NOT catch the omission because `[]` is a legal value, so an explicit `expect(controlCommandsFor('FAILED')).toContain('cancel')` plus an e2e that fails an arm, cancels it, and starts the join is mandatory. Ship it in the same commit as Fixes 3+7, not after.
- RC-5 (Fix 7 silently widens RPH-EXE-002's stated boundary). `cancelExecutionStep` has NO plan-ACTIVE precheck by explicit design (execution.ts:991-997: 'Cancel is CLEANUP, not new work: it is permitted even under a SUPERSEDED/terminal plan (RPH-EXE-002 forbids OPENING new work/attempts, not terminating an existing step)'). A FAILED step is ALREADY TERMINAL — cancelling it is not 'terminating an existing step', it re-writes the outcome of a closed attempt, and with no plan-ACTIVE gate it can be done on a SUPERSEDED / FAILED / CANCELLED plan, mutating the governed record of a closed plan. The design neither notices nor discloses this. Required: gate the FAILED source (and only that source) on plan-ACTIVE — abandoning an arm is a live-plan decision and Fix 3 creates the need for it only on an ACTIVE plan, so nothing is lost. Kill tests: CancelExecutionStep on a FAILED step of a SUPERSEDED plan ⇒ REJECTED; of an ACTIVE plan ⇒ ACCEPTED; and the four pre-existing live source states (READY/QUEUED/RUNNING/WAITING) remain ACCEPTED regardless of plan status — that last one is the anti-regression assertion, and it is exactly the shape F-12 says is missing today.
- RC-6 (F-20's defect shape is reproduced on a path Fix 2 newly creates). Fix 4 says the prune refusal 'gains a code-derived clause' but specifies only the live/not-live axis. The design's own INV-GATE-7 enumerates a row it then leaves un-messaged: `!live ∧ stepState ∈ {RUNNING, WAITING}` ⇒ exit is CancelExecutionStep. On that step `prunableStepIds` excludes it (PRUNABLE_SOURCE_STATES), so execution.ts:1062-1067 still says 'it is still reachable' — an assertion the gate has just denied, which IS F-20 verbatim. Fix 2 makes the state newly reachable (a RUNNING/WAITING step behind a CANCELLED predecessor; or, once F-15/F-21/F-23 land, a RUNNING step on an arm a re-resolved BRANCH just abandoned). Required: three explicit prune-refusal branches keyed on the gate verdict — (a) plan not ACTIVE; (b) live ⇒ 'still reachable — use SkipExecutionStep'; (c) not live but state not prunable ⇒ 'unreachable, but live work is CANCELLED, not pruned' — each with a message kill test. Otherwise the family's headline invariant ('the engine never names a command it will refuse') is untrue on the day it ships.
- RC-7 (Fix 5 deletes a filter that closed a prior BLOCKER, without restating it). `outEdgesOf` (transition-gate.ts:84-85) filters `t.targetStepId !== undefined`, and the comment at :79-83 records why: a half-edge won BRANCH first-match and neutralized every real arm while `buildAdjacency` never saw it. Fix 5 says `inEdgesOf`/`outEdgesOf` 'are DELETED in favour of the maps' and never states that `outEdgesById` must exclude half-edges. Required: specify the half-edge exclusion on `outEdgesById` explicitly, and add a half-edge fixture to KT-5.2 asserting first-match still selects the first REAL arm. (Propose-time refusal of half-edges is not sufficient cover — `liveStepIds` runs on persisted plans, which is the same reasoning the existing comment gives.)
- RC-8 (Fix 1's UI-plane correction is half done, and it is the same class of bug). apps/rph-demo/src/routes/undertakings/[id]/+page.server.ts:137-138 uses a TRUTHY test for BOTH endpoints: `...(t.sourceStepId ? {sourceStepId} : {})` AND `...(t.targetStepId ? {targetStepId} : {})`. The design names only sourceStepId. An empty-string targetStepId is reshaped into a HALF-EDGE on the display plane — RC-7's shape, on the plane where nothing validates and where `transitionRows` renders a disposition for it. Change BOTH to `!== undefined`, matching `toGatePlan`'s convention (handlers/execution.ts), and pin the shaping with a projections/server test. Also note that `toGatePlan` CLONES every edge (`transitions.map(...)`), so the identity contract Fix 5 strengthens holds only within one call — state that so nobody caches a GateContext across `toGatePlan` invocations.
- RC-9 (Fix 5 leaves the wrapper's semantics ambiguous, in a direction that is fail-open). The design does not say whether the retained `inEdgeDisposition` wrapper and the new `edgeDispositions` return the LOCAL or the EFFECTIVE disposition. It must be EFFECTIVE — the :217 liveness veto is what execution-view.ts:359 renders and what transition-gate.test.ts:352-392 (the DWP-08 dead-arm pins) assert. State it. Two more unspecified behaviours that will be guessed wrongly: (a) the wrapper is called with edges NOT in `plan.transitions` by three shipped tests (transition-gate.test.ts:242-245 pass `edge('s1','x')` literals against a plan with `transitions: []`), so an identity-keyed memo lookup MUST fall through to a fresh computation rather than return undefined; (b) `transitionRows`' map lookup must fall back to COMPUTING for a foreign edge, never to a default value — a `?? 'NEUTRALIZED'` would be exactly the silent fail-open execution-view.ts:333-338 warns about, and KT-5.3 as written ('assert a cloned edge is absent from the map') tests the map but not what the renderer does with the absence.
- RC-10 (F-16 is only half-addressed, and Fix 7 re-opens the other half without saying so). DR-004:223 requires that a neutralized in-edge 'not count as a real contribution'; the review's verifier called it 'a specified requirement with no implementation and no test'. Fix 3 removes the FAILED case, which removes the causality INVERSION — but the 'join ran on partial input and nothing records it' shape survives for branch-excluded and pruned arms, and Fix 7 deliberately re-creates it for an ABANDONED arm (cancel s3 ⇒ join releases on one input). The design lists this under 'STILL ACCEPTED, unchanged' without argument. Required: either record the neutralized in-edge set on ExecutionStepStarted, or state explicitly as a DISCLOSED RESIDUAL that the governed record of the partial input is the ExecutionStepCancelled(reason)/ExecutionStepPruned event on the arm — and back that with a kill test asserting the cancel reason is present and non-empty for the Fix-7 path. Silently declining reproduces the exact 'specified, unimplemented, untested' pattern that produced F-16.
- RC-11 (INV-GATE-4 is asserted as proven when it is conditional, and Fix 3's safety argument rests on it). 'Every state that produced a SATISFIED or NEUTRALIZED in-edge is irreversible in the machine' is TRUE for step states — I verified it: the only out-arrow from any terminal state is FAILED→QUEUED. It is FALSE for guard-DERIVED dispositions: a BRANCH terminated by Skip or Prune records no `selectedTransitionId` (F-15/F-21/F-23), so `selectBranchEdge` re-derives first-match on every read and the selection can flip, flipping SATISFIED↔NEUTRALIZED on an arm behind an already-released join. The design flags this only in sequencingNotes as affecting the 'VALUE' of INV-GATE-1; it also invalidates INV-GATE-4 as stated and the Fix-3 claim 'once the join HAS started legitimately, no arm can re-open behind it'. Required: state INV-GATE-4 as CONDITIONAL on the branch-durability family landing, make the proposed joint test a REQUIRED gate rather than a follow-up, and add a fail-closed interim: if a BRANCH source is terminal-success with a conditional out-edge and NO recorded selectedTransitionId, treat its out-edges as PENDING (never SATISFIED/NEUTRALIZED) so the barrier cannot release on a decision the plan never recorded.
- RC-12 (the Fix-7 vocab edit cites an authority this codebase explicitly denies, and overstates the generated surface). `drivesFrom` appears ONLY in packages/rph-contracts/vocab/m3-commands-events.json — grep confirms it is generated into NO file under packages/rph-contracts/src. So 'regenerate contracts' after editing drivesFrom is a no-op and the risk note 'it touches two GENERATED surfaces' is wrong for contracts (it is right for m2-transitions.json → transitions.data.ts). More importantly, three shipped files state that drivesFrom has no ratified authority and that requireFrom is deliberately hand-authored from the machine's in-arrows instead — handlers/command-reissue-guard.test.ts:15, handlers/kit.ts:494, handlers/intent.ts:240. The design's stated discipline ('the JAN-CMDPRE discipline: the handler enforces exactly what the vocab declares') therefore contradicts a documented local norm. Required: resolve it in the design — either cite the JAN-CMDPRE ruling that supersedes those three comments and amend them in the same commit, or drop the vocab edit as the authority and justify the requireFrom widening on the machine arrow alone. Leave it as written and the implementer hits a direct doc-vs-doc conflict.
- RC-13 (name every test that must be rewritten, not three of five). The design names transition-gate.test.ts:64-70, :243 and :416, and 'audit' for the rest. The complete list, verified: (a) transition-gate.test.ts:64-70 (asserts startableStepIds === ['s4'] over a FAILED arm — the title already says the opposite); (b) transition-gate.test.ts:243 ('NEUTRALIZED for a terminal-non-success source' — its source is FAILED, becomes PENDING; and note the fixture plan has `transitions: []`, so it exercises the wrapper on a FOREIGN edge — see RC-9); (c) transition-gate.test.ts:416 (disposition flips to PENDING; the prunableStepIds === [] half stays green); (d) transition-gate.test.ts:166-172 (the vacuous `ok || !startable.has(id)` — DELETE, do not extend, per KT-4.3); (e) execution-start-gate.test.ts:527-542 ('the JOIN still fires when one arm FAILED'); and the message assertions at execution-start-gate.test.ts:690 and :708 (`toContain('still reachable')`) which RC-6's branch (c) must not break. Also confirm the seed bar explicitly: reference-undertaking.ts:556 proposes `transitions: []` for EVERY plan and seed-workbench.ts dispatches no Fail/Cancel step command, so Fixes 1–4 cannot reach it — but Fix 7 regenerates transitions.data.ts, which packages/rph-engine's emitted-event-conformance.test.ts and replay-conformance.test.ts read, so 'rph-engine 69 green with no edit' must be the stated gate on the Fixes 3+7 commit specifically, not on the family as a whole.

## F3-commit-ordering — Commit ordering and point-in-time facts (the "decides once" family)

### Root cause
All seven findings are one defect wearing three masks: **a fact that must be decided once, at a defined instant, and recorded, is instead (i) computed against a view that is missing the very move being made, (ii) computed by only one of the commands that can make that move, or (iii) not written into the governed stream at all.**

Verified mechanism, three limbs.

LIMB 1 — the decision is taken against a pre-move view (F-02/F-07/F-24). `advanceStep` (packages/rph-application/src/handlers/execution.ts:598-676) runs `args.mutateStep(step)` at :656, `makeEvent` at :660 and `commitState` at :667. `completeExecutionStep`'s `mutateStep` (:762-779) compensates by cloning the GatePlan with `stepState: 'SUCCEEDED'` for the acting step (:767-772) — but that clone reaches the grammar through only ONE of two planes. `guardEvaluatorFor` (:108-122) folds the subject with `buildConditionSubject(gatePlan.steps, ctx.store.readAllEvents(), planId)` (:117); `buildConditionSubject` (condition-grammar.ts:208-223) takes `stepState` from the steps argument but seeds `outputArtifactIds: []` with `structuredResult` absent (:215), and `foldExecutionEventInto` (:186-200) is the ONLY writer of those two fields — exclusively from a COMMITTED `ExecutionStepSucceeded`. `GateStep` (transition-gate.ts:35-45) has no field that could carry a result, so the clone structurally CANNOT fix this. Result: for the deciding step itself `RESULT_EQUALS` resolves `resolvePath(undefined, path)` (condition-grammar.ts:128 → false) and `OUTPUT_COUNT` compares 0 (:124). The comment at execution.ts:765-766 ("ask the plan as if this step had already succeeded") is true of one field out of four. `STEP_SUCCEEDED`/`STEP_STATE` read the patched field and `ATTEMPTS` folds already-committed `ExecutionStepStarted` — which is precisely why every shipped fixture is green.

LIMB 2 — the decision is written by one command out of the two that can settle a BRANCH (F-15/F-21/F-23). `selectedTransitionId` has exactly one writer: `completeExecutionStep`'s `mutateStep`. `skipExecutionStep` (:952-988, requireFrom READY|QUEUED) and `pruneExecutionStep` (:1028-1071) pass no `mutateStep` and inspect no `stepType`. SKIPPED is in `TERMINAL_SUCCESS` (transition-gate.ts:17), so `branchExcludes` (:162-175) treats a skipped BRANCH as settled and calls `selectBranchEdge` (:98-120), whose recorded-decision limb (:109-114) is skipped and control falls into the FRESH first-match loop (:115-118) — re-evaluated on EVERY read of `liveStepIds`/`inEdgeDisposition`/`prunableStepIds`/`startableStepIds`. Both verifiers independently established that PRUNE is not exploitable (`inEdgeDisposition` tests source liveness at :217 BEFORE the branch limb at :228, and `liveStepIds` never expands a non-live node's out-edges at :144-148) and that CANCEL is safe (CANCELLED ∉ TERMINAL_SUCCESS, short-circuit at :169). SKIP is the live vector.

LIMB 3 — the emitted event is not the declared fact (F-25). `advanceStep` :665 is `payload: args.eventPayload !== undefined ? args.eventPayload : command.payload`. An OMITTED `eventPayload` silently substitutes the COMMAND shape for the EVENT shape. `failExecutionStep` (:863-871) and `retryExecutionStep` (:911-941) are the only two step handlers that omit it (every sibling supplies one: :703, :790, :961, :1005, :1042, :1090, :1114). `ExecutionStepFailedPayloadSchema` (messages.ts:1133-1138) and `ExecutionStepRetriedPayloadSchema` (:1145-1149) both declare `stepState: StepStateSchema` REQUIRED, and Retried is a `z.strictObject` with no `retryReason` member — so the emitted payload is rejected by its own declared shape on two counts. Neither event is in `RATIFIED_EVENT_PAYLOADS` (messages.ts:2474-2490; both vocab entries are UNRATIFIED-AUTHORED), so the kit (d2) gate (kit.ts:301) is a no-op for them and the violation commits silently.

The unifying cause: the ONE primitive every step command shares (`advanceStep`) treats "what the world looks like at this instant" and "what this command asserts" as things each HANDLER must reconstruct, optionally, by hand — a projection patch here, an event payload there. Correctness therefore depends on nine handlers each remembering nine things.

### Why it survived
DWP-09 explicitly set out to establish "a BRANCH decides once" and shipped a design that could not hold it. Five structural reasons, each of which my design inverts:

1. **The rule lived in an OPTIONAL hook of ONE handler.** `mutateStep` is `readonly mutateStep?:` on `advanceStep`'s args. Recording the decision was therefore opt-IN per command. Skip opted out by omission — not by decision, and with nothing to review. (This is the DWP-08 lesson recurring: DWP-08 made *reachability* structural "so it cannot be bypassed by any command, present or future" — transition-gate.ts:128-130 — and then left the *decision* keyed to one command.)

2. **The commit-ordering gap was patched, not closed.** Rather than moving the decision to a point where the world already contains the move, DWP-09 hand-simulated the move on a read-model (`GatePlan`) that structurally cannot represent three of the four facts the grammar reads. The patch was correct for `stepState` and invisible-wrong for `outputArtifactIds`/`structuredResult`. There was no mechanism forcing the simulated view to be COMPLETE, and no statement of what "complete" would mean.

3. **The read side was left able to INVENT a decision.** `selectBranchEdge`'s fresh first-match fallback means an unrecorded decision degrades SILENTLY into a re-derived one. "Decides once" was thus never enforceable: the absence of the fact produced a plausible answer instead of a refusal. A property that fails open cannot be an invariant.

4. **The decision was recorded only in mutable aggregate state, never in the governed stream.** Nothing about it is in the event log, so (a) no replay can rebuild it without re-running the evaluator (and would then be exposed to evaluator drift), and (b) there was no artifact against which a test could state the property. DWP-04 had already faced this exact question and answered it correctly by MINTING `ExecutionStepWaitResolved` "so the resume is a governed-stream FACT rather than a state change invisible to replay"; DWP-09 did not apply the same standard to itself.

5. **Anti-vacuity was applied to the outcome, not to the property.** The three DWP-09 tests (execution-start-gate.test.ts:911-1009) all drive the branch by start+complete, and every BRANCH fixture in the repo guards on `STEP_SUCCEEDED`/`STEP_STATE`/`ATTEMPTS` — exactly the three ops the partial patch or already-committed Started events happen to cover. `RESULT_EQUALS`/`OUTPUT_COUNT` appear in no live guard anywhere (only the pure evaluator unit test and the `describeCondition` renderer). `complete()` hardcodes `structuredResult: {}` / `outputArtifactIds: []`, so no fixture could ever have observed the difference.
