Correspondingly for F-25: `emitted-event-conformance.test.ts` PINS `violations === []` over a stream (`driveReferenceUndertaking`) that never fails or retries a step — a cleared register that is vacuous for exactly the two events in question, while `execution-retry-cap.test.ts` drives the cycle four times and asserts only `CommandResult.status`, and `execution-attempts.test.ts:56-57,72,120-121` bakes the NON-conforming shape in as its expected input.

What makes my version structurally sound (point-by-point inversion): ONE evaluation site, inside the shared primitive, reachable by every terminal-success command and declarable-away only by a REQUIRED type-discriminated argument (1); the subject is the committed post-commit view BY CONSTRUCTION — committed state with `stepState := target`, committed log ++ the exact event being committed — with a proof obligation and a test that it equals what a replay sees (2); the read side is three-valued and fail-closed in BOTH directions, so an unrecorded decision is a visible refusal, never an invented arm (3); the decision is written to state AND to the settling event in one atomic commit from one computed value, so replay reads it rather than recomputing it (4); and the kill tests assert the PROPERTY (recorded == recomputed-from-log == on-event) rather than a literal edge id, so they die under mutation of the mechanism, not just of the outcome (5).

### Fixes

**Findings F-02, F-07, F-24 (BLOCKER) — the branch decides against a view that excludes its own result**
- *rule:* CANONICAL RULE B1 (the settlement view). A BRANCH's arm selection is computed against THE PLAN AS OF THE MOVE BEING MADE, defined exactly as: (a) the plan aggregate's committed steps[], with the ACTING step's stepState replaced by this command's target state and nothing else changed; (b) this plan's committed event log, extended by exactly ONE element — the byte-identical event envelope this command is about to commit. It is computed AFTER checkTransition (so an illegal move never decides) and BEFORE commitState (so the decision and the move commit in one revision). Nothing else may be simulated, and nothing the command asserts may reach the evaluator except through the event payload that will actually be written.

INVARIANT ESTABLISHED (the DECISION-REPLAY IDENTITY): the recorded selection is EQUAL to the value obtained by evaluating the branch against the plan's committed state and log at the revision this command produces. Formally, settlementView(c) is field-for-field equal, on every field the grammar reads, to the post-commit view at revision r+1 — proof: stepState is written to exactly `args.target` at execution.ts:656; outputArtifactIds/structuredResult are read by the fold ONLY from ExecutionStepSucceeded events and the post-commit log is `committed ++ [E(c)]`, which is the list passed; attemptsMade counts ExecutionStepStarted over the same list; every other step is untouched because advanceStep rewrites only index idx; plan.status/transitions are untouched. Today's value is NOT reproducible from the log at ANY prefix, which is exactly why it can contradict the event it is recorded beside.

DETERMINISM/REPLAY-STABILITY: the decision depends on (committed prefix, this event's payload) only. It does NOT depend on the clock — makeEvent stamps occurredAt/recordedAt but foldExecutionEventInto reads only stepId / executionStepId / outputArtifactIds / structuredResult, and evaluateCondition is pure with no clock, no random, no cross-aggregate read (buildConditionSubject filters `e.aggregateId !== planId`). No fixpoint risk: the decision is stamped onto the payload AFTER the subject is folded, and the fold never reads selectedTransitionId.
- *sites:* packages/rph-application/src/handlers/execution.ts — `advanceStep` (:598-676): move the `makeEvent` call from :660 to immediately after `checkTransition` (:655), binding `const basePayload = args.eventPayload` and `const pendingEvent = makeEvent(...)`; build `const settlementSteps = steps.map((s,i) => i === idx ? { ...s, stepState: args.target } : s)` and `const settlementPlan = toGatePlan({ ...plan, steps: settlementSteps })`. `guardEvaluatorFor` (:108-122): add a 4th parameter `pendingEvents: readonly ConditionSubjectEvent[] = []` and fold `buildConditionSubject(gatePlan.steps, [...ctx.store.readAllEvents(), ...pendingEvents], planId)`. DELETE the entire `mutateStep` block from `completeExecutionStep` (:757-779) and DELETE the now-unused `loadPlanState` (:93-97) — advanceStep already holds the loaded `plan`, so the second store read of the same aggregate (a real divergence seam) disappears. NO change to packages/rph-domain/src/condition-grammar.ts is required: `buildConditionSubject` already takes the event list as a parameter.
- *mechanism:* The evaluator receives a PROJECTED POST-COMMIT VIEW, and the projection is the commit itself rather than a hand-written approximation of it. Two planes were stale; both are now fed from the same two objects that commitState receives (`nextState.steps` and `event`). Because the overlay IS the event being committed — not a re-derivation from the command payload — it cannot drift from what lands in the log (the event payload renames executionStepId, drops resultStatus and adds resultingExecutionState relative to the command; an overlay built from the command payload would be a second, subtly different shape, which is why option (a) from the register was rejected in favour of option (b)). RESULT_EQUALS over the completing step now resolves against the structuredResult the completion carries; OUTPUT_COUNT reads the real outputArtifactIds length; STEP_SUCCEEDED/STEP_STATE/ATTEMPTS are unchanged (the controls in every verifier probe).
- *killTest:* KT-1 `execution-start-gate.test.ts` › DWP-09 › 'a BRANCH branches on its OWN structuredResult': plan [s1 BRANCH, s2, s3], transitions [cedge(1,2,{op:'RESULT_EQUALS',stepId:stepId(1),path:'outcome',value:'PASS'}), gedge(1,3)]; start(1); complete(1) with structuredResult {outcome:'PASS'}; assert selectedTransitionId === `${PLAN}-t1-2`, start(2) ACCEPTED, start(3) REJECTED. RED today (records t1-3).
KT-2 the OUTPUT_COUNT twin ({cmp:'>=',value:1}) with a real RecordArtifact'd artifact in outputArtifactIds. RED today.
KT-3 (THE ANTI-VACUITY INSTRUMENT — asserts the PROPERTY, not an id) 'the recorded decision equals the decision recomputable from the committed log': after complete(1), rebuild the GatePlan from `store.loadObject(PLAN).state` and rebuild the subject from `store.readAllEvents()` (both strictly POST-commit), call `resolveBranchSelection` and assert it equals the recorded `selectedTransitionId` AND equals the `selectedTransitionId` on the settling ExecutionStepSucceeded event. MUTANTS IT KILLS: dropping `pendingEvent` from the evaluator's event list → RED; patching stepState but not the events (today's code) → RED; over-projecting (e.g. also patching selectedTransitionId into the settlement view) → RED.
KT-4 'the decision does not depend on the clock': drive the identical plan through two engines whose `now()` differ, assert identical selectedTransitionId. Kills a future evaluator that reads a clock.
KT-5 `condition-grammar.test.ts` 'the subject fold ignores selectedTransitionId': fold an ExecutionStepSucceeded payload with and without the field, assert identical ConditionSubject. Kills a future fold that would create a decision-depends-on-itself fixpoint.
KT-3 must be written and observed RED before the fix lands.
- *behaviourChange:* NEWLY CORRECT (previously silently wrong, never refused): a BRANCH whose guard references its own step via RESULT_EQUALS or OUTPUT_COUNT now selects the matching arm instead of always falling through to the SEQUENTIAL default. STILL ACCEPTED, UNCHANGED: every guard over an EARLIER step; self-referential STEP_SUCCEEDED / STEP_STATE / ATTEMPTS; every linear (transitions: []) plan, which never enters this path. NO NEW REFUSAL — not one previously-accepted command is refused by this fix. Deliberate consequence: plans authored against the broken behaviour (i.e. relying on the default arm always winning) will now take the other arm; no such plan exists in the repo (grep: RESULT_EQUALS/OUTPUT_COUNT appear in no live guard anywhere).
- *risk:* R1 — the payload must be constructed ONCE. If a later edit builds the event payload twice (once for the overlay, once for the commit) they can drift and the identity silently breaks. Mitigation: a single `const basePayload`, one `makeEvent` call, and KT-3 asserts state == event. R2 — reordering makeEvent means an eventId is consumed slightly earlier; the count and order per command are unchanged (`evt_${++s}`), and commitState already consumed one on rejection, so no observable change. R3 — the settlement view must NOT include the acting step's selectedTransitionId (that is the output); enforced by constructing settlementSteps with `stepState` as the only edit.

**Findings F-15, F-21, F-23 (MAJOR) — a BRANCH settled by Skip records nothing, so its decision keeps re-deriving**
- *rule:* CANONICAL RULE B2 (the write rule — total by construction). EVERY command that drives a step into a TERMINAL_SUCCESS state (SUCCEEDED or SKIPPED) MUST, when that step requires a decision, take and record it at that instant under Rule B1. 'Requires a decision' is ONE exported predicate used by both planes: `branchRequiresDecision(plan, stepId) === (step.stepType === 'BRANCH' && outEdgesOf(plan, stepId).some(isConditionalEdge))`. The decision is recorded in BOTH planes from ONE computed value, in one atomic commit: onto `steps[idx].selectedTransitionId` and onto the settling event's payload as `selectedTransitionId`.

EXHAUSTIVE ENUMERATION of paths into TERMINAL_SUCCESS (verified over every advanceStep call site in execution.ts): CompleteExecutionStep → SUCCEEDED → RECORDS. SkipExecutionStep → SKIPPED → RECORDS (new). PruneExecutionStep → SKIPPED → RECORDS NOTHING, deliberately (see FIX-4). CancelExecutionStep → CANCELLED, FailExecutionStep → FAILED, RetryExecutionStep → QUEUED, Start/EnterWait/ResolveWait → RUNNING|WAITING: none is terminal-success, none decides. The machine's five `-> SUPERSEDED` step arrows (transitions.data.ts:1478-1482) have no command; if one is ever minted, SUPERSEDED ∉ TERMINAL_SUCCESS so it behaves like CANCELLED.

WHY SKIP DECIDES rather than being refused: SKIPPED is terminal-SUCCESS precisely so the sequence advances past the step, so the flow MUST take an arm and somebody must choose it. §21.1 has already authorized proceeding (a mandatory skip needs a waiver/revision); the plan's own declared logic then picks the arm, evaluated at a defined instant and recorded as a fact. This is not fabrication — it is the same act as at Complete, truthfully evaluated against a step that produced no result. Refusing Skip on a BRANCH (the register's alternative) was rejected: it adds a NEW refusal to a legitimate operator action and leaves no clean exit (Cancel blocks the downstream forever and the plan can then never COMPLETE).
- *sites:* packages/rph-application/src/handlers/execution.ts — `advanceStep`: the branch-decision block moves INTO the primitive, keyed on `TERMINAL_SUCCESS.has(args.target) && branchRequiresDecision(settlementPlan, args.stepId) && step.selectedTransitionId === undefined` (never re-decide an already-recorded branch). `advanceStep`'s args type becomes a DISCRIMINATED UNION on `target`: the `'SUCCEEDED' | 'SKIPPED'` arm carries a REQUIRED `branchDecision: 'RECORD_AT_SETTLEMENT' | 'NONE_STRUCTURALLY_DEAD'`; every other target arm has no such field. Handlers updated: completeExecutionStep and skipExecutionStep declare `'RECORD_AT_SETTLEMENT'`, pruneExecutionStep declares `'NONE_STRUCTURALLY_DEAD'`; start/fail/retry/cancel/wait/resolve cannot declare it (compile error if they try). packages/rph-domain/src/transition-gate.ts — export `branchRequiresDecision`; `resolveBranchSelection` (:182-196) delegates its own guard to it so the write and read predicates are literally the same function. packages/rph-contracts/vocab/m3-commands-events.json — add `selectedTransitionId` (optional, string, id-ref) to ExecutionStepSucceeded (an AUTHORED optional field atop the ratified §16.2 shape, with the same note convention already used for executionProvenance and structuredResult) and to ExecutionStepSkipped (already UNRATIFIED-AUTHORED); then `bun run gen` + prettier. NEVER hand-edit packages/rph-contracts/src/messages.ts.
- *mechanism:* The rule stops being a per-handler hook and becomes a property of the shared primitive, so it cannot be forgotten: a future step command with a terminal-success target does not COMPILE without stating its position. And the decision stops being a state-only mutation: it rides the settling event, so replay reconstructs `selectedTransitionId` by reading it rather than by re-running the grammar — which also immunizes history against a future change to the evaluator's semantics (the drift hazard that makes a state-only 'point-in-time fact' unsound). Adding the field to a strictObject WIDENS the accepted shape; it refuses nothing.
- *killTest:* KT-6 `execution-start-gate.test.ts` › DWP-09 › 'a BRANCH settled by Skip records its decision': reuse `activeLateFlipPlan`; replace start(1)+complete(1) with SkipExecutionStep{stepId: stepId(1), mandatory:false}; assert `stepOf(1)?.selectedTransitionId` is DEFINED (undefined today), then start(3)/complete(3)/skip(4) to flip the guard TRUE and assert `start(2).status === 'REJECTED'` (ACCEPTED today — both arms run).
KT-7 the Rule-B1 identity for the SKIP path: recompute `resolveBranchSelection` from the post-commit state+log and assert equality with the recorded id AND with the id on the ExecutionStepSkipped event.
KT-8 'state and event carry the same decision': for BOTH settling commands assert `event.payload.selectedTransitionId === state.steps[b].selectedTransitionId`. Kills any future edit that writes one plane and not the other.
KT-9 'an already-decided BRANCH is not re-decided': pre-seed a plan whose BRANCH already carries selectedTransitionId, drive the settling command, assert the recorded id is unchanged.
KT-10 (TOTALITY PIN, the anti-recurrence instrument) `execution-step-branch-settlement.test.ts` — a table-driven test that enumerates every step command whose target is in TERMINAL_SUCCESS, drives each against an otherwise-identical BRANCH fixture, and asserts the declared `branchDecision` outcome. MUTANT IT KILLS: adding a new terminal-success step command without a decision position (the union makes it a compile error; this test makes it a RED test even if someone casts around the type).
- *behaviourChange:* NEWLY RECORDED (previously silently absent): SkipExecutionStep on a BRANCH with conditional out-edges now writes selectedTransitionId and emits it on ExecutionStepSkipped. STILL ACCEPTED: the Skip itself — no new refusal, the §21.1 mandatory/waiver rule is untouched. DELIBERATE FLOW CHANGE: after such a skip the arm is FIXED, so a later guard flip can no longer resurrect the losing arm (today it can, and both arms run — the F-15/21/23 defect). ADDITIVE EVENT FIELDS: ExecutionStepSucceeded and ExecutionStepSkipped gain an optional selectedTransitionId; consumers that ignore unknown fields are unaffected, and the (d2) gate on the ratified ExecutionStepSucceeded now ACCEPTS a strictly larger set.
- *risk:* R4 — adding an authored optional field to the RATIFIED ExecutionStepSucceeded shape. Precedented twice on this exact event (executionProvenance, structuredResult, each with an explicit vocab note); it is optional and widening; it must go through the vocab + regen (a hand-edit of messages.ts is reverted by the next `bun run gen`). R5 — the args-union refactor touches nine advanceStep call sites; only three have a terminal-success target and the union prevents the other six from declaring anything, so the mechanical risk is bounded. R6 — a legacy BRANCH with no unconditional default makes resolveBranchSelection return undefined, so nothing is recorded; that lands in FIX-3's UNRESOLVED state, fail-closed and diagnosable, not silently defaulted.

**Findings F-15, F-21, F-23 (the read half) — an unrecorded decision is silently INVENTED by first-match on every read**
- *rule:* CANONICAL RULE B3 (the read rule — never invent a decision; fail closed in BOTH directions). The gate answers a BRANCH's arm question with a THREE-valued verdict, and the two questions the gate asks are separated:
  branchVerdict(plan, source) =
    NOT_A_BRANCH                                  when !branchRequiresDecision(plan, source.id)
    DECIDED{edge}                                 when source.selectedTransitionId resolves to one of its out-edges
    UNRESOLVED{NO_RECORDED_DECISION}              when the source is terminal-success and no id is recorded
    UNRESOLVED{RECORDED_EDGE_NOT_FOUND}           when the recorded id matches no out-edge (an incoherent plan)
START plane (`inEdgeDisposition`): DECIDED → SATISFIED iff this edge is the selected one, else NEUTRALIZED; UNRESOLVED → the new disposition 'UNRESOLVED' (never SATISFIED — nothing downstream may start).
PRUNE/LIVENESS plane (`branchExcludes`): DECIDED → excludes every other edge (unchanged); UNRESOLVED → EXCLUDES NOTHING (the downstream stays LIVE and therefore NOT prunable — a waiver-free prune may never be justified by a decision that was never taken, §21.1).
The fresh first-match loop is DELETED from `selectBranchEdge`. After this, the condition grammar is evaluated for a BRANCH at exactly ONE site in the whole system: `resolveBranchSelection`, on the write path, once per branch, under Rule B1.

INVARIANT ESTABLISHED: an unresolved terminal-success BRANCH BLOCKS its arms and PROTECTS them. The plan stops with a named reason instead of running both arms (today) or offering the whole downstream for a waiver-free prune (the §21.1 back door that RECORDED_EDGE_NOT_FOUND opens today, which no finding noticed). Exits are the authorized ones: a §21.1 waivered Skip per downstream step, a plan revision, or CancelExecutionPlan.
- *sites:* packages/rph-domain/src/transition-gate.ts — add `branchVerdict` (private) and `branchRequiresDecision` (exported); rewrite `selectBranchEdge` (:98-120) to return only the recorded edge (delete :115-119); `branchExcludes` (:162-175) routes through branchVerdict and returns false for UNRESOLVED; `inEdgeDisposition` (:204-235) returns 'UNRESOLVED' for the UNRESOLVED verdict; `InEdgeDisposition` (:63) becomes 'SATISFIED' | 'NEUTRALIZED' | 'PENDING' | 'UNRESOLVED'; `barrierState` (:257-275) is unchanged in code (an UNRESOLVED edge is neither pending nor satisfied, so `stepAtFrontier` correctly returns false) but gains a `firstUnresolved` field so `startStepGate` (:351-400) can emit a specific reason instead of the misleading 'every in-edge is neutralized — the step is unreachable (it should be pruned)'. New reason text: `the BRANCH <id> reached terminal-success without recording which arm it selected — its arms are neither live nor prunable. Resolve it with an authorized plan revision or a waivered Skip (§21.1); the gate will not re-derive a decision that was never taken.` packages/rph-projections/src/execution-view.ts — `TransitionRow.disposition` (:294) is a pass-through, no code change; the type widens. apps/rph-demo/src/routes/undertakings/[id]/+page.svelte:89 — add an explicit tone case for 'UNRESOLVED' (it would otherwise fall to 'muted', which reads as 'excluded' and would be a false statement at the display seam).
- *mechanism:* Today `selectBranchEdge(...) !== edge` answers BOTH 'was this arm excluded?' and 'may this arm start?' with one expression, so 'decided otherwise' and 'never decided' are indistinguishable — and the fallback makes 'never decided' produce a plausible answer that changes over time. Splitting the verdict makes the missing fact visible, and answering the two planes ASYMMETRICALLY (not startable, not prunable) is what makes the fail-closed direction correct: a symmetric 'exclude everything' would hand the whole downstream to a waiver-free prune, which is the F-03/F-04 damage shape.
- *killTest:* KT-11 `transition-gate.test.ts` 'a terminal-success BRANCH with NO recorded decision decides nothing': fixture s1 SUCCEEDED BRANCH, cedge(s1→s2) + default(s1→s3), no selectedTransitionId. Assert `inEdgeDisposition` === 'UNRESOLVED' for BOTH arms; `startableStepIds` excludes s2 and s3; `startStepGate(s2).reason` contains 'without recording which arm'. MUTANT IT KILLS: restoring the fresh first-match fallback → the arms become SATISFIED/NEUTRALIZED → RED.
KT-12 (THE §21.1 PIN) same fixture: `prunableStepIds(plan)` === [] — the downstream of an undecided branch is NOT offered for prune. MUTANT IT KILLS: making branchExcludes return TRUE for UNRESOLVED → the downstream becomes prunable → RED. This test also pins that the fail-closed direction is the RIGHT one.
KT-13 'a recorded id matching no out-edge is UNRESOLVED, not exclusion': same fixture with selectedTransitionId:'no-such-edge'; assert both arms 'UNRESOLVED' and prunableStepIds === []. Today this silently excludes both arms and offers the whole downstream for a waiver-free prune. RED today.
KT-14 'a recorded decision holds across a guard flip' at the DOMAIN layer (today this rule has ZERO domain-level coverage — grep for selectedTransitionId in transition-gate.test.ts returns nothing): DECIDED fixture, flip the subject, assert the disposition set is unchanged.
KT-15 `execution-start-gate.test.ts` end-to-end: a legacy-shaped plan (BRANCH pre-seeded SKIPPED with no selection) → StartExecutionStep on each arm REJECTED with the unresolved reason, PruneExecutionStep on each arm REJECTED as still reachable.
- *behaviourChange:* NEW REFUSAL (deliberate, enumerated): StartExecutionStep on an arm whose source BRANCH is terminal-success with no recorded/resolvable decision is now REJECTED (RPH_ILLEGAL_STATE_TRANSITION) instead of being answered by a re-derivation. REMOVED PERMISSION (a closed back door, not a regression): PruneExecutionStep on the downstream of such a BRANCH is now REJECTED — today a recorded-id-that-matches-no-edge silently excludes every arm and hands the whole downstream to a waiver-free prune. POPULATION AFFECTED: with FIX-2 landed this set is EMPTY for any plan driven by this build; it is non-empty only for plans persisted by an earlier build with a settled-but-unrecorded BRANCH, or an incoherent recorded id. TEST FIXTURES THAT MUST CHANGE (they currently encode the re-derivation): ~13 SUCCEEDED-BRANCH fixtures in packages/rph-domain/src/transition-gate.test.ts (lines ~183, 190, 200, 213, 231, 344, 384, 435) and 3 in packages/rph-projections/src/execution-view.test.ts (~439, 460, 547) must declare the selectedTransitionId a real plan would carry. TYPE CHANGE: InEdgeDisposition gains a fourth member — compile-visible at both consumer sites (execution-view.ts:35/294) and in the demo's tone map.
- *risk:* R7 — the fixture migration is the moment the defect could be re-encoded (e.g. by 'fixing' the tests so UNRESOLVED behaves like DECIDED). Mitigation: land KT-11/12/13 FIRST, before touching any existing fixture; they are independent of the migration. R8 — UNRESOLVED can wedge a legacy plan (not startable, not prunable, so CompleteExecutionPlan refuses). This is intended and is strictly better than running both arms, but the reject message MUST name the authorized exits; verify the demo surfaces the message. R9 — F-03/F-04 (the plan-entry-edge collapse of liveStepIds) touch the same two functions; if their frontier-seed fix has not landed, KT-12 can pass for the wrong reason (liveStepIds empty ⇒ everything prunable). Sequence them together.

**Findings F-15 / F-21 / F-23 (the prune limb, which both verifiers scoped OUT) — the path that legitimately records nothing**
- *rule:* CANONICAL RULE B4 (the declared exception, and the structural fact that makes it safe). PruneExecutionStep records NO branch decision, and that is correct rather than an omission: a pruned BRANCH never decided — it was itself excluded — so recording a first-match evaluation there would MINT A DECISION THAT WAS NEVER TAKEN into the governed stream. The read side does not compensate by re-deriving anything, because it never asks: prune is authorized only when the step is in `prunableStepIds`, i.e. already OUTSIDE `liveStepIds`, and a non-live BRANCH's selection is never consulted.

INVARIANT ESTABLISHED (INV-B4, currently a comment only): (1) `inEdgeDisposition` tests source LIVENESS (transition-gate.ts:217) BEFORE any branch-verdict limb (:228), so an edge out of a dead BRANCH is NEUTRALIZED without the verdict being computed; and (2) `liveStepIds`' forward BFS (:140-149) only expands the out-edges of nodes already IN `live`, so a dead BRANCH's out-edges are never traversed. Together: a dead BRANCH's arms are dead regardless of what it did or did not record. The exception is declared in code as `branchDecision: 'NONE_STRUCTURALLY_DEAD'` — a stated position, not a silence.
- *sites:* packages/rph-application/src/handlers/execution.ts — `pruneExecutionStep` (:1028-1071) declares `branchDecision: 'NONE_STRUCTURALLY_DEAD'` (required by the args union from FIX-2); no other change. packages/rph-domain/src/transition-gate.ts — no code change; the ordering at :217-vs-:228 and the BFS expansion rule at :144-148 become PINNED behaviour rather than commented intent.
- *mechanism:* This is the answer to 'if some path legitimately records none, what must the read side do, and how is that not a re-derivation'. The read side does NOTHING — it never reaches the branch question for a dead source. That is a STRUCTURAL property of the gate's evaluation order, not a re-derivation and not a fallback, which is why it is safe where the SKIP path was not: a skipped BRANCH is still LIVE (it was reached), so its selection IS consulted; a pruned BRANCH is not.
- *killTest:* KT-16 `transition-gate.test.ts` 'a dead BRANCH's arms are NEUTRALIZED regardless of what it recorded': one fixture, three variants (no selectedTransitionId / a valid one / a nonexistent one) where the BRANCH itself is not in liveStepIds. Assert every out-edge is 'NEUTRALIZED' and never 'UNRESOLVED' or 'SATISFIED' in all three. MUTANT IT KILLS: moving the liveness check (:217) BELOW the branch limb (:228) → the no-selection variant returns 'UNRESOLVED' → RED. This is exactly the guard whose absence let DWP-07/DWP-08's resurrection class recur.
KT-17 'liveStepIds never expands a non-live node': assert the transitive downstream of a dead BRANCH is absent from liveStepIds even when the branch carries a valid recorded selection.
KT-18 `execution-start-gate.test.ts` end-to-end: prune a structurally dead BRANCH; assert its selectedTransitionId stays undefined, the emitted ExecutionStepPruned carries none, and its whole downstream remains prunable (the plan can still be closed out honestly).
- *behaviourChange:* NONE. Prune behaves exactly as today; the difference is that the position is now DECLARED (compile-required) and PINNED (KT-16/17) instead of being an unexamined omission indistinguishable from the Skip bug.
- *risk:* R10 — the only hazard is a future refactor that reorders inEdgeDisposition's guards or makes liveStepIds expand unconditionally; KT-16/17 exist precisely to catch that. R11 — if a future command can drive a LIVE BRANCH to SKIPPED without going through skipExecutionStep, the exception must not be copied to it; the args union forces an explicit choice, and KT-10 (the totality pin) enumerates the commands.

**Findings F-25 (MAJOR) — ExecutionStepFailed and ExecutionStepRetried emit the raw command payload**
- *rule:* CANONICAL RULE E1 (the event is never the command by default). `advanceStep` no longer falls back to `command.payload`. `eventPayload` becomes REQUIRED and TYPED by the event registry: `eventPayload: z.infer<(typeof EVENTS)[E]['payload']>` for the handler's declared `eventType E`. An event payload is a projection a handler must STATE; it is never inherited from the command shape.

CANONICAL RULE E2 (the two payloads). ExecutionStepFailed emits `{ stepId, failureReason, ...(failureClass ? {failureClass} : {}), stepState: 'FAILED' }`. ExecutionStepRetried emits `{ stepId, attemptNumber: attemptsMadeForStep(...) + 1, ...(retryReason ? {retryReason} : {}), stepState: 'QUEUED' }` — attemptNumber is the attempt this retry RE-OPENS, and `retryReason` is added to the event's AUTHORED vocab shape rather than discarded, mirroring ExecutionStepCancelled's `reason` ('the governed stream names the control action'). Both handlers must read a properly typed command payload (`FailExecutionStepPayload` / `RetryExecutionStepPayload`) instead of today's `as { stepId: string }` cast, which is how failureReason came to be dropped from the handler's view entirely.

ANSWER TO 'are they schema-INVALID at runtime, and should something validate?': YES against their own DECLARED shapes (both rejected — Failed misses a required field, Retried misses it AND carries an unrecognized key under z.strictObject), and NO against the ENFORCED set — neither is in RATIFIED_EVENT_PAYLOADS, so kit.ts's (d2) gate never runs and the violation commits silently. Something SHOULD validate, but NOT by adding them to RATIFIED_EVENT_PAYLOADS: that map is GENERATED from vocab provenance (gen-messages.ts:202-228), a hand-add is reverted by the next `bun run gen`, and marking the vocab entries ratified would mint the exact kind of invented governance fact the (d2) gate exists to prevent (event-gate.test.ts pins that authored shapes stay ungated). See FIX-6 for the correct gate.
- *sites:* packages/rph-application/src/handlers/execution.ts — `advanceStep` args: `eventPayload` becomes required and generically typed; DELETE the `!== undefined ? : command.payload` fallback at :665. `failExecutionStep` (:863-871) and `retryExecutionStep` (:911-941) gain eventPayload; retryExecutionStep already computes `attemptsMadeForStep` in its precheck (:925) — hoist it so the payload and the cap read one value. packages/rph-contracts/vocab/m3-commands-events.json — add `retryReason` (optional, string) to the ExecutionStepRetried event entry (already UNRATIFIED-AUTHORED); then `bun run gen` + prettier. packages/rph-projections/src/execution-attempts.test.ts:56-57,72,120-121 — update the hand-built fixtures to the REAL emitted shape (they currently bake in the non-conforming one, so the suite can never diverge from the defect).
- *mechanism:* The root cause is an implicit default that substitutes one contract for another when a handler says nothing. Making the field required converts nine opportunities to forget into nine compile errors, and typing it against `EVENTS` makes 'the payload matches its declared shape' a property the compiler checks for the whole step family rather than a property a reviewer must notice. The `satisfies ExecutionStepSucceededPayload` at execution.ts:799 is the one place a previous author reached for this by hand; the fix generalizes that instinct into the seam.
- *killTest:* KT-19 `execution-retry-cap.test.ts` (after any attemptThenRetry()): `expect(ExecutionStepFailedPayloadSchema.safeParse(eventOfType('ExecutionStepFailed').payload).success).toBe(true)`. RED today (missing stepState).
KT-20 same for ExecutionStepRetriedPayloadSchema, plus explicit assertions that stepState === 'QUEUED', attemptNumber === attemptsMade+1, and retryReason round-trips. RED today (missing stepState + unrecognized key).
KT-21 `execution-step-payload-projection.test.ts` (NEW) — a table over all ten emitted ExecutionStep* event types: drive each command once, safeParse the emitted payload against `EVENTS[type].payload`, assert success AND assert the payload is NOT equal to `command.payload` for every command whose two shapes legitimately differ. MUTANT IT KILLS: reintroducing the `?? command.payload` fallback anywhere → RED for Fail and Retry even if the types are cast around.
- *behaviourChange:* NEW FIELDS ON EMITTED EVENTS: ExecutionStepFailed gains stepState:'FAILED'; ExecutionStepRetried gains stepState:'QUEUED' and attemptNumber, and its retryReason moves from an unrecognized extra key to a declared optional field. NO CONSUMER REGRESSES: execution-attempts.ts folds by EVENT TYPE (applyStepFailed hardcodes state='FAILED'; ExecutionStepRetried is deliberately absent from STEP_APPLIERS), so the live read-model is unaffected. NO NEW REFUSAL from this fix in isolation. COMPILE-BREAKING: every advanceStep call site must supply eventPayload (nine sites; seven already do).
- *risk:* R12 — `bun run gen` regenerates messages.ts; a hand-edit is silently reverted. The vocab change and the regen must be one commit, followed by prettier (regen output is format-stale, not content-stale). R13 — the generated ExecutionStepFailed schema types failureClass as z.string() while the vocab notes an enumRef; do not 'tighten' it as part of this fix or a legitimate free-string failureClass starts being refused. R14 — attemptNumber semantics (the attempt re-opened vs the attempt that failed) is not settled by the vocab note; see openQuestions.

**Findings F-25 (the anti-recurrence half) — nothing validates emitted events, and the one test that would is VACUOUS for exactly these two**
- *rule:* CANONICAL RULE E3 (a defect register with no denominator is not a register). The declared-shape sweep must pin the SET OF EVENT TYPES IT OBSERVED, not only the set of violations it found. `emitted-event-conformance.test.ts` currently PINS `violations === []` over `driveReferenceUndertaking`, which never fails, retries, skips, prunes, cancels, waits or branches a step — so its 'register CLEARED / every event this system emits conforms to its own declared shape' claim is vacuous for ten of the eleven ExecutionStep* types. The fix: sweep a SECOND stream that exercises the step-lifecycle corners, and assert the observed type set is a SUPERSET of a pinned required set.

CANONICAL RULE E4 (the runtime gate, correctly scoped). `advanceStep` validates every event it is about to emit against `STEP_EVENT_PAYLOADS` — DERIVED from `EVENTS` by filtering to `aggregateType === 'ExecutionPlan'` and `eventType.startsWith('ExecutionStep')`, never hand-kept — and REFUSES on failure with `RPH_VALIDATION_SCHEMA_FAILED` (the same code the (d2) gate uses; DOC-007 §25.1 'structural validation of the payload failed' does not say COMMAND payload). This is NOT a widening of RATIFIED_EVENT_PAYLOADS and makes no ratification claim: it enforces OUR OWN declaration on OUR OWN events, at the one seam where we both author the shape and emit it. The global (d2) gate's scope and meaning are unchanged, and event-gate.test.ts's assertion (2) — that an authored PWU event with a bad payload is ALLOWED — stays green because it commits through commitState directly, not through advanceStep.

DELIBERATELY NOT DONE: flipping a global declared-shape gate for all ~122 events. That would turn any latent nonconformance in the ~107 authored shapes we have not audited into a runtime refusal — an over-refusal we cannot justify inside this remediation.
- *sites:* packages/rph-engine/src/step-lifecycle-drive.ts (NEW) — `driveStepLifecycleCorners(engine)`: one PWU + one graph plan exercising start→fail→retry→start→complete, a waivered skip, an enter/resolve wait, a cancel, a prune of a genuinely dead arm, a BRANCH settled by Complete and a second BRANCH settled by Skip. `packages/rph-engine/src/reference-undertaking.ts` and `seed-workbench.ts` are NOT touched — the reference seed drives byte-identically (hard constraint 1). packages/rph-engine/src/emitted-event-conformance.test.ts — sweep both streams; add `REQUIRED_EVENT_TYPES` containing the ten emitted ExecutionStep* types and assert the observed set contains all of them. packages/rph-application/src/handlers/execution.ts — the STEP_EVENT_PAYLOADS check inside advanceStep, immediately before commitState. packages/rph-application/src/handlers/step-event-gate.test.ts (NEW) — modelled on event-gate.test.ts, including its PREMISE-pin idiom.
- *mechanism:* F-25 survived not because nobody looked but because the instrument that looks had no denominator: a green sweep over a stream missing the subject is indistinguishable from a clean subject. Pinning the observed set converts 'we found no violations' into 'we checked these ten types and found no violations'. The runtime gate then covers what the compiler cannot: a payload assembled dynamically or through a cast.
- *killTest:* KT-22 `emitted-event-conformance.test.ts` 'PREMISE: the sweep observes every ExecutionStep event type this system emits': assert observed ⊇ REQUIRED_EVENT_TYPES. MUTANT IT KILLS: deleting the fail/retry leg from driveStepLifecycleCorners → RED. This is the test that would have failed on 2026-07-16 and did not.
KT-23 the existing `violations === []` PIN, now evaluated over both streams — RED today for ExecutionStepFailed and ExecutionStepRetried.
KT-24 `step-event-gate.test.ts` 'PREMISE: STEP_EVENT_PAYLOADS covers all ten emitted step event types AND contains no event outside the ExecutionPlan step family' — kills both a shrunken map (vacuous gate) and a map that has quietly grown into a ratification claim.
KT-25 `step-event-gate.test.ts` 'a step event whose payload violates its declared shape is REFUSED and does not reach the store': drive advanceStep with a deliberately malformed payload, assert REJECTED with RPH_VALIDATION_SCHEMA_FAILED and re-read the store to prove nothing was written (a rejected CommandResult looks identical if the commit still happened — the idiom event-gate.test.ts already established).
KT-26 'the (d2) scope is unchanged': re-assert event-gate.test.ts's PREMISE (PwuMarkedReady ungated) and add `RATIFIED_EVENT_PAYLOADS['ExecutionStepFailed'] === undefined` — so a future 'fix' that hand-adds it, or ratifies the vocab entry without a governance decision, goes RED.
- *behaviourChange:* NEW REFUSAL (deliberate, enumerated, and empty in practice): an ExecutionStep* event whose payload violates its DECLARED shape is refused. After FIX-5 all ten emitted types conform, so no legitimate command is refused — verified by inspection of every eventPayload in execution.ts (Started/Succeeded/Skipped/Cancelled/Pruned/Waiting/WaitResolved conform today; Failed/Retried are fixed by FIX-5; ExecutionStepReady is declared but emitted by no handler, so it cannot be hit). NO CHANGE to RATIFIED_EVENT_PAYLOADS, to the (d2) gate, to the vocab's ratification markers, or to any non-ExecutionPlan aggregate.
- *risk:* R15 — a scoped runtime gate can become a hand-kept allowlist by drift; STEP_EVENT_PAYLOADS must be DERIVED from EVENTS by predicate and KT-24 pins the derivation. R16 — driveStepLifecycleCorners must not be mistaken for a second reference seed; it is a conformance FIXTURE, must live outside seed-workbench, and must not be invoked by any test that asserts on the reference undertaking's event counts. R17 — the new drive must itself stay non-vacuous: if a future guard makes one of its legs refuse, KT-22 goes RED rather than the leg silently emitting nothing.

### Invariants
- INV-B1 (SETTLEMENT VIEW). A branch decision is evaluated against the plan's committed steps with the acting step's stepState replaced by this command's target, and the plan's committed event log extended by exactly the event this command commits — nothing more, nothing less. Evaluated after checkTransition and before commitState.
- INV-B2 (DECISION-REPLAY IDENTITY). The recorded selectedTransitionId EQUALS the value a fresh evaluation would produce from the plan's committed state and log at the revision this command produced. Equivalently: the decision is a pure function of (committed prefix, this event's payload), and is reproducible from the log at that prefix. It depends on no clock, no random and no other aggregate.
- INV-B3 (ONE EVALUATION SITE). The condition grammar is evaluated for a BRANCH at exactly one site — resolveBranchSelection, on the write path, once per branch, inside advanceStep. No read path may evaluate a BRANCH's guards.
- INV-B4 (WRITE TOTALITY). Every command whose target state is in TERMINAL_SUCCESS either records the branch decision or declares, in a compile-required argument, that it records none and why. There is no third, silent option.
- INV-B5 (ONE PREDICATE, TWO PLANES). 'This step requires a decision' is one exported function, branchRequiresDecision, used identically by the writer (resolveBranchSelection) and the reader (branchVerdict). The write and read planes cannot drift.
- INV-B6 (TWO PLANES, ONE VALUE). The decision is written to steps[].selectedTransitionId and to the settling event's payload in the same atomic commit, from a single computed value. State and stream can never disagree.
- INV-B7 (NEVER INVENT). A terminal-success BRANCH with no recorded decision, or with a recorded id matching no out-edge, decides NOTHING: its arms are not startable (disposition UNRESOLVED) and not prunable (branchExcludes excludes nothing). No fresh evaluation may fill the gap.
- INV-B8 (STRUCTURAL DEADNESS PRECEDES DECISION). inEdgeDisposition tests source liveness before any branch-verdict limb, and liveStepIds expands only the out-edges of nodes already live. A dead BRANCH's arms are dead irrespective of what it recorded — which is what makes 'prune records nothing' safe.
- INV-B9 (NO WAIVER-FREE PRUNE WITHOUT A DECISION). A step may be pruned to SKIPPED without a §21.1 waiver only when a BRANCH actually DECIDED against its path. Absence of a decision is never grounds for a prune.
- INV-E1 (THE EVENT IS NOT THE COMMAND). advanceStep has no implicit event payload. Every step handler states its event payload explicitly, typed against the event registry.
- INV-E2 (SELF-CONFORMANCE). Every ExecutionStep* event this system emits satisfies its own declared payload shape, enforced at the emission seam — independently of whether the corpus has ratified that shape, and without asserting that it has.
- INV-E3 (NON-VACUOUS REGISTER). A conformance sweep pins the set of event types it observed, not only the set of violations it found. A cleared register over an unobserved subject is not a cleared register.

### Anti-recurrence
"This class recurs because the correct behaviour is expressible as an OMISSION. Four gates, in increasing order of independence from human attention:\n\n1. TYPE-LEVEL TOTALITY (kills 'a future command forgot'). advanceStep's args become a discriminated union on `target`: the SUCCEEDED|SKIPPED arm carries a REQUIRED `branchDecision`, and `eventPayload` becomes required and typed against the EVENTS registry. A new step command cannot compile without stating both positions. This is the direct structural answer to DWP-09's failure mode — the rule was an optional hook, so Skip opted out by silence.\n\n2. ONE PREDICATE, ONE EVALUATION SITE (kills 'the two planes drifted'). `branchRequiresDecision` is exported from rph-domain and used by both the writer and the reader; the fresh first-match loop is deleted so the grammar is evaluated for a BRANCH at exactly one site, on the write path. Today the write predicate (`stepType !== 'BRANCH'`, in the handler) and the read predicate (`stepType === 'BRANCH' && outEdges.some(isConditionalEdge)`, in the gate) are two independent expressions in two packages that agree by coincidence.\n\n3. PROPERTY-SHAPED KILL TESTS (kills 'the test asserted the outcome, not the mechanism'). KT-3 and KT-7 assert `recorded == recomputed-from-committed-log == value-on-the-settling-event` rather than a literal edge id. They go RED under BOTH directions of mutation — dropping the pending event from the settlement view, and over-projecting it — which a `toBe('t1-2')` assertion cannot do. KT-10 (settlement totality), KT-16 (liveness precedes the branch limb), KT-24 (the derived gate map) are the same shape: each names the mutant it kills.\n\n4. PINNED DENOMINATORS (kills 'the register was clear because nothing looked'). `emitted-event-conformance.test.ts` gains REQUIRED_EVENT_TYPES and a second drive (`driveStepLifecycleCorners`) so the ten ExecutionStep* types are provably observed; `step-event-gate.test.ts` pins that STEP_EVENT_PAYLOADS covers exactly the step family; KT-26 pins that RATIFIED_EVENT_PAYLOADS has NOT grown to cover authored shapes — so the honest fix cannot be quietly replaced by a governance claim. Every new PIN follows event-gate.test.ts's own idiom: assert the premise, then assert the property, then re-read the store to prove the write did not land.\n\nAnd one review discipline, because two of these three defects were found only by a probe: any future claim of the form 'the decision is recorded / the register is clear / the guard holds' must name the population it checked. F-25 survived because 'every event this system emits conforms to its own declared shape' was true of a stream that emitted neither of the two events in question."

### Seed impact
"NO CHANGE to packages/rph-engine/src/seed-workbench.ts or reference-undertaking.ts — neither file is edited, and the reference undertaking drives byte-identically. Verified against the code: the seed proposes `transitions: []` (reference-undertaking.ts:556), so it is a LINEAR plan with no BRANCH, no CONDITIONAL edge and no graph gate; it sends only StartExecutionStep (:567) and CompleteExecutionStep (:604) — it never fails, retries, skips, prunes, cancels or waits a step (grep for FailExecutionStep/RetryExecutionStep in packages/rph-engine/src returns nothing).\n\nPer fix: (FIX-1) the advanceStep reorder is behaviour-identical — one event, same id sequence, same payload, and the branch block is keyed on stepType==='BRANCH' which the seed's steps are not. (FIX-2) the required `branchDecision` argument affects handler declarations only; no seed step is a BRANCH so no decision is ever taken or recorded. (FIX-3) UNRESOLVED is unreachable without a BRANCH; `liveStepIds` short-circuits on `transitions.length === 0` (transition-gate.ts:137) so the linear degenerate never enters the branch code at all. (FIX-5) StartExecutionStep and CompleteExecutionStep already supply eventPayload, so the required-payload change compiles unchanged for both. (FIX-6) the new STEP_EVENT_PAYLOADS gate sees only ExecutionStepStarted and ExecutionStepSucceeded on the seed path, both of which conform today (proved by emitted-event-conformance's existing green PIN over >200 events).\n\nEXPECTED rph-engine test delta: 68 of 69 tests untouched. `emitted-event-conformance.test.ts` gains assertions and a second swept stream (driveStepLifecycleCorners), which is additive — its existing PREMISE (types >= 20, checked > 200) and its `violations === []` PIN both continue to hold over the reference stream. No test that asserts on the reference undertaking's event counts, ids or ordering is affected, because that stream is not modified.\n\nOVER-REFUSAL AUDIT against the seed: zero. The three new refusals (start on an arm of an undecided BRANCH; prune of such an arm's downstream; a step event violating its declared shape) each require a BRANCH or a nonconforming payload; the seed produces neither."

### Open questions
- A CONDITIONAL edge off a NON-BRANCH source is still re-derived on every read (transition-gate.ts:173-174 in branchExcludes and :232-234 in inEdgeDisposition) — the identical time-varying instability as the BRANCH case, just without the exclusivity. checkBranchDefaults limb (a) now REFUSES this shape at propose, so the population is legacy-only, but the runtime code path is live. RECOMMENDATION: give it the same UNRESOLVED treatment so the grammar is evaluated on the write path only — which would also make the read-model's conditionEvaluatorFor vestigial and permit a large simplification of rph-projections/execution-view.ts. NOT SETTLED HERE: it changes public signatures across rph-projections and the demo, and the non-BRANCH guarded-edge semantics belong to the grammar family, not to mine.
- kit.ts's `advanceStatus` carries the identical `eventPayload ?? command.payload` fallback for PLAN-level and non-execution events — the same root cause as F-25, on a much wider surface. RECOMMENDATION: the same required-and-typed treatment. NOT SETTLED HERE because the blast radius is every handler in the application layer, and because the reference undertaking DOES exercise the plan events, so emitted-event-conformance already measures that population (its register is genuinely clear there, unlike for the step family). Scope this as its own increment.
- ExecutionStepRetried.attemptNumber semantics. The vocab note ('FAILED->QUEUED; drives PWU.executionState ->RETRYING') does not say whether attemptNumber is the attempt being RE-OPENED (attemptsMade+1, which I chose) or the attempt that just FAILED (attemptsMade). execution-attempts.ts's attempt_number is keyed on ExecutionStepStarted, which is consistent with my reading, but nothing states it. If the projection family reads it differently, one of the two must move.
- Whether the branch decision should instead be minted as a dedicated ExecutionBranchResolved event rather than carried as an optional field on the settling event. I chose the field: it needs no new binding, no multi-event commit (commitState currently produces exactly one event and one producedEventIds entry), and no second aggregateRevision. The counter-argument is that a decision and a step completion are different facts and a replay fold keyed on branch resolution would prefer its own event type. Revisit if an execution-plan replay fold is ever built (there is none today — RPH-PER-006 replay-equivalence is implemented only for the PWU).
- Whether an UNRESOLVED terminal-success BRANCH should be recoverable by an explicit operator command ('RecordBranchDecision', authorized like a plan revision) rather than only by waivered Skip of each downstream step. My design deliberately mints no such command — it would be a new way to author a decision after the fact, which is the opposite of 'decides once' — but a legacy plan with a large downstream is expensive to close out under the §21.1 route. Sponsor question, not an engineering one.
- The Skip-decides ruling (a BRANCH driven to SKIPPED records the arm its own declared logic selects at that instant) is a semantic judgement about what a waivered skip of a decision point MEANS. I chose it over refusing the Skip because refusing adds a new refusal and leaves no clean exit. If the sponsor reads a waivered skip as 'this decision was never taken', the correct design flips to: refuse Skip on a BRANCH with conditional out-edges, and the UNRESOLVED read rule (FIX-3) becomes the only handler of that population. FIX-3 is written to be correct under EITHER ruling — that is deliberate.

### Adversarial critique — verdict `NEEDS_REVISION`

### Over-refusal risks
- ExecutionStepSucceeded is the ONLY step event under the live (d2) gate (RATIFIED_EVENT_PAYLOADS, messages.ts:2474-2490). If the handler emits `selectedTransitionId` before the vocab regen, EVERY BRANCH CompleteExecutionStep is refused with RPH_VALIDATION_SCHEMA_FAILED — reddening apps/rph-demo/e2e/execution-flow.e2e.ts:183 (whose BRANCH plan guards on its own STEP_SUCCEEDED at :193) and execution-start-gate.test.ts:956/965. The reference seed drives no BRANCH (transitions: [] at reference-undertaking.ts:556), so rph-engine's 69 green tests provide ZERO signal on this failure.
- FIX-3's UNRESOLVED wedges any plan whose BRANCH out-edges reach the gate without an `id` (GateTransition.id is optional at transition-gate.ts:49; toGatePlan omits it at execution.ts:84). Today such a plan works via first-match; after FIX-3 its arms are neither startable nor prunable and the plan can only be closed by waivered Skip of every downstream step. Bounded by ExecutionTransitionSchema.id being required at propose (objects.ts:245) — but the design does not say so and provides no test.
- FIX-3 + F-17/F-20's likely fix. F-17 is a live finding that the engine 'refuses the start saying it should be pruned and refuses the prune saying it is still reachable'; the natural remediation is to make an all-NEUTRALIZED step prunable. If UNRESOLVED is not explicitly carved out of that rule, the whole downstream of an undecided BRANCH becomes waiver-free prunable — re-opening the §21.1 back door INV-B7/B9 exists to close, and doing so in the SAME increment.
- FIX-6b's runtime gate turns any unmeasured latent nonconformance in Cancelled / Pruned / Waiting / WaitResolved into a live command refusal. Inspection says they conform, but no test has ever observed them (the conformance sweep runs only over the reference stream, which emits none of them), so the 'zero over-refusal' claim rests on inspection rather than the corners drive that FIX-6a exists to provide.
- FIX-2's Skip-decides ruling records the DEFAULT arm whenever the operator skips a BRANCH (the branch produced no result, so any self-referential RESULT_EQUALS/OUTPUT_COUNT/STEP_SUCCEEDED guard is false). No refusal is added — correct — but the demo's Skip button is rendered for any QUEUED step with no stepType filter (+page.server.ts:598-604 hardcodes mandatory:false), so an operator irreversibly fixes a branch arm with no affordance showing which arm is about to be chosen. Flag as a UI/sponsor consequence of the ruling, not an engineering blocker.
- The FIX-3 fixture migration itself: rewriting ~11 first-match fixtures to carry a pre-recorded selectedTransitionId does not refuse anything, but it silently removes every disposition-layer assertion of the BRANCH ordering rule (first true conditional wins; default LAST). That is the mirror risk of over-refusal — under-refusal by lost coverage — and it is the moment R7's 're-encode the defect' hazard is realised.

### Symptomatic-not-systemic
- THE HEADLINE ONE. F-02's mechanism is that two representations of 'the world' exist — the `GatePlan` threaded through the gate, and the `ConditionSubject` frozen inside `guardEvaluatorFor`'s closure — and `EdgeGuardEvaluator`'s `plan` parameter is DEAD, so patching the plan (which is exactly what mutateStep did) changes nothing the evaluator reads. FIX-1 re-synchronises both at ONE call site inside advanceStep and leaves the two-source structure and the dead parameter in place. The class therefore remains reachable by any other path that builds the pair separately — and one already exists: execution-view.ts:269 `startableStepId` calls `startableStepIds(plan)` with NO evaluator at all, so the UI's scalar frontier evaluates every CONDITIONAL edge as unsatisfied. That is the same defect in a third place, unaddressed.
- The design's own diagnosis (whyItSurvived #1) is that the rule lived in an OPTIONAL hook, yet FIX-1 removes only the USE of `mutateStep`, not the hook itself. Leaving `readonly mutateStep?:` on advanceStep's args leaves the exact opt-out-by-silence mechanism intact for the next handler, while the new `branchDecision` union guards only the branch question.
- FIX-3 replaces `selectBranchEdge(...) === edge` with `branchVerdict(...) === DECIDED{edge}` — still an object-identity comparison. execution-view.test.ts:542 exists because that identity coupling already bit once at the projection seam. Comparing by edge id is the systemic form; the design does not specify it.
- FIX-4's safety argument rests on an ASYMMETRY that is nowhere named as a contract: `inEdgeDisposition` tests source liveness (:217) but `branchExcludes` does not, and `liveStepIds`' BFS compensates by never expanding a non-live node (:144-148). INV-B8 asserts the compensation and KT-16/17 pin it, which is good — but the underlying two-predicates-that-must-agree shape is precisely what recurred three times in the dead-arm class. Hoisting 'the source is live' into one exported predicate used by both, with the recursion precondition stated, is the systemic form.
- INV-B9 ('no waiver-free prune without a decision') is asserted but left un-evidenced in the governed stream. ExecutionStepPruned already declares `selectedByBranchStepId`/`selectedEdgeId` for exactly this and F-37 confirms nothing populates them. The design treats the prune's silence as a declared exception (FIX-4) without noticing that the SAME edit could make the invariant observable — the fix-forward-once move.

### Conflicts / gaps
- MISSING CROSS-FAMILY CONFLICT — F-17 / F-20. sequencingNotes names F-03/F-04, F-22, F-26, F-01, F-16 but not the two findings whose remediation directly collides with FIX-3: F-17 (the start/prune contradiction deadlock) and F-20 (startStepGate and prunableStepIds contradict each other on the same step). FIX-3 mints a third instance of that exact wedge shape and the two families must agree ONE predicate or INV-B7/B9 will be undone.
- MISSING ADJACENCY — F-37. FIX-4 declares 'prune records nothing' without dispositioning the two prune-provenance fields that already exist for the branch-decision fact and that F-37 reports as never populated. The invariant FIX-4 asserts (INV-B9) is exactly the one those fields would evidence.
- MISSING DISPOSITION — F-46 (PLAUSIBLE: STEP_SUCCEEDED and TERMINAL_SUCCESS disagree about SKIPPED) and F-38 (the ratified 'NEUTRALIZED = source SKIPPED' rule is implemented as its opposite). FIX-2's Skip-decides ruling makes both load-bearing: a BRANCH settled by Skip evaluates its own arms against a subject where its stepState is SKIPPED, so any self-referential STEP_SUCCEEDED guard is false while the gate simultaneously treats SKIPPED as a satisfied predecessor. The brief requires an explicit disposition for each PLAUSIBLE finding; F-46 gets none and it is the one closest to this family.
- INTERNAL CONTRADICTION — INV-B1's 'byte-identical' clause versus the design's own stamping order (the decision is written onto the payload AFTER the fold). As written the invariant is false of the design that establishes it.
- INTERNAL OVERSTATEMENT — FIX-3's 'the condition grammar is evaluated for a BRANCH at exactly ONE site in the whole system'. The non-BRANCH guarded-edge limbs (branchExcludes:173-174, inEdgeDisposition:232-234) survive; the openQuestions admit this but the rule text does not, and rule text is what an implementer follows.
- UNSTATED HARD ORDERING — the two vocab edits are not merely 'generated-artefact discipline'. Adding selectedTransitionId to ExecutionStepSucceeded crosses the LIVE (d2) gate; adding retryReason to ExecutionStepRetried crosses only the NEW FIX-6b gate. These are different risk classes and the design treats them identically. (Note the vocab already declares attemptNumber on ExecutionStepRetried, so FIX-5's only vocab delta there is retryReason.)
- TEST-INSTRUMENT GAP — KT-10 and KT-21 are specified as enumerating tables with no stated source of truth, which reproduces Rule E3's own failure mode. The generated COMMANDS / COMMAND_EVENT_BINDINGS registries make a derived denominator available and cheap.
- VACUOUS INSTRUMENT — KT-4 cannot fail under any reachable mutation given evaluateCondition's signature and ConditionSubject's field set; it is a CON-000 B7 violation inside a design whose whole thesis is CON-000 B7.
- NOT A GAP, CONFIRMED SOUND (recorded so the implementer does not re-litigate): RATIFIED_EVENT_PAYLOADS is derived per-EVENT sourceSection, not per-field (gen-messages.ts:202-228), so an authored optional field does NOT drop ExecutionStepSucceeded from enforcement — the executionProvenance/structuredResult precedent holds. ExecutionStepSkipped/Failed/Retried are all UNRATIFIED-AUTHORED, so widening them is free. ExecutionStepSchema already carries selectedTransitionId as optional (objects.ts:241), so the strict state gate at commitState accepts it. The seed analysis is correct: transitions:[] at reference-undertaking.ts:556, only StartExecutionStep (:567) and CompleteExecutionStep (:604), no BRANCH anywhere in rph-engine or the demo's own plan authoring (+page.server.ts:531). RESULT_EQUALS/OUTPUT_COUNT genuinely appear in no live guard. validate.test.ts's 322-id registry pin is unaffected by field additions.

### Missed findings
All seven assigned findings are addressed, and the PRUNE limb that both verifiers scoped out of F-15/F-21/F-23 is explicitly dispositioned rather than silently dropped (FIX-4) — that is the strongest part of the design. F-25 is handled on both limbs (the payloads, and the vacuous register). Nothing is silently dropped. Two adjacent findings that this family's fixes make load-bearing are not dispositioned and should be: F-37 (ExecutionStepPruned's prune-provenance fields are never populated — the very fields that would evidence INV-B9's 'a prune is authorised by a decision'), and F-46 (PLAUSIBLE — STEP_SUCCEEDED versus the gate's TERMINAL_SUCCESS set disagree about SKIPPED, which FIX-2's Skip-decides ruling turns from a documentation gap into a semantic that decides arms). The brief requires an explicit disposition for each PLAUSIBLE finding and F-46 is the one nearest this family. Also worth noting as unowned: execution-view.ts:269 `startableStepId` calls `startableStepIds(plan)` with no evaluator, so the UI's scalar frontier treats every CONDITIONAL edge as unsatisfied — it is the same dual-source defect as F-02 in a third location, appears in no assigned finding, and falls out of FIX-1's root-cause analysis if that analysis is taken to its structural conclusion.

### Required changes
- FIX-1 does not close the root cause it names — CLOSE THE DUAL-SOURCE EVALUATOR SEAM. `EdgeGuardEvaluator = (edge, plan) => boolean` (transition-gate.ts:70) but `guardEvaluatorFor` (execution.ts:108-122) IGNORES its `plan` argument and reads a `ConditionSubject` frozen at construction time. That divergence IS the F-02/F-07/F-24 mechanism: completeExecutionStep patched the `plan` handed to resolveBranchSelection (:767-772) and the frozen subject silently won. FIX-1 re-synchronises the two objects at ONE call site and leaves the two-source structure and the dead `plan` parameter intact — so the next caller that builds a GatePlan and an evaluator separately reintroduces the identical defect with no signal, and one such caller already exists (`execution-view.ts:269 startableStepId` calls `startableStepIds(plan)` with NO evaluator at all). REQUIRED: make it structurally one source — either change the seam to `EdgeGuardEvaluator = (edge, subject) => boolean` with the gate threading a single `{ plan, subject }` evaluation context, or have `guardEvaluatorFor` fold `buildConditionSubject(plan.steps, events, planId)` from the `plan` it is handed (memoised on plan identity) so the argument cannot be dead. Add a kill test that an evaluator built from plan A applied to plan B is inexpressible or RED. Without this, FIX-1 is a per-instance repair of the exact class the standing bias forbids repairing locally.
- DELETE `mutateStep` FROM advanceStep's ARGS TYPE, not merely from completeExecutionStep. whyItSurvived #1 correctly blames `readonly mutateStep?:` — an optional hook on the shared primitive that Skip opted out of by silence. The design removes the only USE (execution.ts:762-779) and leaves the HOOK, so the identical opt-out door stays open for the next handler. Removing it also makes INV-B1 total: after the change the ONLY thing advanceStep writes to the step is `stepState := args.target` plus the branch decision, which is what the settlement-view proof actually requires.
- INV-B1 IS SELF-CONTRADICTORY AS WRITTEN, and one of KT-3's claimed mutants is not killed. INV-B1 says the log is extended by 'exactly ONE element — the byte-identical event envelope this command is about to commit', but FIX-1's own mechanism stamps `selectedTransitionId` onto that payload AFTER the fold, so the folded event is NOT the committed event. Restate INV-B1 as 'equal on every field `foldExecutionEventInto` (condition-grammar.ts:186-200) reads — stepId, executionStepId, outputArtifactIds, structuredResult' and make KT-5 the pin for the residue. Separately, KT-3's claim that it kills the over-projection mutant is FALSE: patching `selectedTransitionId` into the settlement view is a no-op (the fold ignores it, and `resolveBranchSelection` deliberately ignores recorded selections, transition-gate.ts:180). Replace that claimed mutant with a real one — e.g. the settlement view also patching a SECOND step's stepState, or patching `plan.status` — or drop the claim.
- MAKE THE VOCAB REGEN A HARD PRECONDITION OF THE FIX-2 HANDLER EDIT, AND SAY WHY THE SEED CANNOT CATCH IT. `ExecutionStepSucceeded` is the ONLY step event in `RATIFIED_EVENT_PAYLOADS` (messages.ts:2474-2490; vocab sourceSection 'DOC-007 §16.2 (wins) / DOC-002 §26.4', no UNRATIFIED-AUTHORED marker), so kit.ts's (d2) gate strict-parses it TODAY. Emitting `selectedTransitionId` on it before `bun run gen` lands rejects EVERY BRANCH CompleteExecutionStep with RPH_VALIDATION_SCHEMA_FAILED and reds both `apps/rph-demo/e2e/execution-flow.e2e.ts:183` and the DWP-09 tests — and the reference seed proposes `transitions: []` (reference-undertaking.ts:556) with no BRANCH, so rph-engine staying green is NOT evidence of safety. State: vocab edit → gen → prettier in the same commit as the handler edit, plus a named assertion `ExecutionStepSucceededPayloadSchema.safeParse(emittedSucceeded).success === true` for a BRANCH completion. (The provenance question in R4 is answered correctly — gen-messages.ts:202-228 keys ratification on the EVENT's sourceSection, not per-field, and `executionProvenance`/`structuredResult` are the standing precedent — but the ordering hazard is unstated.)
- FIX-3'S FIXTURE MIGRATION AS SPECIFIED DELETES THE COVERAGE IT INHERITS — SPLIT, DON'T ANNOTATE. The ~8 domain fixtures (transition-gate.test.ts:183, 190, 200, 213, 231, 344, 384, 435) and 3 projection fixtures (execution-view.test.ts:439, 460, 547) exist to exercise FIRST-MATCH selection through `inEdgeDisposition`. FIX-3 deletes the first-match loop from `selectBranchEdge`, so 'declare the selectedTransitionId a real plan would carry' silently converts each of them into a recorded-id-lookup test and removes ALL disposition-layer coverage of the D3 ordering rule (first true conditional wins; unconditional default is the fallback; default must be LAST). REQUIRED: each migrated test splits into (a) `resolveBranchSelection` returns the expected edge id (write plane — ordering/first-match), and (b) `inEdgeDisposition` honours that recorded id (read plane). This split IS the mitigation for R7 and must be named as such.
- THE `DECIDED` VERDICT MUST COMPARE BY EDGE ID, NOT OBJECT IDENTITY. Today `inEdgeDisposition:229` does `selectBranchEdge(...) === edge` — object identity — and `execution-view.test.ts:542` documents 'THE IDENTITY TRAP' from precisely that. A `branchVerdict` returning `DECIDED{edge}` re-imports the trap. Specify the comparison as `edge.id !== undefined && edge.id === source.selectedTransitionId`, with `edge.id === undefined ⇒ UNRESOLVED` (fail-closed), and pin it with a test that passes a structurally-equal but non-identical edge object.
- ADD THE MISSING CROSS-FAMILY CONFLICT: F-17 and F-20. FIX-3 deliberately mints a NEW 'not startable AND not prunable' wedge (UNRESOLVED) — which is exactly the shape F-17 ('refuses the start saying it should be pruned and refuses the prune saying it is still reachable') and F-20 ('startStepGate and prunableStepIds directly contradict each other on the same step') are remediating. If that family's fix is 'every in-edge NEUTRALIZED ⇒ prunable' or 'reconcile the two predicates', it will silently sweep UNRESOLVED into prunability and re-open the waiver-free-prune back door INV-B7/B9 exists to close. sequencingNotes must name F-17/F-20 with an explicit carve-out: UNRESOLVED is NOT NEUTRALIZED, and the two families must land ONE shared predicate, not two.
- SPECIFY THAT `branchVerdict` HAS NO LIVENESS LIMB — liveness is the CALLER's precondition — or the refactor recurses infinitely. `liveStepIds` (transition-gate.ts:135-151) calls `branchExcludes` (:147), which FIX-3 routes through `branchVerdict`. If an implementer, reading INV-B8, adds a liveness check inside `branchVerdict`, the call graph becomes branchVerdict → liveStepIds → branchExcludes → branchVerdict: unbounded recursion. State the precondition explicitly at the function contract and add it to FIX-4's mechanism paragraph. Related asymmetry worth hardening in the same edit: `branchExcludes` does NOT test source liveness at all — only `inEdgeDisposition:217` does, and `liveStepIds`' BFS compensates structurally. That asymmetry is the same shape that let the dead-arm resurrection class recur three times; if it is retained it must be a NAMED, commented precondition, not an emergent property.
- MAKE F-26 A LANDING PRECONDITION, NOT A FLAG. FIX-2 promotes the branch decision to a fact on the RATIFIED `ExecutionStepSucceeded` and into aggregate state. `CompleteExecutionStep` has NO plan-ACTIVE precheck (F-26), so that governed-stream fact can be minted on a SUPERSEDED plan — a direct RPH-EXE-002 consequence created by this design. The precheck already exists verbatim on start (:709), retry (:919), skip (:967) and resolve (:1119); requiring it before FIX-1/FIX-2 land is a two-line prerequisite, not a downstream stake.
- FIX-4 ASSERTS INV-B9 BUT RECORDS NOTHING THAT EVIDENCES IT — DISPOSITION F-37. `ExecutionStepPrunedPayloadSchema` (messages.ts:1126-1132) already declares `selectedByBranchStepId` and `selectedEdgeId` — the two fields that name WHICH branch decision authorised a waiver-free prune — and F-37 confirms no producer ever populates them (`pruneExecutionStep`:1042-1047 copies them from an optional COMMAND payload nobody supplies). INV-B9 states 'a step may be pruned to SKIPPED without a §21.1 waiver only when a BRANCH actually DECIDED against its path', while the governed stream cannot say which decision that was. REQUIRED: either pruneExecutionStep DERIVES and emits both fields from the deciding BRANCH found during the liveness computation (never from the command), or FIX-4 explicitly disposes of F-37 and states why the invariant is left un-evidenced. In the same vocab edit, resolve the three-names-one-concept drift: `selectedEdgeId` (Pruned) vs `selectedTransitionId` (step state + the two new event fields).
- KT-10 AND KT-21 NEED A DERIVED DENOMINATOR, NOT A HAND LIST — this is the exact sin Rule E3 names, one level up. Both are derivable from generated registries: `COMMANDS` (messages.ts:1776+) carries `targetAggregateType` and `emitsEvent`; `COMMAND_EVENT_BINDINGS` carries `machine`/`from`/`to`. Derive KT-10's set as 'every binding with machine === ExecutionStep.stepState and `to` ∈ TERMINAL_SUCCESS' and KT-21's as 'every ExecutionPlan command whose emitsEvent starts with ExecutionStep', and PIN the derivation. A hand-kept list makes KT-10 a totality pin that cannot detect the very omission it exists for.
- REPLACE KT-4 (VACUOUS) AND CORRECT KT-24's COUNT. KT-4 ('the decision does not depend on the clock') cannot go RED under any reachable mutation: `evaluateCondition(expr, subject)` takes no clock and `ConditionSubject`/`ConditionSubjectStep` have no time-derived field, so a clock-reading evaluator would not type-check into the seam. Replace it with a structural pin on `ConditionSubjectStep`'s field set, or drop it — as specified it is exactly the worthless guard CON-000 B7 forbids. KT-24 also mis-states its own set: the derived `STEP_EVENT_PAYLOADS` predicate captures ELEVEN types (the ten emitted plus `ExecutionStepReady`, declared but emitted by no handler); state the pin as 'observed-emitted ⊇ the ten' AND 'derived-map ⊆ ExecutionStep* ∩ aggregateType ExecutionPlan'.
- DOWNGRADE FIX-6's OVER-REFUSAL CLAIM FROM INSPECTION TO MEASUREMENT. The design asserts that Started/Succeeded/Skipped/Cancelled/Pruned/Waiting/WaitResolved 'conform today — verified by inspection of every eventPayload in execution.ts'. That is precisely the un-denominated claim Rule E3 forbids: the existing conformance sweep never observes any of them (the reference stream emits neither). State instead that FIX-6b MAY NOT LAND until KT-23 is green over the `driveStepLifecycleCorners` stream, and that any nonconformance found there is a FIX-5-scope repair, never a gate exemption. (Inspection agrees with the claim — `CancelExecutionStepPayload.reason` is required at messages.ts:270-273 so `reason: p.reason` is safe, and Prune/Wait/Resolve spread their optionals conditionally — but that is inspection, not the register's denominator.)
- ENUMERATE TWO UNSTATED UNRESOLVED POPULATIONS AND GIVE EACH A KILL TEST. (a) `GateTransition.id` is OPTIONAL in the gate read-model (transition-gate.ts:49) and `toGatePlan` omits it when absent (execution.ts:84), so `resolveBranchSelection` returns `undefined` and FIX-3 then wedges the plan as UNRESOLVED — even though `ExecutionTransitionSchema.id` is REQUIRED at propose (objects.ts:245). State that propose-time requiredness is what bounds this population to hand-built/legacy plans, and add the negative test. (b) R6's 'BRANCH with no unconditional default' is likewise bounded by `checkBranchDefaults` (:536-547) but has no test. Both belong in FIX-2's behaviourChange enumeration, not only in the risk register.
- CORRECT INV-B3 / FIX-3's absolute. 'After this, the condition grammar is evaluated for a BRANCH at exactly ONE site in the whole system' is FALSE as written: `inEdgeDisposition:232-234` and `branchExcludes:173-174` still evaluate a guard for a CONDITIONAL edge off a NON-BRANCH source. `checkBranchDefaults` limb (a) (:527-532) refuses that shape at propose so the population is legacy-only, but an implementer reading the absolute will delete the non-BRANCH limb and break every such legacy plan. Restate as 'exactly one site for a BRANCH's EXCLUSIVE selection' and keep the non-BRANCH guarded edge explicitly in the openQuestion's scope.

## F4-guard-anti-vacuity — Guard anti-vacuity and coverage (the unkilled-mutant family)

### Root cause
Three defects compose, and only the third is systemic.

(1) `requireFrom` is an ANONYMOUS, OPTIONAL argument. `advanceStep` (packages/rph-application/src/handlers/execution.ts:600-675) declares `readonly requireFrom?: readonly string[]` and each of the nine step handlers passes an inline array literal (execution.ts:698, 756, 869, 917, 958, 1004, 1041, 1087, 1113). The source-state contract is therefore not a first-class object anywhere in the system: nothing can ENUMERATE it, no census sees it, and a new step command can simply omit it with nothing going red. Compare `advanceStatus`, where SPEC-001 §5.4 made `precondition` a REQUIRED property precisely so "no status advance can silently skip its declaration and the count cannot regress" — the ExecutionStep family never received that treatment.

(2) `requireFrom` runs AFTER `precheck` (execution.ts:645 precheck, :647 requireFrom, :654 checkTransition). Every semantic precheck can therefore MASK the source-state guard, which is exactly why F-13's only "negative" (execution-start-gate.test.ts:699) is refused by `canSkipStep`, and why `PruneExecutionStep`'s residual case is unobservable at all (prunableStepIds refuses a SKIPPED step first with RPH_INVARIANT_VIOLATION). A masked guard cannot be kill-tested through the bus.

(3) SPEC-001 §5.1 already states the obligation — "(1) negative re-issue kill test, (2) positive widest-in-arrow test for EACH state in S, (3) mutation red-proof" — but it is DISCHARGED BY HAND, per site, per DWP. DWP-05 discharged it for the 7 plan-level + 4 PWA sites (dwp05-precondition-coverage.test.ts). The ExecutionStep family got only the NEGATIVE half, for 2 of 9 commands (execution-step-reissue-guard.test.ts covers Fail and Retry alone). So the coverage of a ratified obligation is a function of who remembered — which is why 8 of 20 declared arrows and 8 of 13 residual guard states have no assertion, and why F-32/F-35 (the '<=' comparator) survived: `numericCompare`'s five cases are likewise enumerable from `NumericComparatorSchema` and likewise tested by hand-picked examples.

The root cause is therefore: DECLARED CONTRACTS ON THIS SURFACE (source-state sets, machine arrows, comparator enums) ARE NOT MECHANICALLY BOUND TO THEIR PROOFS. Fixing the six named guards with six more hand-written tests re-creates the same condition for command number ten.

### Why it survived
Four masking mechanisms, all confirmed in code:

(a) NOOP admission. `classifyTransition` (rph-domain/src/stateMachine.ts:48-51) returns NOOP for from===to unless a self-edge is DECLARED illegal, and the ExecutionStep.stepState `illegal` table (transitions.data.ts:1484-1491) holds exactly one entry, NOT_READY->RUNNING. `checkTransition` (kit.ts:177-178) admits `LEGAL || NOOP`. So for every step command the same-state re-issue is machine-admitted and `requireFrom` is the SOLE refuser — the single most load-bearing case is the one no fixture ever drove.

(b) Vacuous negatives (the mandate's class (b)). Three shapes were counted as coverage but test a DIFFERENT layer: (i) MACHINE-refused inputs — execution-start-gate.test.ts:608 waits from QUEUED, and QUEUED->WAITING is ILLEGAL_UNDEFINED, so the assertion holds with requireFrom deleted (F-14/F-19); execution-start-gate.test.ts:898-905 prunes a RUNNING step, and RUNNING->SKIPPED is likewise ILLEGAL_UNDEFINED. (ii) PRECHECK-refused inputs — start-gate:699 (canSkipStep, RPH_INVARIANT_VIOLATION), skip-cancel:203 (plan-ACTIVE), start-gate:624 (plan-ACTIVE) (F-13). (iii) STATUS-ONLY assertions — :608/:610 assert only `.status === 'REJECTED'`, never `error.code` or an event count, so they cannot name the refusing layer even when the right layer fires.

(c) Affordance tests mistaken for authority tests. `CONTROL_BY_STEP_STATE` (rph-projections/src/execution-view.ts:136-147) is a hand-authored table with no binding to the handler; execution-view.test.ts:110-130 asserts the table against itself, and the e2e specs (execution-plan.e2e.ts:232/243/247/271) assert button COUNTS. All stay green under every handler mutation. F-40's WAITING->CANCELLED is the sharpest case: the UI renders a cancel button for a WAITING step and `resolveExecutionStepWait`'s own refusal text ends "Cancel the step instead." (execution.ts:1119-1126, asserted verbatim at start-gate:627) — the engine advertises a remediation path that no test exercises.

(d) The enum/catalog gap. Nothing loops over `NumericComparatorSchema.options` (it is not even exported) or over `getMachine('ExecutionStep.stepState').transitions`. `assertNever` catches only DELETION of a case label, never corruption of its body, so `case '<=': return actual < expected` type-checks and survives (proven live by two independent verifiers: 11 files / 220 tests green with the mutant).

### Fixes

**Findings Root-cause enabler for F-11/12/13/14/18/19/40. No finding names it directly.**
- *rule:* Every ExecutionStep-advancing command DECLARES its source-state contract as a first-class, named object, and declaring it is COMPILE-MANDATORY. `advanceStep` may not be called without one, and the set may not be empty.
- *sites:* NEW `packages/rph-application/src/handlers/step-command-spec.ts` — exports `interface StepCommandSpec { readonly commandType: string; readonly target: string; readonly eventType: string; readonly sourceStates: readonly [string, ...string[]] }`, the frozen table `STEP_COMMAND_SPECS: Readonly<Record<string, StepCommandSpec>>` with exactly nine entries (StartExecutionStep RUNNING<-[QUEUED]; CompleteExecutionStep SUCCEEDED<-[RUNNING]; FailExecutionStep FAILED<-[RUNNING]; RetryExecutionStep QUEUED<-[FAILED]; SkipExecutionStep SKIPPED<-[READY,QUEUED]; CancelExecutionStep CANCELLED<-[READY,QUEUED,RUNNING,WAITING]; PruneExecutionStep SKIPPED<-[NOT_READY,READY,QUEUED]; EnterExecutionStepWait WAITING<-[RUNNING]; ResolveExecutionStepWait RUNNING<-[WAITING]), and `export const REQUIRE_FROM_MARKER = 'this command declares drivesFrom'`.
