MODIFY `packages/rph-application/src/handlers/execution.ts`: `advanceStep`'s args lose `target`, `eventType`, `requireFrom?` and gain `readonly spec: StepCommandSpec` (REQUIRED — omission is a compile error, and the non-empty tuple type makes an empty set a compile error); the refusal message at :648-653 is rebuilt from `spec.sourceStates` and interpolates `REQUIRE_FROM_MARKER` rather than the literal string. Each of the nine handlers (execution.ts:691, 749, 865, 913, 954, 1000, 1034, 1083, 1109) passes `spec: STEP_COMMAND_SPECS.<CommandType>` and keeps only its local `precheck` / `mutateStep` / `eventPayload`. NEW `packages/rph-domain/src/stateMachine.ts`: `export function admissibleSources(machine: string, to: string): string[]` returning every state s for which `classifyTransition(machine, s, to).klass` is LEGAL or NOOP (re-exported from rph-domain/src/index.ts).
- *mechanism:* The source sets become enumerable data consumed by production, so a census, a derived conformance sweep and an arrow ledger can all address them; and the required parameter reproduces SPEC-001 §5.4's INV-1 flip for the step family, so command number ten physically cannot ship without a declared set. Values are byte-identical to today's nine literals — this is a refactor with zero behavioural delta on its own.
- *killTest:* `KT-SPEC-1` in the new selftest file: `expect(Object.keys(STEP_COMMAND_SPECS).sort())` equals a LITERAL list of the nine command types (a tenth handler added without a spec fails). `KT-SPEC-2`: for each spec, `STATE_MACHINES['ExecutionStep.stepState'].states` contains `target` and every member of `sourceStates` (a typo'd state name fails). Type-level: deleting `spec:` from any handler is a `tsc` error, proven by the gate's `bun run types`.
- *behaviourChange:* None. Identical accept/refuse partition, identical codes, identical message text (the marker constant reproduces the current wording byte-for-byte).
- *risk:* Mechanical refactor across nine call sites; a transposed spec (e.g. Skip pointing at Cancel's) would be a real defect. KT-SPEC-3 pins it: for each spec, `spec.commandType` equals its table key AND equals the `commandType` the handler is registered under in registry.ts.

**Findings Root-cause enabler for F-13 (canSkipStep masked the guard) and prerequisite for the PruneExecutionStep kill test; removes the masking that made the F-13/F-14 'negatives' vacuous.**
- *rule:* SOURCE-STATE LEGALITY IS EVALUATED BEFORE SEMANTIC ADMISSIBILITY. In `advanceStep` the declared `sourceStates` check runs FIRST; the command's `precheck` runs only for a step already in a legal source state.
- *sites:* MODIFY `packages/rph-application/src/handlers/execution.ts:644-655` — swap the two blocks so the order is: load/find step -> `sourceStates` check (reject RPH_ILLEGAL_STATE_TRANSITION) -> `precheck` -> `checkTransition` -> mutate/append/commit. Update the `requireFrom` doc-comment block at execution.ts:613-627 to state the precedence and why.
- *mechanism:* A command's declared contract is a property of the command alone; a context-dependent semantic rule must not be able to speak first about a step that is not even in a legal source state. This is the same ordering doctrine `evaluatePrecondition`'s ALL_OF already applies (command-precondition.ts:5-8: `denyWaiver` refuses on KIND before STATE 'so a mis-aimed denial reads as the category error it is'), and the same 'guard-mask fix' DWP-05 applied at the plan level (SPEC-001 §5.3 rows CompleteExecutionPlan / ValidatePwa / SupersedeExecutionPlan). It makes `requireFrom` UNMASKABLE, which is what turns every residual state into an observable kill test.
- *killTest:* `KT-ORDER-1` (execution-step-reissue-guard.test.ts): drive a step to SKIPPED under an ACTIVE plan, then SUPERSEDE the plan, then dispatch `SkipExecutionStep{mandatory:false}`. Assert `error.code === 'RPH_ILLEGAL_STATE_TRANSITION'` and `error.message` contains `REQUIRE_FROM_MARKER` — i.e. the source-state refusal WINS over the plan-ACTIVE precheck. Restoring precheck-first makes this RED (it would return the 'not ACTIVE (SUPERSEDED)' message). `KT-ORDER-2`: dispatch `PruneExecutionStep` on an already-SKIPPED step and assert the marker is present, not the 'it is still reachable' message. `KT-ORDER-3` (the no-widening property): a step in a LEGAL source state under a non-ACTIVE plan still refuses with the PRECHECK's message (skip on QUEUED under a SUPERSEDED plan -> 'a superseded/terminal plan opens no new work'), proving the reorder did not disable prechecks.
- *behaviourChange:* ENUMERATED AND DELIBERATE. The accept/refuse partition is provably UNCHANGED (if requireFrom passes and precheck refuses -> still refused; if requireFrom refuses and precheck passes -> still refused; if both refuse -> still refused; if both pass -> still accepted). What changes is WHICH refusal speaks when BOTH would refuse — i.e. a step in an out-of-contract source state that also trips a semantic rule now returns RPH_ILLEGAL_STATE_TRANSITION + the drivesFrom message instead of the precheck's code/message. Concretely affected: Skip from an out-of-set state under a non-ACTIVE plan or with mandatory-no-waiver (was RPH_INVARIANT_VIOLATION §21.1); Prune from SKIPPED or RUNNING (was RPH_INVARIANT_VIOLATION 'still reachable'); Prune from an out-of-set state under a non-ACTIVE plan; Retry from an out-of-set state with the cap exhausted (was RPH_INVARIANT_VIOLATION RPH-EXE-008); Resolve from an out-of-set state under a non-ACTIVE plan (was the 'Cancel the step instead' message); Start from an out-of-set state with an unsatisfied start-gate (was RPH-EXE-005); Complete on SUCCEEDED with a floor-gate block. NO new refusal is introduced anywhere. NO previously-accepted input now refuses.
- *risk:* HIGH-ATTENTION, LOW-PROBABILITY: an existing test or the UI could key on a precheck message for a step in an out-of-set state. Survey performed: the six message-asserting sites I found all target steps INSIDE the declared set (start-gate:627 resolve-from-WAITING; skip-cancel:203 skip-from-QUEUED; start-gate:658 already asserts the drivesFrom message), so all are unaffected. MANDATORY before landing: grep every `error.code`/`error.message).toContain` assertion in packages/rph-application, packages/rph-projections and apps/rph-demo/e2e for the nine step commands, and run the full gate (vitest + playwright + types + lint + boundary + svelte-check). Secondary benefit: prune's expensive `prunableStepIds` (F-34, cubic in fan-out) no longer runs on source-state refusals.

**Findings F-11 / F-18 / F-12 / F-13 / F-14 / F-19 + three gaps no finding named (StartExecutionStep RUNNING re-issue, RetryExecutionStep from READY, PruneExecutionStep SKIPPED re-issue).**
- *rule:* Every residual guard state — every state the MACHINE would admit into a command's target but the command's declared set EXCLUDES — is refused by the declared-source check, appends no event of ANY type, and bumps no revision. Named longhand, one `it()` per case, independent of any generated table.
- *sites:* MODIFY `packages/rph-application/src/handlers/execution-step-reissue-guard.test.ts` (renamed in its header to the ExecutionStep source-contract kill-test file). Residual sets, derived as `admissibleSources(target) \ sourceStates`: Start {WAITING, RUNNING}; Complete {SUCCEEDED}; Fail {FAILED}; Retry {READY, QUEUED}; Skip {NOT_READY, SKIPPED}; Cancel {CANCELLED}; Prune {SKIPPED}; Wait {WAITING}; Resolve {QUEUED, RUNNING} — 13 cases, of which 5 are already covered (Start<-WAITING at start-gate:652; Complete<-SUCCEEDED at start-gate:~665; Fail<-FAILED at reissue-guard:126; Retry<-QUEUED at reissue-guard:150; Resolve<-RUNNING at start-gate:610). The 8 NEW longhand tests: K1 `CancelExecutionStep from an already-CANCELLED step is REFUSED and appends no second ExecutionStepCancelled` (source CANCELLED via seed QUEUED -> Start -> Cancel{reason:'operator aborted'}; re-issue Cancel{reason:'plan superseded'} = a CONTRADICTING reason) [F-12]. K2 `SkipExecutionStep from an already-SKIPPED step is REFUSED and appends no second ExecutionStepSkipped` (source SKIPPED via Skip{mandatory:false}; re-issue with a different waiverOrRevisionId) [F-13a]. K3 `SkipExecutionStep from NOT_READY is REFUSED — a never-scheduled step is PRUNED, not skipped` (source NOT_READY seeded at propose; `mandatory:false` so canSkipStep passes; NOT_READY->SKIPPED is a LEGAL declared arrow (transitions.data.ts:1469-1473), so this is the widening mutant K2 alone does not kill) [F-13b]. K4 `EnterExecutionStepWait from an already-WAITING step is REFUSED and appends no second ExecutionStepWaiting` (Start -> Wait{waitReason:'blocked on approval'}; re-issue Wait{waitReason:'blocked on vendor'}) [F-14/F-19]. K5 `ResolveExecutionStepWait on a never-waited QUEUED step is REFUSED — a resume is not a start` (plan ['QUEUED','QUEUED']; Resolve on step 2) [F-11/F-18]. K6 `PruneExecutionStep from an already-SKIPPED step is REFUSED and appends no second ExecutionStepPruned` (branch fixture; Prune the excluded arm, then re-issue) [NEW; depends on the reorder]. K7 `StartExecutionStep on an already-RUNNING step is REFUSED and opens no second attempt` (source RUNNING; RUNNING->RUNNING is machine-NOOP so the declared set is the sole refuser) [NEW]. K8 `RetryExecutionStep from a READY step is REFUSED — READY->QUEUED is a scheduler arrow no command drives` (source READY seeded; plan ACTIVE, attemptsMade=0 and lastAttemptFailed=false so retryDecision passes, leaving the declared set as the sole refuser) [NEW; also the arrow ledger's REFUSED_RESIDUAL proof for READY->QUEUED]. ALSO strengthen the two existing tests (reissue-guard:126/:150) with the same assertion battery, and correct three false rationale strings in `execution-start-gate.test.ts`: the title at :606 ('the machine gates both' -> the machine gates the WAIT half, the declared set gates the RESUME half), the label at :610 ('RUNNING has no ->RUNNING resume arrow' -> RUNNING->RUNNING is a machine NOOP; ResolveExecutionStepWait's declared set is the refuser), and add `error.code` + marker assertions at :608 and :610. FIX the accidental double dispatch at start-gate:867 (`expect(skip(2).status, JSON.stringify(skip(2)))` evaluates `skip(2)` twice; hoist to a const).
- *mechanism:* Each case isolates THIS guard by construction: the source state is chosen from the machine-ADMISSIBLE set, so `checkTransition` provably cannot be the refuser; the fixture is arranged so every precheck passes (plan ACTIVE, `mandatory:false`, attempts under cap, prunable arm); and the assertion battery names the layer.
- *killTest:* Each K test asserts, in this order: (1) `result.status === 'REJECTED'`; (2) `result.error.code === 'RPH_ILLEGAL_STATE_TRANSITION'`; (3) `result.error.message` contains `REQUIRE_FROM_MARKER` AND the exact `drivesFrom <A>|<B>` rendering of that command's declared set — THE LAYER-ATTRIBUTION ASSERTION, which no precheck message and no `checkTransition` message ('Illegal transition on ...') can satisfy; (4) NO-EVENT: `store.readAllEvents().filter(e => e.eventType === spec.eventType)` count unchanged AND `store.readAllEvents().length` total unchanged (stronger than the existing typed-only count — it catches an append of any event); (5) NO-REVISION: `store.loadObject(PLAN)?.revision` unchanged across the dispatch; (6) NO-CONTRADICTION: where the first event carried a reason/waitReason, its payload still carries the ORIGINAL value; (7) NO-STATE-MOVE: `stepStateOf(i)` unchanged. K5 additionally asserts zero `ExecutionStepWaitResolved` AND zero `ExecutionStepStarted` for that step (pinning both F-11 bypasses: the attempt counter and the RPH-EXE-005 out-of-order start). K7 additionally asserts `ExecutionStepStarted` count for that step still 1 (RPH-EXE-008). MUTATION RED-PROOF, performed live and recorded per SPEC-001 §5.1(3): for each command, widen `STEP_COMMAND_SPECS.<C>.sourceStates` to include the residual state under test and confirm EXACTLY that command's K test goes RED and no other test changes status.
- *behaviourChange:* None — tests only.
- *risk:* K3 and K8 seed NOT_READY / READY at propose time (`activePlan(['NOT_READY', ...])`, the fixture pattern execution-start-gate.test.ts:62-85 already relies on). If a sibling family lands a propose-time 'steps rest QUEUED' rule (F-09), these two fixtures and much of the existing start-gate suite break together — see sequencingNotes. K6 needs a branch fixture in which the pruned arm is genuinely prunable; if the liveness/prunability family (F-03/F-06/F-20) changes `prunableStepIds`, re-derive the fixture.

**Findings THE SYSTEMIC ANTI-RECURRENCE MECHANISM. Covers F-11/12/13/14/18/19 mechanically, plus the positive half of F-40, plus every future step command.**
- *rule:* MECHANIZE SPEC-001 §5.1 FOR THE STEP FAMILY. A table-driven conformance sweep DERIVES, from the declared spec table and the state machine, one negative case per residual state and one positive case per declared source state, and executes them against the real Engine. A residual state that is not proven refused BY THIS GUARD, or a declared source state that is not proven accepted, FAILS THE GATE. The sweep is generated, not enumerated by an author, so a new command inherits full coverage the moment its spec exists.
- *sites:* NEW `packages/rph-application/src/handlers/execution-step-source.expected.ts` — TEST-SIDE ONLY (imported exclusively by the two conformance test files; add a dependency-cruiser rule forbidding any production import). It exports `EXPECTED_STEP_SOURCE_SETS: Readonly<Record<string, { readonly states: readonly string[]; readonly why: string; readonly unreachableSources?: Readonly<Record<string,string>> }>>` — the nine sets RE-AUTHORED BY HAND with a prose justification each — and `ORPHAN_STEP_ARROWS` (see the next fix). NEW `packages/rph-application/src/handlers/execution-step-source-conformance.test.ts` — the sweep. Its harness mirrors `dwp05-precondition-coverage.test.ts`'s `harness()` (dispatch / stateOf / revisionOf / eventsOfType) and adds `driveStepTo(state)`: a declared reach-table, COMMAND-DRIVEN wherever a command route exists (QUEUED = seeded default; RUNNING = Start; WAITING = Start+Wait; SUCCEEDED = Start+Complete; FAILED = Start+Fail; SKIPPED = Skip{mandatory:false}; CANCELLED = Start+Cancel) and SEEDED-AT-PROPOSE only for the three states no command produces (NOT_READY, READY, SUPERSEDED). `driveStepTo` ASSERTS the achieved `stepState` equals the requested one and throws otherwise — a driver that silently lands elsewhere fails loudly instead of testing the wrong state.
- *mechanism:* TWO-KEY DERIVATION, so a widening mutant cannot silently shrink its own obligations. Gate step 1: assert `STEP_COMMAND_SPECS[c].sourceStates` set-equals `EXPECTED_STEP_SOURCE_SETS[c].states` for all nine — editing production alone goes RED HERE, immediately. Gate step 2: derive `residual(c) = admissibleSources('ExecutionStep.stepState', spec.target) \ EXPECTED[c].states` FROM THE EXPECTED LEDGER (never from production), and assert `residual(c)` is NON-EMPTY — a set that already equals the machine's admissible set is a VACUOUS guard and fails (INV-SRC-3). Gate step 3 (negative sweep, 13 cases): for each `c` and each `s in residual(c)`, `driveStepTo(s)` then dispatch `c` and require verdict KILL = {REJECTED, RPH_ILLEGAL_STATE_TRANSITION, message contains REQUIRE_FROM_MARKER}. Verdict ADMITTED (ACCEPTED) fails. Verdict MASKED (REJECTED but the marker is absent — an earlier layer spoke) ALSO fails, and the failure message names the observed code/message so the implementer fixes the FIXTURE, not the assertion. Gate step 4 (positive sweep, 15 cases): for each `c` and each `s in EXPECTED[c].states`, `driveStepTo(s)` then dispatch `c` and require ACCEPTED + `stepState === spec.target` + exactly one new event of `spec.eventType`; a source declared in `unreachableSources` is skipped ONLY with a recorded reason (the SPEC §5.3 'PROPOSED is machine-legal but runtime-unreachable' precedent), and the gate asserts the TOTAL count of unreachable entries equals a literal expected number so the escape hatch cannot grow silently. Gate step 5: assert the sweep actually executed `13 + 15 = 28` cases against literal expected counts — a harness that silently skips everything fails.
- *killTest:* The sweep IS the kill test for all nine guards; its own kill tests are the next fix. Named red-proofs to run and record: widening ANY spec's `sourceStates` -> RED at gate step 1; widening BOTH keys -> RED at gate step 3 for exactly the removed residual state, because that state is still declared as a machine arrow and drops out of the ledger (fix 5) as an uncovered arrow; narrowing any set (e.g. dropping 'WAITING' from Cancel — F-40's exact mutant) -> RED at gate step 1 AND at gate step 4's positive case; deleting the whole source check from `advanceStep` -> RED on all 13 negatives.
- *behaviourChange:* None — tests only.
- *risk:* HONEST LIMIT, DISCLOSED: a deliberate widening edited consistently in BOTH keys plus the arrow ledger passes the gate. That is intended — the mechanism converts a silent one-character mutation into a three-file edit carrying a written justification and a reviewer's eye. It cannot, and should not, make a documented contract change impossible. This is why the eight longhand K tests (previous fix) are retained as a NON-derived floor: they are literal fixtures that survive any table refactor.

**Findings F-40 (both halves: the WAITING->CANCELLED gap and the arrows no command drives).**
- *rule:* EVERY DECLARED `ExecutionStep.stepState` ARROW IS ACCOUNTED FOR, in exactly one of three buckets, and the accounting is OBSERVED AT RUNTIME rather than declared. COMMAND_BACKED = some spec targets `to` and declares `from` as a source -> the arrow MUST appear in the positive sweep's observed (from,to) set. REFUSED_RESIDUAL = some spec targets `to` but excludes `from` -> the arrow MUST appear in the negative sweep's observed set with a proven drivesFrom refusal. ORPHAN = no spec targets `to` at all -> the arrow MUST appear in `ORPHAN_STEP_ARROWS` with a non-empty reason.
- *sites:* `execution-step-source-conformance.test.ts` (the ledger block, run after the sweeps) + `execution-step-source.expected.ts` (`ORPHAN_STEP_ARROWS: readonly { from; to; reason }[]`). The 20 declared arrows (transitions.data.ts:1437-1482) classify as: 13 COMMAND_BACKED — QUEUED->RUNNING (Start), WAITING->RUNNING (Resolve), RUNNING->SUCCEEDED (Complete), RUNNING->FAILED (Fail), FAILED->QUEUED (Retry), RUNNING->WAITING (Wait), READY->SKIPPED (Skip and Prune), QUEUED->SKIPPED (Skip and Prune), NOT_READY->SKIPPED (Prune), READY->CANCELLED (Cancel), QUEUED->CANCELLED (Cancel), RUNNING->CANCELLED (Cancel), WAITING->CANCELLED (Cancel — F-40's headline, newly proven by the positive sweep and by the longhand P1 below); 1 REFUSED_RESIDUAL — READY->QUEUED (proven refused by RetryExecutionStep's declared set, kill test K8); 6 ORPHAN — NOT_READY->READY (trigger 'ExecutionStepReady'; no command mints it) and the five ->SUPERSEDED arrows (NOT_READY/READY/QUEUED/RUNNING/WAITING; `supersedeExecutionPlan` at execution.ts:531-550 is a PLAN-level advance with no step cascade, and `ExecutionStepSuperseded` exists nowhere in contracts). ALSO longhand `P1` in execution-step-reissue-guard.test.ts: `CancelExecutionStep is ACCEPTED from WAITING — the sanctioned exit for a suspended step under a terminated plan` (Start -> Wait -> CancelExecutionPlan -> Resolve REJECTED asserting 'Cancel the step instead' -> CancelExecutionStep ACCEPTED -> stepState CANCELLED), so the engine's own advertised remediation is bound to a test in one narrative, alongside the assertion at start-gate:627 that advertises it.
- *mechanism:* The COMMAND_BACKED and REFUSED_RESIDUAL sets are COLLECTED BY INSTRUMENTATION during the sweeps (the harness records (fromState,toState) for every ACCEPTED dispatch and (fromState,target,refusedBy) for every refused one), NOT declared — so the ledger cannot be satisfied by editing a list. Set equality is asserted in BOTH directions: every declared arrow lands in exactly one bucket (an arrow ADDED to the vocab without coverage fails), and every `ORPHAN_STEP_ARROWS` entry must genuinely classify ORPHAN (a stale entry for an arrow that has since become command-backed fails). DISPOSITION CHOSEN for the six orphans: REGISTER, do not delete, do not mint commands. Deleting arrows from the generated `packages/rph-domain/vocab/m2-transitions.json` is a change to a ratified semantic-model catalog (DOC-003) requiring canon adjudication; minting a step-level supersede command is new capability outside a remediation's scope. The arrows are inert TODAY because no command targets READY or SUPERSEDED — and the ledger makes that inertness a MACHINE-CHECKED fact rather than an assumption: the moment any spec declares `target: 'SUPERSEDED'`, all five entries go STALE and the gate fails until the positive sweep covers them. Record the six in `docs/Command Precondition Legality/RESIDUALS.md` and in a new SPEC-001 §5.5.
- *killTest:* `KT-LEDGER-1`: remove WAITING->CANCELLED coverage by narrowing Cancel's set -> RED at the two-key gate AND the arrow becomes REFUSED_RESIDUAL, requiring a negative observation it does not have -> RED. `KT-LEDGER-2` (selftest, synthetic input): a declared arrow set containing an extra arrow with an empty orphan register -> `arrowLedger` reports UNCOVERED_ARROW. `KT-LEDGER-3` (selftest): an `ORPHAN_STEP_ARROWS` entry whose (from,to) IS command-backed by the synthetic spec set -> reports STALE_ORPHAN. `KT-LEDGER-4`: every orphan entry has a `reason` of non-trivial length (>= 30 chars) — an empty-string rubber stamp fails.
- *behaviourChange:* None — tests and documentation only. WAITING->CANCELLED was already accepted and remains accepted; it is now proven.
- *risk:* Low. The ledger's chief cost is that adding a legitimate new arrow to the vocab now requires either a command that drives it or a registered reason — which is the intended friction.

**Findings The mechanism's own anti-vacuity. Answers 'how does the gate avoid being vacuous'.**
- *rule:* EVERY ANALYSIS PRIMITIVE OF THE CONFORMANCE GATE HAS ITS OWN KILL TEST, driven by LITERAL synthetic input with hand-written expected output. The recursion stops at pure functions.
- *sites:* NEW `packages/rph-application/src/handlers/execution-step-source-conformance.selftest.test.ts`, over the pure functions extracted into `step-command-spec.ts` (`residualGuardStates`, `auditStepCommandSpecs`, `classifyRefusal`, `classifyArrow`, `arrowLedger`) and `admissibleSources` in rph-domain.
- *mechanism:* Seven self-tests, each feeding a SYNTHETIC table and asserting the gate REPORTS A FAILURE: S1 vacuity detector — a synthetic spec `{target:'CANCELLED', sourceStates:[READY,QUEUED,RUNNING,WAITING,CANCELLED]}` (equal to the machine's admissible set) makes `auditStepCommandSpecs` emit VACUOUS_GUARD; delete the emptiness check and S1 goes RED. S2 layer attribution — feed `classifyRefusal` a synthetic result `{status:'REJECTED', code:'RPH_INVARIANT_VIOLATION', message:'Cannot prune step s1: it is still reachable ...'}` (verbatim from execution.ts:1063) and assert it classifies MASKED, and that the sweep's verdict for MASKED is FAIL; drop the marker assertion and S2 goes RED. S3 — `{status:'ACCEPTED'}` classifies ADMITTED = FAIL. S4 marker discrimination — assert `REQUIRE_FROM_MARKER.length >= 20` AND that it is absent from a LITERAL array holding the verbatim first clause of all five precheck refusal messages and of `assertTransition`'s 'Illegal transition on' text; emptying or over-generalizing the marker (which would make `includes()` true everywhere) goes RED. S5/S6 — the two ledger self-tests above. S7 two-key — `EXPECTED_STEP_SOURCE_SETS` set-equals `STEP_COMMAND_SPECS` for all nine, with a per-command diff in the failure message. Additionally the sweep asserts its own case counts (28) and its own driver count (10 states minus the declared-unreachable), so a harness that silently no-ops fails.
- *killTest:* The self-tests ARE the kill tests; their own red-proof is that each is written to fail against a synthetic input, so their failure mode is exercised by construction. Record the live red-proof in the handoff: temporarily invert each primitive's condition and confirm exactly its own self-test goes RED.
- *behaviourChange:* None — tests only.
- *risk:* The recursion is finite by design: the self-tests use hand-written literals rather than derived data, so they cannot be co-mutated with the thing they test. This is the same standard `transitions.test.ts:103-110` already applies ('exactly one declared illegal self-edge exists across all machines, and it is the Baseline row').

**Findings F-32 / F-35 (the '<=' comparator), plus the scope corrections both verifiers folded in ('==' has only a true assertion; '<' has only a true assertion; '>=' has only true assertions).**
- *rule:* EVERY MEMBER OF `NumericComparatorSchema` IS ASSERTED AT ITS BOUNDARY IN BOTH POLARITIES, through both numeric operators, against a hand-written truth table — and the five rows are PAIRWISE DISTINCT, which is a proof that no comparator can be mutated into any other comparator or into a constant without a RED.
- *sites:* MODIFY `packages/rph-domain/src/condition-grammar.ts:16` — `export const NumericComparatorSchema` (currently module-private; the export is required for enum-driven exhaustiveness and adds no behaviour). MODIFY `packages/rph-domain/src/condition-grammar.test.ts` — new describe block `numeric comparators — full truth table at the boundary (CON-000 B7)`, replacing/extending the two hand-picked blocks at :64-72.
- *mechanism:* Declare `const EXPECTED = 2` and `const COMPARATOR_TRUTH: Record<NumericComparator, readonly [boolean, boolean, boolean]>` for `actual` in `[EXPECTED-1, EXPECTED, EXPECTED+1]`, hand-authored as an INDEPENDENT ORACLE (never computed from the implementation): `'=='` [false,true,false]; `'>='` [false,true,true]; `'>'` [false,false,true]; `'<'` [true,false,false]; `'<='` [true,true,false]. Loop `for (const cmp of NumericComparatorSchema.options)` and for each of the three `actual` values evaluate BOTH `{op:'ATTEMPTS', stepId:'sA', cmp, value: EXPECTED}` and `{op:'OUTPUT_COUNT', stepId:'sB', cmp, value: EXPECTED}` through the public `evaluateCondition`, against a subject in which `sA.attemptsMade = actual` and `sB.outputArtifactIds.length = actual` while `sA.outputArtifactIds.length` and `sB.attemptsMade` are DELIBERATELY DIFFERENT values — so a cross-wire of the two routes at condition-grammar.ts:124/126 also goes red. Also assert `ConditionExpressionSchema.safeParse({op:'ATTEMPTS', stepId:'s', cmp, value:1}).success === true` for every enum member, pinning the propose-time surface (execution.ts:190) that admits '<=' today.
- *killTest:* `KT-CMP-1 exhaustiveness`: `expect(Object.keys(COMPARATOR_TRUTH).sort()).toEqual([...NumericComparatorSchema.options].sort())` — adding a sixth comparator to the enum without a truth row FAILS THE GATE (this is the anti-recurrence half; the register's finding was that nothing loops over the enum). `KT-CMP-2 discrimination (the anti-vacuity proof)`: `expect(new Set(Object.values(COMPARATOR_TRUTH).map(r => r.join(''))).size).toBe(5)` — the three probe points produce five DISTINCT signatures, which proves mechanically that every cross-comparator mutant AND both constant mutants ('true' -> [T,T,T], 'false' -> [F,F,F], neither of which matches any row) is killed. Named red-proofs to run and record: `'<=' -> '<'` RED at actual=2; `'<=' -> false` RED at actual in {1,2}; `'==' -> '<='` RED at actual=1; `'<' -> '<='` RED at actual=2; `'>=' -> '>'` RED at actual=2; `'>' -> '>='` RED at actual=2.
- *behaviourChange:* None. `NumericComparatorSchema` becomes an exported symbol (additive API surface, no runtime change). The comparator implementations are CORRECT today and are not touched — both verifiers confirmed 2<=2 true / 2<=1 false against the real module.
- *risk:* Negligible. The one thing to watch: the truth table must remain hand-authored. A reviewer who 'simplifies' it by computing the expected value from `numericCompare` re-creates the vacuity — state this in a comment above the table, and KT-CMP-2 partially defends it (a computed table would still yield five distinct rows, so the comment is the real guard here; flagged in openQuestions).

**Findings Documentation reconciliation for the whole family — the machine/doc half the CMDPRE review flagged as its own finding class.**
- *rule:* The ratified specification records the step family's source sets, its conformance obligation and its disclosed residuals, so a future implementer reads the obligation rather than re-deriving it.
- *sites:* MODIFY `docs/Command Precondition Legality/JAN-CMDPRE-SPEC-001 Command Precondition and Transition Legality.md` — add §5.5 'Per-site fixture obligations — the ExecutionStep family's nine (JAN-EXECREM)', a table in the exact shape of §5.2/§5.3 with columns Command | Authored set S | Residual states (negative fixtures) | Positive fixture | Enumerated code | Special obligation; add a paragraph recording that §5.1 is now MECHANIZED for this family (derived sweep + arrow ledger) rather than hand-discharged, and that §5.4's compile-mandatory flip is extended to `advanceStep`'s `spec`. Bump the changelog line at :9. MODIFY `docs/Command Precondition Legality/RESIDUALS.md` — register the six orphan arrows (R-EXECREM-1: NOT_READY->READY, no command mints ExecutionStepReady; R-EXECREM-2..6: the five ->SUPERSEDED arrows, no step-level supersede command exists and `supersedeExecutionPlan` performs no step cascade) with the machine-checked property that they cannot be silently activated. MODIFY the header of `execution-step-reissue-guard.test.ts` to state the new scope (nine commands, thirteen residual states) and the guard-precedence ruling.
- *mechanism:* SPEC-001 §5.1 already carried the obligation; what was missing was (a) the step family's row and (b) the statement that the discharge is mechanical. Writing both closes the loop that let the obligation apply to eleven plan-level sites and silently skip nine step-level ones.
- *killTest:* `KT-DOC-1`: the conformance test asserts `EXPECTED_STEP_SOURCE_SETS` has exactly nine entries and that each carries a non-empty `why` string — the prose justification is data the gate checks, not decoration. `KT-DOC-2` (optional, cheap): a test that parses SPEC-001 §5.5's table and asserts its 'Authored set S' column set-equals `STEP_COMMAND_SPECS` — making the document a THIRD key that cannot drift. RECOMMENDED: adopt it; it is ~20 lines of markdown-table parsing and it is exactly the drift the CMDPRE post-build review had to fix by hand.
- *behaviourChange:* None — documentation only.
- *risk:* KT-DOC-2 couples a test to markdown formatting; if the table is reformatted the test breaks noisily. Acceptable — a noisy break beats silent drift, and the parser should assert it found exactly nine rows so a formatting change that yields zero rows fails rather than passes.

### Invariants
- INV-SRC-1 (Declared source set). Every ExecutionStep-advancing command declares a NON-EMPTY source-state set in `STEP_COMMAND_SPECS`, and `advanceStep` refuses any dispatch whose step's `stepState` is not a member, with `RPH_ILLEGAL_STATE_TRANSITION` and a message carrying `REQUIRE_FROM_MARKER` plus the rendered `drivesFrom` set. Declaring the set is COMPILE-MANDATORY (a required parameter of non-empty-tuple type), mirroring SPEC-001 §5.4's flip for `advanceStatus`.
- INV-SRC-2 (Guard precedence). Source-state legality is evaluated BEFORE any semantic precheck. A command's declared contract can never be masked by a context-dependent rule, so every residual state is observable at the bus.
- INV-SRC-3 (Non-vacuity of each guard). For every command, `residual = admissibleSources('ExecutionStep.stepState', target) \ sourceStates` is NON-EMPTY (a set equal to the machine's admissible set adds nothing and is a finding, not a pass), and EVERY member of `residual` is behaviourally proven refused BY THIS GUARD — proven by the presence of `REQUIRE_FROM_MARKER` in the refusal message, which no precheck and no `checkTransition` refusal can produce.
- INV-SRC-4 (Widest-in-arrow). EVERY declared source state is behaviourally proven ACCEPTED (SPEC-001 §5.1(2)), or is listed in `unreachableSources` with a recorded reason and counted against a literal expected total. This is the invariant that kills NARROWING mutants — F-40's `WAITING` drop from CancelExecutionStep.
- INV-ARR-1 (Arrow accounting). Every declared `ExecutionStep.stepState` arrow is exactly one of COMMAND_BACKED (observed accepted in the positive sweep), REFUSED_RESIDUAL (observed refused with the marker in the negative sweep), or ORPHAN (registered with a substantive reason AND provably targeted by no command spec). Set equality is asserted in both directions, and the covered sets are collected by runtime instrumentation, not declared.
- INV-CMP-1 (Comparator totality). Every member of `NumericComparatorSchema` has a hand-authored truth-table row asserted at {expected-1, expected, expected+1} through BOTH `ATTEMPTS` and `OUTPUT_COUNT`; the five rows are pairwise distinct, which proves no comparator can be mutated into any other comparator or into a constant without a RED.
- INV-META-1 (Gate non-vacuity). Every analysis primitive of the conformance gate has a self-test driven by LITERAL synthetic input with hand-written expected output, proving the gate REPORTS FAILURE when its condition is violated. The recursion terminates at pure functions with literal inputs.
- INV-5 preserved. Nothing in this family reads or writes an assurance axis; the sweep dispatches only execution commands and asserts only `stepState`, event counts and revisions.
- Fail-closed preserved. No guard is relaxed. The only ordering change moves the STRICTER, purely-syntactic check earlier; absent/unknown source states remain refused.

### Anti-recurrence
The mechanism has four layers, and each answers a different way a future guard could go untested.

1. IT CANNOT BE OMITTED. `advanceStep` takes `spec: StepCommandSpec` as a REQUIRED parameter typed `readonly [string, ...string[]]`. A tenth step command that forgets its source set is a `tsc` error, not a silent gap. This is the exact device SPEC-001 §5.4 used at the plan level ("`precondition` is a required property... omitting it is a compile error, so no status advance can silently skip its declaration and the count cannot regress"), extended to the family that never got it.

2. IT CANNOT BE UNPROVEN. The conformance sweep DERIVES its obligations from the declared table and the state machine: `residual = admissibleSources(target) \ sourceStates` yields the negative cases, `sourceStates` yields the positive ones. A new command therefore arrives with a full kill-test battery already generated — nobody has to remember. A residual state that is not proven refused by THIS guard fails the gate, and so does a declared source state that is not proven accepted.

3. IT CANNOT BE SATISFIED BY THE WRONG LAYER. Every generated negative asserts `REQUIRE_FROM_MARKER` ('this command declares drivesFrom') is present in the refusal message. That string is produced only by `advanceStep`'s source-state branch — no precheck message and no `assertTransition` message ('Illegal transition on ...') contains it. A case refused by an earlier layer classifies MASKED and FAILS, with the observed code and message printed so the implementer fixes the fixture rather than the assertion. Combined with guard precedence (INV-SRC-2), no precheck can mask a residual state any more; and because residual states are chosen from the machine-ADMISSIBLE set, the machine provably cannot be the refuser either. This is the direct, structural answer to the class-(b) vacuous negatives (F-13's canSkipStep at start-gate:699; F-14's machine-refused QUEUED->WAITING at start-gate:608; the prune-from-RUNNING assertion at start-gate:898).

4. IT CANNOT BE EDITED SILENTLY, AND THE GATE ITSELF IS PROVEN. The expected source sets live in a TEST-ONLY second key (`execution-step-source.expected.ts`), so a widening mutation in production goes RED at the equality assertion before any behaviour is exercised. The arrow ledger's covered sets are collected by runtime instrumentation, so it cannot be satisfied by editing a list; the orphan register is checked for staleness in both directions. And the gate's own primitives (`residualGuardStates`, `classifyRefusal`, `classifyArrow`, `arrowLedger`, the marker) each have a self-test with literal synthetic input that asserts the gate REPORTS FAILURE — including a marker-discrimination test that the marker is >= 20 chars and absent from a literal array of all five precheck messages, so emptying or over-generalizing it (which would make `includes()` universally true) goes RED.

DISCLOSED LIMIT, stated honestly: a DELIBERATE widening edited consistently across production, the expected ledger and the arrow ledger will pass. That is by design — the mechanism's purpose is to convert a silent one-character mutation into a three-file, justification-bearing, reviewable change, not to forbid documented contract evolution. The eight longhand kill tests are retained as a non-derived floor for exactly this reason.

DEMONSTRATED VALUE BEYOND THE FINDINGS: applied to today's code the derivation produces 13 negative and 15 positive obligations. Five negatives are already covered and five are the assigned findings — the remaining THREE are gaps no reviewer named (StartExecutionStep re-issued on a RUNNING step, which the machine NOOP-admits; RetryExecutionStep from READY, riding the declared READY->QUEUED arrow; PruneExecutionStep re-issued on a SKIPPED step). Several positives are likewise uncovered (Cancel from READY and WAITING, Skip from READY, Prune from READY). A mechanism that finds work an adversarial review of 118 agents did not is the demonstration that it is not a rubber stamp.

REUSE PATH (recorded, not built here): the same shape — declared spec table, machine-derived residuals, two-key expected ledger, marker-based layer attribution, arrow ledger — applies verbatim to `advanceStatus`'s `precondition` FROM_STATES sites, which today are covered by the hand-written dwp04/05/06/08 files. Fold that into the CMDPRE backlog rather than this family's scope.

### Seed impact
NONE, and this is provable rather than empirical.

No fix in this family changes any guard's ACCEPT set. The accept/refuse partition of every one of the nine step commands is bit-identical before and after: the spec-table refactor moves literals without changing values; the guard reorder changes only WHICH of two refusals speaks when both would refuse (if requireFrom passes and precheck refuses -> still refused; if requireFrom refuses and precheck passes -> still refused; if both refuse -> still refused with a different message; if both pass -> still accepted); the comparator work touches no comparator body; everything else is test and documentation. Therefore no legitimate input the seed drives can be newly refused — the over-refusal risk for this family is structurally zero.

Positively: `packages/rph-engine/src/seed-workbench.ts` + `reference-undertaking.ts` issue only in-contract step commands (that is why the DWP-09 read-only history audit found zero contradictions across 299 events / 104 aggregates), so they never reach a source-state refusal at all and cannot observe the reorder.

VERIFICATION REQUIRED BEFORE LANDING (do not take the argument on trust): (1) `packages/rph-engine` suite green — all 69; (2) full `bunx vitest run` across rph-domain / rph-application / rph-projections / rph-contracts / rph-persistence; (3) playwright e2e (the JPWB 'full gate' is vitest AND playwright, plus types / lint / boundary / svelte-check); (4) a targeted grep, before touching `advanceStep`'s order, of every `error.code` and `error.message).toContain(` assertion on the nine step commands across packages and apps/rph-demo/e2e — the survey I ran found all message-asserting sites target steps INSIDE their command's declared set (start-gate:627, skip-cancel:203, start-gate:658), so all are predicted unaffected, but the grep is the proof; (5) re-run the DWP-09 history-audit runner (RESIDUALS.md §5 carries the reproducible source) and confirm the same 299/104 census with zero contradictions.

Note on Bun: the engine's better-sqlite3 refuses Bun's runtime — run these scripts under vitest/Node, not `bun run`.

### Open questions
- PLAUSIBLE-finding disposition (my family's share). F-41/F-43/F-44 (ALL with an empty operand list is vacuously TRUE) sit in the condition grammar and are adjacent to my F-32/F-35 work, but they are a FAIL-OPEN SEMANTICS defect, not a coverage defect — the fix is a propose-time refusal or an evaluator change, which belongs to the grammar family, not to mine. MY DISPOSITION: out of scope here; I add no `ALL[]` case to the truth table. But if the grammar family's fix lands after mine, the comparator block must not be assumed to cover it. RECOMMENDATION to the orchestrator: assign F-41/43/44 explicitly to the grammar family so it is not orphaned between us. F-46 (STEP_SUCCEEDED vs the gate's terminal-success set disagreeing about SKIPPED) is likewise a semantics question for the transition-gate family, though the arrow ledger will make the disagreement visible.
- Should the DERIVED sweep replace or merely supplement the existing hand-written `dwp05-precondition-coverage.test.ts` pattern at the plan level? I chose SUPPLEMENT (this family does not touch the plan-level files) to keep the blast radius small. But the same root cause (a ratified §5.1 obligation discharged by hand) applies there, and leaving two idioms in the repo invites the next implementer to pick the weaker one. Needs a sponsor/roadmap decision on whether to schedule the plan-level migration as a follow-on DWP.
- The comparator truth table's independence is protected by a COMMENT, not by a mechanism. `KT-CMP-2` (five pairwise-distinct rows) does not detect a table computed from `numericCompare` itself, because a computed table would still be pairwise distinct. I could not devise a mechanical guard that does not itself re-implement the comparators. Mitigations considered and rejected as worse: (a) a second independent implementation to cross-check — that is just the same bug written twice; (b) property-based testing with fast-check against JS's own operators — that IS the implementation. LEAVING IT as a prominent comment plus a review note; flag if a stronger device is wanted.
- Whether `KT-DOC-2` (parse SPEC-001 §5.5's markdown table and assert it set-equals `STEP_COMMAND_SPECS`) should ship. I RECOMMEND yes — it makes the ratified document a third key and is exactly the machine/doc drift the CMDPRE post-build review had to reconcile by hand — but it couples a test to markdown formatting and no such test exists in the repo today, so it is a new precedent. Sponsor call.
- The five `->SUPERSEDED` arrows and `NOT_READY->READY` are REGISTERED as orphans rather than deleted from `packages/rph-domain/vocab/m2-transitions.json`. I chose registration because deleting a ratified DOC-003 catalog arrow needs canon adjudication and the arrows are provably inert today (no spec targets those states, and the ledger fails the moment one does). If the sponsor prefers deletion, it is a vocab change plus `bun run gen`, and the orphan register shrinks to zero — cleaner, but it forecloses a future step-level supersede cascade that DOC-003 evidently anticipated. Recording the fork rather than deciding it unilaterally.
- F-40's second half was DISPUTED by its own code-semantics verifier ('a machine broader than the command set is the DESIGNED relationship on this surface... that half should be dropped from the finding'). I did not drop it — I converted it from a defect claim into a machine-checked accounting obligation, which I believe satisfies both readings (the verifier's point is honoured: an orphan arrow is a residual-register item, and that is precisely where the ledger puts it). Flagging in case the reviewer intends the stronger reading that no register entry should exist at all.

### Adversarial critique — verdict `NEEDS_REVISION`

### Over-refusal risks
- NONE from the design as written — verified, not assumed. No fix changes any command's accept set. The reference seed is untouched: `packages/rph-engine/src/reference-undertaking.ts` seeds every step at `stepState: 'QUEUED'` (:553) and issues only `StartExecutionStep` (:567) on a QUEUED step and `CompleteExecutionStep` (:604) on a RUNNING step — both squarely inside their declared sets, so neither the spec-table refactor nor the guard reorder is observable to the seed, and no prune/skip/cancel/wait/resolve command appears anywhere in it.
- A REAL over-acceptance risk is created if RC-1 is resolved the WRONG way. The vocab declares `StartExecutionStep drivesFrom READY|QUEUED`; adopting the vocab as authoritative (the naive reading of mandate constraint 6) would widen Start to accept a READY step. There is no READY->RUNNING arrow in `ExecutionStep.stepState`, so `checkTransition` would then refuse it as ILLEGAL_UNDEFINED — a widened guard that fails one line later with the machine's generic message instead of the drivesFrom message, defeating the marker-based layer attribution the whole design rests on. The adjudication must correct the vocab, not the code.
- The seven enumerated behaviour changes from the reorder (S2) are all message/code reshuffles on inputs that were ALREADY refused; I verified none is reached by any existing unit test, e2e fixture, or the seed (see RC-6 for the site-by-site survey). No previously-accepted input newly refuses anywhere in the family.
- Watch item, not a defect: the reorder means `CompleteExecutionStep`'s floor gate no longer runs for a step outside RUNNING. That is correct (a SUCCEEDED step's floor was already judged at its first Complete), but if the F-26 family adds a plan-ACTIVE precheck to Complete/Fail, its kill test MUST use a source state inside the declared set or it will be masked in the other direction — the design flags this as D3 and should keep it as a hard handoff, not a note.

### Symptomatic-not-systemic
- The systemic claim is broken by RC-3. The design's root cause is "declared contracts on this surface are not mechanically bound to their proofs", yet it leaves TWO further unbound copies of the same source-state contracts reachable: `PRUNABLE_SOURCE_STATES` (rph-domain/src/transition-gate.ts:23) whose own docstring says it exists to prevent the exact divergence it is unbound against, and `CONTROL_BY_STEP_STATE` / `ADVANCE_BY_STEP_STATE` (rph-projections/src/execution-view.ts:118-147), which are hand-copies of Skip's and Cancel's sets that F-40's verifier already identified as "a hand-authored table with no binding to the handler" whose test "asserts the table against itself". Fixing only the handler side is the local repair pattern the mandate names as the cause of the three-times-recurring dead-arm class.
- RC-1 is the sharper instance: the design invents a NEW authoritative table while a RATIFIED one (vocab `drivesFrom`) already exists and has already drifted in two places. Adding a fourth uncoordinated declaration and then ratifying it in SPEC-001 §5.5 is the root cause performed, not cured.
- The two-key ledger's disclosed limit is honest but understated. With RC-1 unfixed the two keys are both hand-authored by the same implementer in the same changeset from the same source (today's literals), so the "three-file edit carrying a written justification" friction is real for a FUTURE mutation but does not exist for the INITIAL transcription — and the initial transcription is where the vocab divergence gets baked in. Deriving one key from the vocab (RC-1) is what makes the two-key argument actually load-bearing.
- The comparator fix (fix #6) is genuinely systemic for its own scope (enum-driven exhaustiveness + pairwise-distinct discrimination), but its anti-recurrence is scoped to ONE enum. The design's own root-cause statement names "comparator enums" as a class; nothing generalizes KT-CMP-1's "every enum member has a truth row" pattern to the other hand-picked-example enums on this surface (`TransitionTypeSchema`, `StepTypeSchema` classification tables). Recording the reuse path — as the design does for `advanceStatus` — would be consistent; omitting it while claiming the class is not.

### Conflicts / gaps
- CONTRACT CONFLICT (RC-1): `STEP_COMMAND_SPECS` as designed contradicts the ratified vocab for StartExecutionStep and PruneExecutionStep, and the vocab in turn contradicts the ratified m2 machine (READY->RUNNING is declared in `drivesFrom` but is not an arrow in `ExecutionStep.stepState`). Three-way divergence; the design detects none of it.
- INVARIANT CONFLICT (RC-2): the repo already ratified INV-4 ("requireFrom = machine in-arrows", execution-start-gate.test.ts:770-771) as the CMDPRE ruling that added NOT_READY->SKIPPED. The design's invariant set (INV-SRC-1..4, INV-ARR-1) never restates or mechanizes it, and KT-SPEC-2 is too weak to enforce it.
- LAYERING CONFLICT (RC-3): siting `STEP_COMMAND_SPECS` in `packages/rph-application/src/handlers/` makes it unimportable by rph-domain, so `transition-gate.ts`'s duplicate can never be bound to it. The design's own preferred site forecloses its own systemic goal.
- LEDGER LOGIC GAP (RC-7): the three arrow buckets overlap on five declared arrows; the stated rule and the stated "exactly one of three buckets" cannot both hold.
- FIXTURE GAP (RC-8): the derived sweep's `driveStepTo` cannot produce a prunable step, so all three PruneExecutionStep positive cases are unimplementable as specified.
- CROSS-FAMILY (RC-11): D1 is stated as a risk rather than a position, leaving three K tests and four positive cases — including three of F-40's four named non-SUPERSEDED uncovered arrows — conditional on another family's unmade decision.
- PLAUSIBLE-DISPOSITION GAP (RC-12): F-42 is unmentioned though the design's SPEC-001 §5.5 rows and KT-DOC-2 would entrench one side of it.
- INV-5 and RPH-EXE-002: no conflict found. Every fix reads/writes only `stepState`, event counts and revisions; the reorder moves a purely-syntactic state check earlier and relaxes nothing. Fail-closed is preserved (absent/unknown source states remain refused; the stricter check moves first). Generated-file discipline is respected for `transitions.data.ts` (register-don't-delete for the six orphans is the right call, and I confirm the arrows are inert: no spec targets READY or SUPERSEDED, `supersedeExecutionPlan` at execution.ts:531-550 is a plan-level `advanceStatus` with no step cascade, and `ExecutionStepSuperseded` exists nowhere in contracts) — but NOT for `messages.ts`/vocab, per RC-1.

### Missed findings
No assigned finding is silently dropped. F-11/F-18 (Resolve<-QUEUED) are addressed by K5, which I verified is a genuine kill: QUEUED->RUNNING is a LEGAL machine arrow so `checkTransition` provably cannot refuse, and with the plan ACTIVE the precheck passes, leaving the declared set as the sole refuser. F-12 (Cancel) by K1, F-13 by K2+K3, F-14/F-19 by K4 — all four target machine-NOOP-admitted self-edges where the declared set is the sole refuser, so all four are non-vacuous. F-32/F-35 by the truth table. F-40 by the positive sweep (WAITING->CANCELLED, READY->CANCELLED, READY->SKIPPED), longhand P1, K8 for READY->QUEUED, and the orphan register for the five ->SUPERSEDED plus NOT_READY->READY. Two coverage claims are WRONG rather than missing: K7 is already covered (RC-4), and the design's Skip/Prune positive cases and K3/K8 are conditionally unimplementable (RC-8, RC-11). One PLAUSIBLE finding in the family's blast radius is unaddressed: F-42 (RC-12).

### Required changes
- RC-1 (BLOCKING — the design would cement a live authority divergence). The ratified vocab ALREADY declares the thing this design proposes to newly invent, and it already disagrees with the code. `packages/rph-contracts/vocab/m3-commands-events.json` carries `drivesFrom` for all nine step commands (StartExecutionStep at :829-834, and a second duplicate table at ~:6959). Extracted values vs shipped `requireFrom`: StartExecutionStep vocab `READY|QUEUED` vs code `['QUEUED']` (execution.ts:698 — code NARROWER, and its own comment says "drivesFrom QUEUED", which is false against the vocab); PruneExecutionStep vocab `READY|QUEUED` vs code `['NOT_READY','READY','QUEUED']` (execution.ts:1041 — code WIDER, deliberately, per the D5 deadlock comment at :1038-1040). The other seven match. The design never mentions the vocab: it hand-authors `STEP_COMMAND_SPECS` in rph-application, hand-re-authors `EXPECTED_STEP_SOURCE_SETS`, and then ratifies the pair in SPEC-001 §5.5 — a "two-key" system whose two keys agree with each other and disagree with the ratified source, which is the exact root cause ("declared contracts not mechanically bound to their enforcer") reproduced one layer up, and a direct breach of mandate constraint 6 (contracts are generated from vocab). REQUIRED: (a) add the vocab as a key — either generate `STEP_COMMAND_SPECS` from `drivesFrom` via `bun run gen`, or assert `spec.sourceStates` set-equals the parsed vocab `drivesFrom` for all nine as gate step 0; (b) adjudicate the two divergences EXPLICITLY in the design, not silently. Recommended adjudication: the CODE is right and the VOCAB is stale — correct the vocab to `QUEUED` for Start and `NOT_READY|READY|QUEUED` for Prune, regenerate, and record both in RESIDUALS.md + §5.5. Do NOT resolve it the other way: the vocab's `StartExecutionStep READY|QUEUED -> RUNNING` declares a READY->RUNNING transition the m2 machine does NOT legalise (transitions.data.ts:1437-1482 has READY->QUEUED/SKIPPED/CANCELLED/SUPERSEDED only), so adopting the vocab would make Start accept READY and then be refused one line later by `checkTransition` as ILLEGAL_UNDEFINED — an over-acceptance producing a confusing double refusal.
- RC-2 (BLOCKING — KT-SPEC-2 is too weak to encode an already-ratified invariant). The repo already ratified INV-4 "requireFrom = machine in-arrows" (recorded verbatim at packages/rph-application/src/handlers/execution-start-gate.test.ts:770-771, as the CMDPRE ruling that added the NOT_READY->SKIPPED arrow). KT-SPEC-2 as designed only asserts that each declared source name appears in `STATE_MACHINES['ExecutionStep.stepState'].states` — READY is a valid state, so the vocab's illegal READY->RUNNING passes it. REQUIRED: strengthen KT-SPEC-2 to assert, for every spec and every `s` in `sourceStates`, `classifyTransition('ExecutionStep.stepState', s, spec.target).klass === 'LEGAL'` (LEGAL, not LEGAL|NOOP), and name it as the mechanization of INV-4. Also add the check that actually bites for vacuity: `sourceStates` must NOT contain `spec.target` (see RC-9).
- RC-3 (BLOCKING — a second unbound copy of the same contract is left reachable, so the root cause survives). `packages/rph-domain/src/transition-gate.ts:23` declares `const PRUNABLE_SOURCE_STATES = new Set(['NOT_READY','READY','QUEUED'])` with a docstring that says in terms "The states PruneExecutionStep may be issued FROM (its vocab drivesFrom). The read-model must not offer a prune outside this set or it tempts a command the engine refuses — the read-model/authority divergence this module exists to prevent" — and there is NO mechanical binding to execution.ts:1041. Narrowing one and not the other silently recreates the divergence, which is F-40's own diagnosis of `CONTROL_BY_STEP_STATE`. The design does not mention `PRUNABLE_SOURCE_STATES` at all. Worse, its chosen site FORECLOSES the fix: `STEP_COMMAND_SPECS` in rph-application cannot be imported by rph-domain (dependency direction). REQUIRED: site the spec table where rph-domain can consume it (rph-contracts, generated from vocab per RC-1) and have `transition-gate.ts` derive `PRUNABLE_SOURCE_STATES` from it; if that is deferred, add a mandatory conformance assertion binding `PRUNABLE_SOURCE_STATES` to `STEP_COMMAND_SPECS.PruneExecutionStep.sourceStates`. Do the same for `ADVANCE_BY_STEP_STATE` / `CONTROL_BY_STEP_STATE` (packages/rph-projections/src/execution-view.ts:118-147): `CONTROL_BY_STEP_STATE` offers `skip` from exactly READY|QUEUED and `cancel` from exactly READY|QUEUED|RUNNING|WAITING — i.e. it is a third hand-copy of two specs. The design demotes this to "a cheap, high-value follow-on for whichever family owns F-29"; it is the identical root cause on the identical surface and belongs in this family's scope, or the "systemic" claim is not earned.
- RC-4 (FACTUAL ERROR — K7 is not a gap, and it undercuts the design's headline justification). The design presents `StartExecutionStep` re-issued on a RUNNING step as one of three gaps "no reviewer named", and uses "a mechanism that finds work an adversarial review of 118 agents did not" as its proof of non-rubber-stamp. That case is already covered at full strength: execution-start-gate.test.ts:291-298 ("REFUSES to re-start an already-RUNNING step (a NOOP re-issue would inflate attemptsMade)") asserts REJECTED + `error.code === 'RPH_ILLEGAL_STATE_TRANSITION'` + `error.message).toContain('to be QUEUED, but it is RUNNING')` + `ExecutionStepStarted` count === 1. So the residual census is 6 covered / 7 new, not 5 / 8, and Start is FULLY covered on both its residual states. REQUIRED: correct the census; demote K7 from a new longhand test to "strengthen the existing test at :291 with the marker + revision + no-total-event assertions"; and restate the demonstrated-value claim to the two genuinely-new cases (Retry<-READY, Prune<-SKIPPED).
- RC-5 (SCOPE/JUSTIFICATION — the reorder's stated rationale is wrong for two of the three findings it cites, and it is load-bearing for exactly one case). I traced all 13 residual cases against the design's own fixture arrangement (plan ACTIVE, mandatory:false, cap unexhausted, explicit-no-output complete). Exactly ONE is masked by a precheck today: Prune<-SKIPPED, because `prunableStepIds` (transition-gate.ts:326-333) filters `!TERMINAL.has(s.stepState)` and SKIPPED is terminal, so an already-pruned step is never prunable and the precheck answers 'still reachable' (RPH_INVARIANT_VIOLATION) first. Every other residual reaches `requireFrom` today. The design's two cited masks are misdiagnosed: (i) F-13 — start-gate:699's `mandatorySkip` targets a QUEUED step, which is INSIDE Skip's declared set, so `requireFrom` is not masked there at all; it was simply miscounted as coverage, and the reorder does nothing for it; (ii) F-14 — the masking layer is the MACHINE (`checkTransition` on QUEUED->WAITING = ILLEGAL_UNDEFINED), and `enterExecutionStepWait` has NO precheck, so the reorder cannot touch it. I also confirmed no alternative fixture can unmask Prune<-SKIPPED (SKIPPED is terminal by definition of the residual), so S2 is genuinely required — but on one case plus the forward-looking invariant, not on the stated grounds. REQUIRED: restate fix #2's `findings` line and rationale honestly ("the single masked residual today is Prune<-SKIPPED; the invariant's value is that no FUTURE precheck can mask a residual — see D3"), and correct the F-13/F-14 attributions.
- RC-6 (POSITIVE VERIFICATION TO RECORD, plus one addition). I independently ran the message-assertion survey the design makes mandatory, and it comes out clean — record it so the implementer does not have to re-derive it: execution-step-skip-cancel.test.ts:217 (skip on QUEUED under SUPERSEDED — QUEUED is in-set); execution-retry-cap.test.ts:130-133/:140 (`attemptThenRetry` leaves the step FAILED at the refused retry — in-set); execution-start-gate.test.ts:689-690 and :708 ('still reachable' on QUEUED steps — in-set); :626-627 ('Cancel the step instead' on a WAITING step — in-set); :658 (already asserts the drivesFrom message); apps/rph-demo/e2e/execution-sequencing.e2e.ts:176 (Start on a QUEUED step 2 — in-set). None of the seven enumerated behaviour changes is reached by any existing test, e2e fixture, or the seed. ADD to the survey list two sites the design missed: execution-start-gate.test.ts:295-296 (Start<-RUNNING, asserts the drivesFrom-family message verbatim — must keep passing after the message is rebuilt from `spec.sourceStates`, so the `REQUIRE_FROM_MARKER` refactor MUST preserve the `requires step X to be A or B, but it is C` prefix byte-for-byte, not only the marker clause), and the accidental double dispatch at :866 (`expect(skip(2).status, JSON.stringify(skip(2)))` — arguments evaluate left-to-right, so the second `skip(2)` is a real second dispatch, refused, and the diagnostic string reports a REJECTED result for an assertion checking ACCEPTED; the design flags this but cites line 867).
- RC-7 (IMPLEMENTABILITY — the arrow ledger's three buckets are not mutually exclusive as written). "REFUSED_RESIDUAL = some spec targets `to` but excludes `from`" collides with COMMAND_BACKED on four declared arrows: WAITING->RUNNING (backed by Resolve, residual for Start), QUEUED->RUNNING (backed by Start, residual for Resolve), NOT_READY->SKIPPED (backed by Prune, residual for Skip), READY->SKIPPED and QUEUED->SKIPPED (backed by BOTH Skip and Prune). The design's own 13/1/6 classification silently applies a precedence it never states, so an implementer coding the rule literally gets arrows in two buckets and `classifyArrow` throws or double-counts, and the both-directions set equality fails. REQUIRED: state the precedence explicitly — COMMAND_BACKED if ANY spec backs (from,to); else REFUSED_RESIDUAL if any spec targets `to`; else ORPHAN — and add a selftest (S8) with a synthetic arrow backed by one spec and residual for another, asserting COMMAND_BACKED, so the precedence itself is kill-tested.
- RC-8 (IMPLEMENTABILITY — the positive sweep for PruneExecutionStep cannot run on the specified driver). Prune's three positive sources (NOT_READY / READY / QUEUED) each require the step to appear in `prunableStepIds`, which requires a BRANCH plan with a genuinely excluded arm plus `plan.status === 'ACTIVE'` (transition-gate.ts:323-334). `driveStepTo(state)` as designed is a flat per-STATE reach table over a linear plan and will always yield a reachable step, so all three Prune positives fail with 'still reachable' — and the design's own MASKED verdict then instructs the implementer to "fix the FIXTURE", with no fixture that can work. REQUIRED: the sweep needs a per-COMMAND fixture shape, not a per-state driver. Reuse the two existing shapes: `activeExcludedArmPlan` (execution-start-gate.test.ts:~838) for READY/QUEUED, and the NOT_READY-interior BRANCH plan already written at :772-790 for NOT_READY. Same class of problem, smaller, for Complete (its positive needs the TRANSFORMATION + explicit-no-output payload to clear `validateStepCompletion` AND `floorGateBlock`; see the fixture note at :47-49) and Retry (needs cap headroom). Spell all three out or the sweep is unimplementable as written.
- RC-9 (HONESTY — INV-SRC-3 advertises more than it delivers). `residual = admissibleSources(target) \\ sourceStates` always contains at least the target's own NOOP self-edge, so "residual is NON-EMPTY" can only ever fail for one mutation shape: a spec that declares its own target as a source state. S1's synthetic fixture is exactly that shape, so the selftest passes while the invariant reads as a general vacuity detector. REQUIRED: restate INV-SRC-3 as what it is ("a command may not declare its own target as a legal source; the machine's NOOP self-edge guarantees the residual is otherwise non-empty"), assert `!sourceStates.includes(target)` directly, and rely on RC-2's arrow-legality check plus INV-SRC-4's positive sweep for the real narrowing/widening defence.
- RC-10 (DEAD GUARD — dispose of `checkTransition` inside `advanceStep`). Once RC-2's INV-4 assertion holds, `checkTransition(command, STEP_MACHINE, step.stepState, spec.target)` at execution.ts:654 is provably unreachable for all nine commands: `requireFrom` already dominates it (it runs at :647, before it) and every declared source is a LEGAL arrow into the target. It is already dead today. This is the CMDPRE trap in mirror image ("requireFrom behind a canTransition guard = dead code") and the design neither notices nor disposes of it — leaving an undocumented dead guard inside the very primitive this family is making authoritative, which is precisely the condition that produces the next unkilled mutant. REQUIRED: either delete it and record INV-4 as the reason that is safe, or keep it with a self-test proving unreachability; do not leave it silent.
- RC-11 (D1 must be a hard precondition, not a coordination note). I confirmed `StepStateSchema` (packages/rph-contracts/src/enums.ts:699-710) admits all ten states at propose today, so seeding NOT_READY/READY works. But three of the eight new K tests (K3, K8, and the Prune NOT_READY positive) and four positive-sweep cases (Skip<-READY, Cancel<-READY, Prune<-NOT_READY, Prune<-READY — i.e. three of F-40's four named uncovered non-SUPERSEDED arrows) die if the F-09/F-22/F-27 family lands a propose-time "steps rest QUEUED" rule. The design records this as "COORDINATE EXPLICITLY". REQUIRED: state this family's POSITION, not just the risk — propose-time seeding of non-QUEUED step states must be preserved (it is the established fixture contract relied on by `activePlan(stepStates)` at execution-start-gate.test.ts:62-85 across dozens of tests), or that family ships the migration for both suites in the same change. Without a stated position, F-40 is only conditionally addressed.
- RC-12 (PLAUSIBLE disposition gap — F-42). The design disposes F-41/43/44/46 by referral but never mentions F-42 ("DWP-04's 'Both plan-ACTIVE-guarded' is implemented as one guard"), which sits squarely in this family's blast radius: SPEC-001 §5.5's Wait row and `EXPECTED_STEP_SOURCE_SETS.EnterExecutionStepWait.why` will restate wait's deliberate ungated-ness, and KT-DOC-2 would make that a machine-checked third key — ratifying one side of an open, undisclosed doc contradiction (DS-004 §6 D6 / DR-004 :177/:185/:191/:199 say the opposite, and DR-004 §15 certifies "no divergences"). REQUIRED: add an explicit disposition — recommend recording the divergence in DR-004 §19 as part of this family's §5.5 write-up, since this family is already editing that record and will otherwise silently entrench it.
- RC-13 (MINOR, comparator scope). The design's coverage correction list is accurate but incomplete: at condition-grammar.test.ts, '>=' has only true assertions (:65, :70), '==' only true (:67), '<' only true (:31, :71) — and '>' has only a FALSE assertion (:66), which the design omits. State it so the truth table's red-proof list is complete. Otherwise the comparator fix is sound as designed: I confirmed `NumericComparatorSchema` is module-private at condition-grammar.ts:16 (the type alias is exported, the schema is not), `numericCompare` is module-private at :83-98 and reached only via `evaluateCondition`'s :124 (OUTPUT_COUNT) / :126 (ATTEMPTS), and the three-probe five-distinct-signatures argument does kill every cross-comparator and both constant mutants.

## F5-propose-validation — Propose-time contract validation (the malformed-plan family)

### Root cause
`ProposeExecutionPlan` is the ONLY write path that introduces steps or transitions — verified exhaustively: `steps:` is written at `packages/rph-application/src/handlers/execution.ts:263` (propose, `steps: p.steps` verbatim), `:659` (advanceStep, rewrites ONE existing element) and `:769` (completeExecutionStep branch recording); `applyTacticalChange` (:578-586) and `supersedeExecutionPlan` (:531-569) are status-only `advanceStatus` calls. Yet propose runs only three ad-hoc validators (`rejectMalformedTransitionGraph` :128-151, `rejectDuplicateTransitionId` :157-176, `rejectMalformedTransitionCondition` :182-210) and ALL THREE are transition-scoped. Two structural consequences produce the whole family:

(1) THE STEP SET IS UNVALIDATED. Nothing at propose reads `stepState`, `id` uniqueness, `selectedTransitionId`, `executionPlanId`, or the array's emptiness. `ExecutionStepSchema` (`packages/rph-contracts/src/objects.ts:230-242`) accepts all ten `StepState` values because it is ALSO the persisted shape (`ExecutionPlanSchema.steps`, objects.ts:616) validated on every commit (`kit.ts:264 validateAgainst(entry.schema, args.nextState)`) — so the contract cannot carry the propose-time rule, and nobody wrote it anywhere else. Result: an authored step can be born in a state no command can leave (NOT_READY/READY — F-09/F-27), duplicated so only copy 0 is addressable (`advanceStep`'s `steps.findIndex`, execution.ts:636 — F-10), or born already terminal-success so `CompleteExecutionPlan`'s allow-list (:483-491) passes with ZERO work performed (the undisclosed fail-OPEN half found by F-09's test-evidence verifier).

(2) THE ONE STRUCTURAL VALIDATOR SHORT-CIRCUITS. `validateTransitionGraph` (`packages/rph-domain/src/transition-gate.ts:570`) returns `{ok:true}` on `transitions.length === 0`, and the other two validators loop over the same empty array — so a LINEAR plan receives literally no structural validation at all.

Underneath both sits one conceptual error: **the propose payload conflates AUTHORING input with RUNTIME state.** `stepState` and `selectedTransitionId` are facts the machine produces, not facts a proposer declares; the design (DS-004 §3/§6 D5) says steps "rest QUEUED — a convention this design relies on and ENFORCES", and nothing enforces it. The condition-grammar defects are the same error one layer down: `state: z.string()` (condition-grammar.ts:35) and `operands: z.array(...)` with no `.min(1)` (:55-56) admit expressions that are structurally unsatisfiable or vacuously true, so the guard system silently degrades — fail-CLOSED under a bare leaf, fail-OPEN under `NOT` (:134) and under `ALL([])` (:130).

### Why it survived
Four reinforcing reasons.

(a) NO HOME. There was no "propose contract" module and no propose-contract test file. The three validators were private functions in the handler, added one at a time by DWP-01/02, each scoped to the defect that motivated it. Nothing owned the question "what makes a plan structurally executable?", so nobody noticed the answer was only ever given for edges.

(b) THE SHORT-CIRCUIT HID THE HOLE. Every existing propose-rejection test (`execution-start-gate.test.ts:343, :349, :359, :369, :1007`) proposes a GRAPH. The one shape that gets zero validation — the linear plan — is also the shape every other suite uses, so the gap was invisible from both directions.

(c) THE TEST CORPUS DEPENDS ON THE HOLE. `activePlan(['SUCCEEDED','QUEUED'])`, `proposePlan(PLAN,PWU,['FAILED'])`, `mkStep(PLAN_A,S_FAILED,'FAILED')` — four helper functions across four suites seed arbitrary runtime states THROUGH propose because it is the cheapest arrangement mechanism. The absence of the rule had become load-bearing test infrastructure, which is exactly why nobody added the rule. `execution-start-gate.test.ts:6` even documents the convention in a comment ("steps stay seeded at QUEUED") while the file's own helper violates it.

(d) THE SHIPPED CALLERS MASK IT. `reference-undertaking.ts:553` and `+page.server.ts:528` hardcode `'QUEUED'`, so no production path ever exercised the gap. The one exception — `apps/rph-demo/e2e/execution-plan.e2e.ts:150`, which stages `mkStep(STEP3,'NOT_READY')` — did not catch it either: the e2e at :227-232 ASSERTS "no action at all" on that step, codifying the permanent deadlock as expected UI behaviour. The system's only witness testified for the defect.

(e) For the grammar half specifically: the sibling checks that DO exist prove the intent and disguise the gap. `rejectMalformedTransitionCondition` resolves every referenced `stepId` against the declared step set (:199-207) — the identical authoring-typo class — but never looks at `state`. `NumericComparatorSchema` is enum-bound and HAS a kill test (`condition-grammar.test.ts:46`); `state` is neither. And `execution-view.ts:301-309 describeCondition` LAUNDERS the typo: it prints "unparseable condition" only when `safeParse` fails, and a typo'd state parses — so the UI rendered `step …-s4 is SKIPED` as a legitimate guard, defeating its own stated contract (:298-299, "the UI must never present an uninterpretable guard as though it were understood").

### Fixes

**Findings Structural carrier for the whole family (no finding of its own). Enables F-09, F-10, F-22, F-27, F-33, F-39, F-41, F-43, F-44.**
- *rule:* ALL structural admissibility of an execution plan is decided at ProposeExecutionPlan, by ONE pure domain function that is never short-circuited on plan shape. A linear plan (transitions: []) is validated exactly as strictly as a graph plan for everything that is not about edges.
- *sites:* NEW FILE `packages/rph-domain/src/plan-proposal.ts`, exporting:
  • `export interface PlanProposalStep { readonly id?: unknown; readonly executionPlanId?: unknown; readonly stepType?: unknown; readonly stepState?: unknown; readonly selectedTransitionId?: unknown }` (a permissive read-model — the validator must be able to inspect a payload BEFORE trusting its types)
  • `export interface PlanProposalTransition { readonly id?: unknown; readonly executionPlanId?: unknown; readonly sourceStepId?: string; readonly targetStepId?: string; readonly transitionType?: unknown; readonly conditionExpression?: unknown }`
  • `export type PlanProposalResult = { readonly ok: true } | { readonly ok: false; readonly code: 'RPH_VALIDATION_SEMANTIC_FAILED' | 'RPH_VALIDATION_SCHEMA_FAILED'; readonly message: string }`
  • `export function validateProposedPlan(planId: string, steps: readonly PlanProposalStep[], transitions: readonly PlanProposalTransition[]): PlanProposalResult`
ADD `export * from './plan-proposal.js';` to `packages/rph-domain/src/index.ts` (after line 13).
DELETE from `packages/rph-application/src/handlers/execution.ts`: `rejectMalformedTransitionGraph` (:128-151), `rejectDuplicateTransitionId` (:157-176), `rejectMalformedTransitionCondition` (:182-210), and their three call sites at :250-255. `proposeExecutionPlan` (:240-278) keeps the work-unit-existence check (:242-249) and then calls the single validator.
UNCHANGED: `packages/rph-domain/src/transition-gate.ts` `validateTransitionGraph` (:566-589) is called as the LAST limb, byte-identical. No vocab file changes; no `bun run gen`.
- *mechanism:* `validateProposedPlan` runs limbs in this fixed order, returning on the first failure (so an author sees the most fundamental problem first):
  L1 `steps` non-empty                                          → SEMANTIC (FIX-3)
  L2 per-step, in array order: id well-formed; executionPlanId coherent; stepState at-rest; selectedTransitionId absent  → SEMANTIC (FIX-2, FIX-3, FIX-7)
  L3 step-id uniqueness                                          → SEMANTIC (FIX-3)
  L4 per-transition: id well-formed; executionPlanId coherent; CONDITIONAL⇔conditionExpression pairing → SEMANTIC (FIX-3, FIX-5)
  L5 transition-id uniqueness (relocated verbatim from rejectDuplicateTransitionId, SAME message)      → SEMANTIC
  L6 per-transition conditionExpression: `ConditionExpressionSchema.safeParse` (SCHEMA, same message) then `conditionStepRefs` resolution against the declared step ids (SEMANTIC, same message) — relocated verbatim from rejectMalformedTransitionCondition; FIX-4 and FIX-6 land inside the schema this limb calls
  L7 `validateTransitionGraph(steps.map(s => ({id, stepType})), transitions)` — UNCHANGED, still a no-op for a linear plan, which is now harmless because L1-L6 are unconditional → SEMANTIC, same message ('malformed transition graph — … (DR-004 DWP-01)').
The result type reuses the `code` field already DECLARED but never populated on `GraphValidationResult` (transition-gate.ts:410) — L7's `{ok:false,message}` is lifted to `code:'RPH_VALIDATION_SEMANTIC_FAILED'`. `proposeExecutionPlan` becomes a six-line adapter: `const v = validateProposedPlan(p.executionPlanId, p.steps, p.transitions); if (!v.ok) return reject(command, v.code, v.message, [p.executionPlanId]);`
Relocation is message-preserving: every existing message string and refusal code is carried across byte-for-byte, so the five existing propose-rejection tests stay green with zero edits.
- *killTest:* `packages/rph-application/src/handlers/execution-propose-contract.test.ts` (NEW — the single home of the propose-contract battery; its header states the governing rule: 'ProposeExecutionPlan is the only writer of steps[] and transitions[]; every field on ExecutionStep/ExecutionTransition that is RUNTIME state rather than authoring input is pinned HERE').
KT-0 «a LINEAR plan is still structurally validated»: propose `steps:[step('s1'),step('s1')], transitions: []` → expect REJECTED. MUTANT KILLED: moving any step-level limb inside `validateTransitionGraph`, or gating `validateProposedPlan` on `transitions.length > 0`, makes this ACCEPTED → RED. This is the anti-vacuity test for the CARRIER, independent of every individual rule.
KT-0b «relocation is message-preserving»: the five pre-existing rejection assertions (execution-start-gate.test.ts:343 'malformed', :349 'not a declared step', :359 non-BRANCH CONDITIONAL, :369 default-not-last, :1007 'declared more than once') must pass UNEDITED. If any needs editing, the relocation changed behaviour and is wrong.
- *behaviourChange:* None on its own. Pure relocation with identical codes and messages. The behaviour deltas are attributed to FIX-2..FIX-7.
- *risk:* LOW but ordering-sensitive. Land FIRST — every other fix in this family edits `plan-proposal.ts`, and landing them into the old three-validator shape would triple the merge cost. The entry-edge family (F-03/04/05) edits `liveStepIds` (transition-gate.ts:137-…) and possibly `validateTransitionGraph`'s entry counting; this design does not touch either, so the two are independent. Anyone editing `proposeExecutionPlan` for another family must rebase onto the adapter form.

**Findings F-09 (BLOCKER), F-27 (MAJOR), plus the UNDISCLOSED fail-OPEN half surfaced by F-09's test-evidence verifier (authored-SUCCEEDED completion bypass).**
- *rule:* A proposed step's `stepState` MUST be exactly `QUEUED` — the at-rest state DS-004 §3 declares the design 'relies on and ENFORCES' and DS-004 §10 BLOCKER(D5) records as the fix. All nine other `StepState` values are refused at propose with `RPH_VALIDATION_SEMANTIC_FAILED`. THE ANSWER TO (a): forbid authoring below QUEUED; do NOT mint the missing readying/scheduling commands.
- *sites:* `packages/rph-domain/src/plan-proposal.ts`:
  • `export const PROPOSED_STEP_REST_STATE = 'QUEUED' as const;`  (the DS-004 §3 convention, finally expressed as code with one home)
  • `function isProposableStepState(s: StepState): boolean` — an EXHAUSTIVE switch over the ten members of `StepStateSchema`, `case 'QUEUED': return true;` / nine explicit `return false;` / `default: return assertNever(s);`
  • imports `{ StepStateSchema, type StepState } from '@janumipwb/rph-contracts'`. LAYERING-LEGAL and verified: `packages/rph-domain/package.json` lists `"@janumipwb/rph-contracts": "workspace:*"` as a runtime dependency; `packages/rph-domain/src/stateMachine.ts:4` already imports from it; `.dependency-cruiser.cjs`'s `domain-purity` rule forbids only persistence/application/assurance/controller/projections/engine/pwa.
  • a module-private `assertNever` (three lines, mirroring the existing private one at `condition-grammar.ts:79`).
