NOT TOUCHED — and this is a decision, not an omission: `ExecutionStepSchema` (`packages/rph-contracts/src/objects.ts:230-242`), `ProposeExecutionPlanPayloadSchema` (`messages.ts:132-141`), and the vocab (`packages/rph-contracts/vocab/m3-commands-events.json` command[5], `m1-object-fields.json`). NO `bun run gen`.
RETAINED, now reclassified: the `NOT_READY → SKIPPED` arrow at `packages/rph-domain/src/transitions.data.ts:1469-1473` and its test `execution-start-gate.test.ts:773`.
- *mechanism:* WHY THE SCHEMA CANNOT CARRY THIS RULE. `ExecutionStepSchema` is not only the propose payload's element type — it IS the persisted step shape (`ExecutionPlanSchema.steps`, objects.ts:616), and `kit.ts:264` runs `validateAgainst(entry.schema, args.nextState, …)` on EVERY `commitState`. Pinning `stepState` to `QUEUED` there would make every single step advance fail to persist. That rules out the vocab/Zod route decisively; the rule is semantic and belongs in the domain validator.

WHY FORBID AUTHORING RATHER THAN ADD THE MISSING COMMAND — argued from the machine and the design intent:
 1. FROM THE MACHINE. `initialState: 'NOT_READY'` (transitions.data.ts:1435) describes where a step sits BEFORE something schedules it. The two commandless arrows are a coherent, named subsystem: `{NOT_READY→READY, trigger:'ExecutionStepReady', guard:'a step cannot run until preconditions are satisfied (§21.1)'}` (:1439-1443) and `{READY→QUEUED, trigger:'step scheduled'}` (:1444). That is a READYING/SCHEDULING subsystem, not two loose arrows. Its ratified inputs exist and are dead: `ExecutionStep.preconditions` (objects.ts:238, a `ConditionSchema[]` — a DIFFERENT grammar from the DWP-02 condition grammar), `stepMayBecomeReady` (`packages/rph-domain/src/execution.ts:129`, no production caller), and `ExecutionStepReady` as an event payload schema (`messages.ts:1140`, registered :2296) with no emitter anywhere in the repo.
 2. FROM DESIGN INTENT. Minting `MarkExecutionStepReady`/`ScheduleExecutionStep` inside a remediation would ship an operator button that ASSERTS readiness instead of ESTABLISHING it — its guard would be vacuous by construction, i.e. the exact CON-000 B7 defect this whole review exists to eliminate. Building the honest version (evaluate `preconditions` in a second grammar, emit `ExecutionStepReady`, define the scheduler, reconcile with the start-gate and the retry cap, add the two UI affordances) is a Tier-4 feature. FIX FORWARD means fix the defect forward, not build the unbuilt subsystem inside a defect fix.
 3. FROM THE SHIPPED SEMANTICS. Every real caller already authors QUEUED — `reference-undertaking.ts:553`, `apps/rph-demo/src/routes/undertakings/[id]/+page.server.ts:528`, `execution-flow.e2e.ts:49`, `execution-sequencing.e2e.ts:23`, `execution-tier3.e2e.ts:79`. The convention is universal; only its enforcement is missing. A proposed plan enters the system already scheduled, and that IS the shipped meaning of 'proposed'.
 4. ON THE NEW NOT_READY→SKIPPED ARROW. It is NOT the escape hatch for the reachable case and never was: `prunableStepIds` (transition-gate.ts:323-334) additionally requires `!live.has(s.id)`, so a NOT_READY step on the taken path is refused ('it is still reachable'). After this fix NEW plans cannot contain NOT_READY at all, so that arrow's ONLY remaining population is already-STORED plans. It must be RETAINED and its comment updated to say so — it is now precisely a legacy-recovery arrow, and deleting it as 'unreachable' would strand exactly the population this fix cannot reach.

THE SAME RULE CLOSES A FAIL-OPEN DEFECT THE REGISTER DID NOT RAISE AS ITS OWN FINDING. Propose `['SUCCEEDED','SUCCEEDED']`, Approve, Activate, `CompleteExecutionPlan` → ACCEPTED today (verified by F-09's test-evidence verifier), with no step ever started, no `ExecutionStepStarted`, no attempt, no result, and the §8.4 floor gate never consulted, because `completeExecutionPlan`'s allow-list (execution.ts:483-491) reads only the authored `stepState` strings. The at-rest rule kills the fabricated-success bypass and the deadlock with one guard — that is why the rule is 'exactly QUEUED' and not 'not below QUEUED'.

REFUSAL MESSAGE (exact): `ProposeExecutionPlan blocked: step "<id>" is authored at stepState "<value>"; a proposed step must rest at QUEUED (DS-004 §3, §6 D5). Below QUEUED (NOT_READY/READY) no command can drive it — the readying lifecycle is unbuilt — and at or beyond RUNNING the step would carry facts no execution attempt produced.`

REQUIRE, DO NOT NORMALIZE. Silently rewriting an authored `stepState` to QUEUED would be the same laundering pattern F-22 condemns in `describeCondition`: the author's plan would mean something different from what was stored, with no diagnostic. `stepState` is a REQUIRED field on the generated schema, so 'absent' is not a case.
- *killTest:* In `execution-propose-contract.test.ts`:
KT-1a «propose REJECTS a step authored NOT_READY (F-09/F-27 deadlock)» — `steps:[step(1,'QUEUED'), step(2,'NOT_READY')], transitions: []` → REJECTED / `RPH_VALIDATION_SEMANTIC_FAILED` / message contains 'must rest at QUEUED'. Kills deletion of the rule.
KT-1b «propose REJECTS an authored terminal-success step (the fabricated-success bypass)» — `['SUCCEEDED','SUCCEEDED']` → REJECTED; then a SECOND assertion that `CompleteExecutionPlan` was never reachable. Kills the mutant that refuses only below-QUEUED states — the single most likely weakening, because it 'fixes F-09' while reopening the fail-open half.
KT-1c «EXHAUSTIVE over the enum — the anti-vacuity core» — table-driven over all ten `StepStateSchema` values: `QUEUED` → ACCEPTED, the other nine → REJECTED with the same code. Kills EVERY narrowing mutant (any allow-list wider than {QUEUED}) and every over-refusal mutant (an allow-list that excludes QUEUED). No subset-of-the-enum guard can survive this.
KT-1d «the legitimate shape is still accepted» — the reference-undertaking step shape verbatim (single step, TRANSFORMATION, QUEUED, `transitions: []`) → ACCEPTED. The over-refusal proof.
KT-1e «the legacy population still has its exit» — using the LEGACY seeding fixture (FIX-8) place a stored plan with a NOT_READY step on a structurally dead arm, and assert Prune still drives it to SKIPPED. This is `execution-start-gate.test.ts:773` re-homed onto the seam; it keeps the NOT_READY→SKIPPED arrow non-vacuous after propose can no longer create its input.
COMPILE-TIME KILL TEST: `isProposableStepState`'s `default: assertNever(s)` makes adding an eleventh `StepState` to the vocab a `check-types` FAILURE until the author dispositions it. A guard that cannot silently fall out of date.
- *behaviourChange:* DELIBERATE REFUSALS — previously ACCEPTED at propose, now REJECTED:
  • any step authored `NOT_READY` or `READY`  → was a permanent deadlock (F-09/F-27)
  • any step authored `RUNNING` or `WAITING`  → was a live episode with no attempt, `attemptsMadeForStep` (execution.ts:880) at 0, retry cap unspent, floor gate never run
  • any step authored `SUCCEEDED` or `SKIPPED` → was the fabricated-success bypass of §20.1
  • any step authored `FAILED`, `CANCELLED` or `SUPERSEDED` → terminal non-success; permanently blocks CompleteExecutionPlan with no command able to move a terminal step
STILL ACCEPTED: every step authored `QUEUED` — i.e. every shipped caller, every e2e fixture except one, and the entire reference seed.
NOT AFFECTED: already-STORED plans. Propose-time validation gates only NEW proposals; nothing re-validates on load or at Activate (see the invariants). A stored plan with a NOT_READY step keeps its existing behaviour and its existing (prune-only, dead-arm-only) recovery path.
- *risk:* MEDIUM, and entirely in the TEST CORPUS, not in production. Four suites seed non-QUEUED states through propose and go RED without FIX-8: `execution-plan-completion.test.ts` (`activePlan` at :127,134,140,149,155,161,170,179), `execution-start-gate.test.ts` (`activePlan` at :209,216,221,232,241 + inline WAITING/RUNNING at :587,593 + the NOT_READY propose at :776), `dwp05-precondition-coverage.test.ts` (`proposePlan` × 12 at :142-298), `execution-plan-supersede.test.ts` (`mkStep(PLAN_A,S_FAILED,'FAILED')` at :127). Plus `apps/rph-demo/e2e/execution-plan.e2e.ts:150`. FIX-8 is NOT optional and NOT cosmetic: it is what makes this rule landable. Do not weaken the rule to keep the tests green — that inversion is precisely how the defect survived.

**Findings F-10 (BLOCKER)**
- *rule:* Every proposed step declares a non-empty, plan-coherent, plan-unique identity; a proposed plan declares at least one step. Concretely: (i) `steps.length >= 1`; (ii) every `step.id` is a string that is non-empty after trim; (iii) step ids are pairwise distinct within the plan; (iv) every `step.executionPlanId` equals the payload's `executionPlanId`; and the same (ii)+(iv) for `transitions[].id`/`executionPlanId`. All refuse with `RPH_VALIDATION_SEMANTIC_FAILED`.
- *sites:* `packages/rph-domain/src/plan-proposal.ts`, limbs L1 (non-empty steps), L2 (per-step id/planId), L3 (uniqueness), L4/L5 (per-transition id/planId, uniqueness relocated). The addressing collapse this defends is at `packages/rph-application/src/handlers/execution.ts:636` (`const idx = steps.findIndex((s) => String(s.id) === args.stepId);` — first match only) and :657 (`steps.map((s,i) => i === idx ? nextStep : s)`); the counting side is `completeExecutionPlan`'s filter at :483-485. `buildConditionSubject` (`condition-grammar.ts:213-215`) collapses duplicates too, since its accumulator is a `Map` keyed by step id — a second, independent reason ids must be unique.
- *mechanism:* F-10's mechanism verified in code: a duplicate step id makes copy 1 addressable by NO command (every step command funnels through `advanceStep`'s single `findIndex`) while `completeExecutionPlan` still COUNTS it, so COMPLETED becomes permanently unreachable. The precedent is decisive and already in the file: `rejectDuplicateTransitionId` spends a propose-time `RPH_VALIDATION_SEMANTIC_FAILED` on the IDENTICAL hazard one field over, for a strictly WEAKER consequence (an ambiguous BRANCH render), and it has a kill test (`execution-start-gate.test.ts:1007`). The step-id analogue had neither guard nor test. Message, mirroring the sibling verbatim in form: `ProposeExecutionPlan blocked: step id "<id>" is declared more than once — step ids must be unique (every step command addresses its step by id, and a duplicate is unaddressable).`

WHY NOT A ZOD `.superRefine` ON THE PAYLOAD: `ProposeExecutionPlanPayloadSchema` is GENERATED (`packages/rph-contracts/vocab/m3-commands-events.json`, `steps: 'ExecutionStep[]'` → `gen-messages.ts:141` emits `z.array(ExecutionStepSchema)`); the generator has no vocabulary for refinements, so carrying it there means a generator feature. Worse, it would emit `RPH_VALIDATION_SCHEMA_FAILED`, contradicting the established convention for step-set semantics (the transition-id sibling emits SEMANTIC). Domain semantic layer, decided.

WHY `steps.length >= 1` BELONGS HERE: `completeExecutionPlan` ALREADY refuses an empty plan (`execution.ts:477-483`, `RPH_INVARIANT_VIOLATION`, 'an empty plan cannot be COMPLETED'). Today a zero-step plan can be proposed, approved and activated and can only ever be Failed/Cancelled/Superseded — the same deadlock shape, one layer earlier. Refusing at propose is the systemic placement; the completion guard STAYS as defence-in-depth for stored empty plans.

WHY `executionPlanId` COHERENCE: a step or edge declaring a foreign plan id is persisted verbatim and mis-groups in the read-model. Cheap, fail-closed, and verified over-refusal-free — every fixture in the repo sets it correctly (`reference-undertaking.ts:544`, `+page.server.ts:520`, `execution-flow.e2e.ts:41,56`, `execution-sequencing.e2e.ts:16`, and the `step()`/`gedge()`/`cedge()` helpers in every application suite).
- *killTest:* In `execution-propose-contract.test.ts`:
KT-2a «propose REJECTS a duplicate step id on a LINEAR plan (F-10)» — `steps:[step('s1'),step('s1')], transitions: []` → REJECTED / SEMANTIC / 'declared more than once'. The `transitions: []` is load-bearing: it kills the mutant of implementing the rule inside `validateTransitionGraph`, which short-circuits on the empty array (transition-gate.ts:570). This is also KT-0.
KT-2b «propose REJECTS an empty or whitespace step id» — `id: ''` and `id: '   '` → REJECTED.
KT-2c «propose REJECTS an empty transition id» — closes the sibling gap (`z.string()` accepts '', and `rejectDuplicateTransitionId`'s `if (id === undefined) continue` never looked at emptiness).
KT-2d «propose REJECTS a plan with no steps» — `steps: []` → REJECTED / SEMANTIC.
KT-2e «propose REJECTS a step whose executionPlanId names a different plan».
KT-2f (over-refusal proof) «two steps with distinct ids on a linear plan are ACCEPTED» — plus the whole existing corpus.
- *behaviourChange:* PREVIOUSLY ACCEPTED, NOW REFUSED: duplicate step ids; empty/whitespace step or transition ids; a zero-step plan; a step/edge naming a foreign executionPlanId. STILL ACCEPTED: everything the repo actually authors. ONE TEST MIGRATES: `execution-plan-completion.test.ts:140` (`activePlan([])`, which asserts CompleteExecutionPlan refuses an empty plan) must build its empty plan through the FIX-8 seam — the completion guard remains covered, from the stored-plan direction it now exclusively serves.
- *risk:* LOW. No in-repo caller produces any refused shape (the demo mints ids via `mintUiId`; the seed uses one step). The only migration is the single empty-plan test.

**Findings F-22 (MAJOR), F-39 (MINOR — its code-semantics verifier raised it to MAJOR for parity)**
- *rule:* `STEP_STATE.state` is the ratified `StepState` enum, not a free string. An off-contract value is refused wherever the grammar is parsed — at propose (`RPH_VALIDATION_SCHEMA_FAILED`), at the runtime guard evaluator (guard evaluates FALSE), and in the read-model (rendered as 'unparseable condition').
- *sites:* `packages/rph-domain/src/condition-grammar.ts`:
  • line 35 — `z.strictObject({ op: z.literal('STEP_STATE'), stepId: z.string(), state: z.string() })` becomes `state: StepStateSchema`.
  • line 22 — the `ConditionExpression` union member becomes `| { op: 'STEP_STATE'; stepId: string; state: StepState }`.
  • add `import { StepStateSchema, type StepState } from '@janumipwb/rph-contracts';` (the file currently imports only `zod`).
  • the evaluator at :119-120 (`step?.stepState === expr.state`) is UNCHANGED — `string | undefined === StepState` compiles and means the same thing.
SOURCE OF THE ENUM: `packages/rph-contracts/src/enums.ts:699-710` (`StepStateSchema`, the ten values), the SAME schema bound to this field on every step (`objects.ts:240`) and every step event payload (`messages.ts:1123-1197`).
NO REGENERATION: `condition-grammar.ts` is explicitly HAND-AUTHORED, not vocab-generated — its own header (lines 5-8) records why ('the object-schema generator cannot emit a discriminated union / recursive helper … THIS module is its typed interpretation layer, DR-004 §19-B1'). Editing it is the sanctioned path.
- *mechanism:* PLACEMENT IS THE WHOLE FIX. Both findings suggest a handler-side check inside `rejectMalformedTransitionCondition`. That is the WRONG layer and would leave the worst half open. `ConditionExpressionSchema` is `safeParse`d by THREE planes:
  1. propose — `execution.ts:190` (relocated to plan-proposal L6)
  2. the runtime guard evaluator — `execution.ts:119-120`, `return parsed.success && evaluateCondition(parsed.data, subject)`
  3. the read-model — `execution-view.ts:261-262` (the UI's mirror of the gate) and `:301` (`describeCondition`)
Binding the enum in the schema fixes all three with one edit; a handler check fixes only (1) and leaves every ALREADY-STORED plan fail-OPEN. That retroactive reach is the reason to choose the schema.

THE FAIL-OPEN DIRECTION IS THE POINT. A bare typo'd leaf is merely permanently false — fail-closed and dead. Under `NOT` (condition-grammar.ts:134, `return !evaluateCondition(expr.operand, subject)`) the same typo is unconditionally TRUE, so the guarded arm is taken unconditionally, `resolveBranchSelection` RECORDS it as `selectedTransitionId` (execution.ts:762-779), and DWP-09's resolved-once rule (`selectBranchEdge`, transition-gate.ts:109-114) makes the wrong decision permanent and unrecoverable. F-22's verifier reproduced exactly this end-to-end: one missing 'P' in 'SKIPPED' flipped which arm ran. After the fix, a stored typo'd expression fails `safeParse`, so the WHOLE expression — negation included — yields `false`. The fail-open case closes to fail-closed. That is the correct direction and the fix's most valuable property.

THE READ-MODEL LAUNDERING ALSO STOPS, for free: `describeCondition` (`execution-view.ts:298-309`) prints 'unparseable condition' only when `safeParse` fails; a typo'd state parsed, so it rendered `step …-s4 is SKIPED` as a legitimate guard, defeating its own stated contract. After the fix it renders honestly.
- *killTest:* THREE LAYERS, because the mutant has three places to hide:
KT-3a (grammar) `packages/rph-domain/src/condition-grammar.test.ts`, beside the existing bad-comparator case at :46: «rejects an off-contract state value» — `safeParse({op:'STEP_STATE',stepId:'s1',state:'SKIPED'}).success === false` and the same for `'NOT_A_STATE'`. Kills reverting line 35 to `z.string()`.
KT-3b (grammar, over-refusal proof) «every ratified StepState parses» — table-driven over all ten `StepStateSchema` values, each `.success === true`. Kills a fat-fingered or narrowed enum.
KT-3c (propose) in `execution-propose-contract.test.ts`: «propose REJECTS a BRANCH arm whose STEP_STATE names a non-enum state» → REJECTED / `RPH_VALIDATION_SCHEMA_FAILED`.
KT-3d (THE FAIL-OPEN KILL TEST — the one that matters) «a NOT-negated off-contract STEP_STATE never becomes an always-true arm»: rebuild F-22's fixture (`execution-start-gate.test.ts:924-954 activeLateFlipPlan`) with the guard `{op:'NOT',operand:{op:'STEP_STATE',stepId:s4,state:'SKIPED'}}` and assert propose REJECTED. Under the mutant (revert to `z.string()`) propose is ACCEPTED, the vacuous arm is selected and `start(s2)` succeeds → RED. This is the exact behaviour the register reproduced.
KT-3e (retroactive/stored plans) in `packages/rph-projections/src/execution-view.test.ts` beside :477: «a STORED guard naming an off-contract state renders as 'unparseable condition' and its arm is NOT satisfied» — built by constructing the plan read-model directly (no propose). Kills the mutant 'validate only at propose, leave the schema loose', which would satisfy KT-3c while leaving stored plans fail-open.
- *behaviourChange:* NEW PROPOSALS: a transition whose condition names a state outside the ten-value enum is now REFUSED (`RPH_VALIDATION_SCHEMA_FAILED`). Every correctly-spelled guard is unaffected — verified: every `STEP_STATE` occurrence in the repo uses a legal value (`condition-grammar.test.ts:30,59,60,80,120`; `execution-start-gate.test.ts:937`; `execution-view.test.ts:477`).
STORED PLANS — a real, deliberate, fail-CLOSED behaviour change: an existing guard with an off-contract state flips from (bare) false → false [no change] and from (under NOT) TRUE → FALSE [the fail-open arm closes]. A BRANCH that ALREADY recorded `selectedTransitionId` is untouched, because `selectBranchEdge` returns the recorded edge without evaluating any guard (transition-gate.ts:109-114) — so this fix CANNOT retroactively re-resolve a settled branch. Only an UNRESOLVED branch whose winning arm was a typo'd negation changes, and no such plan exists in this repo.
- *risk:* LOW. Two-line schema change; the type widening is source-compatible. The one thing to watch: `ConditionExpression`'s STEP_STATE member becomes `StepState` rather than `string`, so any test constructing an expression with an arbitrary string literal now needs `as ConditionExpression` or a legal value — grep confirms none exist.

**Findings F-33 (MINOR)**
- *rule:* DS-004 §6 D2's pairing is BICONDITIONAL and is now enforced: an edge is `CONDITIONAL` **iff** it carries a `conditionExpression`. A `CONDITIONAL` edge with no expression, and a `SEQUENTIAL` edge that carries one, are both refused at propose with `RPH_VALIDATION_SEMANTIC_FAILED`.
- *sites:* `packages/rph-domain/src/plan-proposal.ts` limb L4, evaluated BEFORE the condition-grammar limb (L6) — because today's `if (t.conditionExpression === undefined) continue` (execution.ts:189) is exactly what let the guardless CONDITIONAL edge through untouched.
The two disagreeing planes this reconciles: `isConditionalEdge` (`transition-gate.ts:88-89`) is an OR (`conditionExpression !== undefined || transitionType === 'CONDITIONAL'`) so the edge IS conditional for SELECTION; `isDefaultEdge` inside `checkBranchDefaults` (`transition-gate.ts:523-524`) is the AND-negation so the edge is NOT the default for VALIDATION. Both stay as written — they remain the correct fail-closed readings for STORED plans; the propose rule makes them provably complementary for NEW ones.
The runtime consequence being closed: `guardEvaluatorFor` (`execution.ts:113-121`) does `ConditionExpressionSchema.safeParse(undefined)` → failure → `false`, so the arm is unconditionally dead on every run.
- *mechanism:* This is the same class the codebase already legislates against for HALF-EDGES — `checkDanglingIds` (transition-gate.ts:464-471) rejects an edge with no target with the stated rationale 'reject it rather than let the two planes disagree'. A guardless CONDITIONAL edge is that defect with the other field. It passes `checkBranchDefaults` (counted as a guarded arm, so the SEQUENTIAL default is still sole and last → `{ok:true}`), is rendered by the read-model as role 'CONDITIONAL' with no condition text (`execution-view.ts:355-358`), and is unreachable on 100% of runs. The only feedback the author ever gets ACTIVELY MISDIRECTS: the start refusal says the arm 'should be pruned' (transition-gate.ts:397), pointing them at deleting the arm rather than at the missing expression.

BOTH DIRECTIONS ARE REFUSED, and the reverse direction is a deliberate choice. `SEQUENTIAL` + expression is not a plane disagreement (both read it as guarded) but the tag lies about the edge, and from a non-BRANCH source it is ALREADY refused today via `isConditionalEdge`'s OR inside `checkBranchDefaults`. Refusing it at propose makes D2 exact, removes the last way a single field can mean two things, and costs nothing: no fixture in the repo authors it (`condEdge`/`cedge` always pair the two; `seqEdge`/`gedge` never carry an expression).

WHY NOT A SCHEMA REFINEMENT: `ExecutionTransitionSchema` (`objects.ts:243-249`, `conditionExpression: z.unknown().optional()`, `transitionType: TransitionTypeSchema`) is — like `ExecutionStepSchema` — the PERSISTED shape, revalidated on every `commitState` via `kit.ts:264`. A `.superRefine` pairing rule there would make every already-stored mismatched plan unloadable and unadvanceable. Semantic layer, decided.

MESSAGES: `ProposeExecutionPlan blocked: transition "<id>" is typed CONDITIONAL but declares no conditionExpression — a guarded arm with no guard is unreachable on every run (DS-004 §6 D2: an edge is CONDITIONAL iff it carries a conditionExpression).` and `ProposeExecutionPlan blocked: transition "<id>" is typed SEQUENTIAL but carries a conditionExpression — retype it CONDITIONAL or remove the expression (DS-004 §6 D2).`
- *killTest:* In `execution-propose-contract.test.ts`, alongside the relocated `:343-373` battery:
KT-4a «propose REJECTS a CONDITIONAL edge with no conditionExpression (F-33)» — BRANCH s1 with `[{s1→s2, transitionType:'CONDITIONAL'} (expression omitted), {s1→s3, SEQUENTIAL} last]` → REJECTED / SEMANTIC / 'no conditionExpression'. Kills restoring the `continue`-before-check.
KT-4b «propose REJECTS a SEQUENTIAL edge carrying a conditionExpression» — kills the one-directional mutant that would satisfy KT-4a alone.
KT-4c (over-refusal proof) «a CONDITIONAL edge WITH an expression and a bare SEQUENTIAL default are both ACCEPTED» — i.e. the entire existing branch corpus (`execution-start-gate.test.ts:303-318, 401-419, 924-954`; `transition-gate.test.ts:119,178,304,313,326`; `execution-flow.e2e.ts:63-66`) stays green unedited.
- *behaviourChange:* PREVIOUSLY ACCEPTED, NOW REFUSED: `transitionType:'CONDITIONAL'` with `conditionExpression` omitted; `transitionType:'SEQUENTIAL'` with `conditionExpression` present. STILL ACCEPTED: the paired forms, which is every edge the repo authors. STORED PLANS: unchanged — the runtime keeps its existing fail-closed readings (`guardEvaluatorFor` returns false for an unparseable/absent expression; `isConditionalEdge`'s OR keeps a guardless CONDITIONAL edge out of the default slot).
- *risk:* LOW. Grep-verified: no fixture, seed, demo route or e2e authors either mismatched shape.

**Findings F-41, F-43, F-44 (all PLAUSIBLE) — DISPOSITION: FIX, narrowly, with an explicit scope statement. Both code-semantics verifiers argued the finding overreaches; both test-evidence verifiers confirmed a real, total coverage gap. Both are right, and the fix must be sized to the part that is right.**
- *rule:* An empty operand list is refused by the grammar: `ALL` and `ANY` each require at least one operand (`.min(1)`). An empty combinator is an authoring or serialization slip, not an authored constant, and the grammar refuses to guess its polarity. Refusal at propose is `RPH_VALIDATION_SCHEMA_FAILED`. THE GRAMMAR DOES NOT AND WILL NOT ATTEMPT TO REFUSE TAUTOLOGIES IN GENERAL — that is a disclosed residual, not a gap.
- *sites:* `packages/rph-domain/src/condition-grammar.ts:55-56` — `operands: z.array(ConditionExpressionSchema)` becomes `operands: z.array(ConditionExpressionSchema).min(1)` for BOTH the `ALL` and the `ANY` members, with a comment recording the reasoning and the residual.
`evaluateCondition`'s fold at :129-132 (`.every` / `.some`) is UNCHANGED — see mechanism.
NOT CHANGED, deliberately: the ratified sibling DSL `packages/rph-assurance/src/applicability.ts:52-54` uses the identical `.every`/`.some` fold with no `.min(1)`. It is a DIFFERENT surface (DOC-007 §18 applicability), out of this family's scope; noted as an openQuestion rather than silently diverged.
- *mechanism:* WHAT THE VERIFIERS ESTABLISHED, and how the fix respects it. (i) `ALL([])===true` / `ANY([])===false` are the correct identity elements — this is NOT a polarity bug, and the fix does not 'correct' the fold. (ii) `.min(1)` does NOT close the constant-true-guard class: `{op:'ATTEMPTS',stepId:s1,cmp:'>=',value:0}` and `{op:'OUTPUT_COUNT',…,'>=',0}` are unconditionally true against any subject (the `?? 0` floors at :124/:126 make the counts non-negative by construction) and produce a byte-identical outcome; three separate probes confirmed it. Detecting tautologies is undecidable in general, and a partial syntactic detector that catches `>= 0` but not `> -1` would itself be a vacuity magnet — exactly the guard-shaped-hole this review exists to eliminate. So the fix is scoped to the ONE shape that is unambiguously an authoring slip.

WHAT REMAINS TRUE AND IS WORTH THE `.min(1)`: (a) an empty combinator is a construction slip (an author or a generator built the operand array and never pushed into it), and today it silently becomes 'always take this arm' — the RECORDED, permanent decision (`selectedTransitionId`), with the genuinely-true arm and the mandated LAST default both neutralized and pruned to SKIPPED (F-43's probe observed exactly that three-way degeneration); (b) it is an UNKILLED MUTANT in the precise CON-000 B7 sense — `grep 'operands: \[\]'` returns ZERO hits repo-wide, so `expr.operands.length > 0 && expr.operands.every(...)` differs from the shipped code on exactly one input that no test supplies. The suite cannot tell the two apart.

WHY THE SCHEMA AND NOT THE EVALUATOR. Changing `evaluateCondition`'s `ALL` case to `operands.length > 0 && …` would create a SECOND source of truth for what an empty conjunction means and would make the fold mathematically wrong. The schema is the single home, and — as with FIX-4 — it is read by all three planes, so a STORED empty-ALL guard now fails `safeParse` at the gate (`execution.ts:119-120`) and in the read-model (`execution-view.ts:261-262`) and yields `false`. Fail-OPEN → fail-CLOSED, retroactively, with one edit. The fold stays honest and becomes unreachable-with-empty through every production path (verified: the ONLY callers of `evaluateCondition` are `execution.ts:120` and `execution-view.ts:262`, and both `safeParse` first).

DISCLOSED RESIDUAL, to be recorded in the remediation's residual register: a constant-true guard remains expressible (`ATTEMPTS >= 0`, `OUTPUT_COUNT >= 0`, `NOT(NOT(x))`, `STEP_SUCCEEDED` on the branch's own settled source) and is NOT refused. That is authored branch logic, and `checkBranchDefaults`'s contract is that first-match always RESOLVES (transition-gate.ts:538) — not that the default is ever TAKEN. A never-taken default is a declared fallback, not dead code.
- *killTest:* KT-5a (grammar) `packages/rph-domain/src/condition-grammar.test.ts`, beside :46: «an empty combinator is refused» — `safeParse({op:'ALL',operands:[]}).success === false` AND `safeParse({op:'ANY',operands:[]}).success === false`. Kills removing either `.min(1)`.
KT-5b (grammar, over-refusal proof) «arity-1 and arity-n combinators still parse» — pins that `.min(1)` is 1 and not 2.
KT-5c (propose) in `execution-propose-contract.test.ts`: «propose REJECTS a BRANCH arm guarded by an empty combinator» → REJECTED / `RPH_VALIDATION_SCHEMA_FAILED`; and the second route, `{op:'NOT',operand:{op:'ANY',operands:[]}}` → also REJECTED. Kills a fix applied to `ALL` alone.
KT-5d (retroactive/stored — THE fail-open kill test) in `packages/rph-domain/src/transition-gate.test.ts`: a `GatePlan` carrying a STORED CONDITIONAL edge whose expression is `{op:'ALL',operands:[]}`, with a SEQUENTIAL default last, resolves to the DEFAULT and leaves the vacuous arm unsatisfied. RED today (the vacuous arm wins first-match). Kills the mutant 'reject at propose only', which would leave every stored plan fail-open.
KT-5e (fold-identity pin, labelled honestly as such and NOT as this guard's kill test) in `condition-grammar.test.ts`: `evaluateCondition({op:'ALL',operands:[]} as ConditionExpression, {steps:{}}) === true` and `ANY === false`, with a comment stating this input is unreachable through the schema and is pinned solely so a `.every`↔`.some` swap cannot pass silently. This closes the B7 hole the test-evidence verifiers identified.
- *behaviourChange:* NEW PROPOSALS: a guard containing `{op:'ALL',operands:[]}` or `{op:'ANY',operands:[]}` at any depth is now REFUSED. No fixture, e2e spec, seed or demo route in the repo constructs one (grep-verified: `operands: []` appears NOWHERE outside deleted probe files), so nothing legitimate is refused.
STORED PLANS — a deliberate fail-CLOSED change: a stored empty `ALL` guard flips from TRUE to FALSE (arm no longer taken); a stored empty `ANY` stays FALSE; `NOT(ANY([]))` flips from TRUE to FALSE. As in FIX-4, a BRANCH that already RECORDED its selection is untouched. No such stored plan exists in this repo.
NOT CHANGED, and stated for the record: constant-true guards expressed any other way are still accepted and still take their arm.
- *risk:* LOW, plus one governance point. These three findings are PLAUSIBLE, and two verifiers argued for NONE. The disposition must be recorded explicitly in the remediation record as 'fixed narrowly, on the anti-vacuity ground, NOT on the fail-open-guard ground the raisers claimed' — otherwise the fix reads as endorsing an overreaching framing, and the next reviewer will infer that tautology detection was attempted and failed.

**Findings No register finding — an EXTENSION found while verifying this family's root cause. Same mechanism as F-09/F-27 (an authored runtime field), and a systemic fix must close it or the root cause is only half fixed.**
- *rule:* A proposed step MUST NOT carry `selectedTransitionId`. A BRANCH's arm selection is a fact the machine records when the branch settles; a proposer declaring it asserts a decision that never happened. Refused at propose with `RPH_VALIDATION_SEMANTIC_FAILED`.
- *sites:* `packages/rph-domain/src/plan-proposal.ts` limb L2, adjacent to the at-rest `stepState` rule (same rule family, same message shape). The field is `ExecutionStepSchema.selectedTransitionId: z.string().optional()` (`packages/rph-contracts/src/objects.ts:241`) — required-optional on the PERSISTED shape (the machine writes it at `execution.ts:762-779`), so again the schema cannot carry the propose-time rule.
- *mechanism:* `selectBranchEdge` (`transition-gate.ts:105-114`) short-circuits on a recorded decision: `if (source?.selectedTransitionId !== undefined) { const recorded = outEdges.find(e => e.id === source.selectedTransitionId); return recorded; }` — and when the recorded id matches no out-edge it deliberately returns `undefined` ('select NOTHING rather than silently falling back'). So an authored `selectedTransitionId` has two failure modes at propose time, both silent: (i) matching an out-edge → the BRANCH is pre-decided before it ever runs, and DWP-09's resolved-once rule makes it permanent; (ii) matching nothing → `selectBranchEdge` returns undefined, every in-edge downstream of that branch is NEUTRALIZED, and the entire subtree is structurally dead from birth. Both are the F-09/F-27 shape exactly: an authored runtime fact the machine can never correct. Refusing it costs nothing — no caller in the repo sets it at propose.
MESSAGE: `ProposeExecutionPlan blocked: step "<id>" is authored with selectedTransitionId "<v>" — a BRANCH's arm selection is recorded by the engine when the branch settles (DWP-09), never declared by a proposer.`
- *killTest:* KT-7a in `execution-propose-contract.test.ts`: «propose REJECTS a step carrying selectedTransitionId» — two cases, one where the id matches a declared out-edge (the pre-decided branch) and one where it matches nothing (the born-dead subtree) → both REJECTED / SEMANTIC. Kills deletion of the rule and, via the second case, kills a mutant that only refuses ids matching no edge.
KT-7b (over-refusal proof) «a step with the field ABSENT is ACCEPTED, and the engine still records a selection when the branch completes» — reuse `execution-start-gate.test.ts:401-419`, which already asserts the recorded selection. Pins that the rule constrains PROPOSE, not the machine.
- *behaviourChange:* PREVIOUSLY ACCEPTED, NOW REFUSED: any proposed step carrying `selectedTransitionId`. STILL ACCEPTED: every step in the repo (grep-verified: no propose call site anywhere sets the field; it is written only by `completeExecutionStep`'s `mutateStep` at execution.ts:762-779). STORED PLANS: unaffected — a recorded selection stays authoritative, which is the DWP-09 rule.
- *risk:* VERY LOW. Zero in-repo callers. Worth flagging to the parent as an out-of-register addition so it is dispositioned rather than absorbed silently.

**Findings Enabling change for FIX-2 and FIX-3. Not optional: without it the at-rest rule cannot land without turning ~35 existing tests red, and the temptation would be to weaken the rule instead — the exact inversion that produced the family.**
- *rule:* Test fixtures arrange step states through a NAMED, explicitly-bypassing seeding seam, never through the ProposeExecutionPlan contract. The seam may only produce states the command bus itself can produce; the one exception (below-QUEUED) is a separately named legacy-only fixture whose docstring says why it exists.
- *sites:* NEW FILE `packages/rph-application/src/handlers/__tests__/plan-fixtures.ts` — the established home for test-only fixtures (precedent: `__tests__/floor-fixtures.ts`, whose header records the pattern: 'Not in kit.ts: that is production. __tests__/ is excluded from tsconfig.build and does not match vitest's *.test.ts include, so this compiles with the tests and ships nowhere').
Exports:
  • `seedStepStates(store: StorageAdapter, planId: string, states: readonly string[]): void` — loads the plan via `store.loadObject`, rewrites `steps[i].stepState` positionally, and writes back via `store.commit({ aggregateType:'EXECUTION_PLAN', aggregateId: planId, objectType:'EXECUTION_PLAN', expectedRevision: loaded.revision, newRevision: loaded.revision + 1, newSemanticVersion: loaded.semanticVersion, currentState: patched, events: [], receipt: <synthetic, commandType 'TEST_FIXTURE_SeedStepStates'> })` (`packages/rph-ports/src/ports/storage.ts:31-53`). THROWS on `NOT_READY`/`READY`.
  • `seedBelowQueuedStepState_LEGACY_ONLY(store, planId, index, state: 'NOT_READY' | 'READY'): void` — the single, loudly-named exception.
  • `seedEmptyStepArray_LEGACY_ONLY(store, planId): void` — for the one empty-plan completion test.
MIGRATED CALL SITES (exhaustive census, four suites + four helpers):
  • `execution-start-gate.test.ts` — helpers `activePlan` (:160-…) and `proposeGraph` (:118-…) propose all-QUEUED then seed; non-QUEUED seeds at :209, :216, :221, :232, :241; inline WAITING/RUNNING proposes at :587, :593; the NOT_READY graph propose at :776 uses the LEGACY fixture.
  • `execution-plan-completion.test.ts` — helper `activePlan`; seeds at :127, :134, :140 (empty → LEGACY empty fixture), :149, :155, :161, :170, :179.
  • `dwp05-precondition-coverage.test.ts` — helper `proposePlan(planId, pwuId, stepStates)` (:99-113); twelve call sites :142-:298.
  • `execution-plan-supersede.test.ts` — `mkStep(PLAN_A, S_FAILED, 'FAILED')` at :127.
  • `apps/rph-demo/e2e/execution-plan.e2e.ts:150` — see mechanism (the e2e has no store access).
- *mechanism:* WHY A SEAM AND NOT A COMMAND-DRIVEN ARRANGEMENT. Driving each predecessor to its target state by command would couple ~35 gate/completion tests to the very handlers under test and would triple their length. The tests are exercising the GATE against an arrangement, not the propose contract; a fixture that arranges state directly preserves each test's intent exactly.

WHY THE SEAM IS NOT A FABRICATION (the `floor-fixtures.ts` lesson). `floor-fixtures.ts` exists because tests were rehearsing a shape the wire could never carry. That is the opposite of this: every state `seedStepStates` may produce IS reachable through the bus (Start/Complete/Fail/Skip/Cancel/Supersede drive them). The seam is a SHORTCUT for a reachable arrangement, not a fabrication of an unreachable one — which is exactly why it must REFUSE `NOT_READY`/`READY`, the two states the bus genuinely cannot reach. Without that refusal the seam would quietly become the back door that reinstates the defect.

WHY A SEPARATE LEGACY FIXTURE. After FIX-2, below-QUEUED steps can exist only in ALREADY-STORED plans. That population still needs its recovery path covered — specifically the NOT_READY→SKIPPED prune arrow (`transitions.data.ts:1469-1473`) and its regression test (`execution-start-gate.test.ts:773`), which would otherwise become an unreachable, untestable, and eventually deleted guard. The loud name (`_LEGACY_ONLY`) plus a docstring stating 'this arranges the population ProposeExecutionPlan can no longer create; it is not a general back door' keeps the exception legible and stops it spreading.

THE E2E. `apps/rph-demo/e2e/execution-plan.e2e.ts` drives HTTP and cannot reach the store, and its NOT_READY fixture (:150) becomes unstageable. Migration: (i) change the fixture to `[QUEUED, QUEUED, QUEUED]`; (ii) DELETE the three 'no action on step3' assertions at :227-232; (iii) ADD a new e2e asserting the command endpoint REJECTS a propose carrying `stepState:'NOT_READY'`. COVERAGE DELTA, stated honestly: we lose one end-to-end proof that the below-queued affordance renders honestly, and we gain one end-to-end proof that the deadlock cannot be created. The lost half is retained at unit level and is UNAFFECTED by this family, because it builds the plan read-model directly rather than through propose — `packages/rph-projections/src/execution-view.test.ts:92-96` (no advance command for NOT_READY/READY), `:129-132` (no control command), `:169-178` (`isBelowQueued`). Given fix-forward, refusing to create the deadlock is the stronger e2e.
- *killTest:* KT-8a in `plan-fixtures.test.ts` (NEW): «the seam refuses below-QUEUED» — `seedStepStates(store, PLAN, ['NOT_READY'])` THROWS. Kills the mutant that turns the fixture into a general back door and thereby silently restores the pre-fix test corpus.
KT-8b «the seam does not launder the propose contract» — after `activePlan(['SUCCEEDED','QUEUED'])` (propose-QUEUED-then-seed), assert the propose command itself was dispatched with all-QUEUED steps. Pins that migrated helpers really do go through the tightened contract rather than around it.
KT-8c (systemic, the real proof) — the FULL gate stays green after migration: `bun run test` (turbo, all packages, including the 69 rph-engine tests) plus `bunx playwright test` in `apps/rph-demo`. Per the standing rule, vitest alone is not green.
- *behaviourChange:* Test-infrastructure only. No production code path changes. Every migrated test asserts exactly what it asserted before; only its arrangement mechanism changes.
- *risk:* MEDIUM effort, LOW correctness risk, but it is the schedule driver for the whole family. Two specific traps: (1) `store.commit` bypasses `kit.ts:264`'s `validateAgainst`, so the fixture must produce a shape-legal plan itself (it does — only enum-legal `stepState` values change); (2) the fixture must patch AFTER Approve+Activate in each helper, or the activation guard sees the seeded states. Engine note: run these suites under Node/vitest, not Bun — better-sqlite3 refuses the Bun runtime (`node ../../node_modules/vitest/vitest.mjs run <file>`).

### Invariants
- INV-PROPOSE-1 (single decision point). ProposeExecutionPlan is the only command that introduces steps or transitions; therefore a plan's structural admissibility is decided exactly once, at propose, by `validateProposedPlan`. Verified exhaustively against every `steps:` write in `packages/rph-application/src/handlers/execution.ts` (:263 propose, :659 advanceStep, :769 branch recording) — `applyTacticalChange` and `supersedeExecutionPlan` are status-only.
- INV-PROPOSE-2 (no shape escapes validation). No limb of propose-time validation is conditional on plan shape. A linear plan (`transitions: []`) is validated exactly as strictly as a graph plan for everything that is not about edges. `validateTransitionGraph`'s `transitions.length === 0` short-circuit survives, but is now the LAST limb and therefore no longer means 'unvalidated'.
- INV-PROPOSE-3 (authoring input ≠ runtime state). A proposed step declares identity, type, purpose and bindings. It does NOT declare state: `stepState` must be exactly `QUEUED` (the DS-004 §3 at-rest convention, now enforced where the doc says it is enforced) and `selectedTransitionId` must be absent. Every field on `ExecutionStep` that the machine writes is pinned at propose.
- INV-PROPOSE-4 (every proposed plan is executable in principle). At least one step; every step id non-empty, plan-coherent and unique; therefore every step is addressable by `advanceStep`'s `findIndex` and countable by `completeExecutionPlan`'s allow-list. A plan that reaches ACTIVE has a path to COMPLETED that no authoring choice has already foreclosed.
- INV-PROPOSE-5 (one field, one meaning). An edge is CONDITIONAL iff it carries a conditionExpression (DS-004 §6 D2, biconditional). The propose-time and runtime planes cannot read the same edge differently — `isConditionalEdge`'s OR and `isDefaultEdge`'s AND-negation become provably complementary for every plan the system will accept from now on.
- INV-GRAMMAR-1 (no silently-false and no silently-true leaf). Every condition leaf is satisfiable in principle and refutable in principle: `STEP_STATE.state` is drawn from the ratified `StepState` enum, and `ALL`/`ANY` carry at least one operand. This is DR-004 DWP-02's stated invariant ('never silently false at runtime') finally met on both halves — the stepId half shipped, the state half did not.
- INV-GRAMMAR-2 (the schema is the single home, and it reaches every plane). `ConditionExpressionSchema` is `safeParse`d by propose (`execution.ts:190` → plan-proposal L6), the runtime guard evaluator (`execution.ts:119-120`) and the read-model (`execution-view.ts:261-262`, `:301`). A grammar rule therefore hardens NEW proposals AND already-STORED plans in one edit, always in the fail-CLOSED direction: an unparseable expression yields `false`, never `true`.
- INV-GRAMMAR-3 (a recorded decision is never re-litigated). Tightening the grammar CANNOT retroactively re-resolve a settled BRANCH: `selectBranchEdge` (`transition-gate.ts:109-114`) returns the recorded edge without evaluating any guard. Only an UNRESOLVED branch can change behaviour under FIX-4/FIX-6, and this repo contains no such stored plan. DWP-09's resolved-once rule survives the remediation untouched.
- INV-BACKCOMPAT-1 (propose-time rules do not re-run on load). Nothing re-validates a stored plan at load, at Approve, or at Activate — and nothing will. Adding such re-validation would wedge legacy plans fail-closed with no remediation command, which is a worse defect than the one being fixed. Legacy plans are handled by the RUNTIME's layered fail-closed posture instead: `safeParse`→false at the guard evaluator, the retained NOT_READY→SKIPPED prune arrow, the `completeExecutionPlan` allow-list, and the `isBelowQueued` honesty affordance.
- INV-5 PRESERVED (exec ≠ assurance). Every rule in this family reads or refuses EXECUTION structure only. No rule reads or writes an assurance axis, no refusal is expressed in assurance terms, and no assurance state is consulted at propose. `validateProposedPlan` is pure — no I/O, no clock, no cross-aggregate read — so it is replay-safe by construction, like every other rph-domain predicate.

### Anti-recurrence
"FIVE MEASURES, ordered by strength.

1. COMPILE-TIME EXHAUSTIVENESS (the strongest). `isProposableStepState` in `packages/rph-domain/src/plan-proposal.ts` is written as an exhaustive `switch` over `StepState` with `default: return assertNever(s)`. Adding an eleventh value to `StepStateSchema` in the vocab and running `bun run gen` breaks `check-types` until an author explicitly dispositions the new state as proposable or not. The same idiom already guards the grammar (`condition-grammar.ts:79, :136, :169`) — this extends it to the propose contract. A guard that cannot silently fall out of date with the vocab it validates against.

2. AN EXHAUSTIVE-OVER-THE-ENUM KILL TEST (KT-1c). The at-rest rule's kill test iterates all ten `StepStateSchema` members rather than sampling one. This is the specific answer to how the family survived: the register's own suggested fix ('reject NOT_READY') would have satisfied a sampled test while leaving the fail-OPEN authored-SUCCEEDED bypass wide open. No subset-of-the-enum guard can survive a table driven off the enum itself.

3. ONE NAMED HOME WITH A STATED RULE. `packages/rph-application/src/handlers/execution-propose-contract.test.ts` is the single home of the propose-contract battery, and its header states the governing rule explicitly: *ProposeExecutionPlan is the only writer of `steps[]` and `transitions[]`; every field on ExecutionStep/ExecutionTransition that is RUNTIME state rather than authoring input is pinned HERE*, followed by the current list (`stepState`, `selectedTransitionId`). A new field on either object now has an obvious place where someone must decide. The absence of such a home is measure 1 of why this family existed at all.

4. THE CARRIER'S OWN KILL TEST (KT-0). A duplicate-step-id proposal on a plan with `transitions: []` goes RED under any mutant that reintroduces shape-conditional validation — including the natural-looking one of folding step rules into `validateTransitionGraph`. This pins INV-PROPOSE-2 structurally rather than by convention, and it is the test that would have caught F-10 on the day `validateTransitionGraph`'s short-circuit was written.

5. A SEEDING SEAM THAT REFUSES THE UNREACHABLE. `seedStepStates` throws on `NOT_READY`/`READY`, and the below-QUEUED exception is a separately named `_LEGACY_ONLY` fixture with a docstring explaining its population. The corpus's dependence on the absent rule is the reason the rule was never written; the seam removes that dependence permanently, and its own kill test (KT-8a) stops the seam degenerating back into a general back door.

RECORD IN THE REMEDIATION DOCUMENT: (i) the disclosed residual that constant-true guards remain expressible and tautology detection is deliberately NOT attempted; (ii) the reclassification of the NOT_READY→SKIPPED arrow from 'dead-arm prune path' to 'legacy-plan recovery path', so a future reachability sweep does not delete it as unreachable; (iii) the disposition of F-41/43/44 as fixed on the ANTI-VACUITY ground, not on the fail-open-guard ground the raisers claimed."

### Seed impact
"ZERO. The reference seed drives unchanged, and this is verified field by field rather than assumed.

`packages/rph-engine/src/reference-undertaking.ts:540-560` proposes, per plan: `steps: [{ id: stepId, executionPlanId: planId, stepType: 'MODEL_INVOCATION', purpose: …, inputBindings: [], outputBindings: [], preconditions: [], postconditions: [], stepState: 'QUEUED' }]`, `transitions: []`. Against each new rule: steps non-empty ✔ (one); id non-empty and unique ✔ (one, minted); `executionPlanId === planId` ✔ (line 544 vs the payload's `executionPlanId: planId` at :541); `stepState === 'QUEUED'` ✔ (:553); `selectedTransitionId` absent ✔ (the field is not written); no transitions, so every edge rule is vacuous ✔; no conditionExpression, so every grammar rule is vacuous ✔. `packages/rph-engine/src/seed-workbench.ts` contains no `ProposeExecutionPlan` of its own. The 69 rph-engine tests are untouched by design, not by luck — no rph-engine file, and no file rph-engine imports for the seed path, is edited by this family.

THE DEMO ROUTE IS EQUALLY CLEAN. `apps/rph-demo/src/routes/undertakings/[id]/+page.server.ts:500-535` mints `planId`/`stepId` via `mintUiId`, sets `executionPlanId: planId` (:520) and `stepState: 'QUEUED'` (:528), with `transitions: []`. No change required.

E2E FIXTURES — three of four clean, one migrates. `execution-flow.e2e.ts` (`mkStep` :40-50 QUEUED with `executionPlanId: f.planId`; `seqEdge` :53-59 SEQUENTIAL bare; `condEdge` :63-67 CONDITIONAL + expression — the biconditional is already honoured by construction), `execution-sequencing.e2e.ts` (:12-24 QUEUED, plan-coherent) and `execution-tier3.e2e.ts` (:79 QUEUED) all pass every new rule unmodified. The single exception is `execution-plan.e2e.ts:150`, `mkStep(STEP3,'NOT_READY')` — the shipped demo plan that can never reach COMPLETED, i.e. the defect itself staged as a fixture. Its migration is specified in FIX-8.

APPLICATION TEST CORPUS. Four suites and four helper functions migrate onto the seeding seam (`execution-start-gate.test.ts`, `execution-plan-completion.test.ts`, `dwp05-precondition-coverage.test.ts`, `execution-plan-supersede.test.ts`) — approximately 35 arrangements, all mechanical, none changing what any test asserts. Every OTHER execution suite already proposes at QUEUED and needs no edit: `execution-assumption-guard` (:58), `execution-detail` (:204), `execution-floor-gate-ai` (:104), `execution-floor-signal0-live` (:128), `execution-floor-subject` (:178), `execution-plan-activation-guard` (:71), `execution-retry-cap` (:57), `execution-step-reissue-guard` (:71), `execution-step-skip-cancel` (:48-61), `pwu.test.ts` (:186).

STORED PLANS. Propose-time validation gates only NEW proposals; nothing re-validates on load (INV-BACKCOMPAT-1). This repo holds no persisted production corpus — the demo database is regenerated by `seed-workbench.ts`, and the only NOT_READY plan in existence is created at e2e runtime through propose, so it is a test migration and not stored data. The only retroactive effects are the two GRAMMAR changes (FIX-4, FIX-6), which reach stored plans through the shared `safeParse` and always move fail-OPEN → fail-CLOSED; a BRANCH that already recorded its selection is provably unaffected."

### Open questions
- Should the below-QUEUED readying lifecycle be formally RETIRED or SCHEDULED? This design forbids authoring into it but leaves the machine arrows (`transitions.data.ts:1439-1444`), the ratified event schema (`ExecutionStepReady`, `messages.ts:1140`, registered :2296), the dead predicate (`stepMayBecomeReady`, `packages/rph-domain/src/execution.ts:129`) and the unread field (`ExecutionStep.preconditions`) in place. That is now a documented, deliberately-unbuilt subsystem rather than an accidental dead end — but a sponsor decision is needed on whether it is a Tier-4 commitment or should be struck from the machine. Striking it would make `initialState` `QUEUED` and simplify four requireFrom sets; keeping it costs nothing but leaves five dead symbols. NOT settled here.
- Is an ADVISORY (non-blocking) authoring lint for subject-independent guards wanted, and where would it live? Three verifiers independently suggested one. This design deliberately does NOT build it: a partial tautology detector that catches `ATTEMPTS >= 0` but not `> -1` would be exactly the vacuous guard CON-000 B7 forbids, and there is no condition-authoring surface in the product today (the demo route only passes `conditionExpression` through — `+page.server.ts:140-142`). Revisit when an authoring surface exists.
- Should the ratified sibling DSL `packages/rph-assurance/src/applicability.ts:52-54` (DOC-007 §18 ApplicabilityExpression) also gain `.min(1)`? It is the identical `.every`/`.some` fold with the identical empty-combinator hole, and after FIX-6 the two grammars diverge on the same question. It is a DIFFERENT surface and out of this remediation's scope, but the divergence should be recorded rather than left to be discovered as an inconsistency.
- The e2e coverage trade in FIX-8: we replace `execution-plan.e2e.ts`'s end-to-end proof that a below-QUEUED step RENDERS honestly with an end-to-end proof that it cannot be CREATED, retaining the render proof only at unit level (`execution-view.test.ts:92-96, :129-132, :169-178`). I judge the refusal proof stronger under fix-forward, but it is a genuine loss of one e2e assertion and a sponsor may prefer to keep both by exposing a test-only store-write endpoint in the demo — which I did NOT propose, because a production route that writes arbitrary object state is a worse hazard than the coverage it buys.
- FIX-7 (authored `selectedTransitionId`) is an out-of-register addition. It is the same root cause as F-09/F-27 and a systemic fix should close it, but it was not raised by any of the 118 agents and should be explicitly dispositioned by the orchestrator rather than absorbed silently into this family's scope.
- Whether the `steps` non-empty rule (FIX-3, L1) should ALSO require that at least one step is a non-BRANCH executable node. A plan consisting solely of BRANCH steps is proposable and passes every rule here, and `completeExecutionPlan` additionally requires at least one SUCCEEDED step (`execution.ts:493-497`) — so it is not a deadlock, but it may be another structurally-pointless shape. I did not settle it: I could not construct a failure that the existing completion guard does not already refuse, and refusing it at propose without such a failure would be over-refusal.

### Adversarial critique — verdict `NEEDS_REVISION`

### Over-refusal risks
- CONFIRMED BREAKAGE, uncensused: lifecycle.test.ts:95 and :128 propose `steps: []` and assert ACCEPTED — FIX-3 L1 refuses both.
- Censused and correct: execution-plan-completion.test.ts:140 `activePlan([])`; execution-plan-supersede.test.ts:127 `mkStep(PLAN_A,S_FAILED,'FAILED')`; dwp05-precondition-coverage.test.ts 12 sites (verified: SUCCEEDED at :142,156,171,178,187,201,250,259,286,298; FAILED at :226; QUEUED elsewhere); execution-start-gate.test.ts :209-241 + inline WAITING/RUNNING at :587,:593 + NOT_READY graph at :776; apps/rph-demo/e2e/execution-plan.e2e.ts:150.
- REFERENCE SEED CLEAN — verified field by field: reference-undertaking.ts:540-556 authors one step, `executionPlanId: planId` (:546) matching the payload's `executionPlanId: planId` (:541), `stepState: 'QUEUED'` (:553), no `selectedTransitionId`, `transitions: []`. seed-workbench.ts issues no ProposeExecutionPlan. Passes L1-L7 unchanged.
- DEMO ROUTE CLEAN: +page.server.ts:500-535 mints ids, `executionPlanId: planId` (:520), `stepState: 'QUEUED'` (:528), `transitions: []`.
- OTHER E2E CLEAN: execution-flow.e2e.ts:49 QUEUED with paired condEdge (:63-66); execution-sequencing.e2e.ts:23 QUEUED; execution-plan.e2e.ts:459 `mkStepFor(H_PLAN,H_STEP,'QUEUED')` (a SECOND fixture in that file, not in the design's census, but it is QUEUED and needs no migration — say so).
- FIX-4 CLEAN: every `STEP_STATE` literal in the repo uses a legal enum value — execution-start-gate.test.ts:937 ('SKIPPED'), execution-view.test.ts:477 ('FAILED'), condition-grammar.test.ts:30,59,60,80,120 ('FAILED'/'SUCCEEDED'). No over-refusal.
- FIX-6 CLEAN on inputs: `operands: []` appears nowhere in packages/ or apps/. But it kills the `'all of ()'`/`'any of ()'` render branches (execution-view.ts:319,321) — dead code, not a test break.
- FIX-5 CLEAN: no fixture authors SEQUENTIAL+conditionExpression, and every CONDITIONAL fixture pairs an expression (cedge/condEdge take it as a required arg).
- FIX-7 CLEAN: no propose call site anywhere sets `selectedTransitionId`; it is written only at execution.ts:778.

### Symptomatic-not-systemic
- FIX-4 fixes the typo'd-`state` fail-open under NOT but leaves its exact twin — a leaf referencing a step absent from the subject — fail-OPEN under NOT for every stored plan, guarded only by a propose-time check that by construction cannot reach stored plans. Same mechanism, same amplifier, same permanence via `selectedTransitionId`. This is the local-repair pattern the brief warns about.
- The fail-closed guard-evaluation wrapper (`safeParse && evaluateCondition`) is DUPLICATED at execution.ts:118-121 and execution-view.ts:260-263. INV-GRAMMAR-2 claims 'the schema is the single home', which is true of the schema and false of the posture built on it. Hoist to one exported function in condition-grammar.ts so a future divergence is impossible rather than merely unlikely.
- The fabricated-success half of F-09 is closed only at propose. `completeExecutionPlan`'s guard (execution.ts:473-497) still reads authored `stepState` strings with no event backing, so 'plan COMPLETED with zero ExecutionStepSucceeded events' remains structurally expressible. The design should name this as an unclosed root cause handed to F-08's family, not treat it as collateral already killed.
- F-27's root cause (two machine arrows with no command) is contained, not fixed. That is a defensible call under FIX-FORWARD, and openQuestion 1 raises it — but the fix section reads as closure. Make the containment explicit in the fix, not only in the open question.

### Conflicts / gaps
- F-31 (RuntimeBinding): that family's fix makes `runtimeBindingId` machine-written, which falsifies INV-PROPOSE-3 as scoped. Coordinate now, not after.
- F-08 (plan-completion allow-list bypass): overlapping claim on the fabricated-success path; this design must not claim the kill.
- F-15/F-21/F-23 (BRANCH selection recording): correctly identified as composing with FIX-7 — no conflict found. `applyTacticalChange` (execution.ts:578-586) and `supersedeExecutionPlan` (:531-569) confirmed status-only via `advanceStatus`, so INV-PROPOSE-1's exhaustiveness holds; the only `steps:` writers in the handlers are execution.ts:71 (read-model shaping), :263 (propose), :659 (advanceStep), :769 (branch recording).
- F-03/F-04/F-05 (entry edges): no semantic conflict — `validateTransitionGraph` is called byte-identically as L7. Only textual conflict in transition-gate.ts.
- INV-5 and RPH-EXE-002: no rule in this family reads or writes an assurance axis or a plan-status arrow; `validateProposedPlan` is pure. Verified clean.
- Generated-file constraint respected: FIX-2/3/5/7 change no vocab; FIX-4/6 edit `condition-grammar.ts`, which is explicitly hand-authored (its header, lines 5-8) and is the sanctioned path. No `bun run gen` needed.
- Layering verified legal: `packages/rph-domain/package.json` already depends on `@janumipwb/rph-contracts`, `stateMachine.ts:4` already imports it, and `.dependency-cruiser.cjs`'s `domain-purity` rule (lines 30-37) forbids only persistence/application/assurance/controller/projections/engine/pwa. `packages/rph-application/tsconfig.json` includes `src` and `tsconfig.build.json` excludes `src/**/__tests__/**`, so the FIX-8 fixture type-checks and ships nowhere, as claimed.

### Missed findings
All nine assigned findings are addressed: F-09/F-27 by FIX-2, F-10 by FIX-3, F-22/F-39 by FIX-4, F-33 by FIX-5, F-41/F-43/F-44 by FIX-6 with an explicit narrow disposition and a disclosed tautology residual. None is silently dropped. Two qualifications: (1) F-27 is closed only for NEW plans — no command drives a live-path NOT_READY step out, and I confirmed the retained NOT_READY->SKIPPED arrow is unreachable for such a step because `prunableStepIds` requires `!live.has(s.id)`; the design must state this as F-27's disposition rather than implying full closure. (2) F-22's mechanism is closed for the `state` field but its structural twin (an unresolvable `stepId` reference, fail-OPEN under NOT for stored plans) is left reachable and is not disclosed as a residual — see requiredChanges item 4.

### Required changes
- OVER-REFUSAL, UNCENSUSED, BREAKS TWO SHIPPED TESTS: FIX-3's L1 (`steps.length >= 1`) refuses `steps: []`, and the census names only `execution-plan-completion.test.ts:140` (`activePlan([])`). It misses `packages/rph-application/src/handlers/lifecycle.test.ts:95` and `:128` — BOTH propose `steps: []` through the real bus and BOTH assert ACCEPTED (`:95` is the canonical PROPOSED->APPROVED->ACTIVE lifecycle test; `:128` is the ExecutionPlanActivated approvalDecisionId contract-drift test). Neither is a state-arrangement fixture, so `seedEmptyStepArray_LEGACY_ONLY` is the wrong migration for them: give each one QUEUED step (behaviour-preserving — neither test reads `steps`). Add both to FIX-8's migration census; the census is currently presented as exhaustive and is not.
- FALSE PREMISE in FIX-5's `sites` and in INV-PROPOSE-5: the design says `isConditionalEdge` (transition-gate.ts:88-89) and `isDefaultEdge` (transition-gate.ts:523-524) are 'two disagreeing planes' that FIX-5 makes 'provably complementary'. They are ALREADY exact De Morgan complements, unconditionally, for every edge: `isConditionalEdge = A || B` where A=`conditionExpression !== undefined`, B=`transitionType === 'CONDITIONAL'`; `isDefaultEdge = !A && !B = !(A || B)`. They cannot disagree about any input, present or future. An implementer following INV-PROPOSE-5 will hunt a divergence that does not exist and may 'repair' one of the two predicates — a regression. Restate the real defect and the real invariant: the disagreement is between VALIDATION (`checkBranchDefaults` counts a guardless CONDITIONAL edge as a live guarded arm, satisfying the exactly-one-default rule) and RUNTIME (`guardEvaluatorFor`'s `safeParse(undefined)` makes that arm false on 100% of runs). INV-PROPOSE-5 should read: 'an edge classified CONDITIONAL always carries a parseable guard, so classification-as-guarded implies the guard is evaluable.' Also restate FIX-5's second direction (SEQUENTIAL + expression) honestly: it fixes NO finding and is not a plane disagreement (that edge is handled correctly today via `isConditionalEdge`'s OR); justify it as tag-honesty only, or drop it.
- ANTI-VACUITY FAILURE — KT-5d cannot fail under mutation as sited. It is placed in `packages/rph-domain/src/transition-gate.test.ts`, but that suite builds its own ad-hoc guard evaluators that never touch `ConditionExpressionSchema` (`:115` `const guard: EdgeGuardEvaluator = (e) => ...`, `:177` `(e) => (e.conditionExpression as { result?: boolean })?.result === true`, `:323`). Reverting `.min(1)` therefore changes nothing there — the schema is not in the call path. Relocate KT-5d to `packages/rph-projections/src/execution-view.test.ts` built through `conditionEvaluatorFor` (execution-view.ts:256-263, the real `safeParse` — the suite already uses it at `:450` and `:466`), or to the application suite through `guardEvaluatorFor`. Apply the same constraint explicitly to KT-3e: mandate `conditionEvaluatorFor`, not a hand-rolled `evaluateGuard`, or it is vacuous for the same reason.
- SYMPTOMATIC NOT SYSTEMIC — FIX-4 closes one half of the fail-OPEN-under-NOT class and leaves its exact twin reachable. A leaf whose `stepId` is absent from the subject evaluates `false` (condition-grammar.ts:117-128) and therefore TRUE under `NOT` (:134) — identical mechanism, identical blast radius (recorded `selectedTransitionId`, made permanent by DWP-09), and the evaluator's own docstring (:113-114) admits it relies on propose to prevent it ('A missing step (bad ref — rejected at propose) yields a conservative false for its leaf'). Propose-time ref resolution (execution.ts:199-207) does not reach STORED plans, which is precisely the retroactive gap FIX-4 was chosen to close for the state half. Systemic closure: add ONE exported wrapper to `packages/rph-domain/src/condition-grammar.ts`, e.g. `evaluateGuardExpression(expression: unknown, subject: ConditionSubject): boolean` = safeParse -> every `conditionStepRefs(parsed.data)` id resolves in `subject.steps` -> `evaluateCondition`; and have BOTH `guardEvaluatorFor` (execution.ts:113-121) and `conditionEvaluatorFor` (execution-view.ts:256-263) delegate to it. This also collapses the currently DUPLICATED `safeParse && evaluateCondition` wrapper into one home — today INV-GRAMMAR-2's fail-closed posture has two implementations that can drift, which is the family's own root-cause pattern. Kill test: a stored guard `{op:'NOT',operand:{op:'STEP_SUCCEEDED',stepId:'ghost'}}` must NOT satisfy its arm (RED today).
- KT-1b's second assertion is vacuous: after propose is REJECTED the plan aggregate does not exist, so `CompleteExecutionPlan` fails for object-not-found, not because the bypass is closed. Replace it with an honest pair: (a) propose `['SUCCEEDED','SUCCEEDED']` -> REJECTED (the real kill test); (b) an explicitly-labelled DISCLOSURE test that a plan seeded all-SUCCEEDED through the FIX-8 seam is still ACCEPTED by `CompleteExecutionPlan` — because the guard (execution.ts:473-497) reads only authored `stepState` strings and is NOT event-backed. The design must stop claiming the at-rest rule 'kills the fabricated-success bypass' unqualified: it kills it only on the propose path. Record the state-vs-event-backed completion guard as a residual and hand it to F-08's family (PWU `rejectUnbackedExecutionSuccess` / plan-completion allow-list) rather than absorbing the claim.
- INV-PROPOSE-3's field census is incomplete and creates a cross-family conflict. It asserts 'every field on ExecutionStep that the machine writes is pinned at propose' while handling only `stepState` and `selectedTransitionId`. Enumerate all eleven `ExecutionStepSchema` fields (objects.ts:230-242) and classify each authoring vs. runtime. Specifically flag `runtimeBindingId` (`:236`): it is authoring-only TODAY only because F-31 established that `StartExecutionStep`'s payload has no such field and the handler's binding branch is unreachable. F-31's remediation will make it machine-written, at which point this family's propose rule must forbid authoring it. Coordinate explicitly with that family or the invariant is false the moment F-31 lands.
- F-27's disposition must be stated, not implied. FIX-2 forbids CREATING a NOT_READY step; it does not give an already-stored one an exit. I verified there is none on a live path: `prunableStepIds` (transition-gate.ts:323-334) requires `!live.has(s.id)`, so the retained `NOT_READY -> SKIPPED` arrow (transitions.data.ts:1469-1473) is reachable only for dead-arm steps, and `NOT_READY -> SUPERSEDED` (`trigger: 'plan revised/superseded'`) has no registered command either. Say plainly: 'F-27 is closed by prevention for all NEW plans; for stored plans the deadlock persists and the only exits remain Fail/Cancel/Supersede at PLAN level. The residual population is empty in this repo (the sole NOT_READY plan is created at e2e runtime through propose).' Do not let the reclassification of the prune arrow to 'legacy-recovery path' imply the live-path case has a recovery.
- FIX-6 leaves dead render branches that the family's own logic condemns: `packages/rph-projections/src/execution-view.ts:319` and `:321` render `'all of ()'` / `'any of ()'`, unreachable once `.min(1)` lands because `describeCondition` (:301) safeParses first. I confirmed no test asserts those strings. Delete both branches with the change (or record them as a disclosed residual with a reason) — leaving a render path for a shape the grammar refuses is the same latent-drift shape this family exists to remove.
- FIX-8 seam mechanics are under-specified in three ways that make it a fabrication risk rather than a shortcut: (a) the synthetic `CommandReceiptRecord` must carry a per-call-unique `commandId`/`idempotencyKey` (the adapter keys receipts by idempotencyKey; helpers patch repeatedly within one test) — specify a monotonic counter; (b) `store.commit` bypasses `kit.ts:264`'s `validateAgainst`, so the fixture must itself `ExecutionPlanSchema.parse` the patched state before committing, otherwise the seam can store shapes the contract forbids — exactly the hazard the `floor-fixtures.ts` precedent is cited to avoid; (c) strengthen KT-8b: assert the seam is table-driven-refusing over the two bus-unreachable states (NOT_READY, READY) rather than the weak 'assert the propose command carried all-QUEUED steps'.
- FIX-1 reorders graph validation from FIRST (execution.ts:250) to LAST (limb L7), and inserts FIX-5's L4 ahead of the condition limb. The design asserts KT-0b as an acceptance gate without doing the precedence analysis. State it: I verified all five pre-existing rejection payloads trip exactly one limb and are order-insensitive (`:343` NONSENSE op — graph valid, L6 fires; `:349` ghost_step — graph valid, L6 fires; `:359` non-BRANCH CONDITIONAL — condition parses and its ref resolves, L7 fires; `:369` default-not-LAST — same; `:1007` duplicate transition id — graph valid, L5 fires). Publish that table and the resulting message-precedence order in the design so a future limb insertion is checked against it rather than against a bare 'tests still pass' claim.
- Factual correction that load-bears on FIX-2's disposition argument: `stepMayBecomeReady` (packages/rph-domain/src/execution.ts:129) is NOT callerless — it is called by `canStartStep` (`:156`). It is `canStartStep` that has no production caller (only `execution.test.ts:96-114`). Correct the 'five dead symbols' inventory in openQuestion 1 to: `canStartStep`, `stepMayBecomeReady`, `StepStartInput.preconditionsSatisfied`, `ExecutionStep.preconditions`, `ExecutionStepReadyPayloadSchema` (messages.ts:1140, registered :2296) plus the two commandless machine arrows. The conclusion (a documented, deliberately-unbuilt readying subsystem) survives; the evidence as written does not.

## F6-readmodel-affordance — Read-model and affordance fidelity (the UI/projection family)

### Root cause
Every defect in this family is one shape: **a read-model projects a NARROWER input set than the authority it claims to mirror, and returns a well-typed plausible value instead of failing.**

- `advanceCommandsFor(stepState)` (rph-projections/src/execution-view.ts:164) mirrors a precondition over (planStatus, stepState) but takes ONLY stepState. `retryExecutionStep` (rph-application/src/handlers/execution.ts:919-924) requires an ACTIVE plan; the read-model cannot see plan status, and the Svelte `{:else}` at +page.svelte:336-341 renders the set unconditionally → **F-29**.
- `executionAttempts` (rph-projections/src/execution-attempts.ts:141-145) mirrors the step lifecycle but folds 3 of the 9 `ExecutionStep*` events. `ExecutionStepCancelled`, `Skipped`, `Pruned`, `Waiting`, `WaitResolved` are silent no-ops at the `STEP_APPLIERS.get(...)?.()` at :159, so the draft opened at :92 (`state:'RUNNING'`) never closes → **F-36 / F-45**.
- `aiNoBinding` (execution-attempts.ts:164) mirrors "this AI step has a runtime binding" but reads `ExecutionStepStarted.runtimeBindingId`, whose only producer is `execution.ts:705`'s spread of `p.runtimeBindingId` — a key `StartExecutionStepPayloadSchema` (messages.ts:254-256, `z.strictObject`) forbids. The advisory is therefore unconditionally TRUE while the step row two lines away (+page.svelte:293) renders the binding the step object DOES carry (objects.ts:237) → **F-31**.
- `ExecutionStepPruned`'s provenance is a DERIVED structural fact, but `pruneExecutionStep` (execution.ts:1044-1045) reads it from the caller, and the sole production dispatcher (+page.server.ts:616) sends `{stepId}` alone. The read-model it is offered from — `prunableStepIds` — returns `string[]`, so the UI structurally cannot supply it → **F-37**.

The unifying failure is that the *missing input was available at the seam* in every case (plan status is on `ExecutionPlanInput.status`; the events are in the same stream already being folded; the binding is on the loaded step inside `advanceStep`; the gate plan is already built in the prune precheck) and simply was not threaded. `startableStepIds`/`prunableStepIds` are the counter-example that proves the correct pattern exists: one pure predicate in rph-domain, consumed by BOTH the read-model and the handler precheck (transition-gate.ts:301,323 + execution.ts:719-727,1055-1060). This family is every place that pattern was not applied.

### Why it survived
Three reasons, all mechanical:

1. **The narrower projection returns a legal value, not an error.** `advanceCommandsFor('FAILED')` → `['retry']` is a correct answer to the question it was asked; nothing signals that the question was the wrong one. Same for `STEP_APPLIERS.get('ExecutionStepCancelled')` → `undefined` → silent skip.
2. **The tests proved the wrong branch.** `execution-attempts.test.ts:100` ("is SILENT for an AI step that DID bind a runtime binding") passes only because the helper at :88-93 hand-crafts an `ExecutionStepStarted` with a `runtimeBindingId` key **no producer in the system can emit** — a vacuous test certifying a production-unreachable path (CON-000 B7). Symmetrically, `execution-tier3.e2e.ts:155,162` are the only two references to `step-action-retry` repo-wide and both merely CLICK it under an ACTIVE plan; there is no negative-affordance assertion anywhere. `ExecutionStepPruned` has no payload test at all (only a registry COUNT at validate.test.ts:93), so the two ternaries at execution.ts:1044-1045 always take the `{}` branch under the shipped suite — deleting both lines leaves the suite green.
3. **The declared omissions were comments, not machinery.** `execution-attempts.ts:137` declares WHY `ExecutionStepRetried` is absent — in prose. Nothing forced the same declaration for the five events minted after that comment was written (DWP-02/03/04 added Cancelled-with-reason, Pruned, Waiting, WaitResolved). Prose does not fail a build.

### Fixes

**Findings F-29 (CONFIRMED, MAJOR→MINOR by both verifiers) — the Retry affordance is rendered with no plan-status gate, violating DWP-06's declared F-11 invariant**
- *rule:* CANONICAL RULE (INV-AFF-1, affordance fidelity): an affordance rendered for a (plan, step, command) triple is offered IFF the same pure predicate the engine's precheck consults admits that command for that (planStatus, stepState). The Svelte template contains NO affordance condition of its own — it renders exactly the sets the read-model returns.

CANONICAL RULE (INV-AFF-2, single requirement home): the set of ExecutionStep commands that may be issued only under an ACTIVE plan is DECLARED ONCE and consumed by both the handler prechecks and the read-model. Today it is written twice — as five independent `precheck` closures in the handlers, and partially as ad-hoc conditions in Svelte.
- *sites:* NEW, packages/rph-domain/src/execution.ts (append after `canStartStepUnderPlan`, :60-67):
  • `export type StepCommandKey = 'start'|'complete'|'fail'|'retry'|'skip'|'cancel'|'wait'|'resolve'|'prune'`
  • `const REQUIRES_ACTIVE_PLAN: Record<StepCommandKey, boolean>` — start:true, retry:true, skip:true, resolve:true, prune:true, complete:false, fail:false, cancel:false, wait:false. Each entry carries an inline citation to the handler line that is its authority (execution.ts:709, :919, :967, :1119, :1049 for the trues; :744/:863/:998/:1081 for the deliberate falses, whose docstrings already state WHY — cancel is CLEANUP §19 L3-M11, wait SUSPENDS already-running work, complete/fail terminate existing work).
  • `export function stepCommandRequiresActivePlan(cmd: StepCommandKey): boolean`
  • `export function stepCommandAdmissibleUnderPlan(cmd: string, planStatus: string): boolean` — FAIL-CLOSED: an unrecognised `cmd` returns false (never fabricate an affordance).

MODIFIED, packages/rph-projections/src/execution-view.ts:
  • `advanceCommandsFor` (:164) and `controlCommandsFor` (:172) KEEP their signature and export, but their docstrings are corrected to "MACHINE-LEGAL, PLAN-BLIND — this is NOT an affordance set. Do not render from it; render from `stepAffordances`/`executionPlanAffordances`."
  • NEW `export function stepAffordances(input: { planStatus: string; stepState: string }): { advance: readonly StepAdvanceCommand[]; control: readonly StepControlCommand[] }` = the machine-legal sets filtered through `stepCommandAdmissibleUnderPlan`.
  • `stepView(s)` (:187) → `stepView(s, planStatus)`; `ExecutionStepView.advanceCommands`/`.controlCommands` (:195-196) are populated from `stepAffordances` — the single construction site becomes plan-aware. `executionPlanView` (:205) passes `row.status`.
  • NEW `export interface StepAffordanceSet { advance; control; start: boolean; prune: boolean }` and `export function executionPlanAffordances(plan: ExecutionPlanView, evaluateGuard?: EdgeGuardEvaluator): Readonly<Record<string, StepAffordanceSet>>` — folds `startableStepIds` (:238) + `prunableStepIds` (:245) + `stepAffordances` into ONE per-step answer, so the UI does one lookup and cannot combine them wrongly.
  • NEW `export interface PlanAffordanceSet { complete: boolean; fail: boolean; cancel: boolean }` and `export function planAffordancesFor(planStatus: string): PlanAffordanceSet`, backed by a `Record<ExecutionPlanStatus, PlanAffordanceSet>` totality table sourced from the handlers' own `fromStates` declarations: complete = fromStates('ACTIVE') (execution.ts:472), fail = fromStates('ACTIVE') (:514), cancel = fromStates('APPROVED','ACTIVE') (:440). These are CORRECT in Svelte today; moving them is anti-recurrence, not a defect fix.

MODIFIED, apps/rph-demo/src/routes/undertakings/[id]/+page.server.ts:
  • `buildExecutionReadModels` (:149-163): replace `startableStepByPlan`/`prunableStepByPlan` with `affordancesByPlanStep: Record<planId, Record<stepId, StepAffordanceSet>>` from `executionPlanAffordances`, plus `planAffordanceByPlan: Record<planId, PlanAffordanceSet>`. `transitionRowsByPlan` is unchanged (it is the read-only edge plane, drives nothing).

MODIFIED, apps/rph-demo/src/routes/undertakings/[id]/+page.svelte — DELETE four affordance conditions:
  • :263 `{#if pl.status === 'ACTIVE'}` (complete/fail) → `{#if pa.complete}` / `{#if pa.fail}`
