  • :275 `{#if pl.status === 'ACTIVE' || pl.status === 'APPROVED'}` → `{#if pa.cancel}`
  • :329 `{#if data.startableStepByPlan[pl.id]?.includes(s.id)}` → `{#if aff.start}`
  • :349 `{#if ctl === 'cancel' || ctl === 'wait' || pl.status === 'ACTIVE'}` → DELETED outright (the iterated `aff.control` set is already filtered)
  • :363 `{#if data.prunableStepByPlan[pl.id]?.includes(s.id)}` → `{#if aff.prune}`
  The ungated `{:else}` arm at :336-341 — the actual F-29 site — is then SAFE unchanged, because the set it iterates is the filtered one. That is the point: the fix is not a sixth condition in Svelte, it is the removal of all five.
- *mechanism:* The plan status is already sitting unused on `ExecutionPlanInput.status` (execution-view.ts:110) at the exact point where `stepView` builds the affordance sets (:195). Threading it turns `advanceCommands` from a function of one variable into a function of the two variables the engine actually consults. `REQUIRES_ACTIVE_PLAN` being a `Record<StepCommandKey, boolean>` makes the compiler REQUIRE a classification for any command added later — the same totality device `ADVANCE_BY_STEP_STATE` (:119) and `CONTROL_BY_STEP_STATE` (:136) already use, extended to the second axis they were missing.

No engine code changes. The engine already refused correctly and fail-closed (verified live by the register's probe: `RetryExecutionStep rejected — RPH_ILLEGAL_STATE_TRANSITION`); this fix stops the UI tempting the refusal.
- *killTest:* 1. UNIT (packages/rph-projections/src/execution-view.test.ts) — `stepAffordances` boundary pairs, BOTH sides of every gate:
   • `{planStatus:'CANCELLED', stepState:'FAILED'}.advance` → `[]`  ← RED today; RED again if the plan filter is deleted.
   • `{planStatus:'ACTIVE',    stepState:'FAILED'}.advance` → `['retry']`  ← kills an OVER-refusal mutant (a filter that drops retry unconditionally).
   • `{planStatus:'SUPERSEDED',stepState:'RUNNING'}.control` → `['cancel','wait']` (NOT `[]`) ← kills a mutant that plan-gates the whole control set; pins the deliberate no-gate on cleanup/suspend.
   • `{planStatus:'SUPERSEDED',stepState:'QUEUED'}.control` → `['cancel']` (skip withheld) and `{planStatus:'SUPERSEDED',stepState:'WAITING'}.control` → `['cancel']` (resolve withheld).
   • `stepCommandAdmissibleUnderPlan('supersede-step','ACTIVE')` → false (fail-closed on unknown command).
   • `planAffordancesFor('SUPERSEDED')` → `{complete:false,fail:false,cancel:false}`; `planAffordancesFor('APPROVED')` → `{complete:false,fail:false,cancel:true}`.
2. CROSS-LAYER CONFORMANCE (NEW, packages/rph-application/src/handlers/execution-affordance-conformance.test.ts) — THE anti-recurrence test. Table-driven over every `StepCommandKey` × every plan status in {ACTIVE, APPROVED, CANCELLED, SUPERSEDED, COMPLETED, FAILED} × every stepState the command is machine-legal from: drive a real Engine to that state, dispatch the command, and assert `engineAccepted === readModelOffers`. Goes RED if a handler gains or loses a plan-status precheck without `REQUIRES_ACTIVE_PLAN` being updated, and RED if the table over-refuses. This is the guard against the CLASS, not the instance.
3. E2E NEGATIVE AFFORDANCE (apps/rph-demo/e2e/execution-tier3.e2e.ts, reusing `stageActivePlan`): activate → Start s1 → Fail s1 → click `plan-fail` → assert `step-action-retry` toHaveCount(0). Then the both-directions half: activate → Start s2 (RUNNING) → `plan-fail` → assert `step-action-cancel` toHaveCount(1) AND `step-action-wait` toHaveCount(1) AND `step-action-complete` toHaveCount(1) (cleanup/suspend/terminate remain, as the engine permits). The first assertion is RED today (the register's probe measured `retry-button-count-under-FAILED-plan = 1`); the second guards against over-refusal.
- *behaviourChange:* WITHHELD that was previously offered — exactly one: the Retry button on a FAILED step under a non-ACTIVE (CANCELLED/SUPERSEDED/COMPLETED/FAILED) plan. That is the F-29 defect.

STILL OFFERED, unchanged: Start (already plan-ACTIVE-gated in the read-model, transition-gate.ts:302); Prune (already gated, :324); Skip and Resolve (previously gated in Svelte at :349, now gated in the read-model — same result); Cancel and Wait under ANY plan status including SUPERSEDED (deliberate — cleanup and suspension of already-running work are permitted post-supersession, execution.ts:991-997 and :1074-1079); Complete and Fail on a RUNNING step under a terminal plan (deliberate — neither handler has a plan-status precheck).

NO engine behaviour change. Zero new refusals. Zero previously-accepted commands now refuse.
- *risk:* LOW. (a) `stepView`'s signature change ripples into `execution-view.test.ts` fixtures that call `executionPlanView(...)` — mechanical, and the plan status is already in every fixture. (b) `advanceCommandsFor`/`controlCommandsFor` remain exported, so a future caller could still render from the plan-blind function; mitigated because kill test 2 asserts the VIEW's sets against the real engine, not the helper's, so a regression through that door still goes RED. (c) The demo load's shape changes (`startableStepByPlan`/`prunableStepByPlan` disappear); grep confirms their only consumers are +page.svelte:329/363.

**Findings F-36 (CONFIRMED, MINOR — Cancel never closes an attempt; RUNNING forever on every replay) and F-45 (PLAUSIBLE, MINOR — both wait events dropped, WAITING step renders as RUNNING). Plus the two gaps both verifiers found beyond the filed findings: ExecutionStepSkipped and ExecutionStepPruned are equally unfolded.**
- *rule:* CANONICAL RULE (P-ATT-1, attempt/aggregate coherence — the replay property, stated exactly): for any event stream E and any step s, let `last(s)` be the last event in E classified as a step-lifecycle event for s. Then the last attempt for s in `executionAttempts(E)` has `state === ` the `stepState` that event drove s to (the SAME value the aggregate holds), and has `completedAt` defined IFF that state is terminal. Consequently there is NO stream in which the aggregate says CANCELLED/SKIPPED/WAITING and the attempt says RUNNING. The property holds under replay because the fold is a pure function of the ordered stream.

CANONICAL RULE (INV-ATT-2, fold totality): every `ExecutionStep*` event type in the contract registry has a DECLARED effect on the attempt fold — OPEN, CLOSE, SUSPEND, RESUME, or an explicitly-reasoned NONE. Silence is not a classification.

F-45 DISPOSITION — treated as CONFIRMED and fixed, not deferred. The two verifiers split; I settle it for the fix. (i) The refuting verifier's strongest argument — "§10.4 declares `state text not null` with no ratified attempt-state vocabulary, so WAITING would MINT vocabulary" — cuts both ways: the same absence means WAITING is not forbidden, and Persistence §35 (quoted verbatim at rph-domain/vocab/m11-execution.json:205) explicitly asks the reconciler to "classify each nonterminal attempt", which requires distinguishing suspended from running. (ii) F-45 and F-36 are ONE mechanism; the refuting verifier's own probe [C] proves the Cancelled half is a real defect. Fixing Cancelled with a fourth Map entry while leaving Waiting unclassified would restore precisely the root cause — a partial, undeclared classification. (iii) P-ATT-1 is only STATABLE if the fold is total. A SUSPEND is explicitly NOT a close: `completedAt` stays undefined, the attempt stays open, `attemptNumber` is untouched — so RPH-EXE-008 and the ratified "a wait/resume cycle continues the SAME attempt" ruling (execution.ts:1104-1105) are preserved byte-for-byte.
- *sites:* MODIFIED, packages/rph-projections/src/execution-attempts.ts:
  • Replace the three-entry `STEP_APPLIERS` (:141-145) with a DECLARED, TOTAL effect table:
    `type AttemptEffect = {kind:'OPEN'} | {kind:'CLOSE'; state:string} | {kind:'SUSPEND'} | {kind:'RESUME'} | {kind:'NONE'; why:string}`
    `const STEP_EVENT_EFFECT = new Map<string, AttemptEffect>([...])` — still a Map (the `__proto__` hardening at :138-140 is deliberate and retained), covering ALL nine:
      ExecutionStepStarted     → OPEN
      ExecutionStepSucceeded   → CLOSE 'SUCCEEDED'  (payload key `executionStepId`, §16.2)
      ExecutionStepFailed      → CLOSE 'FAILED'     (+ error from `failureReason`)
      ExecutionStepCancelled   → CLOSE 'CANCELLED'  (+ terminationReason from `reason`)   ← F-36
      ExecutionStepSkipped     → CLOSE 'SKIPPED'    (+ terminationReason from `waiverOrRevisionId`)
      ExecutionStepPruned      → CLOSE 'SKIPPED'
      ExecutionStepWaiting     → SUSPEND            (state 'WAITING', completedAt UNSET)  ← F-45
      ExecutionStepWaitResolved→ RESUME             (state 'RUNNING', completedAt UNSET)  ← F-45
      ExecutionStepRetried     → NONE, why: 're-queue MARKER; the next Started opens attempt n+1 (§19 L3-3)'
      ExecutionStepReady       → NONE, why: 'NOT_READY→READY scheduling; precedes any attempt'
  • `ExecutionAttemptView` gains `readonly terminationReason?: string` — the `reason` recorded on ExecutionStepCancelled (messages.ts:1120-1123) and the `waiverOrRevisionId` on Skipped are recorded facts; dropping them repeats the same defect class. `AttemptDraft` gains the same field.
  • The fold at :158-160 dispatches on the effect kind. A CLOSE/SUSPEND/RESUME that finds no open draft (`state.current.get(k)` undefined) is a no-op — fail-closed by construction.
  • The module header (:9-22) gains a third Boundary bullet stating INV-ATT-2 and P-ATT-1 in prose, replacing the single ad-hoc `Retried` comment at :137.

MODIFIED, apps/rph-demo/src/routes/undertakings/[id]/+page.svelte:
  • DELETE the local `attemptTone` (:91-97, currently total only via a `'muted'` default, so CANCELLED/WAITING/SKIPPED would all render muted). Render `{stepStateTone(at.state)}` from rph-projections instead — attempt states are now a subset of step states, so ONE tone home (execution-view.ts:149-160, already `Record<StepState, StepTone>`-total and unit-tested).
  • :387 add `{#if at.terminationReason}<span class="dim">{at.terminationReason}</span>{/if}` beside the existing error render.
- *mechanism:* Skipped/Pruned are classified CLOSE 'SKIPPED' even though their handler `requireFrom` sets (READY|QUEUED and NOT_READY|READY|QUEUED) mean no attempt can be open when they fire. This is DELIBERATE and must be DISCLOSED as such in the table comment: 'unreachable today given the drivesFrom sets in execution.ts:958/:1041 — classified anyway so that a future widening of those sets cannot silently reopen the RUNNING-forever hole. The no-open-draft path is a no-op, so this is fail-closed in both worlds.' It must NOT be tested as though it proved production behaviour — that is the F-31 fiction-test trap, and the honest guard against it is kill test 1 below (registry totality), not a hand-crafted stream.

SUSPEND/RESUME deliberately do not touch `completedAt` or `attemptNumber`. `attemptsMadeForStep` in the handler layer counts `ExecutionStepStarted` events independently (execution.ts:~880) and is untouched, so the retry cap is unaffected.
- *killTest:* 1. REGISTRY TOTALITY (NEW, execution-attempts.test.ts) — THE anti-recurrence test: enumerate the contracts' event registry for keys matching `/^ExecutionStep/` and assert every one is a key of `STEP_EVENT_EFFECT`. Minting a tenth step event without classifying it goes RED. This is what makes the whole table non-vacuous, and it is the test whose absence let DWP-02/03/04 mint four events into a three-entry map.
2. PER-ENTRY, all RED today:
   • `[Started, Cancelled{reason:'operator aborted'}]` → `{state:'CANCELLED', completedAt:'…', terminationReason:'operator aborted'}` (register's exact reproduction recipe).
   • `[Started, Waiting{waitReason}]` → `{state:'WAITING', completedAt: undefined}`, and `attempts.length === 1`.
   • `[Started, Waiting, WaitResolved]` → `{state:'RUNNING', completedAt: undefined}`, `attempts.length === 1` — pins 'a resume is NOT a new attempt' (RPH-EXE-008). Deleting the RESUME entry leaves WAITING → RED; misclassifying RESUME as OPEN gives length 2 → RED.
   • `[Started, Waiting, WaitResolved, Succeeded]` → ONE attempt, SUCCEEDED, completedAt set.
   • `[Started, Waiting, Cancelled]` → CANCELLED + completedAt — the compound 'open attempt on a dead plan' case both verifiers reproduced.
   • Replay stability over a stream containing all of the above: `executionAttempts(E)` toEqual itself.
3. P-ATT-1 PROPERTY TEST (fast-check, alongside packages/rph-domain/src/properties.test.ts's idiom): generate a random legal step-event sequence by walking the `ExecutionStep.stepState` machine out of the GENERATED packages/rph-domain/src/transitions.data.ts, fold it, and assert the last attempt's `state` equals the machine's resulting state (and `completedAt` defined iff terminal). This single property subsumes F-36, F-45 and both undiscovered gaps, and would have caught all four.
4. INTEGRATION (packages/rph-application/src/handlers/execution-step-skip-cancel.test.ts, extend): after the existing 'cancels a RUNNING step' case at :168, assert `executionAttempts(store.readAllEvents(), …)[0]` reports CANCELLED with a completedAt — proving it against the REAL dispatch path and real payload keys, not a hand-built stream.
5. E2E (apps/rph-demo/e2e/execution-plan.e2e.ts, extend the existing wait/resolve test at :251-275): after EnterExecutionStepWait, assert `step-attempt` shows WAITING while `step-state` shows WAITING — the two-facts-one-line contradiction, pinned.
- *behaviourChange:* CHANGED DISPLAY, no authority change. Attempts that previously reported `RUNNING` forever after a Cancel/Skip/Prune now report their terminal state with a `completedAt`; attempts on a WAITING step now report WAITING and resume to RUNNING. `attemptNumber`, `idempotencyKey`, `provenance` and the SUCCEEDED/FAILED paths are byte-identical.

Nothing previously accepted now refuses — this projection is display-only. Verified: `executionAttempts` has exactly one non-test consumer (+page.server.ts:174) and no guard, gate, retry cap or transition condition reads `attempt.state`. `aiNoBinding` is unaffected by this fix (see the F-31 fix).
- *risk:* LOW. The one hazard is an existing e2e asserting `#1 RUNNING` after a wait or cancel — grep confirms none exists (execution-plan.e2e.ts:251-275 and execution-flow.e2e.ts:336-371 assert only `stepStateOf`; execution-tier3.e2e.ts:128-129 asserts the happy path SUCCEEDED). Secondary: `terminationReason` is a new field on an exported interface — additive-optional, exactOptionalPropertyTypes-safe via the existing conditional-spread idiom at :93-94.

**Findings F-31 (CONFIRMED, MAJOR) — StartExecutionStep's strictObject payload has no runtimeBindingId, so the handler's read at execution.ts:690/705 is unreachable dead code, ExecutionStepStarted.runtimeBindingId has no producer, aiNoBinding is unconditionally TRUE for every AI attempt, and execution-attempts.test.ts:100 is a vacuous test of a production-unreachable branch.**
- *rule:* ARGUED DISPOSITION — the payload omission is NOT a gap; it is CORRECT, and adding the field would be the wrong fix. THREE pieces of ratified evidence:
  (1) `RequestRuntimeBinding.payloadFields` (rph-contracts/vocab/m3-commands-events.json:2067-2101) = `{runtimeBindingId, executionStepId, roleId, requestedCapabilities}` — a binding is REQUESTED **for a named execution step**. The ratified relation is Binding→Step, established before the step runs.
  (2) `ExecutionStepSchema` (generated objects.ts:230-242 from m1-object-fields.json) already carries `runtimeBindingId?`, and `ProposeExecutionPlanPayloadSchema.steps` (messages.ts:132-135) accepts it — so the binding is part of the AUTHORED, approved plan.
  (3) Three existing consumers already read `step.runtimeBindingId` as authoritative: floor-gate.ts:74, execution-view.ts:200, +page.svelte:293.
A per-invocation binding on the Start command would be a SECOND, LATER, UNAUTHORIZED source of truth for a fact the approved plan already fixes — it would let a caller start a step against a binding the plan never authorized. The `z.strictObject` accidentally prevents exactly that. **DECISION: do NOT add `runtimeBindingId` to `StartExecutionStepPayloadSchema`. No command vocab change.**

CANONICAL RULE (INV-BND-1, binding provenance): `ExecutionStepStarted.runtimeBindingId` records the binding the APPROVED PLAN authored onto the step, read from the loaded step by the authority — never a caller assertion. `aiNoBinding` is TRUE iff an AI-typed step was authored with NO binding.

CANONICAL RULE (INV-BND-2, RPH-EXE-003 at start time): if a step names a `runtimeBindingId`, that id MUST resolve to a RUNTIME_BINDING object whose `authorizationStatus` is AUTHORIZED or PARTIALLY_AUTHORIZED (`bindingPermitsExecution`, rph-domain/src/execution.ts:89), AND — when the plan's recorded `authorizedRuntimeBindingIds` is NON-EMPTY — it MUST be a member of that list. A step naming NO binding is unaffected: that remains an advisory, not a gate, preserving DR-002:185's ruling for the unbound case.
- *sites:* MODIFIED, packages/rph-application/src/handlers/execution.ts (`advanceStep`, :596-676) — a shared mechanism this fix and the F-37 fix both need:
  • Add `readonly eventPayloadFrom?: (step: Record<string,unknown>, plan: Record<string,unknown>) => unknown` to the `args` type (beside `eventPayload`, :612). It is evaluated at :660 AFTER the step is located, `precheck`, `requireFrom` and `checkTransition` have all passed, and AFTER `mutateStep`, and it receives `nextStep` — the POST-mutation, post-`stepState` step — so a payload can name a fact `mutateStep` just computed and cannot drift from what is persisted. Precedence: `eventPayloadFrom` > `eventPayload` > `command.payload`.

MODIFIED, packages/rph-application/src/handlers/execution.ts `startExecutionStep` (:689-739):
  • :690 — replace `command.payload as { stepId: string; runtimeBindingId?: string }` with `command.payload as StartExecutionStepPayload` (the ratified generated type; the old cast was a structural guess reading a key its own schema forbids).
  • :703-707 — delete the static `eventPayload` and its `...(p.runtimeBindingId ? ... : {})` spread (:705, dead). Replace with:
    `eventPayloadFrom: (step) => ({ stepId: p.stepId, ...(typeof step.runtimeBindingId === 'string' && step.runtimeBindingId !== '' ? { runtimeBindingId: step.runtimeBindingId } : {}), stepState: 'RUNNING' }) satisfies ExecutionStepStartedPayload`
    This gives `ExecutionStepStarted.runtimeBindingId` (messages.ts:1159, already `.optional()`; vocab already declares it) its FIRST and ONLY producer. **No contract or vocab change required for the event.**
  • The `precheck` (:708-738) gains a THIRD ordered clause, after the plan-ACTIVE check and BEFORE the start-gate (cheapest-first, and a binding failure is more specific than a sequencing one):
    ```
    const bindingId = typeof step.runtimeBindingId === 'string' ? step.runtimeBindingId : undefined;
    if (bindingId) {
      const binding = ctx.store.loadObject(bindingId);
      if (!binding || binding.objectType !== 'RUNTIME_BINDING')
        return reject(command,'RPH_VALIDATION_SEMANTIC_FAILED', `Cannot start step ${p.stepId}: its runtimeBindingId ${bindingId} does not resolve to a RUNTIME_BINDING …`);
      const authorized = new Set((plan.authorizedRuntimeBindingIds as string[]) ?? []);
      if (authorized.size > 0 && !authorized.has(bindingId))
        return reject(command,'RPH_VALIDATION_SEMANTIC_FAILED', `… binding ${bindingId} is not among the plan's authorizedRuntimeBindingIds …`);
      const check = bindingPermitsExecution(String(binding.state.authorizationStatus));
      if (!check.ok) return reject(command,'RPH_INVARIANT_VIOLATION', `… (RPH-EXE-003): ${check.reason}`);
    }
    ```
    NOTE the precheck signature already provides `(step, plan)` (:605-608) — no new plumbing. `plan.authorizedRuntimeBindingIds` is recorded onto the aggregate by `activateExecutionPlan` (:407).
    REFUSAL CODES (settled, not open): `RPH_BINDING_NOT_AUTHORIZED` is a KERNEL-internal `Check.errorCode` string and is NOT one of the 15 canonical codes (rph-contracts/src/errors.ts:9-25). So per constraint 7: unresolved reference / not-in-allowlist → `RPH_VALIDATION_SEMANTIC_FAILED` (mirroring `stepResultSubjects`, execution.ts:829-839); binding resolves but is REQUESTED/DENIED/REVOKED → `RPH_INVARIANT_VIOLATION` (RPH-EXE-003 is an invariant).

MODIFIED, packages/rph-projections/src/execution-attempts.ts:
  • :164 `aiNoBinding` — UNCHANGED code. It becomes truthful for free, because C1 supplies the producer. The Do-not-change block at :20-22 is amended to name the producer: 'runtime_binding_id comes from the Started event, which the authority copies from the AUTHORED step (INV-BND-1) — not from the command payload, which forbids the key.'

- *mechanism:* The register's verifier was right that `bindingPermitsExecution` (RPH-EXE-003) is dead and that DR-002:185 ruled 'Do NOT add a startExecutionStep guard'. That ruling is no longer safe to leave silent AFTER C1: once the engine RECORDS a binding onto the governed stream, an attestation with no verification is worse than no attestation. So the guard is wired — but MINIMALLY, keyed on evidence that exists, so it can refuse nothing the seed or any current plan does.

The empty-`authorizedRuntimeBindingIds` case is a DELIBERATE, ENUMERATED under-refusal: `[]` is read as 'no allowlist declared' (membership clause skipped), NOT as 'nothing authorized'. Reading `[]` as 'nothing authorized' would refuse every bound step and is exactly the over-refusal constraint 2 forbids — every caller and the reference undertaking pass `[]` (reference-undertaking.ts:563). Disclosed as residual **R-EXE-003-a**: 'the plan-level authorization list is not enforced until a plan actually declares one.' Both sides are pinned by kill tests.

(Today it is byte-for-byte `{stepId, stepState:'RUNNING'}` — the register measured it.)

Its negative twin — same plan, step authored WITHOUT a binding → `true`. Deleting the `&& a.runtimeBindingId === undefined` conjunct at execution-attempts.ts:164 now goes RED against a producible stream, not a hand-crafted one. DEAD CODE REMOVED: execution.ts:690's cast and :705's spread.

Three mitigations: (a) the guard is keyed on `step.runtimeBindingId`, which the seed never sets (grep of packages/rph-engine/src for `runtimeBindingId` returns zero hits) so the seed is provably inert; (b) kill test 6 pins the no-over-refusal boundary from both directions; (c) the empty-list under-refusal is a named residual, not a silent choice. AUTHORITY NOTE: this NARROWS DR-002:185's 'Do NOT add a startExecutionStep guard' ruling and must be disclosed in DR-004 §19 as a deliberate divergence with the C1-makes-it-necessary rationale — not applied silently.

**Findings F-37 (CONFIRMED, MINOR) — ExecutionStepPruned's prune provenance, the sole stated justification for minting the event rather than reusing ExecutionStepSkipped, is never populated by any producer. Plus the stronger fact the test-evidence verifier established by execution: `STREAM CONTAINS selectedTransitionId = false` — no domain event in the entire governed stream names which arm any BRANCH took.**
- *rule:* CANONICAL RULE (INV-PRV-1, derived provenance): `ExecutionStepPruned` records the plan's own exclusion decision as a fact DERIVED by the authority from the SAME gate that authorized the prune. Provenance is never read from the command payload — the command cannot carry it.

CANONICAL RULE (INV-PRV-2, stream self-sufficiency for branch decisions): every BRANCH decision written to aggregate state is ALSO named by the event that recorded it. Today `completeExecutionStep`'s `mutateStep` (execution.ts:762-778) writes `selectedTransitionId` to state only, and its own comment (:761) forecloses re-derivation — 'a point-in-time decision cannot be reconstructed later' — so a pure event replay cannot reconstruct any branch decision at all.
- *sites:* 1. VOCAB (packages/rph-contracts/vocab/m3-commands-events.json), then `bun run gen` + prettier — NEVER hand-edit messages.ts:
  • COMMAND `PruneExecutionStep.payloadFields` — REMOVE `selectedByBranchStepId` and `selectedEdgeId`. Payload becomes `{stepId}` alone. Provenance is authority-derived; a caller must not be able to assert it.
  • EVENT `ExecutionStepPruned.payloadFields` — KEEP `selectedByBranchStepId?` and `selectedEdgeId?`; ADD `excludedEdgeId?` (`required:false`, `type:'string'`, note: 'id-ref: the not-taken transition edge, out of the excluding step, on the path that would have reached this step. The field that makes provenance TOTAL — a non-BRANCH false-guard exclusion has an excluded edge but no "selected instead".').
  • EVENT `ExecutionStepSucceeded.payloadFields` — ADD `selectedTransitionId?` (`required:false`, note: 'id-ref: for a BRANCH step, the out-edge it selected when it succeeded (DWP-09). Absent for a non-BRANCH or an unguarded BRANCH. AUTHORED optional field atop the ratified §16.2 shape — the same posture as executionProvenance/structuredResult already carried there.') — INV-PRV-2.

2. NEW pure derivation, packages/rph-domain/src/transition-gate.ts (appended after `resolveBranchSelection`, :182-196):
  ```
  export interface PruneProvenance {
    readonly excludedByStepId: string;   // the SETTLED step whose out-edge selection excluded the path
    readonly excludedEdgeId?: string;    // its not-taken out-edge on the path to the pruned step
    readonly selectedEdgeId?: string;    // the edge it took INSTEAD (BRANCH only)
    readonly excludedByBranch: boolean;  // true iff excludedByStepId is a resolved BRANCH
  }
  export function pruneProvenanceFor(plan: GatePlan, stepId: string, evaluateGuard?: EdgeGuardEvaluator): PruneProvenance | undefined
  ```
  ALGORITHM (deterministic, replay-stable, O(V+E)):
   a. `live = liveStepIds(plan, evaluateGuard)` (:135). If `live.has(stepId)` → `undefined` (the step is not excluded).
   b. Enumerate CUTS in `plan.transitions[]` ARRAY ORDER: every edge `e` with `e.sourceStepId ∈ live`, `e.targetStepId !== undefined`, and `branchExcludes(plan, e, evaluateGuard) === true` (:162). For each, `selected = selectBranchEdge(outEdgesOf(plan, e.sourceStepId), plan, evaluateGuard, source)` (:98).
   c. For each cut in order, forward-BFS from `e.targetStepId` through edges whose target ∉ `live` (the dead subgraph), in array order. The FIRST cut whose closure contains `stepId` is its provenance.
   d. No cut reaches it → `undefined`; the handler then omits all provenance fields. The prune still proceeds: the PRUNABILITY gate authorizes it, provenance only describes it.
  Determinism: `plan.transitions[]` array order is already the total order `selectBranchEdge`'s first-match relies on (:115-118), so this introduces no new ordering assumption. Replay-stable because the inputs are the committed aggregate plus the committed event fold. `liveStepIds`/`branchExcludes` stay module-private (same file).

3. AUTHORITY, packages/rph-application/src/handlers/execution.ts `pruneExecutionStep` (:1028-1071):
  • :1029-1033 — payload cast → `PruneExecutionStepPayload` (now `{stepId}`).
  • :1042-1047 — delete the static `eventPayload` and BOTH caller-forwarding ternaries (:1044, :1045 — the two mutants the register proved survive today). Replace with `eventPayloadFrom` (the hook added by the F-31 fix):
    ```
    eventPayloadFrom: (_step, plan) => {
      const gatePlan = toGatePlan(plan);
      const prov = pruneProvenanceFor(gatePlan, p.stepId, guardEvaluatorFor(ctx, planId, gatePlan));
      return { stepId: p.stepId,
        ...(prov?.excludedByBranch ? { selectedByBranchStepId: prov.excludedByStepId } : {}),
        ...(prov?.selectedEdgeId   ? { selectedEdgeId: prov.selectedEdgeId } : {}),
        ...(prov?.excludedEdgeId   ? { excludedEdgeId: prov.excludedEdgeId } : {}),
        stepState: 'SKIPPED' } satisfies ExecutionStepPrunedPayload;
    }
    ```
  • `selectedByBranchStepId` is emitted ONLY when `excludedByBranch` — naming a non-BRANCH false-guard source in a field whose vocab note says 'the BRANCH step whose first-match selection excluded this step' would be a lie; `excludedEdgeId` covers that case honestly.

4. AUTHORITY, `completeExecutionStep` (:744-799) — INV-PRV-2:
  • Convert the static `eventPayload` (:790-799) to `eventPayloadFrom: (nextStep) => ({ …the nine existing fields…, ...(typeof nextStep.selectedTransitionId === 'string' ? { selectedTransitionId: nextStep.selectedTransitionId } : {}) })`. Because the hook receives the POST-`mutateStep` step, it reads the value `mutateStep` (:762-778) just computed — the event cannot drift from the persisted state, and the branch decision is not recomputed.

5. DOCS: DS-004 §6 D5's claim ('its event records `pruned: BRANCH <stepId> selected edge <edgeId>`') becomes TRUE; amend it to also name `excludedEdgeId`. DR-004 §19-M1's minting justification becomes delivered; add the `excludedEdgeId` addition and the command-payload REMOVAL to §19 as disclosed changes.
- *mechanism:* The prune precheck already builds `gatePlan` and the guard evaluator (:1055-1060) to authorize the prune; the SAME two objects answer 'why'. Hoisting them so authorization and provenance share one construction also avoids a second event-log scan by `guardEvaluatorFor`.

The pruned step is frequently NOT adjacent to the BRANCH — `prunableStepIds` is a transitive fixpoint over the whole dead subtree (:323-334). So provenance MUST walk the dead subgraph to the CUT, not read the step's own in-edges. That distinction is what kill test 3 exists to enforce.
- *killTest:* 1. PURE (packages/rph-domain/src/transition-gate.test.ts), over the existing DWP-03 branch fixture:
   • `pruneProvenanceFor(plan,'s2')` → `{excludedByStepId:'s1', excludedEdgeId:'<t1-2>', selectedEdgeId:'<t1-3>', excludedByBranch:true}`.
   • `pruneProvenanceFor(plan,'s4')` (the TRANSITIVE downstream of the pruned arm) → the SAME cut — kills a 'read the step's own in-edge source' mis-implementation.
   • `pruneProvenanceFor(plan,'s3')` (a LIVE step) → `undefined`.
   • linear plan (empty transitions[]) → `undefined`.
   • non-BRANCH false-guard exclusion → `{excludedByBranch:false, excludedEdgeId:'…', selectedEdgeId: undefined}` — pins the honesty rule.
2. AUTHORITY (NEW FILE, packages/rph-application/src/handlers/execution-prune-provenance.test.ts — `ExecutionStepPruned` has NO payload test anywhere today): build a real BRANCH plan (s1 BRANCH --COND(never true)--> s2 --> s4 ; s1 --SEQ--> s3), start+complete s1, then dispatch `PruneExecutionStep{stepId:'s2'}` with EXACTLY the payload the sole production dispatcher sends. Assert the emitted payload deep-equals `{stepId:'s2', selectedByBranchStepId:'s1', selectedEdgeId:'<t1-3>', excludedEdgeId:'<t1-2>', stepState:'SKIPPED'}`. RED today (the register measured `{"stepId":"…-s2","stepState":"SKIPPED"}`); deleting ANY of the three spreads → RED. This kills all three of today's unkilled mutants.
3. TRANSITIVE: prune s4 → provenance still names s1/`t1-3`/`t1-2`, not s2.
4. SCHEMA BOUNDARY: `PruneExecutionStep{stepId, selectedEdgeId:'fabricated'}` → `VALIDATION_FAILED / RPH_VALIDATION_SCHEMA_FAILED` — pins INV-PRV-1 (a caller cannot assert provenance), and goes RED if someone re-adds the command fields.
5. INV-PRV-2 (extend packages/rph-application/src/handlers/execution-start-gate.test.ts near its existing :958/:962 state assertions, which assert on the STATE BAG only): `CompleteExecutionStep` on a BRANCH emits `ExecutionStepSucceeded.selectedTransitionId` EQUAL to the persisted `step.selectedTransitionId`. RED today; kills the state-vs-event drift mutant and makes the earlier prune provenance reconstructible from the stream alone.
6. REPLAY: fold the resulting stream twice → identical (provenance is a pure function of committed inputs).
- *behaviourChange:* NEWLY REFUSED: `PruneExecutionStep` carrying `selectedByBranchStepId` or `selectedEdgeId` → RPH_VALIDATION_SCHEMA_FAILED (z.strictObject). Enumerated impact: NOTHING sends them. Repo-wide grep finds `PruneExecutionStep` dispatches only at +page.server.ts:616 and in execution-start-gate.test.ts:377/637/824/914 and zzcs-notready-deadlock.test.ts:153/200 — all `{stepId}` alone. The only caller that ever supplied them was the register's since-deleted probe.
NEWLY RECORDED: three provenance fields on `ExecutionStepPruned`, one on `ExecutionStepSucceeded`. Purely additive to the governed stream.
UNCHANGED: no step-state behaviour change, no new step refusal, no gate change, no prunability change. `prunableStepIds` is untouched.
- *risk:* LOW-MEDIUM. (a) SEQUENCING IS LOAD-BEARING: both event schemas are `z.strictObject`, so vocab → `bun run gen` → prettier MUST land before the handler emits the new keys, or the emitted event fails its own conformance check. (b) `ExecutionStepSucceeded` payload growth touches the §16.2 doc-fidelity surface; precedent exists (`executionProvenance`/`structuredResult` are already AUTHORED-atop-ratified there per the vocab note), but the emitted-event-conformance suite must be checked for key-set enumeration. (c) `pruneProvenanceFor` re-runs `liveStepIds` per cut in the naive form — bound it by computing `live` ONCE and passing it in, or the O(V+E) claim is false on a wide fan-out.

### Invariants
- INV-AFF-1 (affordance fidelity) — for every affordance a rendered on the execution tab: UI-offers(a) ⟹ engine-would-accept(a). No condition that determines an affordance exists outside packages/rph-domain or packages/rph-projections; the Svelte template renders exactly the sets the read-model returns. This is DWP-06's declared F-11 invariant, made structural instead of aspirational.
- INV-AFF-2 (single requirement home) — the set of ExecutionStep commands issuable only under an ACTIVE plan is declared once, as REQUIRES_ACTIVE_PLAN: Record<StepCommandKey, boolean> in packages/rph-domain/src/execution.ts, and is consumed by both the handler prechecks and the read-model. A new step command fails to COMPILE until classified.
- P-ATT-1 (attempt/aggregate coherence, the replay property) — for any event stream E and step s, the last attempt for s in executionAttempts(E) has state equal to the stepState the last step-lifecycle event drove s to, and completedAt defined iff that state is terminal. No stream exists in which the aggregate says CANCELLED/SKIPPED/WAITING and the attempt says RUNNING. Holds under replay because the fold is a pure function of the ordered stream.
- INV-ATT-2 (fold totality) — every ExecutionStep* event type in the contract registry has a DECLARED effect on the attempt fold: OPEN, CLOSE, SUSPEND, RESUME, or an explicitly-reasoned NONE. Silence is not a classification; a newly minted step event fails a TEST until classified.
- INV-BND-1 (binding provenance) — ExecutionStepStarted.runtimeBindingId records the binding the APPROVED PLAN authored onto the step, read from the loaded step by the authority. It is never a caller assertion; StartExecutionStep's strictObject payload continues to forbid the key. Consequently aiNoBinding is TRUE iff an AI-typed step was authored with no binding — an advisory that can now be both true and false.
- INV-BND-2 (RPH-EXE-003 at start time) — a step that NAMES a runtimeBindingId starts only against a resolvable RUNTIME_BINDING whose authorizationStatus is AUTHORIZED or PARTIALLY_AUTHORIZED, and (when the plan declares a non-empty authorizedRuntimeBindingIds) only against a member of that list. A step naming no binding is unaffected.
- INV-PRV-1 (derived provenance) — every provenance field on ExecutionStepPruned is DERIVED by the authority from the same gate that authorized the prune. The command payload cannot carry provenance; a command that tries fails schema validation.
- INV-PRV-2 (stream self-sufficiency for branch decisions) — every BRANCH decision written to aggregate state by mutateStep is also named by the event that recorded it, so a pure event replay can reconstruct which arm any branch took.

### Anti-recurrence
Three MECHANICAL devices, one per failure mode of this family. Prose comments are what failed here (execution-attempts.ts:137 declared the Retried omission and four later-minted events sailed past it), so every device is a compile error or a registry-driven test — not a convention.

1. **COMPILE-TIME TOTALITY on the input axes.** `Record<StepCommandKey, boolean>` (REQUIRES_ACTIVE_PLAN) joins the existing `Record<StepState, …>` tables (execution-view.ts:119,136,149). A new command or a new StepState fails to COMPILE until classified on BOTH axes. This is the device that would have forced someone to answer "does retry need an ACTIVE plan?" at the point advanceCommands was written.

2. **REGISTRY-DRIVEN TOTALITY on the event vocabulary.** A test enumerating the contracts registry for `/^ExecutionStep/` and asserting each is a key of `STEP_EVENT_EFFECT`. Minting a tenth step event without classifying its attempt effect goes RED. This is the device whose absence let DWP-02/03/04 mint four events into a three-entry Map.

3. **CROSS-LAYER CONFORMANCE against the real engine** — `execution-affordance-conformance.test.ts`. For every step command × plan status × machine-legal stepState it drives a real Engine, dispatches, and asserts `engineAccepted === readModelOffers`. This is the only device that catches DRIFT rather than OMISSION: it goes RED when a handler's precondition changes and the read-model does not follow, in either direction. It is the generalization the family needs, because the review's own evidence is that every one of these five findings is "two layers modelling the same rule, one of them narrower."

Supporting discipline, stated as a reviewable rule rather than a mechanism: **a test that hand-crafts an input shape no producer in the system can emit is not evidence.** F-31's execution-attempts.test.ts:100 was exactly that. Every kill test in this design that asserts on an event payload is driven through the real dispatch path (`execution-step-binding.test.ts`, `execution-prune-provenance.test.ts`, the extension to `execution-step-skip-cancel.test.ts`), and the pure-fold tests are kept honest by device 2 rather than by claiming production coverage they do not have — the Skipped/Pruned CLOSE entries are explicitly disclosed as unreachable-today defence-in-depth, not asserted as production behaviour.

### Seed impact
ZERO. Enumerated per fix, with the evidence:

- **F-29 fix (affordances):** the seed drives the engine through `send(...)` in reference-undertaking.ts; it renders no UI and calls no rph-projections function. Untouched.
- **F-36/F-45 fix (attempt fold):** display-only projection with exactly one non-test consumer (+page.server.ts:174). The seed never cancels, skips, prunes or waits a step. Untouched.
- **F-31 fix (binding):** the guard is keyed on `step.runtimeBindingId`. A grep of `packages/rph-engine/src` for `runtimeBindingId|RUNTIME_BINDING` returns ZERO step-level hits — the seed's MODEL_INVOCATION steps (reference-undertaking.ts:547) name no binding, so the guard's `if (bindingId)` is never entered. Additionally `:563` activates with `authorizedRuntimeBindingIds: []`, and the design reads `[]` as "no allowlist declared" precisely so a future bound seed step would still pass. The event producer change is additive: the seed's ExecutionStepStarted payloads are byte-identical (`{stepId, stepState:'RUNNING'}`), so `rph-engine/src/replay.ts`'s hand-authored fixture trace oracle is unaffected.
- **F-37 fix (prune provenance):** the seed authors no BRANCH plan and issues no PruneExecutionStep, so `pruneProvenanceFor` is never called. The `ExecutionStepSucceeded.selectedTransitionId` addition (INV-PRV-2) is conditional on `nextStep.selectedTransitionId` being a string, which `mutateStep` sets only for `stepType === 'BRANCH'` (execution.ts:763) — the seed has none, so its ExecutionStepSucceeded payloads are byte-identical too. **This is the one to verify first**, because `replay.ts` is a fixture trace oracle over event content: if it enumerates payload keys for ExecutionStepSucceeded, the additive-but-conditional field must be confirmed absent in the seed's stream before the 69 rph-engine tests are trusted green.
- **Contract regeneration:** removing two OPTIONAL command payload fields and adding four OPTIONAL event payload fields changes no registry COUNT (validate.test.ts:93 asserts +2 registry entries for ExecutionStepPruned, not field counts) and no required shape.

Every fix in this family is either display-only or gated behind a datum the seed does not set. The single behaviour-changing fix (F-31's INV-BND-2 guard) has kill test 6 dedicated to pinning the seed's exact shape as ACCEPTED.

### Open questions
- DR-002:185 explicitly ruled 'Do NOT add a startExecutionStep guard'. INV-BND-2 narrows that ruling (it gates only steps that NAME a binding, leaving the unbound case advisory-only). I have argued this is necessary once C1 makes the engine RECORD a binding — an attestation with no verification is worse than none — but it is a reversal of a recorded design decision, not an implementation detail. It needs a DR-004 §19 disclosed-divergence entry and, if the program requires it, a sponsor acknowledgement. If the ruling is held instead, the fallback is C1 + C2 only (producer + non-vacuous advisory) with RPH-EXE-003 remaining a disclosed residual alongside its RPH-PWU-010 sibling — the family's other four fixes are unaffected either way.
- `attemptsByStep` (execution-attempts.ts:177-186) is exported and has ZERO production consumers — the UI reads `executionAttempts` directly and only its own test calls the rollup. That is the same dead-exported-surface class as the two never-populated prune fields F-37 is about. I have left it in place (its `latestState` becomes correct for free under P-ATT-1) but not decided whether it should be wired into the UI or deleted. Deleting it removes a surface; wiring it gives the step row a single per-step summary. Needs a call.
- `packages/rph-engine/src/replay.ts` is a hand-authored fixture trace oracle over event content rather than an event-sourced state rebuild. I could not confirm from static reading whether it enumerates payload KEYS for ExecutionStepSucceeded/ExecutionStepStarted. If it does, the additive-but-conditional fields (INV-PRV-2's `selectedTransitionId`, INV-BND-1's `runtimeBindingId`) are still inert for the seed — neither condition fires — but the oracle should be read before step 5 rather than discovered at gate time.
- The prune-provenance tie-break when a dead step is reachable from TWO independent cuts (two BRANCHes both excluding paths that converge on it) is resolved by `plan.transitions[]` array order. That is deterministic and replay-stable and matches the order first-match already relies on, but it is a CHOICE: the alternative is to record all cuts as an array. I chose the single first-authored cut because the event schema's fields are scalars and a `string[]` would be a larger contract change; if an auditor needs the full exclusion set, that is a follow-on, not a defect.

### Adversarial critique — verdict `NEEDS_REVISION`

### Over-refusal risks
- VERIFIED CLEAN — the reference seed. `packages/rph-engine/src/reference-undertaking.ts` grep for `runtimeBindingId|RUNTIME_BINDING` returns exactly ONE hit: `:563 send('ActivateExecutionPlan', …, { authorizedRuntimeBindingIds: [] })`. No seed step carries a step-level `runtimeBindingId`, so INV-BND-2's `if (bindingId)` never fires and the seed's `ExecutionStepStarted` payloads stay byte-identical. The design's seedImpact claim holds.
- VERIFIED CLEAN — existing rph-application tests. Repo-wide grep shows `runtimeBindingId` appears in `rph-application` tests ONLY as a `RequestRuntimeBinding` COMMAND field (`command-reissue-guard.test.ts:74,249`; `execution-detail.test.ts:305`), never as a proposed step field. No existing test proposes a bound step and starts it, so the new guard refuses nothing that is green today.
- VERIFIED CLEAN — the F-29 affordance narrowing. `execution-view.test.ts`'s `plan()` helper (:49-55) defaults `status: 'ACTIVE'`, and it is the ONLY `status:` literal in that file, so making `stepView` plan-aware breaks no existing projection fixture. Skip/Resolve were already Svelte-gated at `+page.svelte:349`, so moving the gate into the read-model is behaviour-identical; Cancel/Wait stay ungated, preserving `execution-step-skip-cancel.test.ts:178` and `execution-start-gate.test.ts:613`.
- REAL RISK — proposed e2e kill test 3 second half asserts `step-action-complete` toHaveCount(1) and `step-action-wait` toHaveCount(1) under a FAILED plan. If F-26/F-42 are fixed by adding the prechecks, this test becomes a false over-refusal alarm against a correct fix. Scope it to `step-action-cancel` only until F-26/F-42 are dispositioned.
- REAL RISK, UNDER-refusal not over-refusal — `authorizedRuntimeBindingIds: []` is read as 'no allowlist declared'. That is the right call (every caller and the seed pass `[]`), and the design names it as residual R-EXE-003-a with a two-directional kill test. Keep it — but note that with the aggregate never carrying the field at all (see requiredChanges #1), R-EXE-003-a is currently not a residual but a total absence of the allowlist rule.
- REAL RISK — removing `selectedByBranchStepId`/`selectedEdgeId` from `PruneExecutionStepPayloadSchema`. Verified safe: the vocab entry is `sourceSection: 'UNRATIFIED-AUTHORED'` (m3-commands-events.json commands[23]), so no ratified contract is broken, and every dispatcher in the repo (`+page.server.ts:616`, `execution-start-gate.test.ts:377/637/824/914`, `zzcs-notready-deadlock.test.ts:153/200`) sends `{stepId}` alone.

### Symptomatic-not-systemic
- **The `aiNoBinding` fix is not retroactive and creates a SECOND source of truth for the binding.** After C1 the advisory is computed from `ExecutionStepStarted.runtimeBindingId`, so it is correct only for streams written AFTER the fix. Any pre-existing stream — including any persisted demo DB — still renders `⚠ AI step, no runtime binding` two lines below `+page.svelte:293` rendering `rb rb_…` for the same step: the exact self-contradiction F-31 filed, still live on old data. Worse, the binding now lives in TWO places (the authored step object and the Started event) and nothing pins them equal — which is the family's own root cause ('two layers modelling the same rule') restated one layer down. The register's verifier offered the systemic alternative and the design does not argue against it: `executionAttempts` ALREADY takes `stepTypeById` from the caller's plan aggregate for exactly this reason (`execution-attempts.ts:20-22, 152-155`). Thread `runtimeBindingIdByStep` the same way, derive the advisory from the AUTHORED step (single source, retroactive), and keep the event recording as PROVENANCE — with a kill test that the two agree.
- **INV-AFF-2 ('single requirement home') is only half-built.** `REQUIRES_ACTIVE_PLAN` is a declaration the read-model consumes, but the five handler prechecks still hand-inline `plan.status !== 'ACTIVE'` (`execution.ts:709, 919, 967, 1049, 1119`). Nothing forces a handler to consult the table — only the conformance test catches drift, and (per requiredChanges #5) that test is currently vacuous. The systemic move is to make the handlers CALL `stepCommandRequiresActivePlan(cmd)` (or route through the already-dead `canStartStepUnderPlan`, `rph-domain/src/execution.ts:60-67`) so the table is load-bearing at runtime, not merely descriptive. As specified, the table is a fourth copy of the rule, not the single home.
- **`ExecutionStepPruned` provenance is derived by the authority but the READ-MODEL that offers Prune still cannot see it.** `prunableStepIds` still returns `string[]` (`transition-gate.ts:323`), so the UI's Prune button remains provenance-blind and the operator is never told WHY a step is prunable. The design has `pruneProvenanceFor` sitting one function away in the same module and does not surface it. Widening `prunableStepIds` to return `{stepId, provenance}` (or exporting a companion) would close the same gap on the display side and give the Prune button an honest tooltip instead of the hard-coded 'the branch went the other way' at `+page.svelte:364`.
- **`attemptsByStep` (`execution-attempts.ts:177-186`) is an exported surface with zero production consumers** — the same never-populated-dead-surface class as the two prune fields F-37 is about. The design raises it as an open question and leaves it. Under the standing 'systemic over simplest' bias, decide it in this design: delete it, or wire it. Leaving a dead export while filing a finding about dead fields is inconsistent.

### Conflicts / gaps
- F-26 (CONFIRMED/MAJOR) and F-42 (PLAUSIBLE/MAJOR) both propose adding plan-ACTIVE prechecks to commands this design hard-codes as `false` in `REQUIRES_ACTIVE_PLAN` (complete, fail, wait). The design mis-names them as F-27/F-28. Its e2e kill test 3 bakes the CURRENT behaviour into a Playwright assertion, so the two families will produce mutually-RED tests.
- F-15/F-21/F-23 (a BRANCH driven to terminal-success by Skip or Prune records no `selectedTransitionId`) will very likely add the same decision-recording to `ExecutionStepSkipped`/`ExecutionStepPruned`. INV-PRV-2 as scoped touches only `ExecutionStepSucceeded`, so the 'stream self-sufficiency for branch decisions' invariant is only 1/3 delivered and the vocab edit will be re-opened by that family. Coordinate the vocab change once.
- `ExecutionStepSucceeded` IS in `RATIFIED_EVENT_PAYLOADS` (`messages.ts:2484`) and is enforced at commit time by the (d2) event gate (`kit.ts:301-328`). The design calls the sequencing risk 'fails its own conformance check'; it is stronger — emitting `selectedTransitionId` before regen makes EVERY BRANCH `CompleteExecutionStep` REJECT with `RPH_VALIDATION_SCHEMA_FAILED` at runtime. State this as a hard gate, not a note. (`ExecutionStepPruned` and `ExecutionStepStarted` are NOT in that map, so those two halves are genuinely unenforced — the design's 'no regen needed for the Started event' claim is correct.)
- The design's `eventPayloadFrom` contract specifies WHICH STEP the hook receives (post-`mutateStep` `nextStep`) but never specifies WHICH PLAN — `advanceStep`'s pre-mutation `plan` (`execution.ts:634`) or the post-mutation `next` (:659). `pruneExecutionStep`'s provenance derivation depends on it. Pin it (pre-mutation `plan` is the right choice: the gate that AUTHORIZED the prune ran against exactly that state).
- F-38/F-46's family may change `TERMINAL_SUCCESS` (`transition-gate.ts:17`). `pruneProvenanceFor` sits directly on `liveStepIds`/`branchExcludes`, both of which read that set. The design flags this correctly but does not say who owns the merge — name it as a hard ordering dependency, not a note.
- `bindingPermitsExecution`'s `errorCode: 'RPH_BINDING_NOT_AUTHORIZED'` (`rph-domain/src/execution.ts:89-97`) is a kernel-internal string, and the design correctly maps it to the canonical codes. But `canStartStep` (:151-160) — the composed kernel that is the ONLY caller of `bindingPermitsExecution` — remains dead after this fix (the design inlines the checks in the handler instead). That leaves a second, now-divergent definition of 'may this step start' in rph-domain. Either route the handler through `canStartStep` or delete it; do not leave two.

### Missed findings
"No assigned finding is silently dropped. All five are addressed with named sites, and F-45 (PLAUSIBLE) gets an explicit, argued CONFIRMED disposition rather than a deferral — the right call, and the argument (a partial classification would restore the root cause) is sound. Two genuine EXTENSIONS beyond the filed findings are correctly picked up: ExecutionStepSkipped/Pruned are equally unfolded (found by both F-36 verifiers), and `STREAM CONTAINS selectedTransitionId = false` (INV-PRV-2). What is MISSED inside the assigned scope: (1) F-31's fourth register consequence — 'ActivateExecutionPlan's authorizedRuntimeBindingIds can never be connected to the attempt that used a binding' — is NOT closed, because the list never reaches the aggregate (requiredChanges #1); the design believes it closed it. (2) F-31's UI self-contradiction persists for every stream written before the fix (symptomaticNotSystemic #1). (3) F-36's register explicitly names `attemptsByStep.latestState` as a propagation path; the design's own risk analysis notes it has no production consumer but never decides its fate. (4) `ExecutionStepReady` is classified NONE ('precedes any attempt') — correct today, but F-27's family may mint a command that emits it; the classification should carry that forward-reference so the NONE is re-argued rather than inherited."

### Required changes
- **INV-BND-2's allowlist clause is dead code as specified — the design reproduces the exact defect class it is remediating.** `packages/rph-application/src/handlers/execution.ts:380-427` (`activateExecutionPlan`) passes NO `mutate` to `advanceStatus` (`kit.ts:460-545`: the aggregate is `nextEnvelope(...) + {[statusField]: target}` only). `authorizedRuntimeBindingIds` therefore lands ONLY on the `ExecutionPlanActivated` EVENT payload (`execution.ts:407`) and is NEVER written to the plan aggregate. Repo-wide grep over `**/src/**` confirms it: the only non-test sites are `messages.ts:145`, `messages.ts:1078` and `execution.ts:394/407`. So the proposed precheck `const authorized = new Set((plan.authorizedRuntimeBindingIds as string[]) ?? [])` reads `undefined` on EVERY plan forever, `authorized.size > 0` is never true, and the membership clause is unreachable — and proposed **kill test 5** ('binding AUTHORIZED but plan activated with `authorizedRuntimeBindingIds: ['rb_other']` → REFUSED') will FAIL, because the command will be ACCEPTED. REQUIRED: either (a) add `mutate: (base) => ({...base, authorizedRuntimeBindingIds: cmd.authorizedRuntimeBindingIds})` to `activateExecutionPlan` and name it as a new persisted aggregate field (with its own kill test asserting the field is on the loaded plan state after activation), or (b) read the list from the last `ExecutionPlanActivated` event via `ctx.store.readAggregateEvents(PLAN, planId)`. Pick one explicitly; do not leave it as `plan.authorizedRuntimeBindingIds`.
- **The F-36/F-45 fold as specified CORRUPTS a closed attempt on a reachable, tested-adjacent path.** `Start → Fail → Retry → Cancel` is fully legal: `retryExecutionStep` drives FAILED→QUEUED (`execution.ts:915-917`), and `cancelExecutionStep.requireFrom` includes `'QUEUED'` (`execution.ts:1004`). Under the proposed table, `ExecutionStepCancelled → CLOSE 'CANCELLED'` looks up `state.current.get(k)`, which still holds attempt #1 — already CLOSED as FAILED with a `completedAt`, because `applyStepFailed` never clears `state.current`. The design's stated safety rule is 'a CLOSE/SUSPEND/RESUME that finds no open draft is a no-op' — but it tests for PRESENCE, not OPENNESS. Result: attempt #1 is silently rewritten FAILED→CANCELLED and its `completedAt` moved. Identical corruption for `ExecutionStepSkipped`/`ExecutionStepPruned` (both drive from QUEUED). REQUIRED: (i) define open-ness explicitly — CLOSE/SUSPEND/RESUME apply ONLY when `draft.completedAt === undefined`, else no-op; (ii) add the kill test `[Started, Failed, Retried, Cancelled]` → exactly ONE attempt, `state:'FAILED'`, `error:'…'` (NOT 'CANCELLED'), which goes RED against the naive implementation.
- **P-ATT-1 as stated is FALSE and, as the fast-check property in kill test 3, would DRIVE the implementer to the corrupting implementation.** The stated property is: 'the last attempt for s has `state` === the stepState the last step-lifecycle event drove s to.' On `[Started, Failed, Retried, Cancelled]` the aggregate says CANCELLED and the correct last attempt says FAILED. The generator described ('walk the `ExecutionStep.stepState` machine out of `transitions.data.ts`') reaches that sequence trivially. REQUIRED: restate P-ATT-1 as an ATTEMPT-scoped property, e.g. 'the last attempt for s is OPEN (no `completedAt`) iff the step is currently in an attempt-bearing state (RUNNING/WAITING); a terminal control event that fires while no attempt is open opens and closes nothing' — and re-derive the property test from that. Also fix the arithmetic: the registry has TEN `ExecutionStep*` events (`messages.ts:2284-2321`: Cancelled, Pruned, Failed, Ready, Retried, Skipped, Started, Succeeded, Waiting, WaitResolved), not the 'nine' the design claims while listing ten.
- **`AttemptEffect` as typed cannot express the extractions the table needs.** `{kind:'CLOSE'; state:string}` carries no stepId key-name, no error extractor and no terminationReason extractor — yet the design's own prose requires `ExecutionStepSucceeded` to read `executionStepId` (not `stepId`, `execution-attempts.ts:107`), `ExecutionStepFailed` to read `failureReason` (:127), `ExecutionStepCancelled` to read `reason`, and `ExecutionStepSkipped` to read `waiverOrRevisionId`. REQUIRED: extend the effect type to `{kind:'CLOSE'; state:string; stepIdKey?: string; errorKey?: string; terminationReasonKey?: string}` (or carry a per-entry close function) so the table is genuinely total and the implementer does not invent the seam.
- **INV-AFF-1's biconditional is unachievable and makes the cross-layer conformance test (device 3, the family's headline anti-recurrence mechanism) vacuous.** `engineAccepted === readModelOffers` is FALSE today for at least six preconditions that are not functions of (planStatus, stepState): the retry cap (`retryDecision`, `execution.ts:927-937` — and `execution-tier3.e2e.ts:155-168` DEPENDS on Retry being rendered while the engine refuses it at cap), the §8.4 floor gate on Complete (:841-856), `canSkipStep`'s mandatory/waiver rule (:975-984), the start-gate (:723-736), the prunability fixpoint (:1056-1067), and — newly — INV-BND-2 itself. The proposed test only passes because its fixtures never reach a cap or an unsatisfied floor: it is scoped-to-pass, i.e. exactly the vacuity CON-000 B7 forbids. REQUIRED: restate INV-AFF-1 as a ONE-WAY implication over the (planStatus, stepState) axis only — `readModelOffers(cmd) ⟹ engine does not refuse for a (planStatus, stepState) reason` — and have the conformance test assert on the REFUSAL REASON/CODE class, with an explicitly enumerated allowlist of the non-state preconditions permitted to refuse an offered affordance. Without that enumeration the test proves nothing.
- **The F-31 guard introduces a NEW F-29-class read-model/authority divergence on the Start affordance, and the design does not notice it.** `startableStepIds` (`transition-gate.ts:301-308`) knows nothing about runtime bindings, so after INV-BND-2 the UI will render `step-action-start` for a step whose authored `runtimeBindingId` resolves to a REQUESTED/DENIED/REVOKED binding (or does not resolve at all) and the engine will refuse it — the identical F-11 breach F-29 exists to close, re-opened by this family's own fix. REQUIRED: either thread a binding-authorization resolver into the gate/read-model (an evaluator hook parallel to `EdgeGuardEvaluator`), or explicitly disclose it as a named residual alongside R-EXE-003-a and add the enumerated exemption to the INV-AFF-1 allowlist above.
- **`REQUIRES_ACTIVE_PLAN`'s justification for `complete:false` and `fail:false` is factually wrong, and it collides head-on with F-26 (CONFIRMED, MAJOR).** The design says the `false` entries cite handler docstrings 'which already state WHY' at `:744/:863/:998/:1081`. Verified: `:998` (cancel) and `:1081` (wait) DO carry that rationale; `:744` (`completeExecutionStep`) and `:863-871` (`failExecutionStep`) carry NONE — which is precisely F-26's claim ('Complete and Fail omit it with NO rationale comment and NO test either way'). The sequencing note further mis-cites the colliding findings as F-27/F-28 (NOT_READY deadlock / RPH-PWU-010); the actual collisions are **F-26** (may add a plan-ACTIVE precheck to Complete) and **F-42** (may add one to EnterExecutionStepWait). Proposed e2e kill test 3's second half — asserting `step-action-complete` toHaveCount(1) AND `step-action-wait` toHaveCount(1) under a FAILED plan — would then be an assertion that the OTHER family's fix is WRONG. REQUIRED: correct the citations to F-26/F-42, mark `complete`, `fail` and `wait` as PROVISIONAL entries owned by those findings, and make the e2e assert only the entries this family actually owns (cancel), deferring complete/wait to whichever disposition F-26/F-42 receive.
- **`pruneProvenanceFor` step (b)/(c) is under-specified for a JOIN and can mis-attribute provenance.** The cut enumeration filters on `e.sourceStepId ∈ live` and `branchExcludes(e) === true` but never checks `e.targetStepId ∉ live`. A cut whose target is a JOIN still reachable via the taken arm (`prunableStepIds`' documented case, `transition-gate.ts:310-321`) excluded nothing, yet the BFS from that live target will walk into genuinely-dead descendants and claim the wrong cut. REQUIRED: add `&& !live.has(e.targetStepId)` to the cut predicate, state that `live` is computed ONCE and passed into the walk (the design's own risk (c)), and add a kill test over a diamond where a dead subtree hangs off a LIVE join target.
- **Un-export the plan-blind helpers instead of merely re-documenting them.** The design keeps `advanceCommandsFor` (`execution-view.ts:164`) and `controlCommandsFor` (:172) exported with a corrected docstring — leaving the loaded gun that caused F-29. Grep over `**/{src,e2e}/**` shows their ONLY importer is `execution-view.test.ts:3,5`. REQUIRED: make them module-private (the test asserts through `stepAffordances`/`stepView` instead). Prose warnings are what the design itself identifies as the failure mode (`execution-attempts.ts:137`); do not add another one.
- **Add the masked-mutant cases the kill-test list omits, and state which mutants are knowingly unkillable.** (a) `stepAffordances({planStatus:'SUPERSEDED', stepState:'QUEUED'}).advance` → `[]` is missing; without it, deleting `start:true` from `REQUIRES_ACTIVE_PLAN` is MASKED by `startableStepIds`' own plan gate (`transition-gate.ts:302`) and by `prunableStepIds`' (:324) for `prune`. (b) `planAffordancesFor` must fail-closed on an unknown/off-contract status — the design specifies a `Record<ExecutionPlanStatus, …>` table with no stated `?? {complete:false,fail:false,cancel:false}` default (constraint 5). (c) The registry-totality test only asserts KEY PRESENCE, so a mis-classification (e.g. `ExecutionStepSkipped` CLOSE 'SKIPPED' → CLOSE 'CANCELLED') is unkillable given the entry is disclosed-unreachable; disclose that explicitly rather than letting the totality device imply coverage it does not give.


---

## Integration synthesis

### Cross-family conflicts
- CONFLICT-1 — `transition-gate.ts` liveness/disposition core is rewritten FOUR times. F2 (Fix 1-5) rewrites `liveStepIds` seeding, DELETES `branchExcludes`, adds `localEdgeDisposition`, collapses `startStepGate`/`startableStepIds`/`prunableStepIds` into `stepStartability`, and memoizes via `GateContext`; F3 (Fix 3) adds `branchVerdict` + an `UNRESOLVED` disposition INSIDE `branchExcludes` and `inEdgeDisposition` (the functions F2 deletes/rewrites) and deletes `selectBranchEdge`'s first-match fallback; F6 (Fix 4) builds `pruneProvenanceFor` ON TOP OF `liveStepIds` + `branchExcludes`; F4 needs `PRUNABLE_SOURCE_STATES` (line 23) bound to its spec table; F5 calls `validateTransitionGraph` byte-identically as limb L7 while F2 rewrites its entry counting. RECONCILE INTO ONE CHANGE (WP-2 -> WP-4): a single `GateContext` built once per public call, carrying (a) indexes, (b) a FOUR-valued `EdgeDisposition = SATISFIED|PENDING|NEUTRALIZED|UNRESOLVED`, (c) a THREE-valued step liveness `LIVE|DEAD|INDETERMINATE`. The reconciled local ladder, ordered fail-closed per F2's own critique RC-3 (terminal-success BEFORE reopenable, so a future terminal-success out-arrow cannot wedge the system): 1 no source -> SATISFIED; 2 source unresolvable -> PENDING; 3 source non-terminal -> PENDING; 4 source TERMINAL-SUCCESS -> {4a BRANCH with >=1 conditional out-edge: recorded selection resolves -> SATISFIED iff this edge else NEUTRALIZED; recorded id matches no out-edge -> UNRESOLVED; NO recorded selection -> UNRESOLVED (F3's never-invent; the first-match fallback is deleted); 4b unconditional -> SATISFIED; 4c guard holds -> SATISFIED else NEUTRALIZED}; 5 terminal non-success AND reopenable (machine-derived: terminal, not terminal-success, has an out-arrow = {FAILED}) -> PENDING (F2's F-16 fix); 6 otherwise -> NEUTRALIZED. Liveness is TWO BFS passes from `entryStepIds`: `live` propagates through {SATISFIED,PENDING}; `possiblyLive` also through {UNRESOLVED}. LIVE=live; DEAD=NOT possiblyLive; INDETERMINATE=possiblyLive AND NOT live. `branchExcludes` disappears (its job is `disposition===NEUTRALIZED`); `pruneProvenanceFor` becomes a method on the same context.
- CONFLICT-2 — F2's INV-GATE-1 and F3's INV-B7 are CONTRADICTORY as written, and F2's is additionally vacuous. F2: `S NOT-live <=> every in-edge NEUTRALIZED <=> UNREACHABLE <=> prunable`. That biconditional is VACUOUSLY TRUE for a zero-in-edge frontier seed (`every` over an empty set), which is how EVERY current graph plan's entry step is shaped (activeParallelPlan's s1, the e2e BRANCH s1) — today only the soon-to-be-deleted `if (inEdges.length === 0) return {ok:true}` at transition-gate.ts:385 saves it. F3 needs UNRESOLVED to be simultaneously NOT-startable and NOT-prunable, which F2's biconditional forbids. RECONCILE: the three-valued liveness above makes both true simultaneously. Restate as INV-GATE-1': `startStepGate(S).code === 'UNREACHABLE' <=> liveness(S) === 'DEAD' <=> (S in prunableStepIds whenever S.stepState in PRUNABLE_SOURCE_STATES)`; and `liveness(S) === 'INDETERMINATE' => neither startable nor prunable, refusal code BRANCH_DECISION_UNRECORDED`. A frontier seed is LIVE by construction (it is a BFS seed), so the vacuity is structurally gone. Kill test must carry BOTH seed shapes (zero-in-edge entry AND source-less-entry-edge target) with an explicit positive `live && gate.ok && NOT prunable`.
- CONFLICT-3 — `advanceStep`'s argument type is redefined by FOUR families, three of them incompatibly. F1 FIX-3 adds a declared-authority lookup evaluated BEFORE precheck and reorders to authority->requireFrom->precheck; F4 FIX-1/FIX-2 replaces `target`/`eventType`/`requireFrom` with a REQUIRED `spec` and reorders to sourceStates->precheck; F3 FIX-2/FIX-5 makes `eventPayload` REQUIRED and typed, deletes the `?? command.payload` fallback, moves `makeEvent` ahead of the settlement view, and adds a REQUIRED `branchDecision` discriminated on `target`; F6 adds an `eventPayloadFrom(step, plan)` hook. F1's critique also demands `mutateStep` be DELETED from the args type (not merely unused), since an optional hook is exactly how Skip opted out of the branch-decision rule. DIRECT PRECEDENCE CLASH: F4's KT-ORDER-1 asserts the sourceStates refusal WINS over the plan-ACTIVE precheck; F1's table would make plan-liveness win. RECONCILE (WP-8, one change): args become `{ spec: StepCommandSpec; eventPayload | eventPayloadFrom; branchDecision (required only on the SUCCEEDED|SKIPPED arm); precheck? }` — `mutateStep`, `target`, `eventType`, `requireFrom` all deleted. ADJUDICATED ORDER: (1) spec.sourceStates, (2) declared authority (planLiveness then pwuOpenness, both from the spec row), (3) per-command precheck, (4) checkTransition, (5) spec-driven branch decision + settlement view, (6) event, (7) commit. sourceStates FIRST because it is the command's own declared contract and a pure function of (command, step) — the most local fact; because F4's three ORDER kill tests are already written to it; and because a NEW rule (plan liveness) must not displace an EXISTING declared contract's refusal code. F1 loses nothing: `liveness/complete-under-superseded-REJECTS` uses a RUNNING step, which is in-contract for Complete.
- CONFLICT-4 — TWO families add the SAME field to the SAME ratified event. F3 FIX-2 adds `selectedTransitionId` to `ExecutionStepSucceeded` (decision durability, INV-B6) and F6 FIX-4 adds `selectedTransitionId` to `ExecutionStepSucceeded` (stream self-sufficiency, INV-PRV-2). This is one vocab edit, and it is HARD-GATED: `ExecutionStepSucceeded` is the ONLY step event in `RATIFIED_EVENT_PAYLOADS` (messages.ts:2484), so kit.ts's (d2) gate strict-parses it at commit. Emitting the key before `bun run gen` lands makes EVERY BRANCH `CompleteExecutionStep` REJECT with RPH_VALIDATION_SCHEMA_FAILED at runtime — not merely a conformance-test failure. RECONCILE: one vocab+regen package (WP-1) lands ALL field changes first, emitting nothing; handlers consume them later. F3 additionally wants the field on `ExecutionStepSkipped` (Skip decides) and F6's family sibling F-15/21/23 will want it on `ExecutionStepPruned` — but WP-4's reconciled rule says a PRUNED branch decides NOTHING (it was itself excluded), so the field goes on Succeeded + Skipped ONLY, and `ExecutionStepPruned` instead gets `excludedEdgeId` (F6). Settle it in the one vocab edit so it is not reopened.
- CONFLICT-5 — the propose-time validator has three claimants. F5 FIX-1 hoists the three private handler validators (`rejectMalformedTransitionGraph` :128, `rejectDuplicateTransitionId` :157, `rejectMalformedTransitionCondition` :182) into ONE pure `validateProposedPlan` in rph-domain; F2 Fix 1 edits `validateTransitionGraph`'s entry counting and adds `checkEntryEdgeShape`; F1 FIX-4 adds an `objectType === 'PROFESSIONAL_WORK_UNIT'` check on `workUnitId`; F6 REMOVES two fields from `PruneExecutionStepPayloadSchema`. RECONCILE: F5's carrier is the correct shape and lands first (WP-6), with F2's entry limbs living INSIDE `validateTransitionGraph` as its limb L7 (so `entryStepIds` has exactly one definition serving both propose-time counting and runtime BFS seeding — F2's INV-GATE-3). F1's `workUnitId` typing stays in the HANDLER, not the pure validator, because it needs `ctx.store`. F6's payload removal is part of the WP-1 vocab batch.
- CONFLICT-6 — FIVE separate anti-vacuity conformance mechanisms are proposed, three of them the same mechanism at different scopes. F1 FIX-7 (ENFORCEMENT_REGISTER + GATE A live-refusal census + GATE B layer-correct coverage + GATE C dead-kernel gate + GATE D declared mutations); F4 FIX-4/5/6 (STEP_COMMAND_SPECS + two-key derived sweep + arrow ledger + a selftest file); F6 (cross-layer affordance conformance driving a real Engine); F3 FIX-6 (registry-totality over `/^ExecutionStep/` event types + a scoped runtime step-event payload gate); F2 (linear/graph parity matrix + the INV-GATE-1 property test). F1's GATE A and F4's negative sweep are the SAME thing; F1's GATE B/C overlap F4's arrow ledger. RECONCILE: ONE `Harness` (dispatch/stateOf/eventsOfType/revisionOf, modelled on dwp05-precondition-coverage.test.ts) driving ONE table-driven sweep over THREE registries — STEP_COMMAND_SPECS (commands), STEP_EVENT_EFFECT (events), ENFORCEMENT_REGISTER (ratified rules) — plus F6's affordance conformance riding the same harness. F2's parity matrix and INV-GATE-1 property stay SEPARATE and pure (rph-domain, no Engine) because they are properties of a pure function, not of a dispatch. F1's GATE B must be re-sited (it cites `m12-conformance.json`, which has ZERO `then` keys; the reject-shaped `then` clauses live in m11/m10/m9's `conformanceTests[]`) and must be given the same DISCLOSED-exemption valve GATE C has, or it red-lines ~15 rules on landing, most outside this remediation.
- CONFLICT-7 — F4 sites `STEP_COMMAND_SPECS` in `packages/rph-application/src/handlers/`, F1 sites the equivalent `STEP_COMMAND_AUTHORITY` in `packages/rph-domain/`. VERIFIED: `.dependency-cruiser.cjs` `domain-purity` forbids rph-domain -> rph-projections, and `projections-browser-safe` permits rph-projections -> rph-domain (rph-projections/package.json depends on @janumipwb/rph-domain). So F4's siting makes the table unimportable by `transition-gate.ts`'s `PRUNABLE_SOURCE_STATES` (:23) and by `execution-view.ts`'s `CONTROL_BY_STEP_STATE` (:136) — the two OTHER hand-copies of the same source-state sets. RECONCILE: ONE table in `packages/rph-domain/src/step-command-spec.ts`, consumed by rph-application (advanceStep), rph-domain (transition-gate's prunable set), and rph-projections (affordances). F4's siting is rejected on layering grounds; F4's COLUMNS (sourceStates, target, eventType) merge with F1's (planLiveness, pwuOpenness, rationale, ruleIds) and F3's (branchDecision) and F6's (affordanceKey) into one row type.
- CONFLICT-8 — F2's Fix 3 (FAILED in-edge = PENDING) REMOVES the capability 'continue around a permanently failed arm', and its replacement is incomplete across three planes. F2's Fix 7 adds the FAILED->CANCELLED machine arrow and widens `cancelExecutionStep.requireFrom`, but VERIFIED: `CONTROL_BY_STEP_STATE.FAILED = []` (execution-view.ts:141, deliberately NOT machine-derived per the F-11 discipline) so no button is minted, and F6 owns that table. Separately, `cancelExecutionStep` has NO plan-ACTIVE precheck by explicit design (execution.ts:991-997, 'Cancel is CLEANUP... terminating an existing step') — but a FAILED step is ALREADY terminal, so cancelling it re-writes a closed attempt's outcome, and ungated it can be done on a SUPERSEDED/CANCELLED plan. RECONCILE (WP-5, ONE package): machine arrow + requireFrom widening + `CONTROL_BY_STEP_STATE.FAILED = ['cancel']` + the demo button + a plan-ACTIVE gate ON THE FAILED SOURCE ONLY (the four pre-existing live sources stay ungated, which is the anti-regression assertion F-12 says is missing today). WP-4 (FAILED=PENDING) MUST NOT LAND WITHOUT WP-5.
- CONFLICT-9 — F5's at-rest rule and F4's conformance sweep are mutually exclusive as designed. F5 FIX-2 refuses any proposed step whose `stepState !== 'QUEUED'`; F4's kill tests K3 (Skip from NOT_READY), K8 (Retry from READY) and four positive-sweep cases (Skip<-READY, Cancel<-READY, Prune<-NOT_READY, Prune<-READY — three of F-40's four named uncovered non-SUPERSEDED arrows) SEED those states through propose. RECONCILE: F5's `__tests__/plan-fixtures.ts` seeding seam IS F4's arrangement mechanism, and it must land FIRST (WP-0), before either rule. F4's sweep is written against `seedStepStates` / `seedBelowQueuedStepState_LEGACY_ONLY`, never against propose. Two additional corrections are mandatory: `seedStepStates` must itself `ExecutionPlanSchema.parse` the patched state before `store.commit` (which bypasses kit.ts:264's `validateAgainst`), and must mint a unique `commandId`/`idempotencyKey` per call (the adapter keys receipts by idempotencyKey and helpers patch repeatedly within one test).
- CONFLICT-10 — `condition-grammar.ts` (223 lines) is edited by FOUR families. F5 FIX-4 (`state: z.string()` -> `StepStateSchema`, line 35) and FIX-6 (`.min(1)` on ALL/ANY operands, lines 55-56); F4 FIX-7 (export `NumericComparatorSchema`, line 16, for an enum-driven truth table); F3 (INV-B3 one-evaluation-site, and the `guardEvaluatorFor` dual-source seam); F2 Fix 6 (the F-46 comment + pin at line 121-122). None conflict semantically but all four touch the same 40 lines. RECONCILE: ONE grammar package (WP-7) carrying all four edits plus the SHARED wrapper both F3's critique and F5's critique independently demand — `evaluateGuardExpression(expr, subject)` = safeParse THEN all `conditionStepRefs` resolve in subject THEN evaluate — with BOTH `guardEvaluatorFor` (execution.ts:113-121) and `conditionEvaluatorFor` (execution-view.ts:256-263) delegating to it. That single wrapper closes F5's undisclosed twin of F-22 (an unresolvable `stepId` is fail-OPEN under NOT for every stored plan, exactly as a typo'd `state` was) and removes the duplicated safeParse-then-evaluate posture that is the family's own root-cause pattern one layer down.
- CONFLICT-11 — F1's FIX-5 (skip authorization) is UNIMPLEMENTABLE as specified and must be re-scoped before it is sequenced. VERIFIED: `approveDecision` (governance.ts:245-257) carries an explicit kind predicate refusing any decision whose `decisionType === 'WAIVER'` ('a waiver becomes effective only via GrantWaiver'), and the only WAIVER->EFFECTIVE path (RequestWaiver -> GrantWaiver) mandates the assurance-plane fields `waivedPolicyId`/`waivedCriterionId`/`waivedFindingIds` — the exact INV-5 bleed FIX-5 exists to prevent. So kill test 8 `skip-auth/valid-waiver-ACCEPTS` cannot be built. RECONCILE: scope the execution-skip authorization to `decisionType === 'REPLAN'` ONLY (mintable today via ProposeDecision -> ApproveDecision, since REPLAN passes the kind predicate), drop WAIVER from the accepted-kind set with the reason recorded in the register row, and correct the design's stated precedent — `ProposeDecisionPayloadSchema` has NO `waiver` field, so 'add the field mirroring waiver' mirrors nothing.
- CONFLICT-12 — F1's FIX-6 (one success definition) would BREAK the reference seed if implemented as a standing invariant, and F1's FIX-4 safety argument depends on the false reading. VERIFIED: `rejectUnbackedExecutionSuccess` (pwu.ts:677) opens `if (p.executionState !== 'SUCCEEDED' || currentExecutionState === 'SUCCEEDED') return undefined` — an ENTRY gate. The seed re-asserts `executionState: 'SUCCEEDED'` at reference-undertaking.ts:676, :698, :806 and :869 with `supportingObjectIds` naming EVIDENCE/ASSESSMENTS, not the plan. Removing the early exit reds the rph-engine suite. RECONCILE: state FIX-6 explicitly as an ENTRY gate, retain the early exit as load-bearing, cite those four seed lines, restate INV-ONE-SUCCESS-DEFINITION as an entry invariant, and add a named DISCLOSED test `unbacked/reissue-after-supersede-is-NOT-rechecked`. Correspondingly DOWNGRADE F1's FIX-4 claim that 'the live-strand scenario becomes UNREACHABLE once FIX-6 lands' to 'materially reduced; the CLEANUP exemptions remain the only guarantee'.
- CONFLICT-13 — F6's INV-BND-2 allowlist clause is DEAD CODE as specified, and F6's own fix re-opens F-29. VERIFIED: `activateExecutionPlan` (execution.ts:376-427) passes `eventPayload` but NO `mutate` to `advanceStatus`, so `authorizedRuntimeBindingIds` lands ONLY on the `ExecutionPlanActivated` event and NEVER on the plan aggregate — `plan.authorizedRuntimeBindingIds` reads `undefined` forever and F6's kill test 5 would FAIL (the command is ACCEPTED). `advanceStatus` DOES support `mutate` (kit.ts:471, :540), so the fix is two lines. Separately: `startableStepIds` (transition-gate.ts:301) knows nothing about runtime bindings, so after INV-BND-2 the UI renders Start for a step whose binding is REQUESTED/DENIED and the engine refuses — the identical F-11 breach F-29 exists to close, re-opened by this family's own fix. RECONCILE (WP-14): add `mutate` to activateExecutionPlan with its own kill test asserting the field is on the loaded state; and EITHER thread a binding-authorization resolver into the gate/read-model OR disclose the Start-affordance exemption in the INV-AFF-1 allowlist (see CONFLICT-14).
- CONFLICT-14 — F6's INV-AFF-1 biconditional makes its own headline conformance test vacuous, and F1's FIX-3 proposes a SEVENTH copy of the plan-status rule. `engineAccepted === readModelOffers` is FALSE today for at least six preconditions that are not functions of (planStatus, stepState): the retry cap (`retryDecision`, and `execution-tier3.e2e.ts:155-168` DEPENDS on Retry being rendered at cap), the §8.4 floor gate on Complete, `canSkipStep`'s waiver rule, the start-gate, the prunability fixpoint, and INV-BND-2 itself.
