RECONCILE: restate as a ONE-WAY implication over the (planStatus, stepState) axis only — `readModelOffers(cmd) => the engine does not refuse for a (planStatus, stepState) reason` — with an explicitly ENUMERATED allowlist of the non-state preconditions permitted to refuse an offered affordance; the conformance test asserts on the refusal CODE/reason class, not on acceptance. And F1's read-model mirror must consume `STEP_COMMAND_SPECS` (CONFLICT-7) rather than re-deriving, or the plan-status rule exists in seven places: five inlined prechecks, `startableStepIds`/`prunableStepIds`, `CONTROL_BY_STEP_STATE`'s prose, and F1's new table.

### Shared mechanisms
- SM-1 `GateContext` — the single canonical liveness/disposition computation (packages/rph-domain/src/transition-gate.ts). Built ONCE per public entry point; carries stepById / inEdgesById / outEdgesById indexes (deleting the O(E) `inEdgesOf`/`outEdgesOf` array filters at :76-85, with `outEdgesById` PRESERVING the half-edge exclusion whose rationale is recorded at :79-83), two identity-keyed memos (edge disposition, guard result), and the two BFS closures. Exposes: `entryStepIds`, `localDisposition(edge)`, `effectiveDisposition(edge)`, `liveness(stepId): 'LIVE'|'DEAD'|'INDETERMINATE'`, `stepStartability(step)`, `pruneProvenanceFor(stepId)`. EVERY public verdict — `startStepGate`, `startableStepIds`, `prunableStepIds`, `edgeDispositions`, prune provenance — is one function evaluated over one context, so the gate and the prune set CANNOT disagree by construction (F-17/F-20 become unrepresentable), the F-34 cubic recomputation disappears (each guard evaluated at most once per edge per call), and F3's UNRESOLVED, F2's REOPENABLE and F6's provenance are three rungs/derivations of ONE ladder rather than three predicates. Kill-tested by a deterministic call-count assertion (an instrumented evaluator invoked <=1x per guarded edge — 101x today), not by wall-clock.
- SM-2 `STEP_COMMAND_SPECS` — the single declaration of every step command's contract (packages/rph-domain/src/step-command-spec.ts; rph-domain because rph-projections MAY depend on it and rph-domain MAY NOT depend on rph-application — verified against .dependency-cruiser.cjs and the package.json graph). One row per command, typed `Readonly<Record<StepCommandType, StepCommandSpec>>` so a tenth step command is a COMPILE ERROR until classified on every axis: `{ commandType, target, eventType, sourceStates: readonly [string, ...string[]], planLiveness: 'REQUIRES_ACTIVE_PLAN'|'CLEANUP_EXEMPT', pwuOpenness: 'REQUIRES_OPEN_PWU'|'CLEANUP_EXEMPT', branchDecision: 'RECORD_AT_SETTLEMENT'|'NONE_STRUCTURALLY_DEAD'|'N_A', affordanceKey, rationale, ruleIds }`. It replaces FOUR hand-copies of the same sets that are verified to exist and to have already drifted: the nine inline `requireFrom` literals; `PRUNABLE_SOURCE_STATES` (transition-gate.ts:23, whose own docstring says it exists to prevent the divergence it is unbound against); `CONTROL_BY_STEP_STATE`/`ADVANCE_BY_STEP_STATE` (execution-view.ts:118-147); and the five inlined `plan.status !== 'ACTIVE'` prechecks. A gate-step-0 assertion binds it to the ratified vocab `drivesFrom`, which is currently STALE in two places (verified: Start vocab READY|QUEUED vs code QUEUED; Prune vocab READY|QUEUED vs code NOT_READY|READY|QUEUED) — the code is right and the vocab is corrected, because the vocab's implied READY->RUNNING is not an arrow in the m2 machine at all.
- SM-3 `advanceStep` v2 — one primitive with a total, compile-required argument and one adjudicated evaluation order (packages/rph-application/src/handlers/execution.ts:598-676). Args become `{ spec: StepCommandSpec; eventPayload | eventPayloadFrom(nextStep, plan); branchDecision (required, and only expressible, on the SUCCEEDED|SKIPPED arm of a union discriminated on spec.target); precheck? }`. DELETED: `target`, `eventType`, `requireFrom`, `mutateStep` (the optional hook through which Skip silently opted out of the branch-decision rule — F3's own diagnosis), and the `eventPayload !== undefined ? : command.payload` fallback at :665 that substitutes the COMMAND contract for the EVENT contract (F-25's root cause, live on Fail and Retry). ORDER: sourceStates -> declared authority (planLiveness, then pwuOpenness) -> precheck -> checkTransition -> spec-driven mutation + settlement view -> event -> commit. `eventPayloadFrom` receives the POST-mutation step, so a derived payload field (branch selection, binding, prune provenance) cannot drift from what is persisted; it receives the PRE-mutation plan, because that is the state the gate authorized against.
- SM-4 `validateProposedPlan` — one propose-time contract validator (packages/rph-domain/src/plan-proposal.ts, pure). Absorbs the three private handler validators verbatim (message- and code-preserving, so all five existing rejection tests pass UNEDITED) and adds the step-set rules nothing owned: L1 steps non-empty; L2 per-step id well-formed / plan-coherent / stepState at-rest QUEUED / no authored `selectedTransitionId`; L3 step-id uniqueness; L4 per-transition id/planId + the CONDITIONAL<=>conditionExpression biconditional; L5 transition-id uniqueness; L6 grammar parse + stepId resolution; L7 `validateTransitionGraph` (unchanged, still a no-op for a linear plan — now harmless because L1-L6 are unconditional). ProposeExecutionPlan is the ONLY writer of `steps[]`/`transitions[]` (verified: the other writes at :659 and :769 rewrite one existing element), so this is the single decision point for structural admissibility. Its anti-vacuity test is a DUPLICATE STEP ID on a plan with `transitions: []` — RED under any mutant that reintroduces shape-conditional validation.
- SM-5 `evaluateGuardExpression(expression, subject)` — one hardened guard-evaluation wrapper (packages/rph-domain/src/condition-grammar.ts). safeParse -> every `conditionStepRefs(parsed)` id resolves in the subject -> `evaluateCondition`. Both `guardEvaluatorFor` (rph-application execution.ts:113-121) and `conditionEvaluatorFor` (rph-projections execution-view.ts:256-263) delegate to it, collapsing a DUPLICATED safeParse-then-evaluate posture that can drift. It closes F-22 AND its undisclosed structural twin that neither family fixed: an unresolvable `stepId` yields `false` (condition-grammar.ts:117-128) and therefore TRUE under `NOT` (:134) for every STORED plan — identical mechanism, identical permanence via `selectedTransitionId`, and guarded today only by a propose-time check that by construction cannot reach stored plans. It also fixes the F3 dual-source seam: `EdgeGuardEvaluator`'s `plan` parameter is currently DEAD (the evaluator reads a subject frozen at construction), which is precisely why `completeExecutionStep`'s hand-patched GatePlan silently loses.
- SM-6 One conformance harness + three registry-totality gates (packages/rph-application/src/handlers/). A single `Harness` (dispatch / stateOf / eventsOfType / revisionOf, modelled on dwp05-precondition-coverage.test.ts) drives ONE table-driven sweep over THREE registries: (a) STEP_COMMAND_SPECS -> for each command, one negative per residual state (`admissibleSources(target) \ sourceStates`) asserting REJECTED + RPH_ILLEGAL_STATE_TRANSITION + a >=20-char marker string producible ONLY by the sourceStates branch (so a precheck- or machine-refused case classifies MASKED and FAILS), plus one positive per declared source asserting ACCEPTED; (b) STEP_EVENT_EFFECT -> every `/^ExecutionStep/` key in the generated event registry (TEN, not nine: Cancelled, Pruned, Failed, Ready, Retried, Skipped, Started, Succeeded, Waiting, WaitResolved) has a DECLARED attempt-fold effect; (c) ENFORCEMENT_REGISTER -> every rule whose ratified `then` reads 'the command is rejected' has a named production refusal observed end-to-end through Engine.dispatch, with `declaredMutations` as data. The gate's own analysis primitives get a selftest driven by LITERAL synthetic input (the recursion terminates at pure functions). F2's linear/graph parity matrix and the INV-GATE-1' property test stay SEPARATE and pure — they are properties of a pure function, and running them through an Engine would only make them slower and less total.
- SM-7 `__tests__/plan-fixtures.ts` — the test seeding seam (packages/rph-application/src/handlers/__tests__/, the established test-only home per the floor-fixtures.ts precedent; excluded from tsconfig.build and from vitest's *.test.ts include). `seedStepStates(store, planId, states)` patches step states via `store.commit` with a synthetic receipt and THROWS on NOT_READY/READY (the two states the bus genuinely cannot reach — without that refusal the seam becomes the back door that reinstates the defect); `seedBelowQueuedStepState_LEGACY_ONLY` and `seedEmptyStepArray_LEGACY_ONLY` are the loudly-named exceptions covering the population propose can no longer create. It must itself `ExecutionPlanSchema.parse` the patched state (store.commit bypasses kit.ts:264's validateAgainst) and mint a unique commandId/idempotencyKey per call. It is a hard prerequisite for BOTH F5's at-rest rule and F4's conformance sweep — the test corpus's dependence on the missing rule (four helpers, ~35 arrangements across execution-start-gate / execution-plan-completion / dwp05-precondition-coverage / execution-plan-supersede) is exactly why the rule was never written.
- SM-8 `stepAffordances` / `executionPlanAffordances` / `planAffordancesFor` — one affordance projection derived from SM-2 (packages/rph-projections/src/execution-view.ts). `stepView` becomes plan-aware (`ExecutionPlanInput.status` is already present and unused at the exact construction site, :195), the machine-legal helpers `advanceCommandsFor`/`controlCommandsFor` become MODULE-PRIVATE (verified: their only importer is execution-view.test.ts, so un-exporting costs nothing and removes the loaded gun that caused F-29 — a corrected docstring is the same prose-warning mechanism that failed at execution-attempts.ts:137), and the demo template LOSES all five of its own affordance conditions (+page.svelte:263, :275, :329, :349, :363) rather than gaining a sixth. The ungated `{:else}` at :336-341 — the actual F-29 site — then becomes safe unchanged, which is the point: the fix is the removal of the conditions, not another one.

### Work packages

#### WP-0 — Baseline evidence, test seeding seam, and fixture migration
- *covers:* No findings. Enables WP-5 (propose at-rest rule) and WP-9 (requireFrom sweep). Builds SM-7.
- *rationale:* Two of the three biggest rules in this remediation are currently BLOCKED by the test corpus's dependence on the absence of the rule: ~35 arrangements across four suites seed non-QUEUED step states THROUGH ProposeExecutionPlan, and F4's conformance sweep needs NOT_READY/READY steps that F5's rule forbids. Landing the seam first removes the temptation to weaken the rule to keep tests green — the exact inversion that produced the family. Ships behaviour-neutral, so it can land alone and be verified independently. Also lands the RED baselines that prove the family exists: F2's linear/graph parity matrix (RED on FAILED today) and the biconditional replacing the vacuous `ok || !startable.has(id)` pin at transition-gate.test.ts:166-172.
- *dependsOn:* none
- *concurrentWith:* WP-1
- *gate:* Full vitest across rph-domain/application/projections/contracts/persistence + rph-engine 69 (must be untouched). No regen. No e2e. Named RED baselines recorded, not fixed. Run under Node/vitest (better-sqlite3 refuses Bun).

#### WP-1 — Vocab + regeneration batch (ALL contract field changes, one commit)
- *covers:* Contract half of F-01, F-15/21/23, F-25, F-30, F-31, F-37.
- *rationale:* Every downstream handler package needs new optional payload fields, and `ExecutionStepSucceeded` is the ONLY step event in RATIFIED_EVENT_PAYLOADS (messages.ts:2484) so kit.ts's (d2) gate strict-parses it at commit — emitting `selectedTransitionId` before regen makes EVERY BRANCH CompleteExecutionStep reject at RUNTIME, not merely fail a conformance test, and the reference seed authors no BRANCH so it gives zero signal. Batching all vocab edits into one regen also avoids the bisect-destroying pattern of three separate `bun run gen` commits. Contents: NoOutputResult helper subtype + `noOutputResult` on CompleteExecutionStep/ExecutionStepSucceeded; `selectedTransitionId` on ExecutionStepSucceeded and ExecutionStepSkipped (the single settlement of CONFLICT-4); `excludedEdgeId` on ExecutionStepPruned and REMOVAL of `selectedByBranchStepId`/`selectedEdgeId` from the PruneExecutionStep COMMAND payload; `retryReason` on ExecutionStepRetried; `ExecutionSkipAuthorization` on DECISION/ProposeDecision/DecisionProposed; correction of the two STALE `drivesFrom` entries (StartExecutionStep READY|QUEUED -> QUEUED; PruneExecutionStep READY|QUEUED -> NOT_READY|READY|QUEUED). NOTE: helperSubTypes is a JSON ARRAY of {name,...} objects, not a map; and a new helper requires the FULL `bun run gen` chain (gen-enums -> gen-objects -> gen-messages), never gen:messages alone, because gen-messages resolves a helper name to <Name>Schema only if it already exists in the generated objects.ts.
- *dependsOn:* none
- *concurrentWith:* WP-0
- *gate:* `bun run gen` + prettier in the SAME commit (regen output is format-stale, not content-stale). Full vitest INCLUDING contracts/validate.test.ts registry-count pins and rph-engine's emitted-event-conformance + replay-conformance. Assert every new field is OPTIONAL and that no emitter yet produces it. No e2e.

#### WP-2 — GateContext + memoization (pure refactor, zero semantic change)
- *covers:* F-34.
- *rationale:* Builds SM-1's plumbing before any rule changes, so every later diff on this file is a RULE change rather than a rule change tangled with an indexing change. `inEdgeDisposition` currently calls `liveStepIds` afresh per edge (transition-gate.ts:217) while `inEdgesOf`/`outEdgesOf` are full array scans, giving the measured 226-244 ms per start-gate call at fan-out 200 against the module's own O(V+E) header claim; `prunableStepIds` already hoists correctly (1.1 ms on the byte-identical plan), which is the internal A/B isolating the cause. Must preserve `outEdgesById`'s half-edge exclusion, whose rationale at :79-83 records a prior BLOCKER (a half-edge won first-match and neutralized every real arm). Semantics are unchanged by construction for any pure guard evaluator, which is every evaluator in the system.
- *dependsOn:* WP-0
- *concurrentWith:* WP-1, WP-8
- *gate:* rph-domain + rph-projections vitest. Deterministic call-count kill test (instrumented evaluator invoked at most once per guarded edge; 101x today at width 100) — NOT a wall-clock test. Table asserting `edgeDispositions` agrees with `inEdgeDisposition` edge-by-edge across the fixture matrix. No regen, no seed re-drive, no e2e.

#### WP-3 — Entry seeding, one entry definition, and the graph-incoherence fail-closed floor
- *covers:* F-03, F-04, F-05 (three BLOCKERs, one mechanism).
- *rationale:* The highest-severity defect and the cheapest fix. `liveStepIds` seeds its BFS with `inEdgesOf(s).length === 0` (:139) while `buildAdjacency` (:483-488) counts only edges with BOTH endpoints, so a contract-legal, explicitly-blessed plan-entry edge (objects.ts:247; :467 'A missing SOURCE is legitimate') makes the frontier EMPTY, every source NEUTRALIZED and every step waiver-free prunable. ONE exported `entryStepIds` serves both propose-time counting and runtime seeding so the two planes cannot drift again. CRITICALLY, this package also lands the AMPLIFIER fix that F2's design omits and its critique demands: `validateTransitionGraph` runs only on the ProposeExecutionPlan PAYLOAD while `liveStepIds` runs on the PERSISTED bag via `toGatePlan`, so any legacy plan or future validation regression re-opens the identical universal waiver bypass. Fail closed at the CONSUMER: for a graph plan, `entryStepIds` empty while `steps.length > 0` => `prunableStepIds` returns [] and the gate refuses GRAPH_INCOHERENT. Also fixes the demo's truthy reshaping at +page.server.ts:137-138, which uses `t.sourceStepId ? ... : {}` AND `t.targetStepId ? ... : {}` — the second mints a HALF-EDGE on the display plane from an empty string.
- *dependsOn:* WP-2
- *concurrentWith:* WP-6, WP-7, WP-8
- *gate:* rph-domain + rph-application vitest. Kill test: entry-edge plan in a MULTI-step graph => startable=[s1], prunable=[], and the two sets disjoint; plus a persisted cyclic/entry-less GatePlan constructed directly (bypassing propose) => prunable=[] and GRAPH_INCOHERENT. Live-bus test through Engine + SqliteStorageAdapter. e2e: execution-flow + execution-sequencing (graph plans). No regen. Seed unaffected (transitions: [] everywhere) but re-drive rph-engine to prove it.

#### WP-4 — Canonical four-valued edge disposition + three-valued step liveness + coherence
- *covers:* F-06, F-16, F-17, F-20, F-38, F-46 (pin), and the READ half of F-15/F-21/F-23.
- *rationale:* The single largest semantic reconciliation, and the one that dissolves CONFLICT-1 and CONFLICT-2. Today ONE question ('can this in-edge conduct?') has THREE answers: `inEdgeDisposition` (:204, knows about terminal-non-success, BRANCH first-match, false guards, dead sources), `branchExcludes` (:162, knows about exactly ONE thing and returns false unless the source is TERMINAL_SUCCESS at :169), and the entry in-degree disagreement fixed in WP-3. Deleting `branchExcludes` and defining reachability AS the transitive closure of non-excluding dispositions makes the gate and the prune set the same computation, so F-17/F-20's contradiction becomes unrepresentable rather than merely repaired. The FOUR-valued alphabet carries F3's UNRESOLVED (a settled BRANCH with no recorded selection decides NOTHING — the first-match fallback at :115-118 is deleted so an unrecorded decision is a visible refusal rather than a silently re-derived, time-varying one) and F2's REOPENABLE=PENDING (FAILED is the machine's only terminal with an out-arrow, so a join must not release over it). The THREE-valued liveness is what lets UNRESOLVED be simultaneously not-startable and not-prunable without breaking the coherence biconditional. Ladder order is terminal-success BEFORE reopenable, and REOPENABLE is derived as `terminal AND NOT terminal-success AND has-out-arrow`, so a future out-arrow on SUCCEEDED/SKIPPED cannot wedge the whole system. The prune-refusal message must become code-derived with THREE branches (plan not ACTIVE / LIVE-so-Skip-instead / DEAD-but-RUNNING-or-WAITING-so-Cancel-instead), or F-20's exact shape survives on the path WP-4 newly creates. Includes the ~14 domain + 3 projection fixture migrations, each SPLIT into a write-plane test (`resolveBranchSelection` returns the expected edge — preserving first-match/ordering coverage) and a read-plane test (`inEdgeDisposition` honours the recorded id), so the migration does not silently delete the D3 ordering coverage it inherits.
- *dependsOn:* WP-3
- *concurrentWith:* WP-6, WP-7, WP-8
- *gate:* rph-domain + rph-application + rph-projections vitest. INV-GATE-1' property over an 8-plan fixture matrix INCLUDING both seed shapes. Linear/graph parity matrix iterated over STEP_MACHINE.states (RED on FAILED today). Machine-derivation pin (REOPENABLE == {FAILED}; CANCELLED/SUPERSEDED have no out-arrows). Live six-command F-06 sequence. Causality assertion for F-16 (the join's Succeeded revision > every arm Started revision). MUST NOT LAND WITHOUT WP-5 in the same increment — see CONFLICT-8. e2e: execution-flow + execution-tier3. rph-engine re-drive.

#### WP-5 — FAILED-arm abandonment across all three planes (the capability WP-4 removes)
- *covers:* No finding of its own; required by the no-over-refusal constraint and by F-12's missing negative coverage.
- *rationale:* WP-4 makes a FAILED in-edge wedge its successors. Without an abandon primitive that is a strict capability loss: a plan whose one arm failed permanently could no longer drive its remaining independent work. Cancel is already the system's terminate-an-existing-step verb. But shipping the machine arrow alone is ALSO a net loss, because `CONTROL_BY_STEP_STATE.FAILED = []` (execution-view.ts:141) is DELIBERATELY not machine-derived (the F-11 discipline), and `Record<StepState,...>` totality does not catch the omission since `[]` is a legal value — so the operator would see a wedged join, a `retry` affordance, and no way to abandon. All three planes ship together: m2-transitions.json FAILED->CANCELLED arrow (regen); `cancelExecutionStep.requireFrom` widened; `CONTROL_BY_STEP_STATE.FAILED = ['cancel']` + the demo button. ONE addition the designs missed: gate the FAILED source (and only that source) on plan-ACTIVE — a FAILED step is already TERMINAL, so cancelling it re-writes a closed attempt's outcome, and `cancelExecutionStep` has no plan-status precheck by explicit design (execution.ts:991-997) for a rationale that does not cover a terminal source.
- *dependsOn:* WP-4
- *concurrentWith:* WP-6, WP-7
- *gate:* `bun run gen:transitions` + prettier. Full vitest INCLUDING rph-engine's transitions/emitted-event/replay-conformance suites (they read the generated machine). Re-issue kill test (a second Cancel on a CANCELLED step refuses and appends no second event) plus one positive per source state in the widened set — a widened requireFrom with no re-issue test is the exact unkilled-mutant shape this repo has been bitten by. Explicit `controlCommandsFor('FAILED')).toContain('cancel')`. e2e: fail an arm, cancel it, start the join. rph-engine 69 must be green with no edit.

#### WP-6 — Propose-time contract validator: one carrier + the step-set rules
- *covers:* F-09, F-10, F-27 (by prevention), F-33.
- *rationale:* Builds SM-4. ProposeExecutionPlan is the ONLY writer of steps[]/transitions[] yet runs three ad-hoc validators, all transition-scoped, and the one structural validator SHORT-CIRCUITS on `transitions.length === 0` — so a LINEAR plan (the shape every other suite uses, and the shape the reference seed uses) receives literally no structural validation. The at-rest `stepState === 'QUEUED'` rule closes TWO defects with one guard: the F-09/F-27 below-QUEUED deadlock, and the fail-OPEN half no finding raised as its own item — propose ['SUCCEEDED','SUCCEEDED'], approve, activate, CompleteExecutionPlan is ACCEPTED today with no step ever started, because the completion allow-list (:483-491) reads only authored strings. That is why the rule is 'exactly QUEUED', not 'not below QUEUED': the obvious narrower fix satisfies a sampled test and reopens the bypass. DISPOSITION on F-27: closed by PREVENTION for new plans only — no command drives a live-path NOT_READY step out (the NOT_READY->SKIPPED arrow needs `!live.has(id)`), so stored plans keep the deadlock and the arrow is RECLASSIFIED as a legacy-recovery path, not deleted. UNCENSUSED BREAKAGE to fix in the same commit: lifecycle.test.ts:95 and :128 both propose `steps: []` through the real bus and assert ACCEPTED (verified) — give each one QUEUED step, since neither reads steps.
- *dependsOn:* WP-0, WP-3
- *concurrentWith:* WP-4, WP-7, WP-8
- *gate:* Full vitest. Anti-vacuity test = duplicate step id on a plan with `transitions: []` (RED under any mutant that folds step rules into validateTransitionGraph). Exhaustive table over all TEN StepStateSchema values (QUEUED accepted, nine refused) — a sampled test would pass the narrower wrong fix. Compile-time `assertNever` on the state switch. All five pre-existing propose-rejection assertions must pass UNEDITED (the relocation-is-message-preserving gate). No regen. e2e: execution-plan.e2e.ts:150 NOT_READY fixture migrates (and its three 'no action on step3' assertions are replaced by a propose-refusal assertion). rph-engine re-drive (the seed authors QUEUED, one step, transitions: [] — provably inert, but prove it).

#### WP-7 — Condition grammar hardening + the shared fail-closed evaluation wrapper
- *covers:* F-22, F-39, F-41, F-43, F-44, F-32, F-35, F-46 (disposition + pin).
- *rationale:* Builds SM-5, and dissolves CONFLICT-10 (four families in a 223-line file). Placement is the whole fix: `ConditionExpressionSchema` is safeParsed by THREE planes (propose, the runtime guard evaluator at execution.ts:119-120, and the read-model at execution-view.ts:261-262 and :301), so binding the enum in the SCHEMA hardens stored plans retroactively where a handler-side check would leave them fail-OPEN. The fail-OPEN direction is the point: a typo'd `state` under `NOT` (:134) is unconditionally TRUE, the wrong arm is selected, and DWP-09's resolved-once rule makes it permanent — verified reproducible with one missing letter in 'SKIPPED'. The shared wrapper additionally closes the STRUCTURAL TWIN neither family fixed: an unresolvable `stepId` yields false and therefore TRUE under NOT for every stored plan, guarded only by a propose-time check that cannot reach them (the evaluator's own docstring at :113-114 admits the dependency). `.min(1)` on ALL/ANY is fixed NARROWLY on the ANTI-VACUITY ground (an empty combinator is a construction slip, and `operands: []` appears nowhere repo-wide so the mutant is unkillable), explicitly NOT on the fail-open-guard ground the raisers claimed — tautology detection is undecidable and a partial detector catching `>= 0` but not `> -1` would itself be a vacuity magnet. The comparator truth table is hand-authored as an INDEPENDENT oracle with five pairwise-distinct three-probe signatures, which mechanically kills every cross-comparator and both constant mutants.
- *dependsOn:* WP-6
- *concurrentWith:* WP-4, WP-5, WP-8
- *gate:* rph-domain + rph-application + rph-projections vitest. `evaluateCondition`'s empty-combinator identity pinned SEPARATELY and labelled as unreachable-through-the-schema (so a .every/.some swap cannot pass silently). The stored-plan kill tests MUST be sited where the schema is actually in the call path — `conditionEvaluatorFor` in execution-view.test.ts, NOT transition-gate.test.ts, whose suite builds ad-hoc evaluators that never touch the schema. Delete the now-unreachable `'all of ()'` / `'any of ()'` render branches (execution-view.ts:319/321). No regen (condition-grammar.ts is explicitly hand-authored, DR-004 §19-B1). No e2e. rph-engine unaffected (no guards in the seed).

#### WP-8 — STEP_COMMAND_SPECS + advanceStep v2 (declared authority, adjudicated order, total arguments)
- *covers:* Structural enabler for F-11, F-12, F-13, F-14, F-18, F-19, F-25, F-26, F-28, F-40, F-42.
- *rationale:* Builds SM-2 and SM-3, and dissolves CONFLICT-3 and CONFLICT-7. Four families each redefine advanceStep's args; this is the one change. The compile-required `spec` reproduces SPEC-001 §5.4's INV-1 flip for the family that never got it ('no status advance can silently skip its declaration'), and `Record<StepCommandType, ...>` makes a tenth step command a COMPILE ERROR until classified on every axis. The ordering swap is the single highest-leverage line in the remediation: `requireFrom` runs AFTER `precheck` today (:645 vs :647), so a semantic rule can MASK the declared contract and a masked guard cannot be kill-tested through the bus. Honest scoping (traced case by case, correcting both designs): exactly ONE residual is masked today — Prune<-SKIPPED, because `prunableStepIds` filters `!TERMINAL.has(stepState)` so an already-pruned step answers 'still reachable' first. F-13's cited mask is a MISCOUNT (start-gate:699 targets a QUEUED step, which is IN Skip's set) and F-14's masking layer is the MACHINE, not a precheck. The invariant's real value is forward-looking: no FUTURE precheck can mask a residual, which matters because WP-11 and WP-12 both add prechecks. Gate step 0 binds the table to the ratified vocab `drivesFrom` and CORRECTS the two verified stale entries. Also binds `PRUNABLE_SOURCE_STATES` (transition-gate.ts:23) to the Prune row, removing the second unbound copy.
- *dependsOn:* WP-1
- *concurrentWith:* WP-2, WP-3, WP-4, WP-6, WP-7
- *gate:* Full vitest. MANDATORY pre-landing grep of every `error.code` / `error.message).toContain(` assertion on the nine step commands across packages + apps/rph-demo/e2e — survey already run and CLEAN (all message-asserting sites target in-set sources: skip-cancel:217, retry-cap:130-140, start-gate:626-627/689-690/708, sequencing.e2e:176), but note start-gate:295-296 asserts the drivesFrom message verbatim, so the marker refactor MUST preserve the `requires step X to be A or B, but it is C` prefix byte-for-byte. Fix the double dispatch at start-gate:866 (`expect(skip(2).status, JSON.stringify(skip(2)))` evaluates skip(2) twice; arguments evaluate left-to-right so the second is a real refused dispatch). Dispose of the now-provably-dead `checkTransition` call at :654 (dominated by sourceStates once every declared source is a LEGAL arrow) — delete it and record INV-4 as the reason, or keep it with an unreachability selftest; do not leave it silent. No regen. e2e full. rph-engine re-drive.

#### WP-9 — requireFrom kill-test battery + machine-arrow ledger
- *covers:* F-11, F-12, F-13, F-14, F-18, F-19, F-40, plus two genuinely-new gaps (Retry<-READY, Prune<-SKIPPED).
- *rationale:* The behavioural proof of WP-8. Thirteen residual states derived as `admissibleSources(target) \ sourceStates`; six already covered (CORRECTING the design's census: StartExecutionStep<-RUNNING is ALREADY fully covered at execution-start-gate.test.ts:291-298 with code + message + event-count assertions, so it is a strengthen, not a new test), five are the assigned findings, and two are new. Every case isolates ITS guard by construction: the source state is chosen from the machine-ADMISSIBLE set so checkTransition provably cannot refuse; the fixture is arranged so every precheck passes; and the assertion battery names the layer via a marker string producible only by the sourceStates branch. Must land AFTER WP-8 or the tests are written to the old ordering. The arrow ledger classifies all 20 declared arrows into COMMAND_BACKED / REFUSED_RESIDUAL / ORPHAN with an EXPLICIT precedence (COMMAND_BACKED if ANY spec backs the arrow, else REFUSED_RESIDUAL if any spec targets `to`, else ORPHAN) — without that precedence four arrows land in two buckets and the both-directions set equality fails. The six orphans (NOT_READY->READY and the five ->SUPERSEDED) are REGISTERED, not deleted: deleting a ratified DOC-003 catalog arrow needs canon adjudication, and the ledger makes their inertness a machine-checked fact that fails the moment any spec targets those states.
- *dependsOn:* WP-8
- *concurrentWith:* WP-10, WP-13
- *gate:* rph-application vitest. Per-case battery: REJECTED + RPH_ILLEGAL_STATE_TRANSITION + marker + rendered drivesFrom set + ZERO new events of any type + unchanged plan revision + unchanged stepState + the original event's reason/waitReason intact. Live mutation red-proof recorded per command (widen sourceStates, confirm exactly that command's test goes RED). No regen, no e2e, no seed change.

#### WP-10 — Settlement view + branch-decision totality (the decides-once family)
- *covers:* F-02, F-07, F-24 (BLOCKERs) and the WRITE half of F-15, F-21, F-23.
- *rationale:* Three masks of one defect: a fact that must be decided once is computed against a view missing the very move being made, by only one of the two commands that can make that move, and is never written to the stream. VERIFIED mechanism: `completeExecutionStep`'s mutateStep clones the GatePlan with stepState SUCCEEDED (:767-772), but `guardEvaluatorFor` (:108-122) IGNORES its `plan` argument and folds a subject frozen at construction, and `buildConditionSubject` writes `outputArtifactIds`/`structuredResult` ONLY from a COMMITTED ExecutionStepSucceeded — so RESULT_EQUALS and OUTPUT_COUNT over the completing step always evaluate against pre-completion facts and the default arm always wins. Fixed by threading the settlement view (committed steps with the acting step's stepState := target; committed log ++ the exact event being committed) through ONE evaluator seam, and by moving the decision INTO advanceStep keyed on `spec.branchDecision`, so Skip records too and Prune's silence is a COMPILE-REQUIRED declaration rather than an omission indistinguishable from the bug. Prune's silence is SAFE structurally, not by convention: the disposition ladder tests source liveness before any branch rung, and the BFS never expands a non-live node — pin both. Kill tests must assert the PROPERTY (recorded == recomputed-from-committed-log == on-the-settling-event), not a literal edge id, or they die only under the outcome mutation and not the mechanism mutation. Correct INV-B1's self-contradiction: the folded event equals the committed event on every field `foldExecutionEventInto` READS (stepId/executionStepId/outputArtifactIds/structuredResult), not byte-identically, because the decision is stamped onto the payload after the fold.
- *dependsOn:* WP-1, WP-4, WP-8
- *concurrentWith:* WP-9, WP-11, WP-13
- *gate:* rph-application + rph-domain vitest. Self-referential RESULT_EQUALS and OUTPUT_COUNT kill tests (RED today). Skip-records-its-decision test (RED today; both arms run). State-and-event-carry-the-same-decision test. Settlement-totality table over every command whose target is in TERMINAL_SUCCESS. Fold-ignores-selectedTransitionId pin (no fixpoint). Assert the emitted ExecutionStepSucceeded parses against its schema for a BRANCH completion (proves WP-1's regen actually landed). No regen (WP-1 did it). e2e: execution-flow BRANCH spec. rph-engine re-drive (seed has no BRANCH; both new fields are conditional and provably absent).

#### WP-11 — RPH-EXE-006 explicit result + the zero-subject floor gate
- *covers:* F-01 (BLOCKER, both limbs).
- *rationale:* `validateStepCompletion({ hasOutput, explicitNoOutput: !hasOutput })` at execution.ts:801-803 is `b || !b` — verified byte-exact. The kernel is correct; the caller supplies one fact twice, and the CONTRACT has no field for the second fact, so the distinction §2.6 requires is unrepresentable on the wire. That is why this needed WP-1's contract change and cannot be fixed in the handler alone. Consequence today: a completion naming nothing is ACCEPTED and, because zero subjects means zero floor-gate iterations, an AI-produced step ships a `structuredResult` into the governed stream with no assessable subject — and WP-10 gives that same `structuredResult` BRANCH-selection authority, which is why WP-11 must not land after WP-10. Honest scoping of the residual, which the design overclaims: after this fix an AI step can still reach SUCCEEDED with zero floor by asserting `noOutputResult` + empty `structuredResult`, and nothing in state can disagree with that assertion — a free caller assertion is structurally the same shape as `!!waiverOrRevisionId`. Add the state-derived corroboration limb both the design's critique and a fresh reading demand: a step that DECLARES `outputBindings` may not assert `noOutputResult` (free for every legitimate caller — the seed and the demo both pass `outputBindings: []`), and register the remaining residual explicitly. FIXTURE PINNING IS MANDATORY: the EXE-006 tests must use a NON-AI step with empty structuredResult so EXE-006 is the only reachable refusal, and the floor tests must use a payload EXE-006 accepts — otherwise the two limbs mutually mask and neither declared mutation is actually killed.
- *dependsOn:* WP-1, WP-8
- *concurrentWith:* WP-10, WP-12, WP-13
- *gate:* rph-application vitest. Four-cell EXE-006 table + the reason split + the outputBindings limb. Sixteen-cell pure unit table for `structuredResultHasContent` with a PINNED emptiness definition (content iff plain object with >=1 own key, OR array length > 0, OR string with a non-whitespace char; everything else including null/undefined/0/false/{}/[]/'' is EMPTY) — the designs define this inconsistently and a stricter reading refuses the demo and the seed. REQUIRED same-commit caller updates: execution-start-gate.test.ts:88-104's `complete` helper (the completion path for a 42-test file, whose comment currently TEACHES the bypass), +page.server.ts:548-563 and :671-690 (the demo's primary Execute and Complete buttons both refuse without it), execution-plan.e2e.ts:332-352. reference-undertaking.ts:604-613 needs NO change (passes proposedEvidenceIds). e2e full. rph-engine re-drive.

#### WP-12 — Plan liveness, PWU openness, one success definition, and resolved skip authorization
- *covers:* F-08 (BLOCKER), F-26, F-28, F-30, F-42 (disposition).
- *rationale:* Four rules that all consume WP-8's spec table, so they land together rather than as four more inlined prechecks. F-26: the axis is not 'opens new work' but 'mints success credit' — Skip and Prune open no work yet both carry the plan-ACTIVE gate, because both mint terminal-success; Complete mints terminal-success AND a durable BRANCH decision AND is the fact the PWU claim rests on, and is verified ACCEPTED on a SUPERSEDED plan today. Fail's omission is CORRECT (a system that makes failure harder to report than success is worse than one that checks neither) and merely undeclared — it becomes a DECLARED CLEANUP_EXEMPT row whose positive test is its own kill test. F-42 is disposed as RECORD-ONLY (code unchanged; amend DS-004 §6 D6, DR-004 :177/:185/:191/:199 and add the divergence to DR-004 §19, which today affirmatively certifies 'none intended'). F-28 wires `canResumeExecutionOnPwu`, which has exactly two repo-wide references — its definition and its own unit test — while the conformance manifest certifies it COVERED, so a pure-predicate unit test was accepted as evidence for a rule whose ratified `then` is 'the command is rejected'. Gate BOTH activation and the step commands: activation-only leaves the hole where a plan was activated before the PWU closed. Label the {BASELINED,ABANDONED,SUPERSEDED} generalisation as an AUTHORED extension with its own DR-004 §19 entry — RPH-PWU-010 names BASELINED only. F-08 is an ENTRY gate (see CONFLICT-12). F-30 is scoped to REPLAN only (see CONFLICT-11).
- *dependsOn:* WP-1, WP-8, WP-10
- *concurrentWith:* WP-11, WP-13, WP-14
- *gate:* Full vitest. Matrix test generated FROM the spec table proving TOTALITY ONLY; the CLASSIFICATION is proved by HARDCODED named cases that do NOT read the table (complete-under-superseded-REJECTS, fail-under-superseded-ACCEPTS, cancel-under-superseded-ACCEPTS, wait-under-cancelled-ACCEPTS, branch-complete-under-superseded-records-no-selection) — otherwise mutating a row flips the generated expectation too and the matrix stays GREEN, which is the tautology family recurring inside its own remedy. F-28's test drives the FULL legitimate path to BASELINED (no shortcuts) so RPH-EXE-001 cannot masquerade as the guard. Skip-authorization: one case per refusal branch (nonexistent / wrong type / wrong kind / not EFFECTIVE / other plan / other step / expired-vs-command-issuedAt / valid REPLAN / optional-with-garbage-id / optional-no-id). Retire the two stale records asserting canResumeExecutionOnPwu is DEFERRED (hollow-kernel-triage.md:45, dead-kernel-census.txt:17) in the SAME commit. No regen (WP-1). e2e full. rph-engine re-drive (seed reaches BASELINED only after execution).

#### WP-13 — Event payload projections + total attempt fold
- *covers:* F-25, F-36, F-45.
- *rationale:* Two shapes of one defect: an implicit default substitutes one contract for another when a handler says nothing (`eventPayload ?? command.payload`, removed structurally by WP-8), and a projection folds 3 of the 10 ExecutionStep* events with the other seven silently skipped. `ExecutionStepFailed`/`ExecutionStepRetried` are rejected by their own declared schemas on two counts (missing required `stepState`; Retried is a strictObject carrying an unrecognized `retryReason`) and neither is in RATIFIED_EVENT_PAYLOADS, so the (d2) gate is a no-op for them and the violation commits silently. THE CORRUPTION BUG BOTH DESIGNS MISS, verified: `applyStepFailed`/`applyStepSucceeded` never clear `state.current`, and Start->Fail->Retry->Cancel is fully legal (retry drives FAILED->QUEUED; cancel's requireFrom includes QUEUED) — so a naive `ExecutionStepCancelled -> CLOSE` looks up the already-CLOSED attempt #1 and rewrites it FAILED->CANCELLED, moving its completedAt. The effect table must test OPENNESS (`draft.completedAt === undefined`), not presence, with `[Started, Failed, Retried, Cancelled]` as a named kill test. P-ATT-1 must be restated ATTEMPT-scoped for the same reason — as written ('the last attempt's state equals the stepState the last event drove to') it is FALSE on that sequence and would DRIVE an implementer to the corrupting implementation. `AttemptEffect` must carry per-entry key extractors (Succeeded reads `executionStepId`, Failed `failureReason`, Cancelled `reason`, Skipped `waiverOrRevisionId`) or the table is not actually total. The conformance sweep that certified 'every event conforms' is VACUOUS for ten of eleven step event types because it runs only over the reference stream, which never fails, retries, skips, prunes, cancels, waits or branches — so the fix is a second drive plus a PINNED observed-type denominator.
- *dependsOn:* WP-1, WP-8
- *concurrentWith:* WP-9, WP-10, WP-11, WP-12
- *gate:* rph-application + rph-projections + rph-engine vitest. Registry-totality test enumerating `/^ExecutionStep/` from the generated registry (TEN types) against the effect table. Emitted-payload safeParse for every step event driven through the REAL dispatch path, never a hand-built stream. The scoped runtime step-event gate lands LAST and only after the corners drive is green — its zero-over-refusal claim is currently inspection, not measurement. Do NOT hand-add to RATIFIED_EVENT_PAYLOADS (generated from vocab provenance; a hand-add is reverted by the next gen, and ratifying the vocab entry would mint an invented governance fact). No regen (WP-1). e2e: assert a WAITING step's attempt row shows WAITING while its step row shows WAITING. rph-engine re-drive.

#### WP-14 — Binding provenance + prune provenance (derived facts, never caller assertions)
- *covers:* F-31, F-37.
- *rationale:* Two never-populated fields whose emptiness makes an advisory unconditionally true and an event's minting justification undelivered. F-31's disposition is ARGUED, not defaulted: do NOT add `runtimeBindingId` to StartExecutionStep's payload — the ratified relation is Binding->Step (RequestRuntimeBinding names an executionStepId), `ExecutionStepSchema` already carries the field, and three consumers already read `step.runtimeBindingId` as authoritative, so a per-invocation binding on the Start command would be a second, later, unauthorized source of truth for a fact the approved plan already fixes; the strictObject accidentally prevents exactly that. The producer therefore reads the AUTHORED step. MANDATORY ADDITION the design misses: `authorizedRuntimeBindingIds` is verified NEVER written to the plan aggregate (activateExecutionPlan passes eventPayload but no `mutate`, though advanceStatus supports one at kit.ts:471), so the allowlist clause is dead code and its kill test would FAIL — add the `mutate` with its own assertion that the field is on the loaded state. Prune provenance is DERIVED from the same context that authorized the prune (WP-2's GateContext already computed `live`), never read from the payload; the cut predicate must include `NOT live.has(e.targetStepId)` or a JOIN still reachable via the taken arm is mis-attributed; and the pruned step is frequently NOT adjacent to the BRANCH, so provenance walks the dead subgraph to the cut rather than reading the step's own in-edges.
- *dependsOn:* WP-1, WP-4, WP-8
- *concurrentWith:* WP-12, WP-13
- *gate:* rph-application + rph-domain vitest. Producer test (RED today: the emitted Started payload is byte-for-byte `{stepId, stepState:'RUNNING'}`). Non-vacuity test converting execution-attempts.test.ts:100 from fiction to fact — that test currently passes only because its helper hand-crafts an event shape NO producer can emit (CON-000 B7). Schema-boundary tests pinning BOTH argued dispositions (StartExecutionStep + runtimeBindingId => RPH_VALIDATION_SCHEMA_FAILED; PruneExecutionStep + selectedEdgeId => same). Prune-provenance payload deep-equality plus the TRANSITIVE case. No-over-refusal case with the seed's exact shape (unbound MODEL_INVOCATION step, empty allowlist) => ACCEPTED. No regen (WP-1). e2e full. rph-engine re-drive: the seed sets no step-level runtimeBindingId (grep-verified zero hits) so both new payload fields are provably absent from its stream — the replay fixture oracle must be READ before landing, not discovered at gate time.

#### WP-15 — Affordance fidelity — one plan-aware projection, zero UI-side conditions
- *covers:* F-29.
- *rationale:* Builds SM-8. `advanceCommandsFor(stepState)` mirrors a precondition over (planStatus, stepState) but takes only stepState, and the Svelte `{:else}` at +page.svelte:336-341 renders the set unconditionally, so Retry is offered on a FAILED step under a CANCELLED/SUPERSEDED plan and the engine refuses — a direct violation of DWP-06's own declared F-11 invariant. The plan status is already sitting unused on `ExecutionPlanInput.status` at the exact construction site. Lands last on the UI track because it must consume WP-8's spec table (not re-derive a seventh copy of the plan-status rule) and WP-5's FAILED cancel affordance. Deleting all five Svelte affordance conditions (:263, :275, :329, :349, :363) rather than adding a sixth is what makes the invariant structural. The plan-blind helpers become module-private — their only importer is their own test, so it costs nothing and removes the loaded gun. INV-AFF-1 ships as a ONE-WAY implication with an enumerated allowlist of the six non-state preconditions permitted to refuse an offered affordance (retry cap, floor gate, canSkipStep waiver, start gate, prunability fixpoint, binding authorization) — the biconditional is false today and would make the conformance test scoped-to-pass. Also DISCLOSE or thread: after WP-14 the projection cannot see PWU lifecycle state at all (ExecutionPlanInput carries no such field), so either thread it in this commit or record that the UI will offer Start on a terminal-PWU plan the engine refuses.
- *dependsOn:* WP-5, WP-8
- *concurrentWith:* WP-12, WP-13, WP-14
- *gate:* rph-projections vitest with BOTH-SIDES boundary pairs (CANCELLED+FAILED => [] AND ACTIVE+FAILED => ['retry'], so the over-refusal mutant dies too; SUPERSEDED+RUNNING => ['cancel','wait'] NOT [], pinning the deliberate cleanup/suspend exemption; SUPERSEDED+QUEUED advance => [], without which the `start` row's deletion is MASKED by startableStepIds' own plan gate). `planAffordancesFor` must fail-closed on an off-contract status. e2e negative affordance (fail the plan, assert step-action-retry count 0) — scope the positive half to `cancel` only until F-26/F-42's dispositions land, or the e2e asserts the OTHER package's fix is wrong. No regen. rph-engine unaffected (drives no UI).

#### WP-16 — Conformance registries and anti-recurrence gates
- *covers:* Anti-recurrence for all six families. No findings of its own.
- *rationale:* Builds SM-6. The SKELETON (harness + empty registries + the selftest file) lands EARLY, immediately after WP-0, and each package adds its rows AS IT LANDS — retrofitting a register is how registers become rubber stamps, and rows authored in the same commit as the guard they certify are the drift mode the design itself warns about, so the vocab-equality assertion must ship WITH the first rows, not as a follow-on. Three registries, one harness: STEP_COMMAND_SPECS (derived negative + positive sweep, marker-based layer attribution, arrow ledger with an explicit bucket precedence), STEP_EVENT_EFFECT (registry totality over the ten step event types), ENFORCEMENT_REGISTER (a live refusal observed through Engine.dispatch for every rule whose ratified `then` is 'the command is rejected', with declaredMutations as data). The final closure lands here: the layer-correct coverage gate must be re-sited (m12-conformance.json has ZERO `then` keys; the 15 reject-shaped clauses live in m11/m10/m9's conformanceTests[]), must account for PREFIX-level coverage (COVERAGE_BY_PREFIX['RPH-EXE'] points at a rph-domain unit test and COVERED_BY_ID holds only 11 ids), and must have a DISCLOSED_UNENFORCED valve with reason + ticket or it red-lines ~15 rules on landing, most outside this remediation, and gets softened on first contact. Same for the dead-kernel gate's DISCLOSED_UNWIRED list, seeded from a RECONCILED source rather than the two stale records.
- *dependsOn:* WP-0 (skeleton); rows accrue from WP-3 onward; gates B and C land after WP-15
- *concurrentWith:* all
- *gate:* Full vitest + the selftest file (each analysis primitive fed LITERAL synthetic input and asserted to REPORT FAILURE). Meta-assertions: distinct (ruleId, refusalMarker) pairs so two rows cannot be satisfied by one refusal; marker >= 20 chars and absent from a literal array of all five precheck messages and assertTransition's text; a probe's only tool is a Harness whose surface is dispatch + stateOf, so no row can be satisfied by a direct predicate call — the exact confusion that produced F-28's false coverage. Sweep asserts its own case counts. No regen, no e2e, no seed change.

#### WP-17 — Documentation, divergence, and residual-record reconciliation
- *covers:* F-38 (doc side), F-42 (record side), plus every disclosed divergence this remediation creates.
- *rationale:* Lands last so the prose describes what actually shipped rather than what was planned. DS-004 §5's three disposition bullets and §6 D7 currently state the OPPOSITE of shipped behaviour ('NEUTRALIZED = source SKIPPED'), and DR-004 §15 affirmatively certifies 'Divergences from DS-004: none intended' — a live, wrong normative statement whose one-sentence digest in DR-004 §3 is exactly what a second-consumer implementer would build from. Required entries in DR-004 §19: the FAILED=PENDING reclassification as a disclosed divergence from D7 (not a restatement of it); the FAILED->CANCELLED authored arrow; the terminal-PWU generalisation beyond RPH-PWU-010's ratified BASELINED; the narrowing of DR-002:185's 'Do NOT add a startExecutionStep guard' ruling; the enumerated refusal-code changes from the ordering swap (the INV-7 pattern JAN-CMDPRE established); the F-42 record correction (DS-004 §6 D6 and DR-004 :177/:185/:191/:199). SPEC-001 gains §5.5 (the ExecutionStep family's nine source sets and the statement that §5.1 is now MECHANIZED rather than hand-discharged). RESIDUALS.md gains the six orphan arrows, the empty-allowlist under-refusal, the tautology-detection non-goal, the NOT_READY legacy population, and the structuredResult individuation gap.
- *dependsOn:* WP-15, WP-16
- *concurrentWith:* none
- *gate:* Docs only, no code. Optional (recommended) machine check: parse SPEC-001 §5.5's table and assert its source-set column set-equals STEP_COMMAND_SPECS, asserting exactly nine rows found so a formatting change fails rather than passes. Full gate re-run once for cleanliness.

### Residual risks
- BLOCKING PRECONDITION, not a risk to accept: F1's FIX-5 as written is unimplementable. `approveDecision` (governance.ts:245-257) refuses `decisionType === 'WAIVER'` outright and the only WAIVER->EFFECTIVE path forces the assurance-plane fields. An implementer who forces the kill test through RequestWaiver with a dummy policy+criterion produces exactly the INV-5 bleed the fix exists to prevent. Scope to REPLAN before sequencing WP-12, or drop the WAIVER limb with the reason recorded.
- F-08's guard is and remains an ENTRY gate. After WP-12 a PWU's SUCCEEDED claim is backed AT THE MOMENT OF THE CLAIM and never re-validated: a plan later SUPERSEDED, FAILED or CANCELLED leaves a standing, unrechecked SUCCEEDED claim. Removing the `currentExecutionState === 'SUCCEEDED'` early exit to close it would RED the reference seed at reference-undertaking.ts:676/:698/:806/:869, which re-assert SUCCEEDED citing assessments rather than the plan. Disclose as a named residual with a pinned test, not as a closed hole. Consequently F1's claim that FIX-6 makes FIX-4's terminal-PWU gate strand nothing is FALSE and must be downgraded — the CLEANUP exemptions remain the only anti-stranding guarantee.
- INV-CLEANUP-ALWAYS-AVAILABLE is FALSE at HEAD and stays false until F-27's stored population is addressed: `cancelExecutionStep.requireFrom` is READY|QUEUED|RUNNING|WAITING and excludes NOT_READY, so a stored NOT_READY step on a LIVE path has no accepted exit. WP-6 closes it by PREVENTION for new plans only. The matrix tests must NOT assert the invariant holds for NOT_READY. State the invariant as aspirational-pending, or an implementer will write a test that is false.
- Legacy stored plans are never re-validated. Propose-time rules gate only NEW proposals; nothing re-validates on load, at Approve, or at Activate — and adding such re-validation would wedge legacy plans fail-closed with no remediation command, which is worse than the defect. The only retroactive reach is the two GRAMMAR changes (WP-7), which move fail-OPEN to fail-CLOSED via the shared schema. A stored plan with an unrecorded BRANCH selection becomes INDETERMINATE after WP-4: neither startable nor prunable, exits only via waivered Skip per downstream step, a plan revision, or CancelExecutionPlan. This repo contains no such plan (the demo DB is regenerated by seed-workbench), but the wedge is real and the refusal message must name the authorized exits.
- Tautology detection is DELIBERATELY not attempted. `.min(1)` on ALL/ANY closes the empty-combinator slip only; `{op:'ATTEMPTS', cmp:'>=', value:0}` and `OUTPUT_COUNT >= 0` remain unconditionally true against any subject (the `?? 0` floors make the counts non-negative by construction) and produce a byte-identical outcome. A partial syntactic detector catching `>= 0` but not `> -1` would itself be a vacuity magnet. Record the disposition explicitly as 'fixed narrowly on the ANTI-VACUITY ground, NOT on the fail-open-guard ground the raisers claimed', or the next reviewer will infer that tautology detection was attempted and failed.
- `structuredResult` individuation gap survives WP-11. An AI step that names one floor-satisfied artifact AND ALSO ships a large `structuredResult` still gets that inline content into the event stream un-individuated — the §8.4 L844 gap already disclosed at floor-gate.ts:116-118. Closing it means requiring structuredResult content to be recorded as an Artifact, which is a contract change and a behaviour change for every AI step. Registered as an open question, not silently carried. Sharpened by WP-10, which gives that content real control-flow authority.
- The corroboration limb added in WP-11 (`outputBindings` declared => `noOutputResult` refused) does not fully close the free-assertion class. A step declaring NO outputBindings can still assert its way past the floor with an empty structuredResult, and the resulting SUCCEEDED is real authority (it satisfies STEP_SUCCEEDED grammar guards, it is the `some(SUCCEEDED)` the PWU-backing rule accepts, and it is terminal-success for the start gate and plan completion). Register it as an explicit ENFORCEMENT_REGISTER row rather than leaving it implicit.
- The two-key conformance ledger's disclosed limit: a deliberate widening edited consistently across production, the expected ledger and the arrow ledger PASSES the gate. That is intended — the mechanism converts a silent one-character mutation into a three-file, justification-bearing, reviewable change; it cannot and should not forbid documented contract evolution. The eight longhand kill tests are retained as a NON-derived floor precisely for this reason. The sharper version of the risk: the INITIAL transcription of both keys is done by the same implementer in the same changeset from the same source, so the friction does not exist for the transcription — which is where the vocab divergence would get baked in. Deriving one key from the ratified vocab is what makes the argument load-bearing.
- Vocab `drivesFrom` is STALE in two verified places and the correction direction is not symmetric. StartExecutionStep declares READY|QUEUED while the code declares QUEUED, and the vocab's implied READY->RUNNING is NOT an arrow in the m2 machine at all — adopting the vocab would widen Start to accept READY and then have checkTransition refuse it one line later with the machine's generic message, defeating the marker-based layer attribution the whole conformance design rests on. Three shipped files (command-reissue-guard.test.ts:15, kit.ts:494, intent.ts:240) additionally state that `drivesFrom` has NO ratified authority and that requireFrom is deliberately hand-authored from the machine's in-arrows. Resolve the doc-vs-doc conflict in WP-1 or an implementer hits it head-on.
- After WP-14 the UI can offer Start for a step whose authored binding resolves REQUESTED/DENIED/REVOKED — the projection has no binding resolver and `ExecutionPlanInput` carries no PWU lifecycle field either, so WP-12's PWU-openness axis is likewise unmirrorable. Both are fresh F-11 breaches created by this remediation's own fixes. Either thread the two facts into the projection input in the same commits, or DISCLOSE both in INV-AFF-1's enumerated exemption allowlist. Do not leave them unstated: the family that goes out of its way to protect that invariant would otherwise be the one that re-breaks it.
- `aiNoBinding` becomes truthful only for streams written AFTER WP-14. Any pre-existing stream still renders the self-contradiction F-31 filed (the advisory two lines below the step row that shows the binding). Worse, the binding now lives in TWO places (the authored step and the Started event) with nothing pinning them equal — the family's own root cause one layer down. The systemic alternative exists and is cheap: `executionAttempts` already takes `stepTypeById` from the caller's plan aggregate for exactly this reason, so threading `runtimeBindingIdByStep` the same way makes the advisory single-sourced and retroactive, with the event recording kept as provenance and a test that the two agree.
- Prune provenance records ONE cut. When a dead step is reachable from two independent BRANCH cuts, the tie-break is `plan.transitions[]` array order — deterministic, replay-stable, and the same total order first-match already relies on, but a CHOICE. The event schema's fields are scalars; recording the full exclusion set would be a larger contract change. Flag rather than defect.
- The conformance gate's layer-correct coverage limb will name work outside this remediation on the day it lands (~15 reject-shaped rules across m11/m10/m9, most of them RPH-DEC/RPH-GOV). Without a DISCLOSED_UNENFORCED valve carrying reason + ticket it will be softened on first contact and become the rubber stamp it exists to prevent. Same for the dead-kernel gate, which will surface predicates beyond this family (`canStartStep` is dead after WP-14 inlines its parts; the dead-kernel census already lists candidates).
- Two records currently assert `canResumeExecutionOnPwu` is DEFERRED (hollow-kernel-triage.md:45 -> W2/W3 WP-2-004/007; docs/_working/dead-kernel-census.txt:17). Wiring it in WP-12 without retiring both leaves THREE artefacts disagreeing about the rule's status and seeds the dead-kernel gate's allow-list from a stale source. Retire them in the same commit.
- The ordering swap in WP-8 changes refusal CODES (never accept/reject outcomes) for wrong-state commands carrying invalid payloads. The pre-landing grep came back clean, but this is the one change in the remediation whose blast radius is measured by absence of evidence rather than presence of it. Two named watch items: start-gate.test.ts:295-296 asserts the drivesFrom message verbatim (the marker refactor must preserve the prefix byte-for-byte), and execution-plan.e2e.ts:332 asserts only `not.toBe('ACCEPTED')` on a QUEUED step, so it will start passing for a NEW reason while silently ceasing to prove the machine guard it was written for — give it a valid payload so the refusal it asserts is the refusal it names.
- WP-4 widens PruneExecutionStep (the downstream of a CANCELLED/SUPERSEDED predecessor becomes prunable) — and Prune is the command whose unchecked form was a universal §21.1 waiver bypass. The argument that this cannot launder a plan to success rests ENTIRELY on `completeExecutionPlan`'s allow-list (execution.ts:483-491, pinned by execution-plan-completion.test.ts:160 'REJECTS completing with a CANCELLED or SUPERSEDED step'). Name that test as a load-bearing co-dependency that no other package may relax.
- WP-5 gives an abandoned arm's plan no route to COMPLETED (a CANCELLED step fails the §20.1 allow-list), so such a plan must end via FailExecutionPlan or CancelExecutionPlan. Whether 'abandon an optional arm and still complete the plan' is a capability the sponsor wants is a §21.1 governance question, not an interpreter question, and it is out of scope here. Similarly, DR-004's 'a neutralized in-edge must not count as a real contribution' remains unimplemented: WP-4 removes the causality INVERSION for FAILED arms, but the 'join ran on partial input and nothing records it' shape survives for branch-excluded and pruned arms and is DELIBERATELY re-created for an abandoned one. Either record the neutralized in-edge set on ExecutionStepStarted, or disclose that the governed record of the partial input is the ExecutionStepCancelled(reason) event on the arm — with a kill test that the reason is present and non-empty.
- Plan size is unbounded. rph-contracts declares `steps`/`transitions` as arrays with no `.max()`, and `transitionRows` renders a per-edge disposition on every page render. WP-2 makes the cost O(V+E), but memory and render remain unbounded and there is no propose-time size cap. A cheap hardening, but a contract change outside every family's scope — record it with a named recipient rather than as an open question with no owner.
- Sequencing fragility worth stating plainly: three orderings are load-bearing and a violation is silent rather than loud. WP-4 without WP-5 in the same increment is an enumerated capability removal with no replacement. WP-11 landing after WP-10 means unassessed AI content acquires branch authority before the floor gate can see it. WP-9's kill tests written before WP-8's reorder are written to the wrong refusal codes and will need rewriting. None of these produces a red test if violated — they produce a correct-looking green tree with a hole in it, which is precisely how this family of defects was built.
